- Main follows the CLI's rule for symbolic links: the skill folder itself may be a link, which main resolves first, and any link inside it must point inside that resolved folder, or the skill is refused with a reason that names the link. Cycles are detected.
- Main parses no YAML and checks no content rule. The controller checks every skill with one function ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md) section 2, `skill`), and its refusals show under the skill's row in the import dialog. A second check in main could only disagree with it.
- The renderer uploads each archive with client-core's `skill.import`, one skill at a time, and holds one archive at a time.
- The renderer never sends a path, only a skill's name from main's last `Picked` answer. With the rule for links, a compromised renderer can therefore read nothing but the folder the user picked in the dialog.
- Main remembers the last picked folder. The renderer holds only the path to show, which no channel takes, so main keeps no copy of data the renderer holds ([Rules](#rules), rule 7).
- At most 10 MiB cross the bridge per call, and one call runs at a time.

`folder.pick` is unchanged: it describes a git repository for New project, a different answer from a skill folder's.

## Native behaviour

Each item below is an acceptance criterion. The end-to-end test checks it where it can.

- **Title bar:**
  - `titleBarStyle: "hiddenInset"`, with the traffic lights sitting in the sidebar's top strip. *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* While the sidebar is hidden, they sit over the main pane's top band ([The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout)).
  - `trafficLightPosition: { x: 19, y: 16 }` puts the native lights where the Bureau pages draw them: centres at 26, 46 and 66 pt from the left, 24 pt from the top. The renderer draws no lights of its own.
  - The top 52 pt of the window drags it, across its full width. The shell draws this as one strip over the top of the window that paints nothing and is not a compositing layer; the sidebar's top strip and the thread's chrome row both sit inside it. Each control placed in the band is marked `no-drag`, so it takes clicks.
- **No flash:**
  - The window is created hidden, with `backgroundColor` set to `--bg` of the current appearance: `#f4f3f0` for Whitehaven and `#1a1310` for Orient Express. These are the sRGB values Chromium draws for the `oklch` tokens, and a unit test derives them from `tokens.css`. *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* The colour is `--bg` of the theme in use, any of Bureau's five ([Settings](#settings) › Appearance).
  - It is shown when the frame that draws its first screen, fonts included, has reached the window, or 3 seconds after its page first painted, whichever is first. It is also shown at once when its page fails to load or its renderer exits. Showing on `ready-to-show` would show an empty page for a few frames: `ready-to-show` fires when the bare HTML first paints, 50 to 80 ms before the first screen has painted.
  - The 3-second limit means a renderer that fails before it reports still gets its window. It sits well above the "connecting" screen's 1-second delay, because that delay starts only once the renderer's code has loaded and its router has started, and fonts and a few frames follow it. So a healthy renderer shows its window by reporting, even on a slow Mac's cold launch. A crash or a failed load does not wait for the limit.
  - The renderer reports its first screen through the IPC contract once the frame that draws it has been presented: it times a sentinel element that draws nothing with Element Timing, whose entry arrives only once its frame has been presented, or has failed to present. A frame that fails to present is rare; the window then shows a frame early, and the screen appears with the next frame. Two animation frames are not enough, because the second can run before the first frame has reached the window. The "connecting" screen reports too, so a slow controller does not keep the window hidden.
  - When the appearance changes, main updates the background colour. *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* So does a change on the Appearance page.
- **Theme follows the system, live:** Whitehaven when macOS is light, Orient Express when it is dark, through `prefers-color-scheme`. ~~Bureau's other three themes, and a glass setting, arrive with Settings.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* These are the defaults. Appearance picks the day and the night theme, or one theme that does not follow the system, and the glass level ([Settings](#settings)).
- **Accessibility settings:**
  - Reduce transparency sets `--glass-level` to 0, which removes the blur completely.
  - Reduce motion stops every animation.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* Each is on when macOS's setting is on or the Appearance page's is. The app's toggle can add the setting, never remove macOS's.
- **Window size:**
  - The first launch opens a 1440 × 900 pt window, the size of the Bureau pages, centred on the display. On a smaller display it fills the work area.
  - The window cannot be made smaller than 800 × 500 pt. Below a width of 776 an empty thread's composer no longer fits the main pane, and below a height of 440 its start cards no longer fit.
- **Window state is remembered:** size, position and full-screen. A position that no longer lands on a display is moved onto the nearest one.
- **Closing the window hides it.** `⌘W` and the red light hide the window, the dock icon shows it again, and `⌘Q` quits. The app keeps running while the window is hidden, so the dock badge and notifications keep working.
- **The last open thread reopens at launch.** *(Amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* The app reopens what was open when it quit:
  - The renderer stores the open thread's id per controller URL. Opening a thread stores it; leaving it for a screen that is not a thread removes it, so a quit on the new-thread screen launches on the new-thread screen.
  - When the stored thread is gone at launch, the id is removed and the new-thread screen shows, because the user did not ask for that thread this time. A gone thread the user opens during use shows "This thread was not found." with a link to start a new thread.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* With Appearance's Open on set to The office, a launch opens the Office instead.
- *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394) §12.)* **~~The last open thread~~ The last screen reopens at launch.** It replaces the stored thread above:
  - The renderer stores the last screen per controller URL, in place of the thread id. Every screen counts: a thread, the new-thread screen, an assistant's Conversation, the Office, Intake, and each later screen with no rule of its own.
  - Settings, the first run, the connect screen and sign-in are not stored. Opening one leaves the stored screen as it was, so Intake, then Settings, then a quit reopens Intake.
  - Only the screen is restored: not Intake's tab or view, not a selection, not a scroll position. The split's widths are kept as they always are.
  - A stored screen that is gone at launch, such as a deleted thread, falls back to the new-thread screen, as a gone thread does above. *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796).)* A stored thread that has a saved copy draws from the copy first. Once the read of the newer rows answers `not_found`, the app goes to the new-thread screen, as it does for any stored screen that is gone at launch ([Thread copies](#thread-copies)).
  - A thread id stored before this change reads as that thread's screen, so the first launch after the update still reopens the thread.
  - When the app restarts itself to install an update, it should reopen exactly where the user was, Settings section included, so they can look at the new version. The app does not update itself yet ([Post-v1](#post-v1)), so this waits for that.
  - With Open on set to The office, a launch still opens the Office.
- **Menu:**
  - The standard app, Edit and Window menus, so text editing shortcuts work in every field. *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#767](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/767).)* Edit holds the items of Electron's own Edit menu on macOS, spelled out, except Undo `⌘Z` and Redo `⇧⌘Z`. They are items of the app's own, because the roles' Undo and Redo ask Chromium to step through its own edit history, and the message field keeps a history of its own. Chromium sends the page `historyUndo` only when it has an edit to undo, and a paste into an empty message field is not one, so the role's Undo does nothing there. Chromium sends `historyRedo` only for an edit it undid itself, and the message field undoes by itself, so the role's Redo does nothing. (With the keyboard the editor takes ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z before the menu does, so only choosing the item with the mouse is affected.) Choosing one asks main to step in the web contents that has the keyboard focus, and it never shows or focuses a window, as the roles never did. For the app's page, main sends the page an `undo` or `redo` menu command and the page steps in the focused field (see **The message field** under **The thread** in [Design system](#design-system)). For another web contents of the app, such as the developer tools, main asks it to step natively. When no web contents of the app has the focus, a native control has it, such as the search field of the folder sheet, and main sends `undo:` or `redo:` to the macOS first responder, as the roles did, so that control steps its own history. With the window hidden or minimized nothing visible changes. They are enabled while signed out too, because every screen has text fields.
  - The menus, in order: the app menu, File, Edit, Go, Thread, Window. The development build adds View, with Reload and Toggle Developer Tools, after Edit.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* The release build has a View menu too, after Edit: View › Threads `⌥⌘1` and View › Hercule `⌥⌘2` switch [the sidebar's face](#the-hercule-face). They are `⌥⌘` because `⌘1` to `⌘9` already pick an item in Go. The development build adds Reload and Toggle Developer Tools to the same menu, under a separator.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* Below a separator, View › Toggle Sidebar `⌘B` hides and shows the sidebar ([The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout)). It is enabled only while signed in, as the faces are. The development build's items come after it, under a separator of their own.
  - *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* Go › Intake `⌘⇧I`, after Go › Office, opens [Intake](#intake).
  - *(Added 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* Go › Workflows `⌘⇧W`, after Go › Intake, opens [Workflows](#workflows). `⌘⇧W` is free in every menu the app builds, its roles' menus included. Some Mac apps use it for Close Window; this app's File › Close Window is `⌘W`, so nothing collides.
  - File › New Thread `⌘N`.
  - Thread › Send `⌘↵`. *(Amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* It is always enabled. It sends what the open thread's composer or the draft holds, as ⏎ in the message field does, and does nothing when there is nothing to send.
  - *(Added 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* Go › Office `⌘⇧O`, the first item of Go, opens [the Office](#the-office).
  - Go › the first nine threads of the sidebar, `⌘1` to `⌘9`, in sidebar order. *(Amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* Each thread is listed once, by its title: a waiting thread is listed where "Waiting on you" shows it, and a thread a "more" row hides is not listed. With no thread, and while signed out, Go holds one dimmed "No Threads". *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453).)* A waiting assistant is listed too, by its name, where Waiting on you shows it, so `⌘1` to `⌘9` count the items in sidebar order. "No Threads" shows only when there is nothing to list. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* A row of sessions is listed once, by its title. Its unfolded lines and the Done shelf's rows are not listed. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* A group is listed once, where the list shows it first, top to bottom, and the item opens what that first place opens. So a group with a session waiting on the user is listed where Waiting on you shows it, and opens the session that asks, not the row's session; its row in the project adds no second item.
  - While the project picker is open, `⌘1` to `⌘9` pick a project instead, as spec 14 says. Choosing a thread in Go with the mouse closes the picker.
  - Sign Out, in the app menu.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* Settings… `⌘,`, in the app menu under About, opens [Settings](#settings).
  - *(Amended 2026-10-05, [#410](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/410).)* File › New Thread, Go › Office and Settings… are dimmed while signed out, as Sign Out is, because only the shell carries them out: on the connect and sign-in screens, choosing one would do nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* So are Go › Intake, View › Threads and View › Hercule. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* So is Go › Workflows.
- **Dock badge:** the number of threads waiting on you. A thread waits on you while its session has an open Request (~~`Session.openRequest`~~ `Session.openRequests` is not empty, whoever asked: its own agent or a subagent *(amended 2026-10-05, [#355](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/355))*, [./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md)). *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* The badge counts waiting sessions: a Thread's, and an Agent session's outside runs and assistants, as Waiting on you lists them.
  - *(Amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* macOS shows an app's dock badge only once the user has allowed the app to notify. The app asks when the user signs in, so the badge can show from the first waiting thread.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453).)* The badge also counts the assistants waiting on you. An assistant waits on you while its current session, the newest session of its main conversation, has an open Request (see [The assistant](#design-system)).
  - *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394) §3.)* The badge keeps one meaning, an agent is blocked on you, so it counts threads and assistants only, never signals. Intake's count shows on the sidebar instead ([The Hercule face](#the-hercule-face)). Waiting on you stays agent Requests only, for the same reason. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* Waiting on you also lists "From runs", the sessions of live runs that wait on the user ([Workflows](#workflows)). Those rows are agent Requests too, but the badge and the notifications do not count them, and the Go menu does not list them. A run's agent waits inside a workflow's work, not in a conversation the user holds, and one busy workflow could otherwise fill the badge. Whether they should count is post-v1 ([Post-v1](#post-v1)).
- **Notifications:**
  - When a thread starts waiting on you and the window is not focused, main shows a native notification.
  - Clicking it focuses the window and opens the thread.
  - *(Amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* The notification's title is the thread's title and its body is the question, such as "Run git push?".
  - A thread has at most one notification. A new Request on the thread replaces it; when the new Request opens while the window is focused, the old notification is removed and no new one shows.
  - *(Added 2026-10-05, [#355](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/355); [#353](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/353).)* A thread can have several Requests open at once. Its notification shows the newest, and its body ends with "+N more waiting" when others are open. A subagent's Request names it: the body starts with "<subagent> asks:". Clicking the notification opens the thread, where the dock pages through every Request. When a Request closes and others stay open, the notification is not shown again.
  - Launching the app, or signing in, shows no notification for the threads that already wait; the badge counts them.
  - Signing out, or connecting to another controller, hides the badge, removes every notification and empties Go. They stay empty until the user signs in again.
  - *(Added 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453).)* An assistant waiting on you gets a notification by the same rules as a thread. Its title is the assistant's name, and clicking it opens the assistant's Conversation, `/assistants/$assistantId`. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* While the Office is open, the click opens the Conversation in the Office's drawer instead ([The Office](#the-office)).
  - *(Added 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453).)* Main keys each notification by where its click goes: the thread, or the assistant, never the session. So an assistant has at most one notification, as a thread does, also when rotation gives its Conversation a new session.
  - *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394) §3.)* **A new Now signal** gets a notification while the window is not focused: a signal of priority `urgent` that arrives on To do. No other signal notifies.
    - Its title is the signal's source and its body is the signal's title, such as "PagerDuty" and "Checkout error rate above 5%".
    - Clicking it focuses the window and opens Intake with that signal selected.
    - A Now signal back from snooze counts as new, so it notifies again. A snoozed signal never notifies ([./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#97-snooze-and-not-urgent)).
    - The rules above hold for it too: launching the app or signing in shows no notification for Now signals already open; signing out or connecting to another controller removes them.
    - A signal that leaves Now, because it is resolved anywhere, snoozed or lowered with Not urgent, loses its notification when the `signal` push arrives.
- **Answered anywhere clears everywhere** ([ADR 0027](../adr/0027-a-decision-resolves-when-its-question-is-answered-wherever.md)):
  - When a Request is answered anywhere - here, in the web app or from the CLI - its notification is removed and the badge drops, at the moment the `session` nudge arrives.
  - A Request opening or closing already sends that nudge ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Live model).
- **Links:** external links open in the default browser, and the window itself never navigates.
- **No signs of a web page:**
  - The chrome cannot be selected as text. Transcript text, code and fields can.
  - Buttons and rows keep the arrow cursor, as native controls do.
  - The window does not bounce as a whole. Scroll areas keep macOS's elastic scrolling.
  - There is no pinch zoom and no page zoom.
  - A focus ring shows only for keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`).
  - ~~Scroll bars are the system's overlay scroll bars, unstyled, so they follow the System Settings choice.~~ Scroll bars are thin and drawn in the theme's ink, with no track behind them. *(Amended 2026-10-20.)* macOS draws classic scroll bars, 15px wide on a white track, whenever a mouse is attached, and they clash with every theme. `scrollbar-width: thin` and a `scrollbar-color` of the ink at 30% on a transparent track replace them (`styles/base.css`). The thumb darkens to the ink at 50% while the pointer is over the scroll area; CSS cannot target the thumb itself. `scrollbar-color` is declared on every element, so a hover restyles only the hovered elements: 0.1 ms for a 3,900-element list, against 1.6 ms when it is set once on the root and overridden on hover. Both macOS styles keep their behaviour: with a trackpad the bar is an overlay that takes no room and fades out while the list is still, and with a mouse the bar is 11px wide (`--scrollbar-width`). The `::-webkit-scrollbar` pseudo-elements stay unused, because they always take room, even where macOS draws an overlay. The cost is CSS alone: no process, no timer and no work while idle, and +206 bytes of CSS in the bundle (+42 gzipped). It does add a small style cost on a theme change and on a pointer move, and nothing is measured yet on a streaming transcript in the packaged app; both are recorded in [Measured](#measured).
  - Right-clicking text opens the native context menu (Copy, Look Up, Search). Right-clicking chrome opens nothing.
- **The composer draws the ~~`+` attach and~~ voice ~~buttons~~ button, with nothing behind ~~them~~ it yet.** ~~They keep~~ It keeps the composer's full shape, as in the web app ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §App shell). ~~They show their~~ It shows its hover and pressed states, and a click does nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-08, [#465](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/465): `+` attach now attaches images; see [Images in prompts](#images-in-prompts).)*

## Design system

**The pixel reference.** The pixel reference is the Crew Bureau book and its desktop pages, in [`docs/design/crew-bureau/`](../design/crew-bureau/). The folder is a copy of `prototype/design-systems-2/c1-bureau/` at commit a130074e on the `prototype/design-systems` branch, kept byte for byte and never edited. Its pages link to two sibling folders, copied the same way beside it: `docs/design/shared/` (the book's frame scripts and the brief) and `docs/design/c0-crew/` (the original Crew, for the book's before-and-after frames). Open `docs/design/crew-bureau/index.html` in a browser to read the book.

*(Amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* **The pixel reference is now the book's second edition,** in [`docs/design/crew-bureau-2/`](../design/crew-bureau-2/). Open `docs/design/crew-bureau-2/index.html` to read it. The second edition started as a copy of the first, and #313 changed it in three ways:

- The Office's scene styles moved to `office.css`, and `office.js` shares its drawing code, so the first run furnishes the same room. The Office page draws the same pixels as before.
- It adds the First run chapter and its page, `desktop/first-run.html` ([The first run](#the-first-run)).
- `crew.js` and `desktop/session-empty.html` gain the fresh install: Hercule's look, and the draft's starter threads and intake note (**A new thread**, below).

The first edition stays in `docs/design/crew-bureau/`, byte for byte as above, as the record of what #275 was built from. Everything below that names "the book" means the second edition, and `pnpm compare:bureau` compares the app with it.

- Where this text and the pages disagree, the pages decide a measurement and this text decides a behaviour. This is spec 14's rule for its prototypes.
- Behaviour comes from [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md): §App shell (the Threads face), §The thread surface, and §The composer is the thread's configuration.

**Where Bureau changes a behaviour, the desktop follows Bureau.** The changes the first milestone takes from the book's Spec section:

1. **Waiting on you tops the sidebar,** above the threads. ~~The first milestone has only the Threads face, so the section sits at the sidebar's top.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512).)* It sits above both faces, under the face switch, as the book draws it ([The Hercule face](#the-hercule-face)).
2. **No monospace outside code.** Monospace is kept for code, commands and diffs. Spec 14's monospace time separators, workspace labels and branch values use the UI face.
3. **Six marks.** Queued, cancelled and skipped lose their marks and become words.

**A thread's face is seeded by its session id,** not by its title. The book derives a face from a name. ~~A thread's title is written by the agent after the first message and can change, and a face must not change when its title does.~~ *(Amended 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): a thread's title is set once, from the opening prompt's first non-empty line, as the contract's `Session.title` says. The id still seeds the face, because two threads can open with the same line, and each is its own colleague.)* The book's stored look (its Spec change 3) is for workflows and assistants, and arrives with them.

**A face shows its colleague's state as a pose.** The eight poses, as the book draws them. Every pose is still, except that the working pose's paws type while its turn runs ([Rules](#rules), rule 2). Slice 4 maps a session's state to a pose in `@hercule/client-core`.

| Pose | Read to assistive technology as | Drawn | Mark |
|---|---|---|---|
| working | working | lowered eyes with a glint, brows, a flat mouth, paws on a typewriter | yes |
| waiting | waiting on you | wide eyes, brows, an "o" mouth, a raised arm with a `--you` palm | yes |
| idle | idle | eyes, a smile | yes |
| asleep | asleep | closed eyes, a small mouth, two z's, desaturated | no |
| failed | failed | eyes, brows, a wavy mouth, a plaster, a red badge with an X | yes |
| paused | paused | line eyes, a flat mouth, an outlined badge | yes |
| done | done | smiling eyes, a smile, a green badge with a check | yes |
| away | can't be reached | offset dots, a dotted mouth, an outlined badge, faded and desaturated | no |

A face's accessible name is its label and its pose's words: "Fix 3-D Secure checkout for EU cards, waiting on you".

**A thread's pose** *(added 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275))* is decided by `decideThreadPose` in `@hercule/client-core`. The first row that matches wins. The sidebar row's end is decided with it (`decideThreadRowEnd`):

| The session | Pose | The row's end |
|---|---|---|
| has an open Request (any agent's, *amended 2026-10-05, [#355](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/355)*) | waiting | the waiting mark |
| is held by the crash-loop guard, or has exited and cannot be resumed | away | its age |
| runs on a runner that is offline or unreachable | away | the word "offline", or its age once the session has exited |
| has exited and can be resumed | asleep | its age |
| is queued | working | the word "queued" |
| is starting or busy | working | the working mark |
| is idle | idle | its age |

- Waiting comes first, because Waiting on you is defined by the open Request. A waiting thread on an offline runner stays waiting: the Request is still the user's to answer, though the answer reaches the agent only when the runner returns.
- A runner the runners list does not hold counts as reachable, because a missing cache entry is not evidence.
- failed, paused and done are never produced yet: a session does not record why it ended, so the app cannot tell done from failed. [#278](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/278) adds the end reason.
- [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) (#162) calls an exited, resumable thread Idle, "indistinguishable from one whose process is running". The desktop draws its row exactly like an idle one (its age, no mark), so that holds for what is drawn. It names the pose asleep, the word [CONTEXT.md](../../CONTEXT.md) gives an assistant's session in the same state, so the foot's idle count means threads that are loaded and free, and the accessible name says "asleep".

**The sidebar** *(added 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275))* follows spec 14's Threads face and the book's session page, with these differences:

- **Sections are capped, as the book's swarm state draws them.** A "more" row expands its section in place.
  - Waiting on you shows its 3 newest threads, then "`<n>` more waiting on you". *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453).)* Waiting threads and waiting assistants share the section and its cap: the 3 with the newest activity show, whichever they are. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* It lists sessions, one entry for each Thread or Agent session that waits, with the row's name.
  - ~~A project shows at most 5 threads, then "`<n>` more threads".~~ *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* A project shows at most 5 rows, then "`<n>` more", which counts rows (the label reads "3 more"). The 5 are chosen in this order: waiting, then working (queued, starting, busy), then the rest; ~~the newest activity first within each~~ *(amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626))* within each, the most recent first by the later of the row's last activity and when it last started (created or reopened), so a thread reopened from the Done shelf is never hidden behind "more" by newer idle threads; ties broken by session id. ~~The selected thread is always shown as well, so a project can show 6.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* The row that holds the open session is always shown as well, so a project can show 6 rows. The lines of an unfolded row never count toward the 5.
  - Shown rows keep ~~the grouped order~~ the project's order, newest created first *(amended 2026-10-08, [#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473))*. ~~A workspace with no shown thread is left out, with its label.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-08, [#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473): there are no workspace labels.)*
  - Expanding is not stored: a relaunch collapses every section. The book draws no way to collapse one, so there is none.
- ~~**Threads are grouped by project, then by workspace,** as spec 14 says. The book draws no workspace label, so the label is Bureau's lane label, in the UI face (item 2 above). A lane label that follows a row has 8px more space above it than one under a project header, so it does not read as the row's third line. A row's second line is the model's name, because the lane label already names the workspace.~~
  *(Amended 2026-10-08, [#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473).)* **Threads are grouped by project only, newest created first.** Spec 14 groups a project's threads by workspace; the desktop does not, so a project reads as one list, as T3 Code's sidebar does.
  - ~~A row has three lines: the title and its end, the model's name, and the workspace in `--faint`: the branch for a worktree, `<repo> · <machine>` for a main workspace, "No workspace" for none. The machine's name is never cut short. The row is 59px tall with its text centred, which leaves about 5px above and below the text, so neighbouring rows read as separate threads.~~
  - *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* **A thread row has two lines** and is 52px tall: 6px of padding above and below, 6px between the lines, and 2px between rows. The book still draws the 35px row, so `compare:bureau` redraws the book's rows as the app draws them before it compares the sidebar.
    - Line 1 is the title, 13/18 at `--w-medium`, and the status slot at its end. The slot shows, in this order: Working in `--accent-ink`; Approval or Question in `--you-ink`, bold, while a Request waits; Queued; Offline; otherwise the age in `--faint`. There is no Failed word until the session has a failed state ([#278](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/278)), and Working shows no duration, because no field says since when the thread has been busy.
    - Line 2, 12/16, is the provider's mark at 12px, then the detail: the branch when a person named it (`main` counts), else the main workspace as `<repo> · <machine>`, else the machine. A branch the controller generated, `hercule/thread-<hex>`, is never shown in a row: it tells threads apart by nothing a person knows. A long detail is cut in the middle, so the end of a branch and the machine's name stay. The draft's row follows the same rule.
    - The model's name leaves the row for the hover card and the row's accessible description.
    - A working row is drawn at 72% opacity, and at full opacity under the pointer or with focus, so the threads that need nothing from the user recede. The open thread's row never recedes.
  - ~~*(Amended 2026-10-08, [#484](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/484).)* **A thread row's age is the time since the thread was created**, the time the list is sorted by, so the order and the labels agree. The age in the header's tabs stays the time since the thread's last activity.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* **A thread row's age is the time since the thread's last activity,** as in the header's tabs. The age tells the user how stale a thread is; the order tells them where to find it, so the two no longer need to agree.
  - ~~Inside a project, threads are sorted by when they were created, newest first, ties by session id. Projects are sorted by their newest thread's creation time,~~ *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* Inside a project, threads are sorted by when they last started, newest first, ties by session id: when the thread was created, or reopened after being marked Done, whichever is later. A reopened thread climbs to the top, because reopening is the user picking it up again. Projects are sorted by when their newest thread last started, then by project id, the draft's project first and "No project" last. Sorting by latest activity made a row climb to the top whenever its thread worked, so the list moved under the user's pointer; when a thread last started changes only when the user reopens it. The cap above still picks waiting and working threads first, so a hidden thread that starts working appears in its place.
  - Hovering a thread row for 400ms shows a details card beside the sidebar: the full title, the project, the machine, the branch and the model with its provider's mark. *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* The card names the branch even when the controller generated it. It is one glass popover for the whole list, takes no pointer events and no focus, and hides when the pointer leaves the rows, on a click, on scroll and when the window is resized. Moving to another row while it shows switches it at once. The row's accessible description carries the same details. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* The card of a row of 2 or more sessions also lists the row's sessions, one line each: its label, its dot, its model with its provider's mark, and its status. It stays read-only and inert, as above. A lone Thread's card is unchanged. The sessions come most urgent first (waiting, then working, then the rest, in the row's order among equals), the order of the dots, and the card lists 8 at most. When a live update adds sessions while the pointer rests on the row, the card measures itself again and stays inside the window.
  - A row whose composer holds unsent text or images, and the draft's row, take a light tint and a pencil before the title, so unsent work is found again. Picks alone do not count.
  - The workspace label's `+` and its workspace-details button leave the sidebar: the thread header's `+` and the composer's workspace button do the same. A workspace no thread uses, kept or failed, is no longer shown in the sidebar; [#472](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/472) gives it a place.
- **A project header has no thread count,** where spec 14 puts one: the book draws none, and the pages decide what is drawn. *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* A header whose threads wait on the user shows "`<n>` need you", or "1 needs you", in `--you-ink`, before its `+`, so a collapsed project still shows that it needs the user.
- *(Added 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* **Marking a thread done.** The book draws no Done action; the controller's `session.markDone` and `session.reopen` back these.
  - Under the pointer or with the keyboard's focus, a Done button, "Mark done", crossfades over a row's status slot. Pressing it takes the row off its project at once, before the controller answers, and shows a toast at the bottom of the list: "Marked done" with Undo, which reopens the thread. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* The button belongs to the row, not to one Thread: it marks every Thread of the row that is not yet Done, in one `session.markDone` call, and the toast reads "Marked done" for one and "Marked 3 done" for three. Undo reopens exactly that list in one `session.reopen` call. The row leaves its project at once only when that makes every Thread Done and every Agent session ended; a live Agent session keeps the row in the list, with the Done Threads among its quiet lines. The toast shows for five seconds, and stays while the pointer is on it.
  - A move the controller refuses puts the row back where the controller has it, and the toast shows the error as an alert, with no Undo.
  - A done thread whose Request still waits stays in Waiting on you, because the Request still needs the user. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* A Done Thread that still waits stays listed there, and in the row's lines, but the row's status, dots and second line ignore it.
- *(Added 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* **The Done shelf** sits at the bottom of the thread list: a "Done" header with the number of done threads, closed by default. Whether it is open is kept in memory only, so a relaunch closes it. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* The shelf holds rows, not Threads: its header counts rows, and the bullets below say "row" where they say "thread".
  - Open, it lists the 10 most recently marked done, newest first by when they were marked done, then a "Show 25 more" row while more are hidden.
  - A done row is drawn at 60% opacity. Its detail is the time since it was marked done, its status slot holds a Reopen button, and ~~it has no hover card~~ *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* it has a hover card, the same as a row in a project: the full title, the project, the machine and the branch, then each session of the row, Agent sessions too. Reopening puts the row back at the top of its project. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* Reopen reopens every Thread of the row, with one Undo ("Reopened" or "Reopened 3"). The row climbs to the top of its project because reopening moves when the row last started.
  - The selected thread, when it is done, always has a row on the shelf, even when the shelf is closed or the row is past the ones shown.
  - The shelf's rows are part of the virtualized list, so a shelf of hundreds of threads mounts no more rows than fit.
- *(Added 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* **What the rows and the Done moves cost.** No process, no polling, no live topic and no new read: a move writes the thread list already read and reads it again once the controller answers. The only timer is the toast's five seconds, running only while a toast shows. The Done button's crossfade and a working row's recede change only opacity. A row shrinks from 59px to 52px, so the virtualized list mounts a few more rows for the same height. Bundle bytes: two small modules in the shell's chunk, the Done moves and the toast, and the shelf's and the status slot's functions in `@hercule/client-core`. No number is recorded in [Measured](#measured): the perf script measures launch, memory, streaming and the sidebar's hide, show and drag, but not how many rows a list of 500 threads mounts or how many rows a live push redraws. Measuring those needs a new scenario in the perf script. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* "What rows of sessions cost", below, states the cost of the grouped rows, and the read that feeds them replaces the one this bullet names.
- *(Added 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* **A row is the sessions of one workspace in which a Thread works.** Until now a row was one Thread. The sidebar now holds Threads and Agent sessions (`Session.kind` `thread` and `agent`, [spec 02](./02-domain-model.md) Session), and `buildSessionGroups` in `@hercule/client-core` makes the rows. Where this document says "thread row", read "row": a row of one session is exactly the row described above, with no dots, no unfold, the same hover card and the same Done button.
  - **Who is in a row.** The Threads and the Agent sessions outside runs and assistants that share a workspace, ended ones included, so their transcripts can still be opened. Run steps and assistant sessions are never in a row. A Thread with no workspace stands alone. A workspace that holds only Agent sessions draws no row. Sessions are grouped by their workspace id, not by `Workspace.sessionIds`, which no longer lists a session that has exited.
  - **The lead** is the oldest Thread the sidebar holds in the workspace, Done or not. It names the row (the row's title is the lead's title) and gives the row its project. A Thread moved to another project stays in the row. Marking Done never changes a row's sessions, its lead, its name or its project. The next Thread names the row only when the lead was marked Done more than 14 days ago, because the read then no longer holds it (see the known limits below).
  - **Counted sessions** are the Threads not marked Done and the Agent sessions that have not ended. Only they decide the row's status, its dots and its second line. A Done Thread and an ended Agent session stay in the row, quiet.
  - **Line 1** is the title and the status slot of the most urgent counted session: waiting, then working, then queued, then the rest, and among equals the most recently active. A row with no counted session shows its lead's status.
  - **Line 2**, while a counted session waits, is its question in `--you-ink`, as "review asks · Ship v2.4?": the session's label, then the question. When the lead asks, or the row has one session, the question stands alone. With several asking, the one whose last activity is oldest is shown. When nothing waits, line 2 is the place, as above.
  - **The dots.** A row of 2 or more sessions shows a dot after its title for each counted session, the most urgent first, at most 3, then "+n" for the rest. A dot is waiting, working (queued counts) or quiet. The dots are one button, not one button each. It unfolds and folds the row, and its accessible name counts every session in words, such as "3 sessions: 1 working, 1 done, 1 ended". `→` on a focused row unfolds it, `←` folds it.
  - **Unfolded lines.** Each session of the row gets a 32px line under it, in the row's order (the lead first among the Threads, then by start): its label, its agent (the provider's mark and the model) and its status. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* When the line is too narrow, the model's name leaves whole, never cut short with an ellipsis, so the provider's mark stays alone; the session's label shrinks only after that. The hover card's session lines do the same. A Done Thread is drawn with a check among the quiet lines. ~~When more than 2 sessions are quiet, they fold into one line, "`<n>` quiet", a button that shows them;~~ *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* When more than 2 sessions are quiet and at least one other line still shows (a waiting or working session, or the open one), the quiet ones fold into one line, "`<n>` quiet", a button that shows them; a row whose sessions are all quiet, such as a row on the Done shelf, draws every line, because a lone "`<n>` quiet" line would only make the user press again. The open session always keeps a line of its own. There is no Done on a line, because Done belongs to the row. Whether a row is unfolded, and whether its quiet lines are shown, are kept in memory only, so a relaunch folds every row.
  - **Opening.** A click on the row opens the session last open in that row, or the lead the first time in the window. A click on a line opens its session. Opening a session from the header never unfolds a row and never scrolls the list. While the Office is open, every row, line and shelf row opens the session it opens in the Office's drawer when that session has a colleague there, and on its own screen when it has none. One rule decides it for Threads and Agent sessions alike, `isSessionSeated`, and it is asked about the session the click opens, not the row's lead: a row that opens a partner follows the partner. The Go menu and a notification ask it too, so a live Agent session opens in the drawer from them, as from the sidebar. See [The Office](#the-office).
  - **Labels.** A session in a row is called its role, or else its title cut short. Sessions that share a role, ignoring case, are numbered by when they started: "review", "review 2" ([spec 02](./02-domain-model.md) Session, `role`). `labelWorkspaceSessions` decides it, and the header's tabs call the same function over the same workspace's sessions, so the header and the sidebar always agree.
  - **Order and cap.** A row sorts by when it last started: the lead's creation, or the latest reopen of any of its Threads, whichever is later. A partner's spawn does not move the row, so the list moves only when the user picks something up again. The cap above applies to rows: 5 chosen by priority (a row with a waiting session first, then one with a working session, then the rest), plus the row that holds the open session.
  - **Waiting on you names the row and who asks.** An entry shows the row's name, and the label of the asking session when it is not the lead. A waiting Agent session in a row is listed too. A Done Thread that still waits stays listed, because a question never hides, though its row's status ignores it. The open Thread is part of the sessions Waiting on you is built from, as it is of the sidebar's rows, so a Thread marked Done more than 14 days ago that is open and still asks is listed too, and counted by the dock badge and notified, though the sidebar's read leaves it out.
  - **Done moves a whole row.** The button reads "Done" and its accessible name stays "Mark done", for a lone Thread and a row alike; the drawing's "Finish" is "Done" everywhere. The ids it sends are the row's Threads not yet Done, as the client shows them: a Thread the user has not seen yet stays live and keeps the row in the list, instead of vanishing to the shelf unseen. Agent sessions are skipped, because they cannot be marked Done. `session.markDone` and `session.reopen` take a list of 1 or more Thread ids and apply it in one transaction, all or nothing.
  - **The Done shelf holds a row** once every Thread is Done and every Agent session has ended, so a working reviewer never hides on the shelf. It has one row for each, 52px: the lead's title, then "3 threads · done 09:40", the time of the latest Done (a lone Thread keeps today's row). The shelf's header counts rows. The open session's row is still always on the shelf.
  - **A row on the shelf unfolds,** so an ended Agent session's transcript can still be opened without reopening the work. A shelf row of 2 or more sessions has the same dots button as a row in a project: it draws no dot, because nothing on the shelf is counted, but it keeps its chevron and its accessible name, such as "3 sessions: 2 done, 1 ended. Unfold". Unfolded, it draws the same 32px lines as a row in a project, and `→` and `←` work the same. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* Every session on the shelf is quiet, so it draws every line and no "`<n>` quiet" line, except when the open session is the one other line (see Unfolded lines above). Line 2 stops before the dots button and the chevron beside it, as a row in a project does, so the chevron never touches its text. Its second line stays "3 threads · done 09:40", and Reopen and Done change nothing. Whether a row is unfolded is kept per row, so a row keeps its state when it moves between its project and the shelf. The hover card of such a row says how to unfold it, as a row's in a project does.
  - **The read: known limits.**
    - A workspace whose Threads were all marked Done more than 14 days ago, but whose Agent session is still live, shows no row: it has no Thread to name it.
    - `doneSince` does not bound Agent sessions, which are never Done, so the read returns every Agent session outside runs and assistants.
    - A batch Done or Reopen accepts at most 500 ids, the page limit, as `signal.markDone` does. A row has far fewer Threads.
  - **What rows of sessions cost** (stated before the measurement, [Performance](#performance), as ADR 0037 asks). No new process, no polling, and no timer beyond the toast's five seconds, which runs only while a toast shows. Nothing runs per streamed token. The number of reads stays the same: one read replaces the thread read. It now also returns the Agent sessions (about 2 for 292 Threads) and no Done Thread older than 14 days. On the controller, the read costs by the sidebar's sessions, not by every run step. Grouping the rows is linear in the sessions the sidebar holds (one grouping by workspace, then a sort inside each group and a sort of the groups). It is one preparation (`prepareSessionPresentation`, in a provider at the shell), so the sidebar, its foot, Waiting on you and the routing of a destination share one result, and it runs again only when one of the lists it reads or the open session's record is a new object: a push that changes none prepares nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* A known limit: the read bounds Threads by time (`doneSince`) but not Agent sessions, because an Agent session is never Done, so a long-lived workspace keeps its Agent sessions in the read. For 292 real Threads the old read was about 500 KB; the fixture's 829,664 bytes is for 465 sessions with long fixture titles, so the two are not a like-for-like comparison. The 500-Thread measurement is recorded in [Measured](#measured).
- **The threads with no project have a header, "No project",** where spec 14 gives them none. In place of the identity tile, it has the tile's outline in `--faint` with no fill. ~~It has no `+`, because a draft always starts in a project.~~ *(Amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): a draft can have no project (see **A new thread** below), so the header has a `+` that opens that draft.)* Without a header, a capped project's "more" row sits between two groups and reads as belonging to either.
- **A waiting thread is listed twice:** in Waiting on you, and in its project with the waiting mark, as the book draws it. In Waiting on you, its second line is the open Request as one question: "Run git push?", "Change adyen.ts?", "Change 3 files?", "Read `<file>`?", "Run `<tool>`?", or a question's first line in the agent's words. With nothing waiting, the section is not drawn. *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453).)* A waiting assistant is listed twice the same way: in Waiting on you, and in the Assistants section with its word in `--you-ink`. *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* The rule holds for each waiting session: it is listed in Waiting on you, and its row in the project shows the question in `--you-ink`. In Waiting on you the entry's first line is the row's name, and its second line is "`<label>` asks · `<question>`" when a session other than the lead asks, such as "review asks · Ship v2.4?", otherwise the question alone.
- **The foot counts threads by pose:** "`<n>` working · `<n>` waiting · `<n>` idle". *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* It counts sessions: the Threads and the Agent sessions not marked Done or ended, by their own pose, not rows. A row with one working and one idle session counts in both.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* Threads marked Done are not counted, waiting or not: the user put them away.
  - Asleep and away threads are not counted, so a fleet with hundreds of old threads reads "3 working · 2 waiting · 4 idle", not "470 idle".
  - The book's "paused" count is left out, because no thread is paused yet.
  - Every count shows at 0. The waiting count takes `--you-ink` only above 0, because the attention hue means something needs the user.
- *(Added 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* **New thread, Search and Office share one row** at the sidebar's top. The book draws New thread and Search as two rows, and puts the office's row in the Hercule face's Work section. ~~The desktop has only the Threads face, and~~ The Office belongs to both faces, so its way in sits in the part of the sidebar both faces share *(amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395): the desktop now has both faces, and the Office's row is still not repeated under Work)* ~~.~~ *(amended 2026-10-15: the Hercule face's Work section now also has the book's "The office" row, first, with no count, beside the shared button.)* New thread keeps its label and `⌘N`. Search (`⌘K`, still inert) and Office (`⌘⇧O`) are icon buttons, each with a tooltip that gives its name and shortcut. The Office button shows as pressed while the Office is open.
- **Search `⌘K`~~, the hide-sidebar button~~ ~~and Settings~~ ~~are~~ is drawn and inert** *(amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626): the hide-sidebar button hides the sidebar, see [The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout))* until their slices build them, like the composer's ~~`+` and~~ voice ~~buttons~~ button *(amended 2026-10-08, [#465](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/465))*: they show their hover states, do nothing when pressed, and carry `aria-disabled`. `⌘K` is not registered. *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* The foot's Settings button opens [Settings](#settings), and shows as pressed while Settings is open. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* The foot is the same on both faces, and its Settings button is the Hercule face's Settings row ([The Hercule face](#the-hercule-face)).
- ~~**The thread list reads every page** of `session.query`, so no thread is left out.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* **The sidebar makes one read, of every page,** in place of the thread read: `session.query` with `kind: ["thread", "agent"]` and `doneSince` set to 14 days before the window first made the read. The cutoff is fixed for the window's life, so a Thread marked Done since then stays in the read instead of dropping out under the user. The 14 days are fixed, with no setting. Why: reading every Thread ever was about 500 KB of JSON for 292 Threads, and the read ran again after every change. The open Thread, when it is Done and older than 14 days, keeps its row on the Done shelf through the open-thread read that already exists. The web app reads the first 500.

**The sidebar's layout** *(added 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626))*. The user resizes the sidebar and hides it. The book draws the sidebar at a fixed 272px, with its hide button and no hidden state. The handle borrows its look from [the split](../design-language.md#the-split)'s handle, but none of the split's rules for hiding a pane.

- **Width.** 272px until the user changes it, at least 232px and at most 420px, and never so wide that the main pane is narrower than 520px. In a window too narrow for both minimums the sidebar keeps its 232px. The window's minimum width, 800pt, leaves the sidebar 280px. `fitSidebarWidth` in `@hercule/client-core` decides the width, and the shell's CSS applies the same limits, so a window made narrower narrows the sidebar without a render.
- **The handle** is an 8px strip centred on the sidebar's right edge, the window's full height. It runs through the drag strip, so it is `no-drag`: a press there resizes the sidebar and never moves the window. It draws a 2px `--accent` line while the pointer is over it, while it is dragged and while it has keyboard focus, and shows the resize cursor. It is a `separator` with its width as its value, and its minimum and maximum.
  - **Drag** resizes the sidebar as the pointer moves. Let go with the edge left of 160px, the sidebar hides and keeps the width it had when the drag started, so it shows again at that width.
  - **Double-click** gives the sidebar 272px again.
  - **Keys:** ← and → move the edge 16px, Home and End give the sidebar its narrowest and widest width, and Enter hides it.
- **Hiding and showing.** The sidebar's own button, "Hide the sidebar ⌘B", View › Toggle Sidebar `⌘B` and Enter on the handle hide it. While it is hidden, a "Show the sidebar ⌘B" button in the main pane's top band and `⌘B` show it.
  - The sidebar slides: its box's width moves to 0 over `--dur-2` when it hides, and back over `--dur-3` with `--ease-out` when it shows, while the sidebar fades. The sidebar keeps its full width inside the box, which clips it, so its rows are never laid out at another width. A change of mind halfway turns the slide around from where it is. Under Reduce motion the change is immediate.
  - The slide starts one frame after the frame that draws the click or the key, as [Intake's split](#the-split-and-its-minimums) does: the box follows the sidebar's state in an attribute of its own, `data-sidebar-box`, set a frame late. So the click's own work never shares a frame with the slide's first frame.
  - A hidden sidebar is ~~`inert`~~ `content-visibility: hidden`, which takes it out of the focus order and the accessibility tree. It turns hidden when the slide shut ends, and shown at once when it opens, by a `content-visibility` transition. Chromium keeps a hidden element's style and layout, so showing the sidebar again restyles none of its rows. *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626): setting `inert` on the sidebar's 500 threads cost 232 ms in the slide's first frame, and `visibility: hidden`, which every row inherits, cost 1.2 to 2.2 ms in a frame; see [Measured](#measured).)* When hiding it would leave the focus on nothing, the focus goes to the Show button, and when showing it from that button, to the handle.
- **While the sidebar is hidden,** the main pane has the whole window, and the traffic lights sit over its top band. The Show button sits at 80px from the left and ~~10px from the top~~ 14px from the top: 8px after the last light, as far as the lights are apart, ~~and as high as the Hide button sits in the sidebar's top strip~~ and on the centre line of the floating header's 36px pills, 4px below the lights' centre line *(amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626): level with the Hide button it sat 4px above the header's controls beside it, and read as out of line with them)*. A screen's controls in the band, the bar of Settings and Intake and the floating header of a thread, an assistant, a draft and the Office, start at 116px, after the button. Each screen's stylesheet moves its controls there by a rule on the shell's hidden state, not by an inherited property, so hiding the sidebar restyles those controls alone and not every element of the open screen. The Office's thread drawer sits at the window's right edge and keeps its own start.
- **Kept per device.** The layout, its width and whether it is hidden, is kept in the renderer's `localStorage` under `hercule.sidebar.layout`, because it belongs to this Mac and not to the account. It is read before the shell's first frame, so the app opens as it was left. It is written only when a change ends: a drag let go, a key pressed, the sidebar hidden or shown. While the user drags, React draws nothing: the handle writes one CSS property, `--sidebar-w`, at most once a frame, on the sidebar's box and the handle, and the shell's style lays out the sidebar's box and the main pane. The property is registered as not inherited, so a new value restyles those two elements alone.
- **What it costs.** No process, no timer, no polling, no live topic and no read: the layout is one `localStorage` entry. While idle it does nothing, and a streamed token costs it nothing. Bundle bytes: two small modules in the shell's chunk, the handle and the layout's store, and `fitSidebarWidth` in `@hercule/client-core`. The perf script's sidebar scenario (`scripts/perf-sidebar.ts`, `--sidebar-only`) hides, shows and drags the sidebar with a thread of 500 transcript rows open beside 500 threads, and checks rule 2's bar for a layout transition: no dropped frame, and at most 2 ms of style and layout in a frame ([Rules](#rules)). It also traces a press and release on the handle and one arrow key on it. The numbers are recorded in [Measured](#measured). ~~If the slide misses the bar, the box slides the sidebar with `transform` instead.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626): the slide met the bar, so the `transform` fallback was not built.)*

**The thread** *(added 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275))* follows spec 14's thread surface and the book's session pages, with these differences:

- **A turn's work is shown per work stretch,** as the book draws it. A work stretch is the steps between two messages of one turn. Each stretch gets its own divider, and each agent message stands on its own with its face, its meta line and its body. Talk and work keep the order they happened in. Spec 14 and the web app draw one divider per turn, and join the agent's messages under it.
  - The divider reads "Worked for 2m 14s ›" and a summary of the stretch. The summary counts its steps by kind, in the order each kind first appears: "ran 2 commands", "edited 3 files", "searched the web", "used 6 tools", "ran 1 subagent", "made a plan", "compacted the context", "hit 1 error", "did 1 other step". Reasoning is not counted, and a stretch of reasoning alone draws nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#479](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/479).)* The list also has "read 3 files" and "searched 2 times".
  - ~~The book's "read 6 files" is not drawn: no transcript item says that a tool read a file, so such a read counts as a tool.~~
    *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#479](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/479).)* **The summary counts reads and searches,** from the `file_read` and `file_search` item kinds the adapters emit ([./06-providers.md](./06-providers.md) section 6.3, [#477](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/477)). "read 3 files" counts distinct paths, as "edited 3 files" does. "searched 2 times" counts searches, and reads "searched once" for one.
  - Clicking the divider expands the stretch in place, ~~one line per step, with spec 14's verb, target and result~~ *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#479](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/479).)* one row per step, as below. Expansion is not stored. This departs from spec 14: its rows are a verb, a target and a result, and the web app keeps them.
    - **A row is an icon, a label and a chevron.** The icon shows the step's kind. The label is the verb in the UI face, then the target, for example "Read /tmp/411-shots/app.png", on one line and cut with an ellipsis. A target that is code (a command, a path, a search pattern, also when a tool call names one) is in mono. A target that is words (a web search's query, a subagent's or a tool's description, an error's message) is in the UI face, because mono is kept for code ([Design system](#design-system), item 2). The time the step started shows on hover only, through CSS, with no timer. The chevron at the end shows only when the row has something to open. When hidden, it keeps its space, so the columns line up.
    - **Each kind has a verb and an icon.** `buildWorkRows` in `@hercule/client-core` decides them:
      - `file_read`: "Read", eye
      - `file_search`: "Searched", search
      - `command_execution`: "Ran", terminal
      - `file_change`: "Edited", file
      - `web_search`: "Searched the web", globe
      - `tool_call`: the tool's name, puzzle
      - `subagent`: "Subagent", crew
      - `plan`: "Planned", list
      - `reasoning`: "Thought", sparkle
      - `context_compaction`: "Compacted the context", more
      - `error`: "Error", close
      - an unknown kind: "Step", more
    - **Two or more steps of one kind in a row fold into one row:** "Read 3 files", "Searched 2 times", "Ran 3 commands", "Edited 2 files", "Searched the web 2 times", "Used WebFetch 3 times", with the tool's name. A tool call folds only with calls of the same tool. A subagent call, reasoning, a plan, a compaction, an error and an unknown step never fold. The folded row opens to its steps.
    - **Opening a step shows the text it returned:** `item.completed`'s `detail.content`. A string result is used as it is; the adapter has already cut it to 4096 characters. A list of content blocks (Claude Code's tool result) is read for its text blocks, joined by line breaks, and the client cuts that text to 4096 characters, cutting each block to the room left so a long result never builds a long string. A cut never leaves half a character at the end. ~~Any other block, such as an image, is not drawn.~~ Any other block is not drawn. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#478](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/478).)* An image the tool returned is drawn under the text, as the bubble's 210px tiles at 4:3, as many as fit in a row; a click opens it in the lightbox, named "Image 1", "Image 2" and so on among the step's stored images. The runner stores each image and leaves a reference in the content ([./06-providers.md](./06-providers.md)), so a tile reads its thumbnail through `attachment.readContent` the first time it draws, and an image that could not be kept is a quiet line with the reason, in the faint ink. A step that returned images and no text has something to open, and draws no empty text box. Codex sends no result content today, so a Codex step has nothing to open and shows no chevron. The text sits in a scroll box with a maximum height, in the book's mono face, each line kept whole and scrolling sideways. Only an open step puts its text in the page.
    - **A failed step shows a quiet failure mark,** in `--muted`, not tomato (`--fail`), as the warning line's dot does. A declined, running or awaiting-approval step shows that word in `--faint`.
    - **The transcript keeps which rows are open,** as it keeps which stretches are expanded, so a row stays open when it scrolls out of the mounted range and back. A row keeps its key when a second step folds onto it, so an open row stays open and then shows its steps. It is not stored.
  - The stretch that is running reads "Working for 12s ›" and counts up. It shows no divider until its first step that is not reasoning, so no divider shows before the first step, or while the agent only reasons. It does not shimmer, as spec 14's does, because only the working face animates ([Rules](#rules), rule 2). While a Request is open, the stretch stops at the Request's opening and reads "Worked for".
  - *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#504](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/504), replacing the 2026-10-08 amendment of [#481](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/481).)* **The thread never draws an agent row with no text.** A face and a meta line with nothing beside them read as a response that has started, and a response can take minutes to begin. The face is drawn only beside text, because the first thing the agent does after the user's message may be tool calls, not a message. While the agent has drawn nothing, a status line stands where the next block will be, in the divider's drawing (muted, 12px, indented to the message column, with its rule):
    - **"Working for 12s"** from the start of the wait, counting up: right after the user's message, while the agent only reasons, and after a message when the next step is not yet a tool call. The start is the stretch's own start, so when the first step arrives the line becomes the stretch's divider and the count goes on.
    - **"Starting…"** while no turn has started: a session or a subagent that is starting.
    - **"Writing…"** while the agent has started a message and not yet written a word. The message's row is hidden, and shows once the first word lands in the paragraph being written. The live hook paints that paragraph outside React, so the stylesheet decides, not the component.
    - **No line** while a running work stretch is the last block: its "Working for" divider already says it.
    - Each line is a live region (`role="status"`) whose spoken text changes with the state, "Starting", "Working" or "Writing", and not with the count, so a screen reader does not read the seconds out.
- *(Added 2026-10-08, [#476](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/476).)* **A runtime warning is a quiet line where it happened,** such as a retried request, or an event the runner shrank or dropped because it was larger than a frame ([./06-providers.md](./06-providers.md) section 6). The line is the book's `.dot`, named "Warning" for screen readers, the warning's text as the runner wrote it, and its time, all in 12px `--muted` where the waiting note sits. The book draws no warning in a thread, so the line is built from the book's parts. It is quieter than the book's notice card and the waiting note, because the work went on and the user owes no answer.
  - The dot is muted, not coloured. The book has no caution colour, and its two near ones mean something else: tomato (`--fail`) means something failed, and marigold (`--you`) means it is the user's move. A retry or a shrunk event is neither.
  - The text wraps, and the dot stays on its first line. The text is selectable, so it can be copied into an issue; the time is not.
  - A warning does not end a work stretch. A run of retries would otherwise split one divider into many, so the stretch's divider stays above the warning and the steps after it still join it.
  - A subagent's warning shows on the subagent's page, because each page reads only its own agent's rows.
  - The thread specimen shows two warnings among the Fix thread's work with `?state=warning`. `pnpm compare:bureau` does not compare that state, because the book has nothing to compare it with.
- *(Added 2026-10-18, [#733](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/733).)* **A runtime error is the warning's line in the failure colours:** the book's tomato dot (`--fail`), named "Error" for screen readers, and the error's text in `--fail-ink`, or its class when the runner sent no text. The time stays `--muted`. It has the warning's size and place, it does not end a work stretch either, and a turn the error failed still ends with its own ending. A session that could not start, such as one whose skill could not be loaded, shows its error this way, and so does a turn that failed, such as one whose context window is full. The specimen's `?state=warning` shows one beside the first warning.
- **Messages carry their own time,** and there are no time separators, where spec 14 draws one above each turn. A user message has its time under the bubble; an agent message has it in its meta line, "Claude Code · Opus 5.5 · 09:04". The time is `09:04` when it falls on today in the system time zone, else `4 Sep 09:04`.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#511](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/511).)* **A message another session's agent sent names that agent.** It is the user's bubble, on the right, with three differences:
    - A chip above the bubble names the agent, on the right, its right edge on the bubble's. The chip is the book's `.mention`: the agent's 18px idle face and its name, bold, in the agent's `--who-ink`. The chip is at most 240px wide, and a longer name ends in an ellipsis, with no tooltip: only a session's title can be that long, and the chip links to the thread, which shows it in full. The chip's width never widens the bubble: a short bubble under a long name keeps its own width, and the chip reaches past it to the left. A chip that links is lit with the book's `--hover` while the pointer is on it, as a row the book can open is, with the arrow kept; "Another agent", which links nowhere, is not.
    - The bubble takes the agent's `--who-tint`. In the dark theme the tint is mixed 70% into the neutral bubble's colour, because the full dark tint reads as a block of colour, and a tint mixed into `--surface` is darker and greyer than the user's bubble beside it.
    - The message is a group named "Message from" and the agent's name, so a screen reader never takes it for the user's.

    The name and the link follow `describeSender` from `@hercule/client-core`, so both apps agree. An assistant's session shows the assistant's name and face and links to its page. Any other session the user can read shows its title and links to its thread; a session with an empty title is named "session 7c82ebeb", the tail of its id, so the chip is never blank. A session the user cannot read, deleted or forbidden, shows "Another agent" in its chip and "Message from another agent" as the group's name, with no link, and is never an error. ~~The thread's loader reads each sender once:~~ *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796).)* When the thread opens without a saved copy ([Thread copies](#thread-copies)), its loader reads each sender once: it reads every sender they name, and waits for those reads up to one second (`OPTIONAL_READ_WAIT_MS` in `@hercule/client-core`, shared with the web app), and never longer: a sender that answers in time is named, chip and tint included, at the first paint, and a controller that never answers one cannot keep the thread from opening. A sender still unread after that wait, or one that first appears while the thread streams, fills in when its read answers. Until then, the message is the plain bubble with the chip's row held empty at its height, and a queued row holds the chip's place empty where the owner's row shows its clock, untinted, so nothing moves when the name arrives and nothing names a sender that may be wrong. A read that fails, for a network error or a 5xx, shows "Another agent" as an unreadable sender does, and is not made again while the app holds it: not retried, not by the next message that mounts, not when the thread opens again, and not when the window is focused again. *(Added 2026-10-19, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796).)* A thread opened from its copy does not wait: its senders' reads start with the loader and fill in when they answer, as a sender still unread after the wait does. A read that answered is not made again while the app holds it either, which is while a screen shows the sender and for five minutes after, because the name comes from the session's title, Agent and conversation, which never change; no push reads it again.

    A message steered into a running turn says so under its bubble, "Steered · 09:04", the owner's as well as an agent's. The owner's other messages are drawn exactly as before. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#620](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/620).)* A message on its way into the session reads "Sending…" under its bubble in place of its time, at the end of the transcript, above the status line when there is one. A message another session's agent sent keeps its chip and tint. It is drawn until the queued inputs read it `delivered`. The transcript's own row for the message can arrive a moment earlier, and both then show for the length of one read; [#623](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/623) links the two exactly. Such a message is a queued input the controller has handed to the runner, or the one a waiting start carries (`splitQueuedInputs` in `@hercule/client-core`, shared with the web app). It is never a queued row, so a resumed thread's message does not show as queued while its harness starts. The queued rows show Steer only while a turn runs (`canSteerQueuedInputs`), and Cancel as before.

    **A queued input from another session's agent** shows the same chip where the owner's row shows its clock, with the face's left edge where the clock's is. The row's glass is tinted with the agent's `--who-tint`, 60% in light and 35% in dark, and its rim is the glass's, as on the owner's rows. The row is a group named "Queued message from" and the agent's name. Steer and Cancel work as on the owner's rows. The row keeps the book's items as its own direct children (the clock or the chip, the images, the text, the status, Steer and Cancel), each in a grid track of its own, so a note under the text starts where the text starts, however wide the chip is. An owner's row on its own is drawn exactly as before.

    Several queued rows stack into one block, owner's and agents' alike: only the top row has the glass's lit top edge, and a row under another draws no line along its top, so one 1px line parts two rows and the block has one outline. Before #511 each row drew its whole rim, and two rows met in a 2px line.

    The chip's link opens the sender's thread or assistant in the main pane, so in the Office's drawer it leaves the Office today. Whether the drawer should open the sender in place is open, in [#511](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/511).
- *(Added 2026-10-20, [#810](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/810).)* **A bubble keeps its text inside it, and a long one is collapsed.** The user's bubble and an agent's bubble behave the same, in the thread and in the Conversation.
