The sidebar still lists them.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-15, [#704](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/704).)* A thread marked Done has no colleague either, unless it waits on the user: then it stays until it is answered, so the Office never hides a Request (`isThreadSeated`). *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* The rule is now `isSessionSeated`, which also seats an Agent's session outside runs while it is working, waiting or idle, and is asked by the sidebar and the Go menu as well as the Office. *(Amended 2026-10-17, [#717](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/717).)* The Office seats every thread the sidebar lists in a project: every thread not marked Done, in any pose. An asleep or away thread rests in the Lounge, as an idle one does, and keeps its desk to walk back to: the sidebar draws an asleep thread like an idle one, and spec 14 calls it Idle. It dozes in its armchair, as an assistant dozes in its own, and no colleague visits it. An idle colleague that falls asleep stays in the armchair it sits in. A resting session in the café rests at its table, as before (`isRestingPose`). A Thread is never idle-unloaded, so it falls asleep only when its process ends: a runner restart ends every one at once, and a crash or a stop ends one. Seating only threads with a running process emptied the Office at every runner restart, while the sidebar still listed every thread: the Office then no longer showed the user's unfinished work. The sidebar foot and the top bar still count only working, waiting and idle, so the counts are unchanged.
- *(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* **Every assistant is always drawn, in every pose,** ~~unlike a thread~~ *(amended 2026-10-17, [#717](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/717): a thread not marked Done is too)*. Assistants are few, the sidebar lists every one of them, and an assistant that can't be reached should be easy to spot. Each assistant has its own corner in the Secretariat (see the fixed rooms below), and the corners follow the sidebar's order, by name. `decideOfficeSeating` returns the assistants in that order as `secretariat`. An assistant with no session yet is idle, as in the sidebar.
- Each project with a seated thread has a room. Rooms keep the order in which the controller lists the projects, and are not sorted ~~by latest activity as the sidebar's projects are~~ by their threads *(amended 2026-10-08, [#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473): the sidebar's projects are sorted by their newest thread's creation time)*: a room is a place, and a place that moves whenever a thread works cannot be found again. The threads with no project share one room, "No project", which comes last.
- Inside a room, desks are grouped by workspace, in ~~the sidebar's~~ the web sidebar's workspace order (`rankLane`) *(amended 2026-10-08, [#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473): the desktop sidebar no longer groups by workspace)*, so threads that share a working copy sit side by side. Inside a workspace the oldest thread sits first, so a desk keeps its place when a new thread starts.
- A thread with an open Request stands in the queue at the user's desk, longest waiting first. An idle thread sits in the Lounge. Both keep their desk, to walk back to. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* A waiting assistant stands in the same queue, together with the threads, longest waiting first. An idle assistant stays at its writing desk, not in the Lounge.
- A colleague wears its thread's look: `buildLook` seeds it with the session id, as for the sidebar's faces, so a thread's face and its colleague match. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* An assistant's colleague wears the assistant's look: `buildAssistantLook` seeds it with the assistant's id, as for the sidebar's faces.
- *(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* A pose change never builds the building again. Only adding, removing or renaming an assistant changes the Secretariat, as only a new room or desk changes the rest of the building.
- *(Added 2026-10-15, [#706](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/706).)* **Arrivals and departures.** The colleagues and the simulation outlive the building: when a colleague joins or leaves, only the building is rebuilt, and the scene is not reset.
  - **Opening the Office** finds everyone already in place. ~~Nobody walks in.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#729](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/729).)* A walk in or out that started while the Office was closed is still under way: a walk starts when its change happened, not when the Office saw it, so a user who opens the Office a moment after starting a thread, or marking one Done, sees the rest of that walk.
    - The records hold when each change happened. A session came in when it was created, and a Thread when it was last reopened (`decideArrivedAt`). A Thread left when it was marked Done, a run when it finished, and an Agent's session outside a run when it exited (`listWork` with `endedSince`).
    - As the Office opens, it reads the work that ended in the last minute: `run.query({ activeSince })` with the sessions of the runs that finished, and `session.query({ kind: ["agent", "run", "assistant"], status: "exited", activeSince })`. The Threads marked Done are in the sidebar's read already. It reads the live runs afresh too: the shell holds the `run` topic only while the window shows, and the Office is not always open, so the cached list can still hold a run that finished since, and a run that both reads hold counts as finished. The router keeps none of the Office's data once it closes, so each opening waits for these reads: the opening world is built once, from them. Opening or closing the drawer is no new opening, so it reads nothing more.
    - The Office opens on a world that still seats that work (`buildWorld` with `endedSince`), and moves on to the world of now at once, so those colleagues walk out. A colleague who came in, or whose work ended, less than a minute before is put on its walk as far as it has got by now: the sim runs the walk on at once, with no frame drawn. A walk that ended before the Office opened ends there, and its colleague sits down, or is gone before the first frame. The longest walk measured takes about 30 s, so a minute leaves room for larger offices.
    - A walk under way is past the door already, so it does not wait its turn there. Two walks that started less than 0.7 s apart can overlap at the door.
    - The times come from the controller's clock, and the Office compares them with the Mac's. The two can differ when the controller runs on another machine, so the times are read only as the Office opens. A change seen while the Office is open walks from its start, as before. A walk that seems to start after the Office opened, because the controller's clock is ahead, walks from its start.
    - A Thread marked Done while it waited on the user stays until the user answers, and leaves then. No record holds when it was answered, so the Office counts its walk from when it was marked Done, and a user who opens the Office just after answering may not see it walk out.
  - **A colleague who joins** while the Office is open appears on the pavement in front of the front door and walks in to where its pose puts it: its desk, the queue or the Lounge. A colleague of the café walks along the pavement, over the zebra crossing and in through the café's own door.
  - **Several arrivals at once** set off one after another, 0.7 s apart (the gap that spaces errands), so they do not stack in the doorway. Until its turn, an arrival is not drawn.
  - **A colleague who leaves** (its work is over, so it is no longer in the world) stands up, walks out the way it came in, to the pavement, and is then removed. Its desk stays in the building until the last colleague walking out has gone, so a room never shrinks around someone still in it. A colleague who comes back before it is out turns back to its seat.
  - **When the building is rebuilt,** a colleague at rest is put on the same seat or place in the new building (its seat moves with its room). A colleague on its way carries on from the same spot in its room. A visit or a cup of tea in progress ends early: the colleague heads straight to where its state puts it.
  - **Speeds.** A colleague walks at 1.35 m/s (it was 1.1). An arrival walks in at a brisk 1.8 m/s, never a run: a brisk human walk tops out near 2 m/s, and a run starts near 2.5. The gait lengthens its stride with speed, so the cadence rises less and the feet stay planted.

**Every session in the Office.** *(Added 2026-10-15, [#704](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/704).)* The point of the Office is one look at everything going on, so every session has a colleague while it works, not only threads.

The Office draws **work**, not sessions: a Thread is one piece of work with one session, a Run is one piece of work with a session per agent step, and a session an Agent spawned outside a run is one piece of work of its own. A piece of work's sessions sit together, arrive with it and leave with it. *(Amended 2026-10-15, [#706](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/706).)* Each of them walks in from the street and out again, through the front door or the café's. See **Arrivals and departures** in [The Office](#the-office). Work is how the desktop app reads the records the controller already serves (~~`listLiveWork`~~ `listWork` in `@hercule/client-core` *(amended 2026-10-18, [#729](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/729): it also lists the work that ended a moment ago, for the world the Office opens on)*); the controller has no Work record and no operation for it. Each kind of work has its own rule for when it is over:

| Kind | Seated while | Leaves when |
|---|---|---|
| Thread | ~~working, waiting or idle, and not marked Done~~ not marked Done, in any pose *(Amended 2026-10-17, [#717](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/717).)* | it is marked Done, unless it waits on the user~~, or it falls asleep or away~~ |
| A run's agent step's session | its run is running or pending, in any pose but asleep. Once the step has finished, its session is `done`, and rests in the Lounge | its run completes, fails or is cancelled. All of the run's sessions leave together. The run record decides, not a guess from the sessions |
| Any other session an Agent spawned | working, waiting or idle | its session exits. Only a thread can be Done |
| Assistant | always | never |

- **`done`** is the pose `decideThreadPose` never returns: an agent step's session that has exited, while its run goes on. The face is the book's done face. The session record does not say whether the step succeeded (#278), so `done` says only that the session has stopped working. The top bar counts it beside working, waiting and idle.
- **Which room.** Work sits in its project's room. A run belongs to no project, so `decideWorkProjectId` reads one for the Office alone: the project every repo of the run's workspace belongs to, the first in the project list when several do. Work with no workspace, or whose repos share no project, sits in the café. Nothing about a project is stored on the run or the session: a Project only organises information (CONTEXT.md).
- **Inside a room,** each piece of work sits at its own desks: a thread at one desk, a run's sessions at desks pushed together into a bench. The pieces fill rows from west to east, as many desks to a row as the Lounge's rule allows side by side, so a room grows wider rather than deeper. Work in the same working copy sits side by side, in the sidebar's order of working copies, oldest first inside one. A project's agents stay in its room, so the room's size shows how much is going on in the project.
- **What a colleague wears.** A thread's colleague wears its thread's look. An agent step's session wears a clerk's eyeshade, a visor, and any other Agent's session a flat cap, so the kind shows on its head wherever it stands: at its desk, in the Lounge or in the queue (`buildSessionLook`). Only the Office draws these looks yet; the sidebar's and the transcript's faces of those sessions are still `buildLook`'s.
- **Its card** names an agent step's session "Workflow step" and another Agent's session "Agent", as it names an assistant "Assistant", and its button reads Open session. The drawer shows the session's screen, as for a thread.
- **The café.** The sessions with no project sit in the **Café des Égarés**, the café of the strays, a building of its own across a side street east of the Lobby, not in a room of the bureau. Its door opens onto the pavement, so its colleagues never pass through the bureau to reach their seats; *(Amended 2026-10-15, [#706](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/706).)* an arrival walks in along the pavement and over the zebra crossing to its door; a waiting one walks across the zebra crossing to the queue at Your Office, and back. A colleague in the café rests at its own table, not in the Lounge. The café is a 1930s Paris café: warm glazed tiles, a zinc counter with a brass espresso machine, a cash register, beer taps and a stand of croissants, a mirrored back bar of bottles against its windowless back wall, marble tables on cast-iron tripods with bentwood chairs, a striped awning and a blade sign over the street, and a chalkboard on the pavement. Each piece of work has one table: a long table for a run's sessions, a round table for a thread or an Agent's session. The tables fill rows of at most 7.5 metres, the café growing a row at a time, and a strip along the street wall stays clear, for the door and so the awning never hides a sitter. While nobody sits in it, it still stands, so the street has a far side, but it is closed: its chairs are up on the tables, and it has no lamp and no lit window in the evening. The Rooms directory lists it under "Across the street", with "no project" beside its name, because the sidebar calls its sessions No project. The side street runs the whole depth of the site.
- The Office Map names the café in `detached`: the building across the side street. Its geometry, like every room's, is the code's.

**The Office Map.** The Office is built from a plain, typed `OfficeMap` value in the renderer, not from code that knows the Bureau. v1 has one map, the Bureau. The value holds:

- its id and its name;
- the name of its growth strategy: `"gallery-wings"`, wings of rooms along the Gallery, the main corridor;
- what a wing and a room stand for. In v1 a wing stands for nothing (`wing: "none"`) and a room for a project (`room: "project"`). A map of a code base would set `wing: "project"` and `room: "module"`;
- the furniture at each desk;
- the fixed rooms, each with its furniture and the spots that furniture offers: a seat, a place to stand, a place in the queue.

The growth strategy, the furniture's geometry and the animations stay in code. The value has no schema, and nothing imports or stores a map: both arrive with the Office Map system ([Seed: the Office Map system (#336)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/336)).

**The fixed rooms:**

| Room | What happens there |
|---|---|
| Your Office | The user's desk, and the queue in front of it |
| The Lounge | Idle colleagues sit there. *(Amended 2026-10-17, [#717](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/717).)* So do asleep and away ones, dozing |
| The Triage room | Triage's desk and the case board. The desk stays empty until Triage exists ~~([#91](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/91))~~ ([#533](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/533), amended 2026-10-10 by [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395)), and Triage's sessions then sit there. The first run's room draws Triage at that desk, so Triage shows in the first run and not yet in the Office |
| The Secretariat *(added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505))* | Every assistant has its corner there: a writing desk, an armchair and a bookcase. One longcase clock stands against the north wall. The room is the annex south of Your Office |
| The Lobby | The front door, from the street |
| The Café des Égarés *(added 2026-10-15, [#704](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/704))* | Across the side street: the sessions with no project, see [Every session in the Office](#every-session-in-the-office) |

*(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* **The front row** holds four fixed rooms along the street, in this order: Triage, the Lounge, Your Office, the Lobby. **The Secretariat is an annex:** it stands south of Your Office, between it and the street, as wide as Your Office, and its one door opens into Your Office. A waiting assistant has a short walk to the queue. The building is then a T, so it grows in two directions: project rooms widen it, and assistants deepen the Secretariat a row of two corners at a time. The second assistant opens the second column, which widens Your Office once, to the Secretariat's width; from then on only the depth grows. A fifth room in the front row would have made the building one room wider for every user, and with few projects every row is stretched to the building's width. An Office Map names its one annex, and the room it stands south of, in `annex`. With no assistant there is no Secretariat, so no annex and no lawn: an empty room would cost GPU memory and depth for nothing, and the Office looks as it did before assistants.

*(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* **The lawn.** The pavement stays one straight line along the street, at the Secretariat's south wall. The ground either side of the Secretariat, between the front row and the pavement, is lawn, and a paved path crosses it from the front door to the pavement. The lawn west of the Secretariat holds picnic tables, up to three. Nobody walks on the lawn yet: the tables are scenery, and a later ticket may send colleagues out to them.

*(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* **The Secretariat** is one room shared by every assistant, not a room each. Most users have one to three assistants, so a room each would be mostly empty walls, and every room costs GPU memory. A room in the Office Map also stands for a project, so rooms for assistants would need a second kind of room in the rows meant for projects.

- **Each assistant's corner** is a writing desk, an armchair and a bookcase, grouped together. The corners follow the sidebar's order, by name (`buildAssistantRows`). The room grows with the number of assistants, as the Lounge grows by its seating groups. With one assistant, the room still looks furnished, not bare.
- **The bookcase** has empty shelves, sized for 24 topic books and one ledger: 24 is memory's topic cap (spec 12 §6.4), and the ledger stands for `core`. The shelves are empty because memory does not exist yet ([#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93)), so an assistant remembers nothing. A bookcase has no hover state and no click.
- **The longcase clock** stands against a wall, where it reads well from the overview. Its hands show the current local time and move once a minute, which costs one frame a minute. They move also while the Office stands still, because a clock that stops on battery is wrong. While the window is hidden, the clock draws no frames, and when the window shows again, the hands jump to the time. The hands cast no shadow, so the sun's shadows, which are drawn once, are never drawn again for them. The pendulum hangs still, and the clock has no chime, no hover state and no click.
- The clock and the bookcases come to life with [#506](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/506), once heartbeats ([#94](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/94)) and memory exist. Until then neither shows anything that is not true: the clock tells the real time, which claims nothing about heartbeats, and the shelves are empty.
- "Secretariat" is a room name, like the Lounge, and is set in the UI face, as every room name is.

*(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* **Where an assistant is, by pose.** The pose is `decideAssistantPose`'s, as in the sidebar.

| Pose | Where | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| working | its writing desk | writes |
| idle | its writing desk | the Office's happenings: reads the newspaper, sips tea, fetches tea |
| waiting | the queue at Your Office, together with threads, longest waiting first | raises its hand, as a thread does, and walks back to its desk once answered |
| asleep | its armchair | dozes: eyes closed, head tilted. Only the ambient breathing moves, and nothing moves while the Office stands still |
| away | its armchair | wears the away face, and its name tag reads "can't be reached" (`describePose`) |

Moving between poses uses the same walks as a thread's pose change:

- **Waking,** from asleep or away to working or idle: the assistant stands, stretches and walks to its desk.
- **Falling asleep, or going away:** it walks to its armchair, sits and dozes.

The prototype's Post Room, Parlour, Library, Records, Dispatch and Reading Room are left out, because nothing happens in them yet.

**Room names and plaques are set in the UI face.** The prototype set them in Limelight, which is used only for the wordmark and numerals ([Design system](#design-system)), and room names in it are hard to read.

**Selecting a colleague:**

- Hovering over a colleague makes it perk up and shows its name tag.
- Clicking a colleague glides the camera to it and follows it, and opens its card. The card shows the colleague's face, its thread's title and pose, its ~~open Request~~ oldest open Request *(amended 2026-10-06, [#440](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/440))* with the Requests dock's answers, and its room, machine and model. The card's words are `@hercule/client-core`'s, as the dock's are.
- Answering from the card answers the Request as the dock does. The colleague lowers its hand and walks back to its desk.
- Open thread on the card, or ⏎, opens the thread screen as a drawer from the right, over the Office. The drawer is the thread screen itself, with its transcript, Requests dock and composer.
  - *(Added 2026-10-06, [#440](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/440).)* The drawer shows the thread's spawn lines and the Request pager. Their links open the subagent's page in full, which leaves the Office. The drawer has no tally and no side pane. It holds the `subagent` topic while it shows a thread, so the spawn lines' states stay current.
- *(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* **An assistant's card** shows:
  - its face, its name and "Assistant", where a thread's card shows the thread's title;
  - the pose's word;
  - its oldest open Request, with the Requests dock's answers;
  - its room, "Secretariat", the machine and the model;
  - "Last active 2 h ago", from its current session's `lastActivityAt`. The line is not drawn when the assistant has no session.

  The card's words are `@hercule/client-core`'s, as for a thread.
- *(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* Open conversation on an assistant's card, or ⏎, opens the assistant's Conversation screen as a drawer from the right, over the Office, as Open thread does for a thread. The drawer holds the transcript, the Requests dock and the composer. It does not hold the Conversation's own drawer (Heartbeat, Reminders, Memory), as the thread drawer has no side pane. It holds the `conversation` live topic while it shows an assistant.
- The top bar holds the overview, the Rooms directory and one count per pose. Each count selects the next colleague in that pose. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* The counts include assistants. The Rooms directory lists the Secretariat under "The Office", and `countColleaguesByRoom` counts its assistants.

**Keys:**

| Key | What it does |
|---|---|
| Esc | Steps back one level: the drawer, then the card and the selection, then the room |
| Tab / ⇧Tab | Selects the next or previous colleague waiting on the user, longest waiting first, threads and assistants alike *(amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505))*. Only while the focus is on the Office itself: in the top bar, the card, the drawer and the menus, Tab moves the focus as everywhere else |
| ⏎ | Opens the selected colleague's thread in the drawer. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* For an assistant, opens its Conversation in the drawer |
| Q / E | Turns the building 45 degrees |
| `=` / `-` | Zooms in and out |
| F | Finds the followed colleague again |

**The pointer,** a Mac trackpad first and a mouse second: two-finger scroll pans; a pinch zooms toward the pointer, as a mouse wheel does; a left drag grabs the floor; a right or ⌥ drag orbits; a double click on the floor glides there. Walls between the camera and the room in view drop to the dado rail, so the room can always be seen.

**Fixed settings.** v1 has no controls. The theme follows the app's theme, and the light follows the theme: day in a light theme, evening in a dark one. Name tags are the prototype's Smart setting, and its characters are Bean. Its quality is sharp on a Retina display, with the sun's shadows drawn once and redrawn only when the building changes, and no ambient occlusion, which draws the whole Office a second time in every frame. Its liveliness is Calm, and Still when the Office stands still ([Performance](#performance)). While the Office is open, the window draws no blur, and its glass surfaces draw solid ([Rules](#rules), rule 5).

**Printer rage** is the Office's one activity. At random, and at most once every 5 minutes for the whole Office, a working colleague walks to the printer, kicks it and walks back. A colleague that waits on the user never goes, and neither does an assistant, which writes at its own desk while it works *(amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505))*. The printer is furniture in the Office Map that offers a spot and an animation, the pattern later activities follow. It never happens while the Office stands still. A trigger on a thread's failed tool calls is left to the Office Map system, because it could fire too often.

**The colleagues' looks:**

- Hats sit on the head, not painted on it.
- Two more of the eight sets of accessories in `WARDROBE` (`apps/desktop/src/renderer/faces/look.ts`) gain a tache, so four of eight wear one. The sidebar's faces wear the same looks, so the book's ACCESSORIES table in `crew.js` changes with it, and `pnpm compare:bureau` keeps comparing equal looks.
- The Office draws details the sidebar's faces have no room for: waistcoat buttons, a watch chain, a pocket square, a flower in the buttonhole, spats. They never contradict the face: a detail is chosen from the look, as an accessory is.
- *(Added 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* An assistant wears its headwear, a cloche, a beret or a headset chosen from its id, as its face does everywhere else (see **The assistant** in [Design system](#design-system)). A thread never wears it.

**What v1 leaves out:**

- the prototype's controls panel and Simulate;
- the performance readout, which only a development build shows;
- the Tower and the Campus, whose code stays on the prototype branch;
- event flow and the pneumatic tubes ([Revisit event flow and the tubes in the Office (#331)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/331));
- the book's "Office: List | Floor" switch, because the List is All sessions, which is post-v1;
- ~~assistants and~~ the sessions of workflow runs, because the app lists only threads. *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448).)* The app now lists assistants too. ~~The Office still seats only threads, until a ticket decides where an assistant sits.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* Assistants sit in the Secretariat, so only the sessions of workflow runs are left out.

## The Hercule face

*(Added 2026-10-10, [Write the Intake changes and the build tickets that replace #91 (#395)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); decided in [The desktop Hercule face (#512)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512) and [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394) §1.)* The sidebar has two faces, as [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §App shell settled and the book's sidebar draws them (`crew.js`): **Threads**, the user's threads and assistants, and **Hercule**, the places of Hercule itself. [Intake](#intake) is the Hercule face's first screen. #512 builds the face, as slice 24.

A row for Intake above the threads, on the Threads face, was considered and dropped: it is the "Stack" form that spec 14 rejected after its prototype rounds ([#51](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/51)).

**The switch.** A segmented control, **Threads | Hercule**, sits at the top of the sidebar, under the traffic lights' strip, as the book draws it.

- Under it come the shared row of New thread, Search and Office, then Waiting on you. Both show on both faces. Then comes the face's own list, and the foot, which is the same on both faces.
- The control is reachable with Tab, and ← and → move between its two segments, like any segmented control. View › Threads `⌥⌘1` and View › Hercule `⌥⌘2` switch it from anywhere ([Native behaviour](#native-behaviour)).
- **The Hercule segment shows Intake's To do count,** "Hercule 8", in marigold, the book's `count--you`, and hides it at 0. So the work that waits on Intake stays in sight while the user works in threads. The count is defined under [Intake](#intake). The segment's accessible name carries it: "Hercule, 8 to do".

**The face follows the screen:**

- Intake ~~shows~~ and Workflows show the Hercule face *(amended 2026-10-11, [Desktop Workflows: the workflow list (#550)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/550): Workflows' row is on that face, as Intake's is, and the book draws the Workflows page with it)*.
- A thread, the new-thread screen and an assistant's Conversation show the Threads face.
- The Office and Settings keep the face that was showing, because their ways in sit on both faces: the Office in the shared row, Settings at the foot. So nothing on the sidebar moves when Settings opens, as [The frame](#the-frame) requires.
- The switch changes the face without leaving the open screen. The next screen that has a face of its own sets it again.
- At launch, a screen that keeps the face shows the Threads face.

**The rows.** Only rows whose screen exists are drawn, in the book's order, and each new screen adds its row. ~~No row is drawn inert.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512).)* Fleet and Connections are the exception: they lead to Settings sections that are not built yet, Machines ([#417](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/417)) and Connections ([#416](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/416)). Until those land, both rows are drawn inert, as the Settings list draws its unbuilt rows ([The frame](#the-frame)): they show their hover state, do nothing when pressed, and carry `aria-disabled` and the tooltip "Not built yet". The Connections row carries its red dot all the same. Each row becomes a link when its section is built, with no change to the face. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#416](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/416).)* Connections is built, so its row leads to Settings › Connections and keeps its red dot. Fleet stays inert until Machines lands. With Intake built, the face reads:

- **Work:** Intake, with its To do count in marigold, hidden at 0. The Office's row is not repeated here: it is in the shared row, because it belongs to both faces ([#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332)).
  - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* Workflows, after Intake, opens [Workflows](#workflows). The book puts it after Runs; Runs is not built, so Workflows follows Intake until it is. The row has **no count**. A total of the user's workflows gives the user nothing to act on: what waits on the user from a run shows in Waiting on you, under "From runs".
  - *(Added 2026-10-14, [#665](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/665).)* Discussions, then Wiki, after Workflows, open the two read-only screens of [./19-agent-coordination.md](./19-agent-coordination.md) section 8. The book has no page and no row for them, so they are the exception to the book's order: they follow Workflows. Neither row has a count. ~~Their costs are written here by the desktop ticket ([#669](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/669)) before the screens are built.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-15, [#669](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/669).)* Their costs are in [What Discussions and Wiki cost](#what-discussions-and-wiki-cost). Both screens show the Hercule face, as Workflows does.
- **System:** Fleet, which opens Settings › Machines, and Connections, which opens Settings › Connections and carries the red dot Settings' list carries ([The frame](#the-frame)).
- **Settings is the foot's Settings button.** The book draws Settings twice, as a row in System and as the foot's button, and both open Settings. The desktop draws it once, at the foot, on both faces; on the Hercule face it sits below Fleet and Connections, where the System section's last row would be.
- A row is selected while its screen is open. Fleet and Connections are selected while their Settings section is open, as the foot's button is pressed.
- Before Intake is built (slice 24 alone), the face holds ~~the System section only~~ Waiting on you, which sits above both faces, and the System section, with Fleet and Connections inert *(amended 2026-10-10, [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512))*, and the segment shows no count.

**The face's rows are a plain list,** not a virtualized one. The list is bounded: it holds one row per screen, so rule 1, which virtualizes every unbounded list, does not reach it ([Rules](#rules)).

### What the Hercule face costs

The plan for slice 24, measured by it and recorded in [Measured](#measured):

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| Memory | the face's rows only | the perf script, with the Hercule face showing |
| Idle | no work: no timer, no polling, no animation | the perf script's idle sample, with the Hercule face showing |
| Reads | none beyond Intake's To do count, which arrives on the `signal` topic the app holds once Intake is built. Before that, none | the Network panel, once |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows by the switch, the face's rows and the two View commands; they join the first screen's chunk | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |

## Intake

*(Added 2026-10-10, [Write the Intake changes and the build tickets that replace #91 (#395)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); decided in [The desktop Intake screen in spec 17 (#394)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394) and the tickets it draws on: [#385](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/385), [#386](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/386), [#387](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/387), [#388](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/388), [#390](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/390), [#391](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/391), [#392](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/392), [#393](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/393), [#397](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/397), [#398](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/398).)* Intake is the list of what a source or an agent asks of the user: each item is a Signal. This section owns how the desktop draws it. What a signal is, how it is raised, when it leaves, and what its answers do are owned by [./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md) §9, starting at [The record](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#91-the-record). Every operation named here is in [./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#signal). Intake shows no Notification: Notifications are Hercule's reports on itself, and go to Check-in.

- **The pixel reference** is the Intake drawing, [`docs/design/intake-directions/asks/desktop/intake.html`](../design/intake-directions/asks/desktop/intake.html), drawn on the book's tokens and system. Where the drawing and this section disagree, this section decides: #394 changed the section names, `↩`, the toast, Undo and inbox zero's motion after the drawing was made.
- *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#800](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/800).)* **Tracking's pixel reference** is the Tracking drawing, [`docs/design/tracking/index.html`](../design/tracking/index.html), drawn on Round Trip in the design session #800. Its [BRIEF](../design/tracking/BRIEF.md) lists its states, its keys, its motion and what each costs. Tracking itself, its states and its count are written into this section by [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801) and the slices after it, from that drawing. Where the drawing and this section disagree, this section decides.
- **The route** is `/intake`, a child of the shell, and a chunk of its own (rule 6). The way in is the Hercule face's Intake row and Go › Intake `⌘⇧I`.
- **The screen,** top to bottom: the bar, with the tabs, the views and the header menu; under it [the split](../design-language.md#the-split), with the list as its main column and the open signal in its side pane; under the list, the foot.

### The count and attention

- **The To do count** is every open signal on To do, across every tab, `fyi` and the triage workflow's rows included. A snoozed signal never counts, until its snooze runs out. Example: 1 Now, 4 from GitHub and 3 from triage show as 8. A count that left rows out would make inbox zero untrue.
- *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* **Tracking's waiting rows count too.** The To do count is the open signals plus the Tracking rows that wait on the user ([Tracking](#tracking)). In this slice those are the Finished and Failed rows. A Working row never counts, and neither does a Stopped one: the user stopped it. Example: 7 signals and 4 waiting rows show as 11, so inbox zero stays true while a result waits for its tick.
  - They count in the global To do count and in the All tab's count, and in no plugin's tab ([Tabs](#tabs)).
  - **The count shows on the Hercule segment on every screen, so its reads are shell data.** Tracking's read (below), and the `task` and `run` topics that keep it current, are the shell's, not the Intake screen's. The shell loads it with its other data and keeps it for the life of the window.
- It shows on the Hercule segment, on Intake's row, and on Intake's tabs. It hides at 0.
- **A new Now signal notifies** while the window is not focused ([Native behaviour](#native-behaviour)). Nothing else on Intake notifies.
- **The dock badge, Waiting on you and the Go menu show no signals.** They stay about agents waiting on the user.

### Tabs

- **The tab set is stable:** All, then each plugin with at least one signal kind on, with its mark, then Triage, only while a triage workflow is named ([The Triage tab](#the-triage-tab)). A plugin's tab stays while it has nothing open, so tabs never move under the pointer. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* The tab set is worked out from the plugins' Intake settings and the To do list, which every view reads, so a switch between views keeps the same tabs. A plugin has a tab when it has a signal kind switched on, or a signal on To do now, or its tab is the one pressed. A plugin with every signal kind switched off keeps its tab only while it has a signal on To do. The pressed tab goes with the user to the other views. On To do, Later and Done it is state of the Intake route and ends with the window; on Everything it is the URL's `source`.
- **Core signals have no tab of their own** unless the triage workflow raised them. Those from another workflow, an assistant, the user or Hercule show under All, each labelled with its source.
- *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#800](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/800).)* **Tracking belongs to To do, not to a tab.**
  - It shows the same rows on every tab, so it never moves when the user switches tabs.
  - Its rows that wait on the user count in the To do count and in the All tab's count. A plugin's tab counts its own signals only.
  - Why: a Task can have legs from several sources, such as a Linear issue whose PR then failed its checks on GitHub. It has no one tab to count on, and counting it on each tab would count it twice.
  - The signal that makes a row Needs you is an ordinary signal, and it shows on its own tab.
- **A tab's count is its To do count,** in every view, hidden at 0.
- **When the tabs do not fit the bar:**
  1. The plugin tabs drop their names and keep the mark and the count. The name moves to the tooltip and the accessible name.
  2. If they still do not fit, tabs from the end move into a trailing **More ▾** menu, each with its name, mark and count. More shows the summed To do count of what it holds, in marigold, when it is above 0.
  - All and Triage never shrink and never move into More.
- A tab shrunk to its mark keeps the mark when Appearance's Marks is off, because nothing else names it. Rows follow the Marks setting, as every row with a source mark does ([Appearance](#appearance)).

### Views

The bar's four views: **To do**, **Later**, **Done** and **Everything**. To do, Later and Done list signals, as `signal.query`'s views define them ([./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#97-snooze-and-not-urgent)). Everything lists events ([Everything](#everything)). The view and the tab combine: GitHub's tab in Later lists GitHub's snoozed signals.

- **Later** shows when each signal comes back: "Back Monday 09:00".
- **Done** shows each signal's outcome, newest first, as [When a signal leaves](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#96-when-a-signal-leaves) records it. A replaced signal reads "Replaced by a newer one" and links its successor.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* **A handed-off signal shows its trip.** Its row is led by the face of the agent it went to (22px, the handed-to workflow's face, as in the Done drawing), its source's mark moves to the start of the second line at 12px, and the second line is the trip of its handoff's leg ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#task), `task.queryLegs`) in place of the outcome line. Each signal shows its own leg, with no folding. The line reads "Handed to *workflow*", then "tried again once", "tried again twice" or "tried again *n* times" when the handoff's run was run again (any re-run of it, or of a re-run, counts once), then where the leg ends:
    - **The Task was handed on again** (a later handoff entry follows this one, by time, then by the order they were appended): the standing of the leg, then "continued: *next workflow*". "Handed to Implement → finished → continued: Fix bug".
    - **The Task is done or cancelled:** "done" or "cancelled". "Handed to Fix bug → tried again once → done". Only the Task's own status says so. A Task whose row is missing from Tracking is never read as done.
    - **The Task is in progress and its row in Tracking agrees with the leg:** the word of the row's Tracking state, in lower case ("needs you", "failed", "finished", "working", "stopped"). It is in marigold for Needs you and Failed, and in green for Finished. When the leg has no live run and its own standing is another word than the row's (the run finished, but a signal has since put the Task in Needs you), the standing comes first: "Handed to Review PR → finished → needs you".
    - **The row agrees with the leg when** the row is for the leg's Task, the row's newest handoff is this leg's, and the leg has no next. *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* Tracking's word is the Task's latest observed standing, so it ends the line of the Task's latest leg whatever the runs of earlier legs are doing: an earlier leg's re-run that is still live, or one started after the latest handoff, does not hide it, and neither does a Task with more live runs than a leg lists (a leg lists at most 20). Done and Tracking are read separately and can disagree for a moment while one of them refreshes. That converges, because both are read again on the same pushes and no push is lost.
    - **Anything else,** such as an open Task, or an in-progress Task whose row is not loaded or does not agree: the standing of the leg, in no colour.
    - **The standing of a leg** is "failed" when its newest run failed; else "working" when one of its runs is live; else "finished" when the newest run completed, "stopped" when it was cancelled, and "working" when it is pending or running. The newest run is the newest by `createdAt`, then id.
  - The face's pose is the row's pose while the row in Tracking agrees with the leg; "done" for a done Task; "idle" for a cancelled Task; in every other case, including every earlier leg, the pose of the leg's standing (finished: done, failed: failed, stopped: idle, working: working).
  - **A signal with no trip keeps its outcome line,** as before: one with no `resolution.runId`, or whose run is not a handoff's run (a signal handed off before Tracking started an untracked run). Its source's mark leads the row and its outcome line is the second line.
  - **The trip is worked out on read,** in one `@hercule/client-core` function, `describeHandoffTrip`, with its own tests, from the leg that `task.queryLegs` returns and the shell's Tracking rows. It uses a leg only when the leg's signal and run are the signal's own (`resolution.runId`). A leg carries its newest run as an id and a status only, which is all the trip reads. Opening the line goes to the Task's row in Tracking whenever the Task is in progress and has a row in Tracking, for every leg of it, including the earlier ones and whether or not the row agrees; else to the leg's newest run (see The pane).
  - **Each Done page reads its legs in one batch:** `signal.query` for the page, then one `task.queryLegs` with the page's distinct `resolution.runId`s, skipped when the page has none. A page is at most 50 signals, which is the most one request takes. A signal the pane shows on Done whose leg is on no loaded page, such as one reached by a link or a reload to a signal past the first page, reads its own leg with one `task.queryLegs` call. See What the screen reads for how the legs stay current.

### The list on To do

- **Sections on the All tab:**
  - **Now:** the signals of priority `urgent`, pinned at the top. The section is drawn only while it holds a row.
  - **Signals:** plugin signals, and core signals from other workflows, from assistants, from the user and from Hercule, each labelled with its source. "Signals" is the one word for them across the app, the CLI and the docs.
  - **From triage:** the rows the named triage workflow raised, drawn quieter.
  - A plugin's tab shows Now and Signals for that plugin only. The Triage tab shows From triage only.
- **Hercule's offers,** the Ignore Rule suggestions ([Ignore Rules](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#99-ignore-rules)), sit in Signals with Hercule's face as their mark. They are not on the Triage tab, because triage did not raise them.
- **Order inside each section:** the rows marked "Back" first, oldest `until` first, then the rest oldest first. Now too: the signal that has waited longest comes first.
- **A row** is ~~one line~~ two lines, each cut with an ellipsis, in both densities: the source's mark, the title, then who asks, the kind and where, then the age. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* The Intake drawing (`asks.js`) puts who asks, the kind and where on a second line, and the app follows the drawing. The split's motion depends on rows ~~of one line~~ whose text never wraps ([The split and its minimums](#the-split-and-its-minimums)).
  - A signal of priority `high` shows a small marigold mark at the row's end. It does not reorder the list.
  - A Now row shows its Your work line, such as "Fixing in *Fix 3-D Secure checkout*", when a thread or a Task is on it; otherwise who asks, the kind and where. "Escalates at 11:23" waits for a `when` block, which has no schema yet.
    - *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* Built: a Now row with Your work leads its second line with "Your work: *Task title*" (the title in bold, of the Task whose agent's face leads the row, else the first Task of Your work), then " · kind · where". Who asks is left out, as the Intake drawing's burning row leaves out the alerting system. A thread with no Task on it is not built. The line is built in `@hercule/client-core` beside the row's other line.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* A signal whose Your work holds a Task that is a row of Tracking is led by that agent's face (24px, the "done" pose, the first such Task when there are several), and its source's mark moves to the start of the second line at 12px. The face and the Now line are read from the shell's Your work and Tracking rows by the list, which gives each row only the plain values it draws, so a change to one signal's Your work draws only that row again. The lead column of every row stays 20px, as in the Intake drawing. A face overflows it by 2px on each side, so its centre and the start of the text (42px in) are where the drawing has them.
  - `unsure` shows its kind: "Unsure".
  - A row whose snooze ran out shows "Back".

### Tracking

*(Added 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* The place in To do where the user follows the work they handed to agents, until they mark it done. It is drawn in [`docs/design/tracking/index.html`](../design/tracking/index.html); its [BRIEF](../design/tracking/BRIEF.md) lists every state, key, motion and cost, and this section is the part of it the app builds. Why it exists, and what a handoff does, are [ADR 0046](../adr/0046-handing-a-signal-off-tracks-the-work-on-a-task.md) and [`signal.handOff`](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#signal). The screen never calls a row a Task. **This section covers the first slice, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801):** the states Failed, Working, Finished and Stopped. Needs you, the agent's face on a signal about a tracked Task, and the pane come with [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802) and [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803); each place for them is named below. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* The second slice, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802), builds Needs you and Your work (below). The Request's answers in a row and the pane are not built; they wait for [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803) or a follow-up. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* The third slice, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803), builds the pane (The pane of a row, below). The Request's answers in the row are not built, in the row or in the pane: the thread has them. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* The fourth slice, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804), builds Try again, the pane's answer that starts a handoff again on the same Task (The pane of a row, below).

- **What it lists:** the Tasks that have a handoff entry and are `in-progress`, read with `task.queryTracking` ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#task)). Ticking a row off sets its Task `done`, which takes it out of that read. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* A run started again keeps its Task wherever it is started: from Tracking, from a run's page in Workflows, or with the CLI's `hercule run rerun`. So its Task's row shows in Tracking and turns Working, whoever pressed the button ([spec 07](./07-workflows.md) §7.4).
- **Its runs:** the same read returns each Task's newest run and newest live run, with the facts a row draws, so the app makes no read per Task or per run. Each Task's state is worked out from those two runs. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* The row also carries `liveRunIds`, the ids of every pending or running run of the Task, newest first, because a Task can have two live runs at once and a Request of the older one must still turn the row Needs you ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#task)). The read is one request under one key, and returns the rows' facts together, so they agree with each other and every component gets the same rows.
- **Where it shows:** on the To do view only. Later, Done and Everything draw no Tracking, and `T` does nothing there. Tracking belongs to To do, not to a tab: it shows the same rows on every tab ([Tabs](#tabs)). *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* Done draws no Tracking list, but it reads the shell's Tracking rows to tell the trip of a handed-off signal (see Views, Done). Opening Tracking from Done, which the pane's button "Go to Tracking" does, shows To do, because Tracking belongs to To do; closing Tracking afterwards stays on To do.

**Either Intake or Tracking, never both.** Two lists of the same rows in one column are hard to tell apart.

- **Folded,** Tracking lies over the foot of the list. Its head reads "TRACKING 7 · 4 wait on you · Show all ⌃". Under the head are the two rows that changed state last, newest first. A handoff lands there. The list keeps room under its last row for it.
- **Opened** (a click on the head, or `T`), it covers the whole list column under the title bar's hairline.
  - At the top: "‹ Intake", the title "Tracking 7" and "4 wait on you".
  - Then every row, grouped by state under the state's mark.
  - At the foot: a row that sums up the signals ("SIGNALS 7", the sources' marks, "Back to Intake"), which closes Tracking.
- **While it is open,** `J` and `K` walk only its rows.
- **Empty:** the head and one line: "Hand a signal to an agent, and follow it here until you mark it done."
- **"N wait on you"** is what Tracking adds to the To do count ([The count and attention](#the-count-and-attention)).

**States.** Each row is in one state, worked out from its Task's runs in one `@hercule/client-core` function with its own tests. Highest first:

- *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* **Needs you:** the work waits on the user, whatever its runs did. It does not come from a run, see Needs you below.
- **Failed:** the last run failed.
- **Working:** a run is live. Its line names the signal triggers the run only waits on, from the run's `awaitedSignalIds` (see the table below).
- **Finished:** no run is live, and the last run completed.
- **Stopped:** the last run was cancelled.

The last run is the newest by `createdAt`, so a re-run moves its row to the state of the re-run. ~~Needs you, the highest state, is added by [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* Needs you, the highest state, does not come from a run: it is added over the runs' state (Needs you, below).

**Order:** ~~Failed, Finished, Working, Stopped.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* Needs you, Failed, Finished, Working, Stopped. Inside a state, the oldest handoff first. Needs you, Failed and Finished lead because they wait on the user; Working and Stopped do not. ~~Needs you goes first once #802 adds it.~~

**The row** leads with the face of the agent that has the work (the workflow's colleague), in its state's pose, not with the source's mark. Then the title and one line under it:

| State | Line |
|---|---|
| *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* Needs you, by a Request | the Request's question in plain text, such as "Run git push?", in marigold, with no workflow name |
| *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* Needs you, by a signal | "*Kind* on *place*", such as "Checks failed on payments-api #1302", in marigold; the kind alone when the signal has no place |
| Failed | "*Investigate* failed · *reason*", in marigold |
| Finished | "*Review PR* finished", in `--ok`, on a wash of `--ok-tint` at about half strength |
| Working | "*Fix bug* · working", or "*Implement* · waiting for *the awaited signal trigger ids*" |
| Stopped | "*Implement* · stopped" |

- The workflow's name is in its colleague's hue.
- The time on the right is how long the row has been in its state. It is bold, in marigold for Failed and in `--ok` for Finished.
- **Finished is green, not marigold.** It waits on the user and counts, but it is good news: the work only needs a tick.
- *reason* is the failure message of the run: a validation failure's message, the failed edge's message, or the failed step's error. When the run has none, it is the failure reason in plain words. The controller works it out and sends it as the run's `failureCause` in `task.queryTracking`, so the app reads no run for it. A run with no `failureCause` reads "*Investigate* failed".
- **Stopped reads "stopped", not "stopped by you".** The run's facts do not say who cancelled the run, so the line cannot tell.
- **A Working run that only waits on signal triggers** reads "waiting for *ids*", where *ids* are the awaited signal trigger ids joined with ", ", such as "waiting for checks_passed". The drawing's "waiting for checks on #1311" needs the run's plan and inputs, which the run's facts do not carry.
- **The agent's latest message is not shown in slice 1.** No field, read or push carries it, and the `session` topic does not bring it. The Working row reads "*Fix bug* · working". Showing the message needs a new field on the session, kept by the controller, and is left to a follow-up ticket.
- **Faces on signals.** A signal about a tracked Task, such as the checks that failed on the agent's PR, ~~will wear~~ wears the agent's face, with its source's mark moved to the start of its second line. ~~That comes with #802, because it needs the Task Your work names. The row leaves the place for it.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* Built with #802 (see Intake rows). *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* Done does the same for every signal that was handed to an agent, built with #805 (see Views, Done).

**Needs you.** *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* The state above all the others: an agent or a source is blocked on the user. The rows are built on the client, in one `@hercule/client-core` function with its own tests, from the Tracking read, the sessions that wait (the shell already holds them for Waiting on you) and Your work (below).

- **What makes a row Needs you,** either of these:
  - A session of any live run of the row's Task has an open Request. The session belongs to the run by its `runId`, and the run is live when its id is in the row's `liveRunIds`. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)*
  - An open signal on To do names the row's Task in its Your work. A snoozed signal is not on To do, so it does not count.
- **Request before signal.** When both wait, the row shows the Request, because an agent is blocked until it is answered. When several Requests wait, or several signals, the row shows the oldest of each: a Request by when its session was last active, a signal by when it was raised. A newer one arriving does not change the row.
- **Its line** is the Request's question, or "*Kind* on *place*" for a signal (table above). **Its time** is how long since the session's last activity, or since the signal was raised.
- **It counts toward To do,** like Failed and Finished. A signal that makes a row Needs you is an ordinary signal, so it counts too. The drawing's 7 signals and 4 waiting rows make 11, and this one Task adds 1 as a row and 1 as a signal.
- ~~**It has no Mark done, and `E` does nothing on it.** Opening it goes to what waits (see Opening a row).~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* **Mark done follows one rule** (see Take-off): a Needs you row that waits on a Request has none, and one that waits on a signal has it while no run of its Task is live. The pane holds what waits, with a button that opens it (see The pane of a row).
- **Not built: the Request's answers in the row or in the pane.** The drawing puts Allow once, Always allow and Deny under the question, in the row and in the pane. ~~They wait for the pane ticket [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803) or a follow-up.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* Neither is built. The pane shows the question and "Open the thread", where the user answers, with the dock's own answers.

**Your work.** *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* The open Tasks a signal is about, as [spec 10 §9.5](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#95-blocks) defines it: the Tasks whose provenance has a `ref` equal to the signal's thread ref. The controller works it out on every read and never stores it. The app reads it with `signal.queryYourWork` ([spec 11](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#signal)) and matches it to the To do list it holds.

- **One shell read, `yourWorkQuery`,** under one stable key: `signal.queryYourWork`, one request whatever the number of signals. The reply is the open and in-progress Tasks that carry the thread ref of an open signal, on To do or snoozed. Each Task comes once, newest first, with its id, title, status, update time and `signalIds`, the ids of the open signals it belongs to. It holds no description and no provenance, so its size follows the number of Tasks that match, not the Tasks there are. With no thread ref on an open signal, the reply is empty. The shell's loader loads it with `ensureShellData`, with the shell's read options. It does not wait for the read of To do.
- **Which Task belongs to which signal** is a `@hercule/client-core` function with its own tests (`matchYourWork`). It intersects each Task's `signalIds` with the To do list the app holds now, so To do alone decides which signals show Your work. A signal the user moves off To do stops being Your work at once, before the next read of Your work answers. A snooze that runs out needs no new read, because the reply already covers snoozed signals, and the signal joins To do on its own read. The Tasks keep the order the controller gave them.
- **It is read again** in two cases:
  - On every push on the `task` topic, because a Task's status or provenance may have changed.
  - On every push on the `signal` topic, because a signal may have been raised or resolved. Your work has its own key, outside the `signals` prefix, because that prefix is also read again by every `workflow`, `connection` and `plugin` push, which cannot change Your work.
- **Each reader builds it.** Your work (the match of Tasks to signals) and Tracking's rows are built by the component that draws them, with `useMemo` over narrow selects: the To do signals that a Task of Your work names, the Tasks of the Your work read, the Tasks of the Tracking read and the Requests that wait. A change to a signal that no Task names changes none of them, so nothing is built or drawn again. One build is one pass over the Tasks and the signals, so building once and sharing the result is not worth its code.

**Take-off.** Taking a row off Tracking is one act, whether it is Mark done or Cancel. `E`, and Mark done on hover over the row, mark a row done: `task.update { taskId, status: "done" }`. ~~Mark done is not drawn on a Working row, and `E` does nothing there, because it would leave a run going.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* ~~The same holds for a Needs you row, which may wait on a Request of a live run.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* **One rule decides it, for the row's control, `E` and the pane's answer:** a row can be marked done when no run of its Task is live and the row does not wait on a Request. It is one `@hercule/client-core` function with its own tests, `canMarkTrackingRowDone`, and everything that asks whether a row can be taken off asks it. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* One exception sits on top of it, in the screen and not in that function: a Task whose re-run is starting cannot be taken off (the last bullet below).

- A Finished, Failed or Stopped row can, because its runs have ended.
- A Working row cannot, because marking it done would leave its run going.
- A Needs you row that waits on a Request cannot, because a run is live and blocked on the question.
- A Needs you row that waits on a signal can while its agent has finished, because the signal is what remains. It cannot while a run of its Task is live, such as a re-run. The drawing offers Mark done on such a row too.
- *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* A Task whose re-run is starting cannot be taken off, from the press of Try again until Tracking's read shows the new run or the request is refused (The pane of a row, below). `canMarkTrackingRowDone` does not change. The screen's take-off (`takeOffTask` in `useTrackingControls`) also asks `useRerunTracking`, which reports the lock, and does nothing when a re-run of the row's Task is starting. So `E`, the row's Mark done control and the pane's Mark done and Cancel all do nothing on that row.

The row fades over `--dur-1`, then the gap closes over `--dur-3`. A failed take-off shows its error in the toast's place, in `--fail`, and the row stays ([The foot, the toast and failures](#the-foot-the-toast-and-failures)).

**Opening a row.** ~~A click, or `↩`, opens its last run's page in Workflows (`/runs/$runId`, [The routes](#the-routes)), until the pane lands with [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).~~ ~~A Needs you row goes to what waits instead: by a Request, the thread of the session that asks (`/threads/$sessionId`); by a signal, Tracking closes and that signal is selected in To do, on the All tab, so its pane opens whichever tab the signal belongs to.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* A row no longer leaves the screen. Selecting it opens its pane in Intake's split, where the signal's pane goes (The pane of a row, below):

- **In opened Tracking,** a click selects the row and shows its pane. `↩` does the same and moves the focus into the pane (see Keys).
- **In folded Tracking,** a click opens Tracking with that row selected and its pane shown, as "Show in Tracking" does ([The pane](#the-pane)).
- **The selected row is in the address,** as the search param `task`, the id of the row's Task. It sits beside `tracking`, and the reason is the same: a run's page, a thread or a signal is a screen of its own, so coming back from one finds Tracking open on the same row, with its pane. Closing Tracking, or opening a signal, clears it. A `task` that names no row of Tracking, such as a Task marked done elsewhere, is no selection. Like the selection of a signal, it replaces the history entry. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* **When the selected row leaves Tracking, the selection moves on,** whatever took it off: the user's own take-off, another client, or a push. It goes to the row the same rule as Intake's picks (the first row after it that stays, else the last one before it, else none), counted over the rows as they were drawn just before it left, so the next row is the one the user saw below it. This is one rule, in `useTrackingControls`, and the take-off has no selection step of its own: a push that takes the row off before the controller answers the request moves the selection just the same. Focus goes where it goes for any selection change: when the row's pane held it and the pane goes with the row, it goes to the sheet, so `J`, `K`, `E`, `O` and Esc keep working. The pane and this rule follow the last read, not the rows the sheet draws: while the pointer holds the sheet, a row that another client took off stays drawn, but its pane has nothing true to show, so the selection leaves it at once and `J` and `K` walk past it. The sheet does not move under the pointer, and the row goes when the pointer leaves.
- **The pane's links** leave Tracking: the run's page in Workflows (`/runs/$runId`, [The routes](#the-routes)), the thread of a session (`/threads/$sessionId`), or, for a signal, Tracking closes and that signal is selected in To do, on the All tab, so its pane opens whichever tab the signal belongs to.

**The pane of a row.** *(Added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* While Tracking is open, the split's pane ([The split and its minimums](#the-split-and-its-minimums)) shows the selected row, where it shows a signal otherwise. The split follows the row: the pane shows while a row is selected and the window is wide enough, the bar's pane toggle reads "Tracking pane", and Esc hides the pane (see Keys). The pane reads its row from the last read of Tracking, not from the rows the sheet draws, so it shows what is true now while the sheet may still hold a change back. It is drawn as the Tracking drawing's pane: one trip from top to bottom, joined by a hairline, in the classes the drawing calls `rt-*` and the app calls `tr-*`. The screen never calls the row a Task.

- **The head.** The colleague's face (18px, in its row's pose), the workflow's name, "·" and the row's state in its colour (Needs you in marigold, Failed in `--fail-ink`, Finished in green; Working and Stopped in no colour), then "Open the run" at the right, which opens the row's newest run's page in every state. Under it the Task's title, and the standing line:
  - Finished: "Finished at 09:20 after 8m". Failed: "Failed at 09:26 after 6m". The duration runs from when the run started, and is left out for a run that never started.
  - Stopped: "Stopped at 08:47".
  - Working: "Started at 09:33 · 8m so far", "Started at 09:33 · 1h 4m so far", or "just now" in the first minute. It follows the shared age clock, which keeps one timer for every label on screen, so the pane adds none, and it changes at each whole minute (`findNextTrackingElapsedChange`).
  - Needs you: "Waiting on you since 09:33", from when the row began to wait.
  - Times are in the user's time zone setting. Durations read in whole minutes, the same way on every line, for a run that has ended and for one that goes on: "40s" under a minute, then "8m", then "1h 4m". The words and the choice of line are one `@hercule/client-core` function with its own tests (`describeTrackingStanding`).
- **You asked.** The first message of the trip, from the user: their avatar, "You asked", and in small text when the Task was first handed off and to which workflow ("09:12 · handed to Review PR"). Under it a quote block: the source's mark, who asked and where, and the kind, then the first block of the signal as text, cut at 2 lines. It is the signal of the Task's **first** handoff, because a Task handed off again keeps its first ask. The signal is read with `signal.read`, as the signal's own pane reads it, and its id is the `firstHandoff.signalId` of `task.readTracking`, which also holds the workflow's name, `firstHandoff.workflowName`: the workflow of the run that handoff started, which may be older than the runs the pane lists.
- **The agent's message.** The second message of the trip: the colleague's face (28px), the workflow's name in the colleague's hue, and a small stamp ("finished 09:20", "failed 09:26", "stopped 08:47", "working", or "waiting on you since 09:33" for a row that waits on a Request). A Needs you row that waits on a signal is stamped by its run's ending, because its agent has ended and the signal is what waits. Its parts, in this order:
  - **The failure reason,** Failed rows only, first, in `--fail-ink` with a 2px `--fail` rule at its left: the row's run's `failureCause`.
  - **What it did,** *(amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803))* when the row is not Working and its run is the Task's newest run, which has not always ended. So a Needs you row that waits on a Request of a live newest run shows the output of that run's last completed agent step, as context for the question, as the drawing's `?state=needs-you` does. Not on a Working row, and not on a Needs you row that waits on a Request of an older live run while the newest run completed: "From the step *Review*", the step's name, then that step's output drawn by its shape, as the BRIEF decides ([What it did, by the output's shape](../design/tracking/BRIEF.md#the-pane)). The controller shapes the output into a bounded reply (`task.readTracking`, [spec 11](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#task)), so the pane never holds a whole output:
    - A step **with an `outputSchema`** (`fields`): one ruled row for each top-level field, in the schema's order, at most 12, the label in a 132px column and the value beside it. A string shows as it is, cut at 2 lines (the controller also cuts it at 300 characters). A number shows as it is, a boolean as "yes" or "no", and a missing value as "none". A list and an object show how many they hold, "3 items", "1 field", in the quiet colour. Under them one link, "See the whole output in the run", to the row's run's page. The words are `describeTrackingOutputValue`.
    - A step **with no `outputSchema`** (`text`): the output's text as markdown, cut by the controller to 1,000 characters, at a break where it can, and ending with "…" ([spec 11](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#task) has the rule). Under it "Read the rest in the thread", to the thread of the step's session, shown only when the output has a session.
    - A run that completed no agent step, or whose step left nothing to draw, has no output, and the part is left out.
  - **The runs,** one line for each run of the Task, the newest 20, oldest first, drawn only when the Task has two runs or more, or its newest run is live (`shouldListTrackingRuns`), as the drawing does. A single run that has ended would only repeat the stamp beside the agent's name, and the head's "Open the run" already opens it. Each line has the time, a mark for the run's state, and the workflow's name in its hue, which links to that run's page, then what the run did ("finished", "failed: *reason*", "stopped", "working", "waiting for *ids*"). A run that has a session ends in a quiet "Thread" link to it. The line's words, the mark's pose and the stamp's word come from one place inside `describeTrackingTrip`. What the trip draws, and which parts show, is one function, `describeTrackingTrip`, with its own tests; the components only draw it.
  - **The action step the run ended on,** when the workflow did something itself after the agent: a line with the bolt icon and the step's name, such as "Open a pull request". It shows only when the row's run is the newest run, it has completed, and its last completed step is an action step. The controller sets it for a completed run only, so a live run, which has not ended on anything, shows no ending action step even where it shows an output, and a failed run is explained by its failure reason, so neither shows it. A row that names an older run, such as a Needs you row whose Request belongs to an older live run, shows neither this line nor the output above, because both are the newest run's and would sit under the older run's name. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* The same holds when the pane's two reads disagree: the row can name a newer run than the Task's read lists, because a new run's push brings the Tracking read before the Task's read. Both show only while the newest run of the read is the row's run, so a run's output never sits under another run's name for the moment between the two answers.
  - **The Request,** for a row that waits on one: a card in `--you-tint` with the waiting mark, the Request's question, and a button, "Open the thread", that opens the thread of the session that asks. The user answers there.
  - **Linked work** is not drawn. It comes with [#807](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/807), and the pane leaves no place for it.
- **The signal that waits,** for a Needs you row that waits on a signal: a third message in the trip, from the source. It has the source's mark, its name and, in small text, the kind and place, then a card in `--you-tint` with the signal's title and a button, "Open the signal", which closes Tracking and selects the signal in To do (see Opening a row).
- **The answers,** at the foot of the pane, drawn as the signal pane's answers: a button, a describe line, and the key that presses it. At most one is suggested, and `↩` never presses Stop the run.

  | Row | Answers | Suggested |
  |---|---|---|
  | Working; Needs you by a Request; any row with a run of its Task live | Stop the run | none |
  | ~~Finished~~ | ~~Mark done, Cancel~~ | ~~Mark done~~ |
  | ~~Failed, Stopped~~ | ~~Mark done, Cancel~~ | ~~none~~ |
  | ~~Needs you by a signal, no run live~~ | ~~Mark done, Cancel~~ | ~~none: Open the signal is the move~~ |
  | *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* Finished | Mark done, Try again, Cancel | Mark done |
  | *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* Failed | Try again, Mark done, Cancel | Try again |
  | *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* Stopped | Try again, Mark done, Cancel | none |
  | *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* Needs you by a signal, no run live | Try again, Mark done, Cancel | none: Open the signal is the move |
