  - **Which answers a row gets** follows `canMarkTrackingRowDone` (see Take-off): a row that cannot be marked done has a run going, so its only answer is Stop the run, ~~and a row that can has Mark done and Cancel~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* and a row that can has Try again, Mark done and Cancel. Try again is offered on exactly the rows Mark done is, because both need the same thing: no run of the Task going, and no Request waiting on an answer. Starting a second run beside a live one would leave two agents on one Task. The choice and the words are one `@hercule/client-core` function with its own tests (`listTrackingAnswers`).
  - **Stop the run** calls `run.cancel` on the Task's newest live run, and the row turns Stopped. It keeps the run's workspace (`keepWorkspace: true`): the Task stays open, and the user may want to see what the agent left, ~~which Try again will need ([#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804))~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* which Try again does not delete: a re-run gets a fresh workspace, and the old one stays. The run's page lets the user choose, with its "Delete workspace" box; the pane has no dialog, so it takes the choice that loses nothing. There is no confirmation either, because the row stays in Tracking. Describe line: "Stops *Fix bug*. The row stays in Tracking, as Stopped". It names the workflow of the run it stops, which is not always the row's own run (a Failed row can belong to a Task whose older run still goes), and says "as Stopped" only when stopping that run leaves the row Stopped: the run is the Task's newest, no other run stays live, and the row does not wait on a signal. Otherwise it ends at "The row stays in Tracking". `listTrackingAnswers` returns the run with that fact.
  - **Mark done** sets the Task `done` and **Cancel** sets it `cancelled`, both through the take-off above, so the row fades out, the selection moves on to the next row (the rule of the selected row in the address, above) and the toast says what happened: "Marked done: *title*" or "Cancelled: *title*". Describe lines: "Takes it off Tracking and marks the work done. Nothing is sent" and "Takes it off Tracking as cancelled. Nothing is sent". Cancel is quiet, and `E` presses Mark done.
  - **A failed answer.** A failed Stop shows its error under its button, in `--fail`, as a failed answer does in the signal's pane, and never in a toast. A failed Mark done or Cancel shows its error in the toast's place, in `--fail`, and the row stays, as a failed take-off does.
  - ~~**Try again** is not an answer. It waits for [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804), and the pane draws no disabled button for it.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* **Try again** calls `run.rerun` on the Task's newest run, in its default mode, re-stamp. The new run keeps the Task (`taskId`, [spec 07](./07-workflows.md) §7.4), so the row turns Working on the same Task, and the trip's list of runs gains a line.
    - **Why re-stamp.** It starts the workflow as it is stored now, so a workflow the user fixed after the failure is picked up. Replay would run the old plan again and fail the same way. The pane has no choice of mode.
    - **When it is refused.** A run of a workflow that was deleted, or one sent with `run.start`, has no stored workflow to re-stamp, and `run.rerun` refuses it (spec 07 §7.4). The error shows under the button, in `--fail`, as a failed Stop does, and never in a toast. The same holds for any other refusal, such as a workflow whose inputs changed. The row stays as it was. The refusal stays only while it is about the Task's newest run: once the newest run changes, the old refusal is gone. The pane shows the controller's error as it is, and never replays by itself, because the user would get the old plan without knowing it. A refused re-stamp ends with "Replay the original run's plan instead.", but the pane has no replay. The run's page has it: "Open the run" (or `O`) leads to its Re-run dialog, which offers replay.
    - **Describe line:** "Re-runs *Implement* on *PAY-212* from the start". It names the workflow of the run it re-runs (the Task's newest run), then the Task's title. There is no fine print under it.
    - **Locked until Tracking's read shows the new run.** From the press, the re-run is "starting". It stops starting when Tracking's read (`task.queryTracking`, the read the rows come from) shows a newer newest run for the Task, or when the request is refused. `run.rerun` answers before the new run shows in any read, and until then the Task still has no run going, so a second press would start a second run. While the re-run is starting:
      - The pane's answers are locked, and Try again sends nothing.
      - `E` and the row's Mark done control do nothing on that row, as on a Working row. Closing the Task now would leave the starting run on a closed Task.
      - The lock belongs to the Task, not to the pane. `useRerunTracking` keeps no state of its own: the re-run requests are kept in the app's query cache, not in a screen, with no time limit. A new press first removes that Task's earlier, settled requests, so the cache holds at most one for each Task. The lock ends only when Tracking's read shows a newer run or the request is refused, never after a time. So it holds when the user moves to another row and back, closes Tracking, or leaves Intake and comes back.
      - A refusal ends the lock, so the user can press Try again or choose another answer.
    - **No confirmation.** It starts work, but it loses nothing: the old run, its record and its kept workspace stay, as they are for any re-run.
    - **The label is never "Retry".** The glossary avoids it for runs, because a re-run starts over and never resumes.
- **The pane's foot** lists the keys, as the signal pane's does: "J K move"; "↩" and the suggested answer, when one is suggested; "E mark done", when Mark done is an answer and not the suggested one; "O open the run".

**Departures from the drawing.** *(Added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* The drawing and the pane differ in these places, and this section decides:

- The standing line has no harness, model or runner. No read carries them.
- The action step the run ended on shows the step's name only, not "Opened draft PR #1302". No record keeps a summary of what an action step did, and a follow-up ticket can add one.
- A Request's Allow once, Always allow and Deny are not in the pane. The card has "Open the thread" instead, where the thread's own answers are.
- There is no foot line ("Leaves Tracking when...") and no fine print under the answers, as the signal's pane has none. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* The drawing's Try again has fine print, the harness, model and runner of the run it starts, and the pane draws none: no read carries them.
- Stopped never says "by you": no field says who stopped the run.
- A missing value in a field of the output reads "none", where the drawing writes the value as it is.

**The pane's reads.** *(Added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* One `task.readTracking` for the open row and one `signal.read` for the first handoff's signal, when the pane opens ([What Intake costs](#what-intake-costs)). The first is under `queryKeys.trackingDetail(taskId)`, one key for each Task, and only the open pane reads it, so a row nobody opened costs nothing. A `task` push that names the Task reads it again, and so does a `run` push that already reads Tracking again (see Live updates). There is no topic of its own. When the Task is gone, the read fails with `not_found`, which is not tried again, and the pane shows that the Task was not found.

**Motion.** Every motion here is `transform` or `opacity` on an HTML element, plays once, only for rows on screen, and stops with Reduce motion ([Rules](#rules), rule 2). A change the user did not make waits while the pointer is on the list column, as [Intake's own changes do](#motion).

- **Opening:** the sheet rises from where folded Tracking stood, in 240ms, with a slight spring (`cubic-bezier(0.3, 1.2, 0.5, 1)`). Its head, sections and rows cascade in, 16ms apart from 60ms, each over `--dur-2`.
- **Closing:** the sheet drops back in 180ms, easing in.
- **A handoff:** a lifted copy of the signal's row (`--shadow-2`) flies from Signals to the top of folded Tracking over `--dur-3`. The rows below it in Signals close up.
- **A state change** (Working to Finished): the row's line fades in anew over `--dur-2`. When its place changes, the rows between slide over `--dur-3`.
- **Marking done:** as above.

**Live updates.** The `task` and `run` topics. A `task` push reads Tracking again. A `run` push reads it again only when it names the newest run or any live run of a row (*(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* a live run is any id in the row's `liveRunIds`, so the end of an older live run changes which Requests the row waits on), when it is the creation of a run (a re-run keeps its Task and makes no `task` push), or when it names no run. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* A `task` push also reads Your work again, because a Task's status or provenance may have changed. A `signal` push reads To do again, as before, and Your work with it, because a signal may have been raised or resolved. The snooze timer reads To do alone when a snooze runs out, because Your work already covers snoozed signals (see Your work above). A `run` push, the window being hidden and shown, and a Request that opens or closes read nothing for Your work. A Request that opens or closes is not a Tracking read: the rows are built again from the waiting sessions the shell already holds. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* The open pane's read, `task.readTracking`, follows Tracking's: a `task` push that names its Task (or names no ids) marks it out of date, and so does a `run` push that marks Tracking out of date, by the same test. A run that has ended never changes again, so an older run in the pane's list cannot be what a push is about; what can change is the Task's newest run and its live runs, which the test covers. Only the open pane's read is made again, and the others are read when their pane opens. *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* The Done lists the app holds, for every source tab, and the leg a Done pane read on its own, are read again (their signals and their legs) as follows. A `task` push reads them again only when it names the Task of a held leg, or names no Task, because a new handoff always resolves a signal and a `signal` push covers that. A `run` push reads them again when it is the creation of a run (a re-run starts a run no leg holds yet and makes no `task` push, and a run a cron fire starts looks the same until the lists are read), when it names no run, or when it names the newest run or a live run of a held leg. A `signal` push reads them already. A read that is still in flight is always read again after any `task` or `run` push, because its answer may predate the change and it holds no leg yet to judge the push by, so no change is lost. The reads are not cancelled while under way. The creation of a run costs two requests per held page (`signal.query` and `task.queryLegs`), and there is no timer.

### Row controls and the bulk bar

A row shows its controls on hover and on keyboard focus. Every control here only changes the user's own list and sends nothing to the source, which is why the design language lets them sit on a row ([../design-language.md](../design-language.md)). An answer, which sends something, is never on a row.

| Row | Controls |
|---|---|
| To do: a plugin kind, or `fyi` | Snooze, Done, checkbox |
| To do: `proposal`, `offer` or `unsure` | Snooze, checkbox |
| Now | Snooze, Done, Not urgent, checkbox |
| Back | as its kind |
| Later | Unsnooze, and Done when its kind has one |
| Done view | none |
| Everything | none |

- **Done's tooltip is its describe line,** such as "Takes it off your list. GitHub is not told". Done is the core's action ([Actions, Done and Hand to an agent](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#94-actions-done-and-hand-to-an-agent)); the app calls `signal.markDone`.
- **Dismiss is never on a row.** It is an answer, in the pane.
- **Checks.** A checked row wears the selected tint. Checks belong to one view, and switching views clears them.
- **The bulk bar** shows while any row is checked: "3 selected", then its buttons.
  - Snooze always shows. Done shows when at least one checked row has it. In Later, the bar offers Unsnooze and Done.
  - The app sends only the ids the action applies to. With one `github/mentioned` and two `proposal` rows checked, Done sends one id.
  - Rows the action skipped stay checked, so the bar then reads "2 selected · Snooze".
  - There is no confirmation step: the highlight below shows what a button will do before the click.
- **The bulk highlight.** While the pointer or keyboard focus is on a bulk button:
  - the checked rows it skips fade to 40% opacity;
  - the rows it acts on get a 2px accent rule at their left edge, in green, because it marks the move the user is about to make;
  - the bar names the split: "Done 3 · keeps 2 proposals". The wording names kinds, never "from triage", because any run can raise a core kind.
  - When the button acts on every checked row, nothing fades and every row gets the rule.
  - The highlight shows with no delay, so it keeps up with the pointer. On `E` or a click, the acted-on rows hold for one beat, `--dur-2`, then leave; the skipped rows return to full opacity, still checked. With Reduce motion, the highlight shows with no transition and the beat is skipped.
- **No Undo** in v1. A mistaken Done stays done and is found in the Done view; a mistaken snooze is undone with Unsnooze in Later.

### Snooze and Not urgent

- **Snooze** opens a menu of five choices, from client-core, in the user's time zone setting:
  1. In 1 hour
  2. This afternoon at 14:00, shown before 13:00; This evening at 18:00, from 13:00 until 17:00; greyed out after 17:00, so the keys never move
  3. Tomorrow 09:00
  4. Monday 09:00, a week ahead on a Monday. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#525](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/525).)* On a Sunday it is eight days ahead, the Monday after tomorrow, so it is never the same time as Tomorrow
  5. Pick a time
  - The app calls `signal.snooze`; Unsnooze calls `signal.unsnooze`. What a snooze does to a signal is [Snooze and Not urgent](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#97-snooze-and-not-urgent)'s. A Now signal can be snoozed.
- **Not urgent** sits on a Now row, and in the pane of a Now signal. It calls `signal.lowerPriority`, which lowers the signal from `urgent` to `high`: the row moves to Signals and wears the `high` mark. It sends nothing to the source. It only lowers: v1 has no way to raise a signal to Now.

### The pane

The open signal fills the split's side pane. Top to bottom:

- **The provenance line:** the source's mark and name, and the kind's label, such as "GitHub · Mentioned". A plugin signal's line links its kind's row in Settings › Intake, with the signal's Connection selected: `/settings/intake?kind=github/mentioned&connection=<id>` ([Settings](#settings)). A core signal's line names its source, links nothing, and reads "Hercule" for the core's own offers.
- **The title,** who asks, where, and when.
- **The blocks,** drawn as [Blocks](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#95-blocks) defines them, at most 8:
  - Text as markdown.
  - Messages as a conversation: each message with its author's avatar, or initials when there is none, its time and text. The message that mentions the user carries a marigold rule at its left. A message's file and line, its recipients, its attachments' names, "and N more" for the messages left out, and "Read the rest" for a cut message, which opens its `url`.
  - Change as the branch pair, the file count, additions and deletions, and the checks' summary.
  - Checks as one row per failed or pending check, with its log in monospace when it has one, then the passed count and "and N more".
  - A block of a type the app does not know draws one quiet line, "This part can't be shown here.", never nothing, so the user knows something was left out.
  - Images inside blocks load only here, for the open signal ([Content-Security-Policy](#content-security-policy)).
- **Your work and Sources** have fixed places outside the blocks, under them: Your work names the threads and Tasks on the signal, such as "Fixing in *Fix 3-D Secure checkout*" or "Triage at 11:00 added 38 events to this task"; Sources links the events behind it. Both are computed on read, never stored.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* **Your work, built.** One block per Task of Your work, under the blocks and above the answers, in the order Your work lists them. The read covers every open signal, but Your work is shown only for signals on To do, so a pane on Later or Done shows none. A thread alone, with no Task on it, has no block yet.
    - A Task that is a row of Tracking: the agent's face (28px), "Your work", the Task's title in bold, "In Tracking · *state*" with the state in lower case, in bold and marigold when the row waits on the user, and a small "Show in Tracking" button. The button opens Tracking with that Task's row selected, so the sheet scrolls to the row and focuses it. It clears the selected signal, as opening Tracking does. The face is in the pose of the row's state, except on a row that waits on a signal, where it is the agent as its last run left it ("done", or "failed"), because the agent itself asks nothing, as the drawing has it.
    - An open Task that is not a row of Tracking (no one was handed it): the Tasks icon, "Your work", the title in bold and its status, "Open" or "In progress". No button, because there is no row to show.
    - The words and the choice between the two forms are one `@hercule/client-core` function with its own tests.
- **The build-failed line.** When the plugin could not draw the signal, a quiet line reads "GitHub couldn't draw this signal. Showing the event as it came in." ([Actions, Done and Hand to an agent](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#94-actions-done-and-hand-to-an-agent)).
- **A Screening line,** when the signal was raised without one: "Not screened: the Screener failed" or "Not screened: no workflow screens signals" ([Screening and the Screener](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#98-screening-and-the-screener)).
- **The answers,** at the foot of the pane, in this order: the plugin's actions, then Hand to an agent (one per workflow that takes the signal's kind), then Done. At most one is primary, drawn as the suggested answer. Each answer shows its describe line on hover and on focus. *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#634](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/634).)* A plugin chooses its answers' labels, so a label wider than the 156px button is cut with an ellipsis inside the button's padding. It is never wrapped and never widens the button: the describe line is where an answer says what it does, and the button's accessible name keeps the whole label. What each does is [Actions, Done and Hand to an agent](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#94-actions-done-and-hand-to-an-agent)'s.
  - **The Reply box.** An action with a `field`, such as Reply, Request changes or Comment, opens a text box with its placeholder. The box shows the typed text in full, and the describe line leaves it out. `⌘↩` or the button calls `signal.act { signalId, actionId, text }`.
  - Every other answer calls `signal.act { signalId, actionId }`, except Done, which calls `signal.markDone`.
  - **A proposal** offers Accept, the primary answer, which creates the Task, and Dismiss. The pane shows the Task Accept will create.
  - **Hercule's offer** offers Ignore, the primary answer, and Dismiss. Its pane counts the matching open signals when it opens: "3 open now". *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* It counts them in To do, which the shell holds, and in Later of the rule's kind, read once when the pane opens; with none it reads "None open now". A signal snoozed while the pane stays open is counted only at the next opening.
  - **A failed answer** shows its error under its button, in `--fail`, as a failed save does in Settings. The typed text stays, so the user can try again. It never goes in a toast.
  - In the Done view, the outcome shows in place of the answers. ~~A "Handed to *workflow*" outcome shows the run's state, such as running or failed, read-only; it never puts the signal back on To do.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* The outcome section of a handed-off signal shows its trip (see Views, Done), read-only. Its bold line is the trip itself: the first step bold, the other steps regular, and the end toned as on the row. The moment line comes under it, then one button in the outcome's text column. The pane looks a leg up only on Done: a signal resolved elsewhere while it shows on To do or Later keeps its plain outcome. When the trip's button has the focus and the trip goes (the Task was deleted, so the leg leaves the read), the focus moves to the pane, so the screen's keys keep working. The button is "Go to Tracking" while the Task's row in Tracking agrees with the leg, which opens Tracking with that Task selected ([The pane of a row](#tracking)); else "Open the run", which opens the leg's newest run in Workflows. Neither puts the signal back on To do. A signal with no trip shows no button, and its outcome as before. When the pane's own read of the leg fails (the leg is on no loaded page), the outcome says so: "Couldn't read where this handoff stands." and the controller's reason, with a Try again button that reads the leg again; a refusal from the controller (403, 404) is not asked again by itself. The pane draws no step-by-step timeline of the handoff (see the BRIEF, Done).
  - A teammate's review on a team review request shows as context in the pane. It does not end the signal.
- **Resolved elsewhere.** When the open signal is resolved while it is open, from GitHub, the CLI or another client, the pane stays, and the outcome shows in place of the answers: "Resolved elsewhere: answered on GitHub by Ana". A reply being typed is kept. The pane changes only when the user moves on.
- **The pane's menu** holds "Open on GitHub" (the source's name), and "Ignore signals like this…" for a plugin kind. It is hidden for core kinds, and never on a row. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* "Ignore signals like this…" shows only when the kind declares a match field the signal holds. Its items share the Snooze menu's style: height, padding, type and hover. ↓ and ↑ move between its items and wrap at the ends; opened from the keyboard, its first item is focused and drawn active.
  - **The Ignore dialog** lists the kind's match fields, each a checkbox with its label and the signal's value, all checked. At least one must stay checked. It says how many open signals the rule will also take off the list: "This also takes 3 open signals off your list". It links the kind's row in Settings › Intake: "To stop all review requests, switch the kind off". Create calls `ignoreRule.create` ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#ignorerule)); what a rule does is [Ignore Rules](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#99-ignore-rules)'s. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* The built dialog reads, top to bottom:
    - the first line, "From now on, Hercule ignores review requested signals that have every checked value.";
    - one checkbox per match field the kind declares and the signal holds, in the order the kind declares them (a value longer than a rule can hold, which only a signal stored before that limit can have, gets no checkbox, and a signal with no other value offers no Ignore), with the label and, under it, the value in `--ink`. The last checked box cannot be unchecked; it stays focusable and its tooltip reads "A rule needs at least one value, so this one stays checked.";
    - the count, "This also takes 3 open signals off your list.", or "No open signal matches it now." with none, read as the offer's pane reads it;
    - "To stop every signal of this kind, switch the kind off.", where only "switch the kind off" is the link.
- **The pane's foot** lists the keys.

### The Triage tab

The Triage tab shows the signals the named triage workflow raised: the signals whose `origin.workflowId` is the workflow the user setting `intake.triageWorkflowId` names, set at the first run to the shipped Triage. The app picks them out of the To do list it already holds, so the tab needs no read of its own. What triage does is [./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md) §1 and §2's.

- **The header:** "Last ran 11:00 · 212 events · next 13:00". The count is Intake's events inside the last run's window ([Intake's events and handlings](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#910-intakes-events-and-handlings)); "next" is the workflow's trigger's `nextFireAt`. After a failed run it reads "Last run failed 11:00"; the failure itself goes to Check-in.
- **The summary:** the run's final message, under the header, in a quiet tone, on the Triage tab only.
- **The header's menu** has "Use another workflow…", a picker of workflows. When the named workflow is deleted or disabled, the tab shows "No triage workflow. Pick one", with the same picker, and the header hides.
- There is no Run now in v1.

### Everything

Everything lists events, not signals: Intake's events, newest first, back to the retention horizon. Which events those are, and what came of each, is [Intake's events and handlings](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#910-intakes-events-and-handlings)'s. The app reads them with `event.query` and its `intake` flag ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#event)).

- **The header** states the horizon: "Events from the last 90 days". *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* The number is the `retention.events` setting, and reads 90 while the setting is not set. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* The list asks for the horizon itself, with `event.query`'s `since` set to that many days before the moment Everything opened, because the controller removes older events only at its next sweep. The moment is taken once per opening, so the day headings, the row times and the horizon do not move while the list stays.
- **A search field** searches titles and authors. Typing waits about 200ms before it queries. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* Only typing starts that wait. A `text` that arrives from outside, such as a link, replaces what the field holds and cancels a wait that is running, so the field never writes an older text back.
- **The filter menu** has no title and no counts. Its options are captions: "All events", "Raised a signal", "Pending triage" and the others. Its accessible label is "What came of it". The word "handling" is never shown. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* It is a native select, as the drawing's `field--select`. Its options are "All events", "Raised a signal", "Ended a signal", "Evidence for a signal", "Added to a Task", "Known to running work" and "No action": one per handling the contract names. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* The contract names `ignored` too, so the menu gains "Ignored by a rule" after "Ended a signal", in the contract's order. "Pending triage" is not offered, because the contract has no such handling.
- **A row** shows the system's mark, the title, the author, the time, and what came of the event: the first 2 handlings, in spec 10's order, then "+N" for the rest, such as "→ signal · on your To do list · +2". A fixed number needs no measuring on resize, and a test can check it. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* The row's second line starts with the author, when there is one: "Marta · → signal · on your To do list · +1". The drawing has no author on the row; the spec asks for it. The time is the clock time, as on the To do list's rows, and the day heading above the rows gives the date. A title that is missing falls back to the event's kind, such as `slack.message`, drawn in the mono face. An event nothing came of reads "no action". Day headings carry no counts, where the drawing draws them.
  - "→ ignored · rule *text*" links the rule's row in Settings › Intake, or reads "deleted" when the rule is gone. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* ~~Not built: the contract's handlings do not include an ignore rule, so the row cannot show one.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* Built: the row reads the rule's text from the event's `ignoredBy`, and "deleted" from its `ruleDeleted`. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* The row reads "→ ignored by a rule", or "→ ignored by a deleted rule" when the rule is gone, and leaves the rule's text to the pane, because a rule's text is too long for a row. The pane's block reads "Ignored by a rule", the rule's text in bold, and "In Settings › Intake" as the link to the kind's row, or "Rule deleted" with no link.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* A signal that was snoozed and whose time has passed reads "on your To do list". Work that knows the event reads "known to" and its Thread or run. A Thread links to its screen. A Task or a run is plain text, because the pane has no screen to open for it.
- **No counts and no row controls** on Everything. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* The tabs above the list keep their counts of the To do list on every view: they count signals, and Everything lists events.
- **The event pane** holds, top to bottom: the system's mark, the event kind and "Open in *system*"; the title and the time it was received; **What came of it**, one block per handling, each with its link; then the Connection and the event's refs. No body and no payload. The kind links its row in Settings › Intake, with the event's Connection selected. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* "Open in *system*" shows only when the event has an address. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* It reads "Open on *system*", as the signal pane's does. An event the list does not hold, such as one an address names that lies past the pages read, is read alone with `event.read`, which carries no facts. Its pane leaves "What came of it" out rather than claim that nothing came of it. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* The header line reads the system's mark and name, a dot, and the kind in the mono face, muted, as the link to Settings › Intake. Each block of "What came of it" is the handling's caption in small type, the record it is about in bold, and where the record stands under it, such as "on your To do list" or "Thread"; "No action" has no record and draws its caption in bold. A block links its record where the app has a screen. The Connection reads "*System* · *label*", as Settings' Connections list does, and the refs are in the mono face. A Thread known to the event, whose title the viewer may not read, reads "a Thread".
- **It does not update live.** It reads again when it opens and when the search or the filter changes. It is virtualized (rule 1) and read page by page. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* It also reads again when the source tab changes, and starts from its first page each time the view opens: a listing already held shows its first page at once and is read again behind it, and the pages scrolled to on the last visit are dropped, so a visit never reads more pages than the user scrolls through. The rows are 54px, 8 more are drawn beyond the screen on each side, and the next page is read when the rows drawn come within 8 lines of the list's end, day headings counted as lines. After the last page the list ends with ~~"No older events."~~ "No more events from the last 90 days.", which names the horizon the header states; with nothing to list it reads "No events yet." when no search or filter is set, and "No events match." when one is. A first read that fails shows its error in the list's place, with a Retry, and keeps the bar, the search and the filter. A page that fails ends the list with its error and a Retry; scrolling does not ask for that page again, only Retry does. The empty, no-match, end and error lines share one style: small, faint text at the list's left padding.
- *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* **The address.** Everything is `/intake?view=everything`, with `event` for the open event's id, `text` for the search, `handling` for the filter, which holds one handling, and `source` for the pressed source tab. `text` holds what the search's wait has committed, not each keystroke. A change of search, filter, tab or selection replaces the history entry, and a change of view adds one. A new search, filter or tab drops `event`, because the selected event may not be in the new list. J and ↓ select the next event, K and ↑ the one before.

### The split and its minimums

Intake uses [the split](../design-language.md#the-split): the list is the main column and has the width, and the pane takes the rest.

- The list's width starts at the drawing's 432px. Its minimum is 360pt and the pane's is 400pt. Below a window of about 1032pt, with the sidebar showing at its 272px, the pane hides and its toggle says why: "Widen the window to show the pane". *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* The split measures its own width, so a wider sidebar raises that window width and a hidden one lowers it ([The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout)).
- ~~With the pane closed, rows are at most 960px wide.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* With the pane closed, rows run the list's full width. A cap left empty space beside rows that sat on the left, which read as a broken layout, and its reason, keeping a row to one line, no longer holds: a row is two lines.
- A click on a row selects it and opens the pane. The pane closes when nothing is selected.
- **Esc:** the first Esc closes the pane and keeps the row selected; a second Esc clears the selection. Anything smaller, such as an open menu, is let go first.
- Opening and closing animate the list's width with the pane's slide, ~~the one width animation rule 2 allows~~ a layout transition rule 2 allows once measured *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* ([Rules](#rules)), because ~~every row is one line~~ no row's text wraps *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)*. A row that wraps would end the exception.

### Keys

Keys work only while focus is in the list or the pane, never while it is in a text field.

| Key | Does |
|---|---|
| `J` / `K`, `↓` / `↑` | moves the selection down or up. *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* While Tracking is open, it walks only Tracking's rows |
| `X`, `⇧J` / `⇧K`, `⇧↓` / `⇧↑` | checks the selected row; the shifted keys check while moving |
| `↩` | on a row: opens the pane and puts focus on the suggested answer, whose describe line is then in view. A second `↩` presses it. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* On a Tracking row it selects the row, shows its pane and puts the focus on the suggested answer, or on the pane when no answer is suggested, so a second `↩` presses nothing. It never presses Stop the run, which is never suggested. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* On a Failed row the suggested answer is Try again, so `↩` focuses it and a second `↩` starts the run again. `E` stays Mark done on every row. *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* On a handed-off signal in Done it opens the pane and puts the focus on the trip's button, and a second `↩` presses it. The foot of the pane names it "go to Tracking" when the button is Go to Tracking |
| `E` | Done, on the checked rows or else the selected one. *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* ~~On a Tracking row it marks a Finished, Failed or Stopped row done, and does nothing on a Working one~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* On a Tracking row it marks the row done when no run of its Task is live and it waits on no Request ([Tracking](#tracking), Take-off), and does nothing on any other. It acts on the row that has the focus, else the selected one |
| `T` | *(Added 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* opens and closes Tracking, on the To do view |
| `H`, then `1` to `5` | Snooze, with the menu's choice |
| `U` | Unsnooze, in Later |
| `R` | opens the Reply box; nothing when the signal has no action with a field |
| `⌘↩` | sends the Reply box |
| `O` | opens the signal on its source, in the default browser. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* While Tracking is open it opens the selected row's newest run, in Workflows, and does nothing with no row selected. *(Amended 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* On a handed-off signal in Done whose trip button is Open the run, it opens that run, in Workflows, in place of the source; the foot of the pane names it "open the run" |
| Esc | steps back, as above. *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* While Tracking is open it steps back in order: the pane, then the selection, then Tracking. ~~Until the pane lands (#803), the selection, then Tracking~~ *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* The first Esc hides the pane and keeps the row selected, the second clears the selection, and the third closes Tracking |

`↩` never answers from the list in one press. The drawing's `↩` took the suggested answer at once, so `J`, `J`, `↩` could approve a pull request the user never read. The Requests dock already answers from the keyboard only while it has focus.

### Motion

Every animation here is `transform` or `opacity` on an HTML element, at most `--dur-3` per step, and stops with Reduce motion. Rule 2's amendments allow it ([Rules](#rules)). *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* The arrival and the row motion follow macOS's Reduce motion only, through `prefers-reduced-motion`. The Appearance page's Reduce motion does not stop them yet: no part of the app reads that setting into CSS, though [Appearance](#appearance) says `theme-init.js` should; [#412](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/412) wires it.

**Inbox zero's arrival** plays only when the user's own action empties To do. Opening Intake on an empty list, or a change made elsewhere that empties it, shows the same picture still. With Reduce motion, it shows still.

1. The last row fades, over `--dur-1`, and the pane slides shut.
2. The sunburst draws itself: 24 brass rays, each its own HTML `<span>`, long and short in turn, grow outward from their inner end, `scaleY` from 0 to 1 over 320ms, each 12ms after the one before, clockwise from the top. The drawing animated SVG paths, which rule 2 forbids. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* The drawing (`asks.js`, `buildSunburst`) has 32 rays. This spec's 24 stands, and the app draws 24.
3. At 120ms, Hercule hops in: the face scales from 0.6 to 1 on `--ease-spring` while its wrapper hops 8px, in the pose `done`.
4. At 260ms, six brass sparks fly out from behind Hercule and fade, once.
5. From 240ms, "Inbox zero", the line, the cleared count, the source pills and the triage line rise 6px each, 60ms apart.

The arrival takes 800ms, and then nothing runs. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* The 800ms start with the last row's fade, which takes the first 120ms (`--dur-1`); the sunburst starts after it, so the times in steps 2 to 5 count from the end of the fade. When the pane shows as the arrival starts, it closes once the last row has left, because the selection goes with that row, and the list widens over `--dur-3`. The whole arrival then waits `--dur-3` first, with nothing drawn, so it plays in the settled column instead of sliding sideways, and takes 1,120ms in all. Whether it waits is decided when the arrival starts, so the pane closing during it changes nothing. **The line** follows the moment, in the user's time zone setting:

- "The rest of the morning is yours." before 12:00;
- "The rest of the afternoon is yours." from 12:00;
- "Go home." from 18:00;
- "Have a good weekend." on Friday from 12:00;
- "Nothing needs you this weekend." all day on Saturday and Sunday.

With a triage workflow named, the triage line under it reads "triage runs at 13:00", from its trigger's `nextFireAt`. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* The app leaves the triage line out until [#533](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/533) says how the triage workflow is named. Until then inbox zero ends with the cleared count and the source pills.

**Changes the user did not make:**

- A new signal fades in and drops into place over `--dur-2`. The rows below make room with a transform, never by animating height.
- A signal resolved elsewhere leaves as the user's own Done does: it fades, then the gap closes.
- A burst animates as one block: a triage run that raises 30 signals slides in once, not as 30 rows one after another. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* A burst is one motion: every row on screen starts together, with one animation per row.
- **Nothing moves under the pointer.** While the pointer is over the list, arrivals and departures wait, and apply with their motion once it leaves. Otherwise the user aims at Approve on #1294, a Now row arrives above it, and they approve the wrong signal. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* Only the list waits. The counts on the source tabs and in the sidebar follow each read at once, because they sit outside the list and nothing under the pointer moves when they change. The user's own changes never wait. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* The hold covers every view's list, To do, Later and Done. It also holds while a snooze menu opened from the list, from a row or from the bulk bar, is open. While it holds, every change the user did not make waits, including rows added after the last one shown and an edit to a row on screen, such as a lowered priority that moves the row to another section. Reading the first page, or a new page of Later or Done, is the user's own scrolling: the list shows the read at once, with no motion and nothing held. The user's own change applies at once too. When it takes rows off the list, only those rows leave. When a row they changed stays in the list, such as a signal snoozed again to another day in Later, the list shows the read whole, the waiting changes included: the user's own action moves rows anyway, and the days stay in order.
- The keyboard selection stays on the same signal, never on the same position.
- Only rows on screen animate. A change to a row out of view applies at once.

### The foot, the toast and failures

- **The foot** sits under the list: "7 cleared today. 3 left on their own: answered elsewhere or withdrawn. See Done." With none left on their own: "7 cleared today. See Done." With none cleared, there is no foot. "Today" starts at midnight in the user's time zone setting. A replaced signal is not counted.
- **The toast** says what the user's last move did: "Approved #1294", "Snoozed until Monday 09:00", "Marked 3 done. Kept 2 proposals". It fades after 5 seconds, stays while the pointer is on it, and the next toast replaces it. It has no Undo.
- A failed answer shows under its button, never in a toast ([The pane](#the-pane)). A failed Done, Snooze or Not urgent shows its error in the toast's place, in `--fail`, and the rows stay where they were.

### What the screen reads

- `signal.query`'s To do view, read whole, because it is short by nature; Later and Done page by page.
- `event.query` with `intake` for Everything, page by page.
- The triage workflow's last run, and its trigger's `nextFireAt`.
- `plugin.query`, for each plugin's name and mark.
- *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* `workflow.query` and `assistant.query`, for the name of the workflow or the assistant that raised a core signal ([./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md) Section 9.1). The shell already reads the assistants. The workflows are read each time Intake opens, not kept current by the `workflow` topic, because the controller pushes on that topic at every cron fire, which would cost requests while the app is idle. A workflow renamed while Intake is open shows its new name the next time Intake opens.
- *(Added 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* Tracking's reads, which are shell data ([The count and attention](#the-count-and-attention)): one read of `task.queryTracking`, under one stable key, which returns every tracked Task with its title, its handoffs, its newest run, its newest live run *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* and the ids of all its live runs. The read holds the Tasks as the controller returns them; the rows are built from it, from the sessions that wait and from Your work, so a Request that opens or closes needs no read. The shell's loader loads it through `ensureShellData`, with the shell's read options that keep it for the life of the window, and each component reads it where it draws it. The Hercule segment's count and the Tracking rows read the same cache, so they cannot disagree.
- *(Added 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* Your work, which is shell data too, because the To do count shows on every screen and a Needs you row counts in it: one read, `yourWorkQuery`, under one stable key, which calls `signal.queryYourWork`. The controller works out the open Tasks that belong to an open signal, on To do or snoozed, each with the ids of those signals, and the app intersects them with the To do list it holds, so the app sends one request whatever the number of signals and keeps no copy of the Tasks beyond the reply. It is loaded by `ensureShellData` with the shell's read options, and it does not wait for the read of To do. A push on the `task` or `signal` topic reads it again. It has its own key, outside the `signals` prefix, so a `workflow`, `connection` or `plugin` push does not read it (see [Tracking](#tracking), Your work and Live updates).
- *(Added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* The Tracking pane's reads, which only the open pane makes: `task.readTracking` for the selected row, under `queryKeys.trackingDetail(taskId)`, and `signal.read` for the Task's first handoff's signal, under the key the signal's own pane uses ([The pane of a row](#tracking)). Neither is shell data, and both are kept current by the live topics ([Tracking](#tracking), Live updates).
- *(Added 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* The `task` and `run` live topics, subscribed by the shell: a push re-reads Tracking's read as [Tracking](#tracking) says. `task` stays subscribed while the window is hidden, as `signal` does. `run` is held like the Workflows pages hold their topics: while the window is visible, dropped while it is hidden, and read again when it shows ([Rules](#rules), rule 3).
- *(Added 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* The legs of the handoffs on the Done pages, read with each page: one `task.queryLegs` per page, for the distinct `resolution.runId`s of its signals, skipped when it has none. They are not shell data. Each page holds its legs beside its signals, and the list and the pane look a signal's leg up by the run its resolution started. A pane whose leg is on no loaded page reads it alone, with one `task.queryLegs` call for that run, under a key of its own that a `signal` push does not read; the `task` and `run` pushes keep it current by the same rules. See [Tracking](#tracking), Live updates, for when they are read again.
- The `signal` live topic. A push names the signals that changed, and the app reads To do again. The topic stays subscribed while the window is hidden, as `session` does, because the Hercule segment's count and Now's notifications depend on it ([Rules](#rules), rule 3). The shell subscribes to it, not the screen.
- The To do list is virtualized like every list (rule 1), though it is read whole.
- ~~The first page of Later, soonest first, gives the soonest snooze's `until` for the snooze timer ([Rules](#rules), rule 4). The shell reads it with the count, so the timer runs while Intake is closed.~~ Each read of To do first reads Later with a limit of one, soonest first, for the soonest snooze's `until`, which sets the snooze timer ([Rules](#rules), rule 4). In that order, a snooze that runs out between the two reads is missing from To do but still sets the timer, which reads To do again. The shell keeps that `until` for the life of the window, so the timer runs while Intake is closed. Intake's paged Later is a read of its own. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#525](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/525).)*

**Interpretation goes to `@hercule/client-core`,** each function with its own tests: the sections and their order, which controls a row gets, the tab set and what moves into More, which checked rows a bulk action takes with the bar's and the toast's wording, the foot's two numbers, the triage header, the snooze choices, the handlings' cut-off, and the inbox zero line, *(added 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801))* each Tracking row's state, Tracking's order, and the waiting rows the count takes in, *(added 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802))* what makes a row Needs you and which Request or signal it shows, and which Task is Your work for which signal, *(added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803))* whether a row can be marked done, which answers the pane offers, the words of its standing line, its stamp, its runs and its output's values. How that splits into functions, and their names, is the implementer's choice. One possible split: `groupSignalsIntoSections`, `decideSignalRowControls`, `buildIntakeTabs`, `planBulkAction`, `countClearedToday`, `describeTriageHeader`, `buildSnoozeChoices`, `cutHandlings`, `describeInboxZero`.

### What Intake costs

The plan for slices 25 to 35, each measuring its part and recording it in [Measured](#measured). No prototype measurement comes first: Intake is lists and text, the work the thread list already measured.

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| Idle | no work, except one timer, set for the soonest snooze's `until`. It fires once; the app then reads To do again and sets the next. ~~The app also reads To do again when the Mac wakes.~~ When the Mac wakes, the live connection reconnects and its first push reads every signal list again; the window being shown or focused after the `until` ran out reads the `until` again. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#525](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/525).)* The `signal` topic sends nothing while nothing changes. The toast's 5-second fade is a one-shot timer after a user action, not idle work | the perf script's idle sample, with Intake open and a snooze due in an hour |
| Lists | To do read whole; Later and Done page by page; Everything virtualized and page by page, with no live updates | the perf script, with 200 signals on To do, and with 1,000 events on Everything (`--everything-only`) |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with Intake open and 200 signals on To do. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529).)* The same with Everything scrolled through 1,000 events: the list draws a few dozen rows, however many it holds | the perf script |
| Per push | one compositor-only animation, only for rows on screen; ~~a burst is one animation~~ a burst is one motion: every row on screen starts together, one animation per row. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* | ~~the Performance panel, once, with a burst of 30~~ the perf script, with one arrival and a burst of 30 landing on screen. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* |
| Inbox zero | 800ms of compositor-only animation per arrival, nothing after. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* When the pane was open, a wait of `--dur-3` with nothing drawn comes first | ~~the Performance panel, once~~ the perf script, after the user's own Done of the last signal. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* |
| Opening or closing the split | layout on each frame of the transition, no dropped frame with 50 rows in the list, nothing after | the Performance panel, once |
| Images | loaded only for the open signal | the Network panel, once |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the Go item, the `/intake` route stub, the `signal` subscription and the count. *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* The count also takes in Tracking's reads, the `task` and `run` subscriptions and the state function, because the count shows on every screen. *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* It takes in Your work's read and the matching of Tasks to signals too, because a Needs you row counts. Intake is a chunk of its own, loaded the first time it opens; Everything's screen is a chunk of its own inside it, loaded the first time the view opens | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| Tracking: reads | one read when the shell loads, `task.queryTracking`, under one stable key: the Tasks, their newest runs and the ids of their live runs in one request, on an index (`runs_task_id`), never a read per Task or per run, and no `run.read` for a Failed row's reason, which the read carries. It is shell data, because the To do count shows on every screen | the perf script (`perf.ts --tracking-only`): the reads of the shell's start, of each push and of 10 handoffs, counted from the wire by a CDP collector, with request timings from the protocol; and the controller's benchmark of the real statements, `pnpm test:speed` (`apps/controller/src/tasks/tracking.speed.test.ts`) |
| Tracking: Your work | *(Added 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* One shell read, `yourWorkQuery`, under one stable key: `signal.queryYourWork`, the server's own read of Your work. At load it is 1 request, plus at most 1 each for the first message of the `signal` topic and of the `task` topic. After that it is 1 request for each `signal` push and 1 for each `task` push, whatever the number of signals, and none for a `run` push, the window being hidden and shown, or a Request opening or closing. The reply holds each matching Task once, with its id, title, status, update time and signal ids, and no description or provenance, so its size follows the number of Tasks that match, not the Tasks there are. The controller answers it with two indexed statements (`signals_open_kind` and `task_provenance_ref`) and took 12 to 17 ms in `your-work.speed.test.ts` (200 open signals, 1,000 open Tasks with 50 provenance entries each). Each component that draws Your work or Tracking's rows builds them with `useMemo` over narrow selects. It is shell data, because a Needs you row counts in the To do count on every screen | `apps/controller/src/signals/your-work.speed.test.ts` for the controller's read time; the perf script (`perf.ts --tracking-only`) for the requests, counted from the wire by the CDP collector. The perf script has not measured this design, see [Measured](#measured), Tracking, slice 2 |
| Tracking: live topics | the `task` topic, subscribed wherever the count shows, so also while the window is hidden, and the `run` topic, held by the shell while the window is visible and dropped while it is hidden (it is the only `run` subscription, so a push arrives once). A `task` push, and a `run` push that names the newest run or any live run of a row, a run's creation or no run, read Tracking again, once per push, not once per Task. Other `run` pushes read nothing | the perf script: pushes of Tasks and of runs that are not about a Task, with nothing tracked and with 10 tracked, and the window hidden and shown again, counted from the wire |
| Tracking: idle | no work added beyond a push. The times on the rows read the shared age clock once a minute | the perf script's idle samples of two launches, one with 10 Tasks tracked and one with nothing, with nothing attached. The age clock's fires are counted by Intake's own idle launch, because the rows show hours and add no timer |
| Tracking: opening and closing | one animation for the sheet, plus at most 13 for the cascade, done within 450ms; closing is one animation | the perf script's motion rows, from a Chromium trace |
| Tracking: a handoff | one animation for the lifted copy, plus one transform per row that moves on screen | the perf script's motion rows |
| Tracking: a state change or a take-off | one opacity animation, plus one transform per row that moves; a take-off is as Intake's own Done | the perf script's motion rows |
| Tracking: a Working row's line | not built in slice 1, so it costs nothing. The line would show the agent's latest message, and no field, read or push carries that message yet. It needs a new field on the session, kept by the controller, and is left to a follow-up ticket, which states its cost before it is built | not applicable |
| Tracking: the pane | *(Added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* One `task.readTracking` and one `signal.read` when a row's pane opens, which the controller answers with a bounded reply: the newest 20 runs, one output of at most 12 fields or 1,000 characters, one action step name. It is read again on a `task` push that names the Task, and *(amended 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803))* ~~on a `run` push that already reads Tracking again~~ on a `run` push that names the newest run, newest live run or a live run of this Task, which a push with no ids or the creation of a run also does. A `run` push about another Task's run reads no pane's detail. Nothing per row and nothing while no pane is open: the route's loader reads nothing for the selected row, the pane reads its detail when it mounts, so `J` and `K` with the pane hidden send no request. No new topic, timer or motion beyond the split's own slide; the Working row's standing line follows the shared age clock, which adds no timer. The pane joins Intake's chunk | request counts in the integration tests (`intake.tracking.integration.test.tsx`), the controller's speed test (`tracking.speed.test.ts`), and `pnpm build:desktop`'s size check, before and after |
| Tracking: Try again | *(Amended 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* Per press: one `run.rerun`. Then one `task.queryTracking`, started when the request succeeds, as a backstop so the lock always ends even if the `run` push is lost. Then the reads the new run's `run` push already starts: Tracking's read, which joins the backstop when one is under way, and the open pane's `task.readTracking` (Tracking, Live updates). No new live topic, timer or polling. While the re-run is starting, a second press sends nothing. The answer adds no process, no work while idle and no work for each streamed token; all its code, `listTrackingAnswers` included, joins Intake's chunk only; the first screen's chunk does not change | request counts in the integration tests (`intake.tracking.integration.test.tsx`: one `run.rerun` for a press, none for a second press or for `E` while starting), and `pnpm build:desktop`'s size check, before and after |
| Tracking: bundle bytes | the Tracking screen joins Intake's chunk; the reads and the state function join the first screen's JavaScript (above) | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |

*(Added 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* The rows beginning "Tracking" are the planned costs of [Tracking](#tracking), from the BRIEF's cost list. Their "Measured by" column says how each is measured, and the numbers are recorded in [Measured](#measured) ([#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801); *(added 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802))* Tracking, slice 2, for Your work), as the slice that measures them does ([ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md)).

## Workflows

*(Added 2026-10-11, [Desktop Workflows: spec 17 §Workflows and the book's pages (#548)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548); designed in [#542](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/542).)* The user's workflows, how each is doing, what one does, and one run of it. Two pages: the workflows page, with the list and the open workflow, and a run's page. The run record and its words are [./07-workflows.md](./07-workflows.md) section 7; the web app's pages are [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Runs and §Workflow editing.

- **The pixel reference** is the book's [`desktop/workflows.html`](../design/crew-bureau-2/desktop/workflows.html) and [`desktop/run.html`](../design/crew-bureau-2/desktop/run.html), drawn from the prototype on [`prototype/desktop-workflows`](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/tree/prototype/desktop-workflows) (3a4baf01). Where the prototype and this section disagree, this section decides.
- **State marks, not faces.** A state mark leads every row, where a thread's row draws a face, in the face's 24px slot, so every row's text starts at the same x. An agent step shows AgentIcon and its Agent's name. No face is drawn on these pages; a run that wears its workflow's face stays post-v1 ([Post-v1](#post-v1)).
- **Read-only, with five writes.** The pages write only the workflow's switch, a start trigger's pause switch, Cancel run, Re-run and Delete workspace. Creating and editing a workflow, and Run now, are post-v1.
- **The ways in:** the Hercule face's Workflows row, with no count ([The Hercule face](#the-hercule-face)), and Go › Workflows `⌘⇧W` ([Native behaviour](#native-behaviour)). A run's page is reached from the Runs tab, and a run's session from "From runs".

### The routes

| Route | Shows | Search parameters |
|---|---|---|
| `/workflows` | the list as a table | none |
| `/workflows/$workflowId` | the list as a column, and the open workflow | `full`: `true`, the list hidden. `runsView`: `timeline`, or absent for the list. `daysBack`: a whole number above 0, the day the timeline shows counted back from today, or absent for today |
| `/runs/$runId` | a run's page | `view`: `timeline`, or absent for the graph |

- A search parameter with any other value is dropped, and the page shows its default.
- Changing the Runs tab's view, or stepping the day, replaces the history entry, so Back leaves the workflow instead of stepping through days.
- Switching the view goes back to today.
- The filter and the search are not in the URL. They are the list's state, kept while the user opens and closes workflows.
- The open workflow's tab is not in the URL either. It opens on Runs for each workflow.
- **A workflow that is gone** shows "This workflow was not found." with a link to Workflows, as a thread that is gone does.
- **A run that is gone** shows "This run was not found." with a link to Workflows.
- A run whose workflow was deleted still shows everything, because its page reads the run's plan and step records.

### The list

**One list, three widths.** The list's width follows the pane, not the window:

- **Table:** nothing is open. The list fills the pane, one 40px row per workflow.
- **Column:** a workflow is open. The list is a 340px column beside it, with a `--line-soft` hairline between them. A row is 46px: the mark, then the name over the status, and the time at the far end.
- **Full:** `full` is set. The list is hidden, and the header's button brings it back.
- **Below 800px, the open workflow always fills the pane,** whatever `full` says, and the header's list button is hidden.
- "Hide the list" snaps. Rule 2 lets a split's side pane animate its width only while its rows are one line ([Rules](#rules)), and the column's rows are two.

**The table's columns,** left to right:

| Column | Width | Shows |
|---|---|---|
| (no head) | 14px | the workflow's state mark (below) |
| Workflow | 1.4fr | its name |
| Starts on | 1fr | what starts it: each start trigger's schedule in words ("Daily at 01:00", "Fridays at 14:00", with " (Europe/Amsterdam)" when a schedule has its own time zone, or its cron expression in `--mono`, as code, when it has no words) or its event's kind; "On demand" with no start trigger |
| Last 20 runs | 219px | the strip: the last 20 runs' marks at 10px, 1px apart, oldest first and right-aligned, so every workflow's newest run sits in the same place |
| Success | 44px | completed runs out of completed and failed, among those 20, as a percentage ("85%"); empty when there are none |
| Last run | 1.4fr | the status text (below) |
| (no head) | 44px | when its newest run started: "10:31" today, "Sun" within 6 days, then "1 Oct", with the year when it is not this year |
| Next | 72px | when it next starts: "Off" when the workflow is off; "On demand" with no start trigger; "Paused" when every start trigger is paused; else the soonest fire, "14:00" today, "Fri 14:00" within 6 days, or a date; else "On event" |

**The status text** of the last run:

- Needs you: "review asks: Ship v2.4?", the step and its question, or "Waiting on you".
- Running: "At review", or "Waiting for approved" while it waits on a signal, from the run's `activeStepIds` and `awaitedSignalIds`. With two runs live: "2 runs · at fix, waiting for pr_merged". A run not yet started: "Starting".
- A start trigger in error: "on_issue: <the error's message>".
- Ended: "Failed at review", "Failed", "Cancelled", "Done", or "No runs yet".

**A narrow pane drops columns,** as Settings › Permission profiles drops Used by:

- below 1000px, Starts on;
- below 820px, the strip and Success;
- below 780px, the filters' counts;
- below 640px, the page's name in the header (it stays for the screen reader) and the search field's `⌘F` hint.

**The groups.** A workflow is in the first group whose rule it meets, in this order:

1. **Needs you:** one of its runs waits on the user: a session of the run has an open Request.
2. **Failing:** its newest run failed, or one of its start triggers is in error.
3. **Running:** one of its runs is live.
4. **Everything else.**

The newest run decides between Failing and Running. Examples: Ship release's run from Friday failed and today's run is live, so Ship release is in Running. An older run is still live and the newest run failed, so it is in Failing. A start trigger in error puts a workflow in Failing whatever its newest run is.

- Running is read from the live runs (`run.query` with `status: "running"`, and again with `status: "pending"`, because the filter takes one status), not from the strip, so a live run older than the workflow's 20 newest still counts, and so does a run not yet started.
- Needs you is read from the waiting sessions, through each session's `runId` to its run's `workflowId`.
- A group's heading is a 24px row across the table, with its count right after its name, in marigold for Needs you. An empty group has no heading.
- Within a group, rows are sorted by name.
- **The state mark** leading a row is its group's: waiting, failed or running. In Everything else it is `idle`, or `paused` for a workflow that is off.
- **A workflow that is off** is drawn in muted ink. It is off when its switch is off, or when it has start triggers and every one of them is paused.

**The filters and search,** in the floating header:

- The page's name, "Workflows", leads the header as its title pill.
- **The filters:** All, Needs you, Failing, Running and Off, each with its count, as `aria-pressed` buttons in a `nav` named "Filter workflows". Off keeps the workflows that are off, from every group. A count is taken before the search, so it does not change as the user types.
- **Search,** in its own pill at the header's far end: "Search names, triggers". The filters never shrink; the search gives up its width first. A workflow matches when every word typed is found, ignoring case, in its name, its description, its Starts on text or one of its trigger ids.
- **Over the column,** the page's name is a back tab that leads to the table, with the count of All. With another filter on, that filter shows beside it as a pill with its count. The column's search field sits at the top of the column.
- Empty: "No workflows yet". Nothing matches: "No workflows match".
- **A row opens its workflow**: it is a link to `/workflows/$workflowId`. The open workflow's row is selected, as a sidebar row is.

### The open workflow

Top to bottom: the lead, the graph, and the tabs. A sticky band as tall as the floating header sits at the top of the scrolling pane, so the header's pills never float over half a line. The tab bar sticks under it, with a hairline.

**The lead:**

- **The name, with the workflow's switch** at the lead's right end, the book's `.toggle`, labelled "Enabled".
- **The description**, when it has one.
- **The line under the switch says what the switch does,** in muted ink: "When on, its triggers start runs. Runs already going are not touched." when on, and "Its triggers start no runs while it is off. Runs already going are not touched." when off. Turning a workflow off stops its triggers and nothing else ([./07-workflows.md](./07-workflows.md) section 1): runs in flight carry on, and it can still be run from the web app, the CLI or the API.
- **The chips:** what starts it and what its steps run, in a wrapping row, in a list named "About this workflow".
  - One chip per start trigger: its schedule in words or its event's kind, then " · next Fri 14:00" while the workflow is on, " · paused", or " · error". A trigger in error is `.chip--fail`.
  - "On demand" when it has no start trigger.
  - Then what its steps run: "5 agents · 2 actions · 1 signal".

**The switches save when pressed.** The workflow's switch saves with `workflow.update { enabled }`, and a start trigger's pause switch with `trigger.pause` or `trigger.resume`. Both work as Settings' switches do ([The sections](#the-sections)):

- While the save runs, the switch shows the value pressed.
- When the save fails, the switch goes back to the saved value and "Could not save: <the error>" shows under the name, or under the trigger's row, as an alert.
- Saves of one workflow's switch, and of one workflow's trigger switches, run one at a time, in the order pressed.

**The graph of the workflow's steps** draws no run. It is the plan as the workflow is saved now, parsed in the renderer from `workflow.read`'s source with the contract's parser ([ADR 0029](../adr/0029-workflow-definitions-are-stored-as-their-yaml-source.md)). The web editor's layout lays it out: slice 38 moves that layout, with dagre, into `@hercule/client-core`, so the web app and the desktop app share one copy and draw the same graph.

- **A step** is a raised card: a 28px sunken lead box with AgentIcon, the action's mark, a clock or a bolt, then the step's id and what it runs. **A trigger** is a sunken card with a raised lead box, because a trigger listens rather than works. A plugin's action draws its plugin's mark as [Design system](#design-system) says, never an SVG of the plugin's own; a built-in action draws an app icon.
- **Edges** are 1.5px lines in ink tones. A condition is a mono label on the edge, because it is code; a limit or a count, such as "≤ 3" or "1/3", is in the UI face with tabular figures. Start triggers fan out from a muted dot to the entry steps; an ink dot follows the step that ends the run. A step that waits for every edge into it carries the tag "all".
- **Zoom:** a pill on the graph's bottom-right corner with −, the percentage, + and Fit. The graph opens fitted to the pane, never below 80%. `⌘=` or `⌘+` zooms in, `⌘-` out, `⌘0` to 100% and `⇧1` fits, as in Figma. `⌘9` is not used: Go › `⌘1` to `⌘9` opens a thread from the sidebar. A graph wider than the pane fades out over 48px on the side where it is cut, except for the scroll bar's strip.
- **A step's card.** Each node is a button. A click opens its card beside it: the book's `.pop`, 320px wide, kept inside the window. The node's id is the title, with "Agent step", "Action step", "Start trigger" or "Signal trigger" on the right. Then one label and value row per field its definition sets, in mono for data and expressions:
  - an agent step: Agent, Model, Access (", from the Agent" when it is the Agent's), Session ("A new one each time it runs" for a fresh session), Returns, and the Prompt as a section of its own;
  - an action step: Action, then one row per parameter;
  - either step: Runs if, Waits for ("Every step before it"), Ends the run ("When it completes");
  - a start trigger: Fires on, Filter, Status ("Paused"), Next, Last fired;
  - a signal trigger: Listens for, Matches.

  A click on the same node, Escape or a click anywhere else closes the card.

**The four tabs:** Runs, Triggers (with its count), Inputs (with its count) and Source.

- **Runs, as List or Timeline,** picked with the `.seg` switch "Show the runs as" at the right end of the tab bar's row.
  - **The list:** Status, Started by, Took and Started. Status is the run's mark and its words: the question while it waits on the user, "At review", "Waiting for approved", "Starting", "Completed", "Cancelled", or why it failed and where, such as "Session failed at test" or "Iteration limit at review". A cancelled or starting run has no mark, as Bureau change 3 says: its words stand in the mark's column, and in a strip or on the timeline it is a `--faint` dot the mark's size. Started by is the trigger's id with a clock or a bolt, or the actor ("You"). The list reads a page at a time and reads the next as the user scrolls to its end. It is virtualized. Empty: "No runs yet".
  - **The timeline:** one day of runs across its hours. The axis sits where the list's column heads sit, labelling each hour, or every third hour below 900px of pane. A pill shows the time now, with a stem down to the axis and a line down the board. A run is a 4px bar from its start to its end with its mark at the end, in `--you` or `--fail` when it waits on the user or failed, and at least 7px long, so a run of a few minutes is its mark alone. A run that went on past the day's edge is cut square there, with a faint chevron pointing to the day it went on into, and no mark: its mark is drawn on the day it ends. A fire still to come is a dashed tick on the top track, its time beside it, titled "on_friday starts a run at 14:00". Empty: "No runs today", or "No runs this day".
  - **The day stepper:** "Show the day before", the day ("Today", "Yesterday", "27 Sep"), and "Show the day after", which is disabled on today. Away from today, a quiet "Today" button leads it, as in the Mac's Calendar.
  - **A row or a bar opens the run's page.**
- **Triggers:** Trigger, Does ("Starts a run" or "Resumes a run"), Fires on, and State. State is the trigger's error, "Paused", "Off" while the workflow is off, "Next Fri 14:00", or "On event"; a signal trigger's is empty. **Each start trigger has its pause switch,** named "on_friday starts runs". Empty: "No triggers. Only you, an agent or another run start it."
- **Inputs:** Input, Type (its schema's type, "github/github connection", or "JSON"), Required or Optional, and Default as JSON. Empty: "This workflow takes no inputs."
- **Source:** the stored YAML, as the user wrote it, in a selectable code block.
- **Below 560px of pane,** the Runs list drops Started by and Triggers drops Does.

### A run's page

- **The crumb,** in a `nav` named "The run's place in Workflows": Workflows › the workflow's name › the run's mark, "Run" and when it started: "Today 10:31", "Fri 14:00" within the last week, "25 Sep 14:00" before it. A workflow that is gone shows its name with "not saved" or "deleted" after it, and no link. Its button is **Cancel run…** while the run is live and **Re-run…** once it has ended.
- **The lead:**
  - the run's mark and its state, "Waiting on you", "Starting", "Running", "Completed", "Failed" or "Cancelled", then how long it has run or took;
  - what it asks the user, with **Open thread**, or why it failed: "Iteration limit at count. <the message>", from the run's `failureMessage`, its failed edge's message or the failed step's error, as an alert;
  - "Started by on_friday", or by the actor;
  - its inputs;
  - **the earlier-version note**, when the workflow's text changed after the run started, that is when its `updatedAt` is later than the run's `createdAt`. Turning a workflow on or off does not move `updatedAt` ([./07-workflows.md](./07-workflows.md) section 1), and the page does not parse the current source, so text changed and changed back shows the note too: "This run followed an earlier version of Ship release. Its steps are shown as they were then."
- **The steps, as Graph or Timeline,** under the heading "Steps" with their count and the `.seg` switch "Show the steps as".
