  - **The graph** is the plan the run froze, with the run drawn on it: a 14px mark on an 18px `--surface` disc on each card's top-right corner; a `--you` ring on a step that waits on the user and a `--fail` ring on a failed one; a `--line` ring on a trigger that listens; cards the run has not reached, skipped or cancelled at half opacity; and "×2" after a step's id when the run came to it twice. Edges follow [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Runs: fired, active, not taken and not yet, with a failed edge in `--fail`.
  - **The timeline:** one row per step record, then one per plan step the run has not reached, muted and with no bar, on a shared time axis, Step and Took, with a "now 29m 12s" pill while the run is live. The axis's scale changes only when its labels change; between, a tick of the age clock changes the pill's text and moves the live step's bar end by `transform`, and nothing else.
- **A node's card on a run** is the step's card above, led by a status line with the node's mark: "Not reached in this run", "Waiting to start", "Working", "Waiting on you" with the question in `--you` ink, "Done", "Failed", "Cancelled", "Skipped: its condition was false"; for a signal trigger "Fired", "Listening for its event" or "Has not fired"; for a start trigger "Started this run" or "Did not start this run". Then:
  - its facts: Started, Took or Running for, Input, Output, and Error in `--fail` ink;
  - for an agent step: Model, Tokens ("41.7k", "(incomplete)" or "Not reported"), Tool calls (`Session.toolCalls`) and Cost, Cost only when the session reports one, and its Prompt;
  - for a trigger: what it fires on and its filter, from the plan the run froze, and when it next fires, from the workflow's triggers, the one thing the plan does not hold. A trigger since removed shows no next fire;
  - "Ran 2 times", with one row per time the run came to the step;
  - **Open thread**, a `.line` row with a chevron, which opens the step's session at `/threads/$sessionId`.
- **What the web app's run page shows, this page shows too** ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Runs), with client-core's words:
  - "Re-run of run X", linking to the original run, and the runs it was re-run as, each linking to its page (`describeReruns`);
  - the runner, and whether the controller can reach it, and the workspace, with **Delete workspace** (`workspace.dispose`) when `describeRunWorkspace` offers it, and the workspace's note. Delete workspace asks first in a ConfirmDialog: "Delete the run's workspace?", the danger action Delete ("Deleting…" while it runs), and Keep it, the closing button, focused, because the delete cannot be undone. A refusal reads "Not deleted: " and the reason;
  - why the run waits for a runner (`describeRunnerWait`);
  - what went wrong at a failed edge;
  - the event that started it, as the web's Triggering event card;
  - its output, under the inputs, only when it has one.
- **A refused Cancel, Re-run or Delete workspace** shows why in full, in `--fail` ink, as the web app's page does.
- The book draws the run's lead, graph, timeline, cards and dialogs. The re-run links, the runner and workspace rows, the runner-wait line, the failed edge's message, the triggering event, the output and the refusals have no drawing of their own: they take the facts list's rows and the lead's type, and their words follow the web app's page ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md)).

**Cancel run…** opens a confirm dialog ([Design system](#design-system)): "Cancel this run?", with the danger button "Cancel run" ("Cancelling…" while it runs) and the button that closes it, "Keep running", as the web app's dialog has. "Cancel" beside "Cancel run" could mean either. ConfirmDialog takes the closing button's label for this; every other dialog keeps "Cancel". Keep running is focused.

- The body says what stops: "Its running steps stop and their sessions end, and so do the runs it started. You can re-run it afterwards." The controller cancels every unfinished run the run started, at any depth, and applies the same `keepWorkspace` to their workspaces.
- What happens to the workspace comes from `describeRunWorkspace`, never from the dialog's own guess: a run in a project's main checkout loses nothing. When the reading's `asksOnCancel` is true, a `.choice-box` checkbox "Delete workspace", checked, follows. The reading gives no note in that case, so none shows. Unchecked, the cancel sends `keepWorkspace: true`, as the web app's does. The checkbox follows the run's own workspace only: a run in a main checkout whose child runs have workspaces of their own shows no checkbox, and their workspaces are deleted. The contract has no read of a run's child runs yet, so asking about theirs waits for one.
- A refusal shows in the dialog: "Not cancelled: <the error>".

**Re-run…** opens a confirm dialog: "Re-run with the same inputs?", with the accent button "Re-run" ("Starting…" while it runs). Re-run is focused, because it only adds a run.

- The choices are client-core's `listRerunChoices`, as stacked option rows in a radio group "Re-run from": a `.choice-box` radio, its label, and its explanation under it in muted ink. "From the current workflow" is chosen when the dialog opens; "As it ran" is the other.
- With one choice, its explanation is a paragraph instead of the radios: the workflow was never saved, or was deleted, so the new run follows this run's plan.
- When the new run starts, the app goes to its page. A refusal shows in the dialog: "Not re-run: <the error>".

### "From runs" in Waiting on you

Waiting on you lists the sessions of live runs that wait on the user, below its threads and assistants, under the sub-label "From runs", a 16px line with 7px above it (its 6px margin and the section's 1px gap), indented to the rows' text.

- A session qualifies when it belongs to a run (`runId` is set), it is live, and its `openRequests` is not empty.
- Rows are ordered by their last activity, newest first, as the threads' rows are, and capped at 3, apart from the threads' cap. The hidden ones add to the section's "N more waiting on you" row, and the section's count includes them.
- A row is led by the waiting mark, then the session's title, which for a run's step is "<workflow> · <step>" (`Session.title`), with the question under it. The session read alone draws it. Its accessible name is "<title>, waiting on you". It opens the session's thread, `/threads/$sessionId`; while the Office is open, in the Office's drawer.
- **The Go menu does not list these rows,** and the dock badge and notifications do not count them ([Native behaviour](#native-behaviour)). They stay about the user's own threads and assistants.
- Waiting on you shows on every screen, so its read is a shell read: the shell's loader loads it, and its query options keep it for the life of the window (AGENTS.md, web app layout rule 8).

### Keys

| Key | Where | Does |
|---|---|---|
| `⌘F` | the workflows page | focuses the search field and selects its text |
| Esc | an open workflow | closes it, back to the table. A step's open card, an open popover or a focused text field takes Esc first |
| Esc | a run's page | goes to the run's workflow, or to Workflows when it has none or it was deleted. An open card, popover or dialog takes Esc first |
| `←` / `→` | a tab bar | moves to the previous or next tab and shows it. The open workflow's tabs are ARIA tabs: a `tablist` of `tab`s and their `tabpanel`. Home and End go to the first and last |
| `←` / `→` | a `.seg` switch | moves between its two segments, as any segmented control does |
| `⌘=` `⌘+` / `⌘-` / `⌘0` / `⇧1` | a graph | zooms in, out, to 100%, and to fit |
| `↩` | a confirm dialog | presses the focused button, which is the closing button when the action takes something away; in a field, presses the action ([Design system](#design-system)) |

### Workflows' motion

Motion tells the user that something changed ([Rules](#rules), rule 2). Each motion plays once, on `transform` and `opacity` of HTML elements, within `--dur-3`, and never loops. Reduce motion turns it all off. Only a change made while the page is open plays: a row or card drawn when the page opens, or scrolled into view, stays still, and a row the virtualizer mounts outside the scroll port plays nothing. Colours and edges change in one frame, because edges are SVG, which Chromium animates on the main thread.

1. **The pop, `mark-in`:** a mark whose state changed grows in from scale 0.6 and transparent, on `--ease-spring`. One keyframe, shared by every screen.
2. **The open pane settles:** opening a workflow slides its pane in from 14px to the right and transparent, `--dur-3`, `--ease-out`. Opening another workflow plays it again; widening to full does not.
3. **The list:** a mark whose state changed pops in, in the row's lead and in the strip. When a run joins a strip, the older marks slide left one slot (11px) on `--ease-out` while the new one pops in. Runs that join in one push slide the older marks one slot for each. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#553](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/553): two runs can join between two reads of the strips.)*
4. **The graph, as the run moves** (a run's page): a card the run reaches brightens from half opacity; one it passes by dims to half; a ring fades in with its mark. These play together.
5. **The ring's ping:** when the run comes to ask the user something, or fails, a copy of the ring grows about 3.5px past the card's edge and fades, once.
6. **The timeline's arrival:** opening the timeline or stepping to a day is the user's action, so its bars arrive 30ms apart, each line sweeping from its start to its end (`scaleX`, `--dur-3`, `--ease-out`), and its mark pops in over `--dur-2`, starting `--dur-2` after its line starts. From the ninth bar on, the bars arrive as one layer: the lanes holding them fade in once, so a day of hundreds of runs plays ~~nine layers~~ 17 animations, not hundreds: the lanes' fade and a line and a mark for each of the first eight bars *(amended 2026-10-12, [#553](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/553): the count left out that each bar's line and mark animate apart)*, and the arrival takes 640ms at most, within the 800ms rule 2 allows. A bar that arrives or changes later only pops its mark.

A row that changes group moves there in one frame. Making room for it with motion, as Intake does, is post-v1.

### What the pages read

| The page shows | Read with |
|---|---|
| The workflows | `workflow.query` |
| Starts on, Next, a trigger in error | `trigger.query` |
| A workflow's strip | ~~`run.query({ workflowId, limit: 20 })`, one read per workflow; spec 11 says the workflow list is short~~ `run.query({ newestPerWorkflow: 20 })`, one read for every workflow's strip, every page taken. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#550](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/550): one read per workflow cost 18-23 ms of renderer main thread per push with 50 workflows, over the 16 ms budget, so the strips are one read.)* |
| The Running group | `run.query({ status: "running" })` and `run.query({ status: "pending" })` |
| Needs you, and "From runs" | `session.query({ kind: ["agent", "run", "assistant"], status: <the live statuses> })`, kept where `runId` is set and `openRequests` is not empty |
| Where a live run is | `activeStepIds` and `awaitedSignalIds` on `RunSummary`'s running variant ([#549](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/549)) |
| The open workflow and its graph | `workflow.read`, its source parsed in the renderer |
| The Runs tab's list | `run.query({ workflowId })`, a page at a time |
| One day of the timeline | `run.query({ workflowId, activeSince, until })` ([#549](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/549)) |
| A run's page | `run.read`; `run.query({ originalRunId })` for its re-runs; `session.query({ runId })`; the run's workspace and runner; the workflow's triggers; `workflowAction.query` for `describeRunnerWait`; `workflow.read`, whose `not_found` tells `listRerunChoices` the workflow was deleted |
| Tool calls on a step's card | `Session.toolCalls` ([#549](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/549)) |

- **Every list read takes every page,** as the web app's queries do, except the Runs tab's list, which reads a page at a time as it scrolls.
- **A `workflow` push reads every trigger again.** Today a push for one workflow refreshes that workflow's trigger read only, not the `trigger.query` the list reads, so slice 37 makes the push refresh both in `@hercule/client-core`'s live keys.
- **Live:** the `workflow` and `run` topics are held while a workflow page or a run's page is mounted, as the subagents' topic is held by its screen; the shell does not hold `workflow`. *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* The shell holds `run`, for Tracking's count and for these pages, with the same rule: the pages hold only `workflow`, and there is one `run` subscription, so a push arrives once. A `run` push reads every run list again, within the 50ms window pushes are gathered in. While the window is hidden, the pages drop `workflow` and the shell drops `run`, and both are read again when the window shows ([Rules](#rules), rule 3). "From runs" stays current on the `session` topic, which the shell holds.
- **Times read the age clock** ([Rules](#rules), rule 4): ages, durations, the timeline's now line and a live run's bar. On the day timeline a pixel is about two minutes, so its now line and live bars move once a minute. A moment such as "10:31" is formatted against the age clock's today, and registers local midnight as its next change, so it becomes "Sun" once its day has passed. The Next column registers the time it names.
- **Interpretation goes to `@hercule/client-core`,** each function with its own tests: the rows and their groups, the filters' counts, schedules in words, the graph model, a node's card in each state, the open workflow's rows, the timeline's tracks and bars, and a run page's lead. The prototype's files name a possible split: `workflow-rows.ts`, `schedule-text.ts`, `graph-model.ts`, `node-details.ts`, `workflow-detail-rows.ts`, `run-timeline.ts` and `run-page-rows.ts`.

### What Workflows costs

The plan for slices 37 to 40, each measuring its part and recording it in [Measured](#measured). The prototype measured first, with `pnpm build:desktop`, gzipped:

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| Idle | no work: no timer of its own, no polling, no animation. Times read the age clock, the one timer the sidebar already runs. The `workflow` and `run` topics send nothing while nothing changes | the perf script's idle sample, with a workflow open |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the Go item, the face's row, "From runs" and the routes' stubs. The prototype added 3.7 kB; slice 37 finds where it comes from and removes it | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| The graph's chunk | the YAML parser (about 30 kB), the layout and the graph: 62 kB in the prototype. The layout loads the first time a workflow or a run opens, the parser the first time a workflow opens, never at launch. The list loads neither | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with 50 workflows listed and one open, and with a run's page open | the perf script |
| Lists | the workflow list and the Runs tab's list virtualized; one day of the timeline drawn whole | the perf script, with 50 workflows, and one workflow with 300 runs in a day |
| Opening the list | ~~one strip read per workflow, which Chromium sends six at a time. With 50 workflows: the time until the last strip draws, with the controller local and remote. Past 500ms, the strips become one read, which the contract then adds~~ one read for the strips, beside the list's other reads. With 50 workflows: within 500ms until the strips draw, with the controller local and remote. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#550](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/550): the strips are one read, see Per push.)* | the Network panel, once |
| Per push | one `run` push reads again every run read on screen: ~~each strip~~ the strips' one read, the Running read, the Runs tab's loaded pages and the day, once per 50ms window. With 50 workflows and 50 live runs: within the 16ms of renderer main-thread work per nudge the thread list keeps ([Measured](#measured)). ~~Past it, the same one read for the strips~~ *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#550](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/550): one read per workflow cost 18-23 ms of renderer main thread per push with 50 workflows, over the 16 ms budget, so the strips are one read.)* | the perf script's nudge sample |
| "From runs" | one shell read, live sessions that are not threads, read at launch and again on each `session` push: within the same 16ms per nudge, and no more than one read added to the launch | the perf script's nudge and launch samples |
| Motion | per pushed change, at most each changed visible row's marks (21 when a run joins a strip) and the graph's changed cards, on the compositor alone | the Performance panel, once |
| The timeline's arrival | 640ms of compositor-only animation per arrival, nothing after | the Performance panel, once |

## Discussions and Wiki

*(Added 2026-10-15, [Desktop Discussions and Wiki screens (#669)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/669).)* The two read-only screens of [./19-agent-coordination.md](./19-agent-coordination.md) section 8, which says what they show. This section says what they cost.

- **The pixel reference** is the book's [`desktop/discussions.html`](../design/crew-bureau-2/desktop/discussions.html) and [`desktop/wiki.html`](../design/crew-bureau-2/desktop/wiki.html), drawn from the Workflows pages' parts with no new tokens.
- **Each route is a chunk of its own,** loaded the first time its screen opens ([Rules](#rules), rule 6). The first screen gains only the two sidebar rows and their icons.
- **The lists are virtualized** and read a page at a time as they scroll ([./19-agent-coordination.md](./19-agent-coordination.md) section 8.2). The open Discussion's Posts and shared files, and a wiki page's History, are drawn whole: the contract bounds them at 200 Posts and 200 system Posts, 500 shared files, and the 50 newest revisions ([Rules](#rules), rule 1).

### What Discussions and Wiki cost

The plan for #669, measured by it and recorded in [Measured](#measured):

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| Idle, with the screens closed | no work: no timer, no polling, no live topic | the perf script's idle sample, and the integration tests, which read the topics the app holds |
| Idle, with a screen open | no work: no timer of its own, no polling, no animation. Times read the age clock. The `discussion` or `wikiPage` topic is held only while its screen is open and the window is visible, and sends nothing while nothing changes | the integration tests, which read the topics held with a screen open, closed, and the window hidden |
| Reads | opening a list reads its first page, and the page after it only as the user scrolls there. Opening an item reads it once (`discussion.read` or `wikiPage.read`). The first open of a screen reads them once more, when its topic's subscription starts, because a push may have been missed before it. An author the shell's threads list does not hold is read once (`session.read`) and kept. A push for the topic reads every loaded page and the open item again, once per 50ms window: the list keeps the pages the user scrolled through, so a push after scrolling through 10 pages reads 10 pages | the integration tests |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows by the two sidebar rows, their two icons, and the small modules the new routes share with the first screen, which the build splits into chunks of their own | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| The screens' chunks | one per route: each list, the open Discussion and the open page. The Markdown chunk loads with the first open item, as it does with the first thread | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with a Discussion of 200 Posts open | the perf script, or a one-off run |

## Settings

*(Added 2026-10-04, [Write the Settings port into spec 17, and its build tickets (#402)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402), for [Desktop Settings: a quick port, and a new model for providers (#401)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/401).)* Settings is a port. It brings the web app's Settings to the desktop, drawn in Bureau, with the Appearance page added. It adds no operation to the public API. The one new piece of state is the Appearance, which main keeps in its settings file.

- **The pixel reference** is the book's three settings pages: `desktop/settings-appearance.html`, `desktop/settings-assistants.html` and `desktop/settings-connections.html`. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* A fourth page, `desktop/settings-profiles.html`, draws Permission profiles.
- **The book does not draw the other sections:** Profile, Threads, Machines, Plugins and System. Their content is the web screen's ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §V1 screen inventory). They are drawn only from the pieces the three pages use: `set-sec` with its heading and lead, `set-row` with `set-label`, the segmented control, the toggle, the select field, the table rows of the Connections page, and the tabs of the Assistants page.
- **What a section shows is what the contract holds.** Where the book draws something no operation reads or writes, the section leaves it out and says so below. A row is never drawn with data the app does not have.
- **The renderer imports no web code.** It never imports `@hercule/ui` or `apps/web`. What the web screens interpret, such as thread defaults, provider rows, assistant forms and config fields, is already in `@hercule/client-core`, and the desktop calls the same functions. A web helper that is still in `apps/web` moves to `client-core`, with its test, in the slice that first needs it on the desktop.

### The frame

The frame is the book's: the app's sidebar stays, and the main pane holds the Settings list beside one centred column.

- **Three columns.** The sidebar is ~~the Threads face, unchanged~~ the face that was showing, unchanged *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512))*, 272px *(amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626): at the width the user gave it, or hidden, see [The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout))*. The main pane holds the book's `.settings` grid: the Settings list, 216px, then the section's body, whose column is at most 760px wide and is centred.
  - The book draws the sidebar on its Hercule face, with a Settings row selected. ~~The desktop has only the Threads face ([Scope](#scope-of-the-first-milestone)), so the sidebar shows the threads, and~~ *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512).)* Settings keeps the face that was showing ([The Hercule face](#the-hercule-face)). The foot's Settings button shows as pressed while Settings is open, as the Office button does for the Office.
  - *(Decided 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* The Settings list does not replace the sidebar, as [#401](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/401) first proposed. The book's frame keeps the threads one click away, and nothing on the sidebar moves when Settings opens.
- **The way in:**
  - the sidebar foot's Settings button, which is no longer inert;
  - the app menu's Settings… `⌘,`, under About, as macOS places it. It sends `openSettings` on `menu.command`.
  - Both open the section last opened while the app runs, and Appearance the first time, as the book's foot button links to it. The section is not kept across launches. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* For Permission profiles, the section remembered is its list, `/settings/permission-profiles`, never one profile's page. *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#312](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/312).)* So it is for Skills: `/settings/skills`, never one skill's page.
- **The way back** is the sidebar: a thread, New thread~~ or the Office~~, the Office or Intake *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395))* leaves Settings. Settings has no close button and no back button, because the sidebar never left.
- **The header** is the book's `.bar`: the crumb "Settings /", the section's name as the title, and on the right either the section's primary button ("New assistant", "Add a Connection", "Add a machine") or, on Appearance, "Saved on this Mac". The other sections have nothing on the right. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Permission profiles' list has the primary button "New profile". The page of one profile has the crumb "Settings / Permission profiles /", with "Permission profiles" a link back to the list, the profile's name as the title, and nothing on the right. *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#312](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/312).)* Skills' list has the primary button "Import from a folder". The page of one skill has the crumb "Settings / Skills /", the skill's name as the title, and "Upload new version" on the right; from [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735), a skill with a GitHub source has "Checked 2 hours ago" and "Check now" before it (see [The sections](#the-sections), Skills).
- **The Settings list** is the book's, in its order:
  - You: Profile, Appearance, Threads, Intake *(amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395): the book's list has no Intake row; it sits under You, after Threads, because what Intake shows is the user's own choice)*
  - Crew: Assistants, Skills, Connections, Providers, Machines *(amended 2026-10-18, [#312](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/312): Skills added after Assistants; the book's list has no Skills row. It sits in Crew, after Assistants, because the Skill Library is controller state the crew works with, as assistants and Connections are: every Thread gets it today, and Agents will name skills later ([#737](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/737)). It is not a choice about how the user's own app looks or behaves, which You holds, and it bounds nothing, which Safety does. Until [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732) builds the section, the row is drawn inert, as the rule below says)*
  - Safety: Identities, Permission profiles, Secrets, Bounds
  - System: Plugins, System
  - The open section's row is selected. Connections carries the book's red dot while a Connection's status is `error` or `needs-reauth`.
- **Rows that lead nowhere yet are drawn and inert:** Providers, Identities, ~~Permission profiles,~~ Secrets and Bounds. They show their hover state, do nothing when pressed and carry `aria-disabled`, like the sidebar's Search. Each has a tooltip: "Not built yet". They are drawn rather than hidden so the list keeps the shape the book gives it, and does not change shape when they are built. *(Amended 2026-10-05, [#410](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/410).)* A section whose slice has not been built yet is drawn inert the same way, and its slice makes its row lead to it. The Connections row shows its dot while inert. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#416](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/416).)* Connections is built, and its row leads to its section, dot and all. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Permission profiles' row leads to its section, and is selected on the list and on a profile's page.
  - Providers comes with the provider remodel ([Write the provider remodel into the specs, CONTEXT.md and an ADR, and its build tickets (#406)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/406)). Until then, a runner's providers are logged in to from Machines.
  - Identities, ~~Permission profiles~~ and Bounds are empty states in the web app too. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Permission profiles is built on the desktop: the web app's screen still says "Permission profiles are not editable yet" ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §V1 screen inventory). Secrets is left out of this port: the secrets a section needs, such as a Connection's token or a provider's key, are set from that section.
- **A narrow window.** At the window's smallest width, 800, the body column is about 230px. While the body is narrower than 520px, a `set-row` stacks its control under its label, and a table drops to one column per row. Nothing clips and nothing scrolls sideways. This is a container query on the body, so the sidebar and the list keep their widths.
- **Saving.** A control saves when it changes, because the book draws no Save button. A text field saves when it loses focus, or on `⌘↵`. A failed save puts the field back to the saved value and shows the error, in `--fail`, under its row. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* A toggle button, such as a grant's verb on Permission profiles, saves when it is pressed and goes back to its saved state when the save fails.
- **Routes.** Settings is the layout route `/settings`, a child of the shell, with one child route per section: `/settings/appearance`, `/settings/assistants` and so on. Each is a chunk of its own (rule 6), and none is on the bundle check's list of first-screen routes. Each section's loader prefetches what it reads, and the section subscribes to its live topics only while it is open. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Permission profiles has two routes: `/settings/permission-profiles`, its list, and `/settings/permission-profiles/$id`, one profile's page. Each is a chunk of its own. The page of an id that names no profile navigates to the list instead, replacing its entry in the history. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* Intake's section is `/settings/intake`. It takes two optional search parameters: `kind`, a signal kind or an event kind, whose row it scrolls to and highlights, and `connection`, a Connection id, which it selects in the plugin's Connection control. Intake's provenance line and Everything's event pane link it this way, such as `/settings/intake?kind=github/mentioned&connection=<id>`. A kind or a Connection that no longer exists is ignored, and the section opens as plain `/settings/intake`. *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#312](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/312).)* Skills has two routes, as Permission profiles has: `/settings/skills`, its list, and `/settings/skills/$name`, one skill's page. Each is a chunk of its own. The page of a name that names no skill navigates to the list instead, replacing its entry in the history.

### Appearance

Appearance draws every control on the book's page. Every control is saved on this Mac, in main's settings file.

| Control | Choices | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Theme | Whitehaven, Styles, Orient Express, Nile, End House | - | the theme in use while Follow the system is off |
| Follow the system | on or off, with a day theme (Whitehaven or Styles) and a night theme (Orient Express, Nile or End House) | on, Whitehaven by day, Orient Express by night | switches with macOS's appearance between the day and the night theme |
| Density | Comfortable or Compact | Comfortable | the sidebar's thread rows, see below |
| Text size | four steps | the second | the size of text everywhere except titles, see below |
| Glass | 0 to 100% | 40% | `--glass-level` |
| Reduce transparency | on or off | off | every glass surface solid, whatever the Glass level |
| Open on | ~~Threads or The office~~ Where I left off or The office *(amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395))* | ~~Threads~~ Where I left off | what the app shows at launch |
| Reduce motion | on or off | off | every animation stopped, as macOS's Reduce motion stops them |
| Marks | on or off | on | the source marks on rows |

- **The theme cards.** The card of the theme in use is pressed. Picking a card turns Follow the system off and uses that theme. With Follow the system on, the two selects pick the day and the night theme. A day theme is light and a night theme is dark, so the window's frame always matches macOS's.
- **Density** is saved on this Mac, like every other row. It is a choice about how one client draws its rows, and the desktop and the web draw them differently, so a user may want a different density in each.
  - Compact draws each thread row in the sidebar on one line: ~~its face, its title, its mark and its age~~ its title and its status slot *(amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626): a thread row has no face and no mark, and its status slot holds its status in words or its age)*. Waiting on you keeps its second line, because the open Request is what the user acts on.
  - The book's line, "Compact fits 30% more rows in Intake and the roster", names screens the desktop does not have. The desktop's line is "Compact draws each thread on one line, so more fit in the sidebar." *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* Intake's rows are one line in both densities ([The list on To do](#the-list-on-to-do)), so Density does not change them, and the desktop's line stays.
  - The desktop neither reads nor writes `ui.threadRows`, the user setting the web's Threads › Display row saves on the controller ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §V1 screen inventory, Settings > Threads). That setting stays the web's own.
- **Text size** has four steps. Each step moves the text tokens `--t-11` to `--t-16` by one pixel: the second step is the tokens as they are, the first is one pixel smaller, and the third and fourth are one and two pixels larger. The title tokens, `--t-18`, `--t-20` and `--t-num`, do not change, as the book's line says: "Transcripts follow; titles stay modest." Rows grow with their text; none has a fixed height that clips it.
- **Glass and Reduce transparency.** The page's `--glass-level` is the Glass level.
  - Reduce transparency, the app's or macOS's, sets the level to 0 and the filter to `none`, as the Office does. A Glass level of 0 sets the filter to `none` too (rule 5).
  - The app's toggle and macOS's setting both count: either one makes the glass solid. While macOS's is on, the app's toggle shows as on and is disabled, because turning it off would change nothing. Reduce motion works the same way.
  - `tokens.css` stays the book's file. The renderer never writes `--glass-level` itself: an inline value on the root element would beat the Office's rule, which is not `!important`. It sets a custom property of its own on the root, and a `:root` rule in `base.css` derives `--glass-level` from it. The Office's rule and the Reduce transparency rules are more specific or `!important`, so both still win.
- **Reduce motion** stops what macOS's Reduce motion stops: the CSS animations and transitions, the Office's colleagues (which then stand still) and the first run's room. One renderer function answers whether the app should hold still, from both settings. Every place that reads `prefers-reduced-motion` today calls it instead.
- **Open on.** With ~~Threads, a launch opens the last open thread or the new-thread screen~~ Where I left off, a launch opens the last screen, such as a thread or Intake *(amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394))* ([Native behaviour](#native-behaviour)). A settings file saved before this change holds `threads`, which reads as Where I left off. With The office, a launch opens the Office, with no thread in its drawer. The stored ~~last thread~~ last screen is kept, not removed, so switching back to ~~Threads~~ Where I left off still reopens it *(amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395))*. A first run, the connect screen and sign-in come first either way.
- **Marks** shows or hides the source marks: the mark of the system a row's work came from, such as GitHub on a start card. Today only the draft's start cards draw one. A row that later draws a source mark follows the same setting. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* Intake's rows, its pane's provenance line and Everything's rows follow it. A tab shrunk to its mark keeps it, because nothing else names the tab ([Tabs](#tabs)).

**How the Appearance reaches the window before its first paint.** Main reads the settings file at launch, before it creates the window, so main knows the Appearance before any page exists. Main holds the Appearance though the renderer holds it too, an exception to rule 7, for the reason it holds the window state: it paints the window's background before a renderer exists.

- Main creates the window with the `backgroundColor` of the theme in use: Follow the system's day or night theme by `nativeTheme`, else the chosen theme. Each of the five themes has its exact sRGB `--bg` in `window-background.ts` and in `base.css`, and the unit test that derives them from `tokens.css` covers all five.
- `public/theme-init.js` reads the Appearance through the bridge's `appearance.read`, a synchronous call, and sets `data-theme`, the text size, Reduce transparency and Reduce motion on the root element before the first paint. It still listens to `prefers-color-scheme` and switches between the day and the night theme.
  - `appearance.read` is the IPC contract's only synchronous channel. It has to be: the page must know its theme before it paints, and an asynchronous answer arrives after. Main answers it from memory, without touching the disk. A page that reloads, as it does after the controller URL changes, reads the current Appearance again.
- A change made on the Appearance page applies in the renderer at once, in one frame, with no transition (rule 2), and is sent to main with `appearance.save`. Main writes the file and repaints the window's background. The renderer holds the only window, so main sends nothing back.

### The sections

Each section lists what it reads and writes through the contract, its live topics, and where it differs from the web screen and the book.

**Profile.** Not drawn by the book.

- Reads `user.read`, `settings.read` and `connection.query`. Writes `settings.update` (`user.timezone` and `github.defaultConnectionId`) and `auth.logout`. Live topic: `connection`.
- Shows the user's avatar and name, the time zone, the default GitHub account, and Sign out.
- Same as the web screen. Sign out is also in the app menu, as it is today.
- *(Amended 2026-10-05, [#410](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/410).)* **The time zone's hint differs from the web screen's:** "Schedules run in this zone, such as a workflow's cron trigger and an assistant's heartbeat. This app shows times in your Mac's time zone." The web's hint says every time on screen is read in this zone, but the desktop shows times in the Mac's time zone (see "Messages carry their own time" under [Design system](#design-system)), because the Mac's clock follows the user when they travel. The setting decides when schedules run on the controller.

**Threads.** Not drawn by the book.

- Reads `settings.read`, `runner.query`, `provider.query` and `profile.query`. Writes `settings.update` (`thread.instanceId`, `thread.model`, `thread.accessMode`, `thread.profileId`, `thread.workspace`). Live topic: `provider`.
- The defaults a new thread starts with: provider instance and model, access mode and permission profile, and the workspace a thread opens in.
- Differs from the web screen: no Display row. The desktop's density is Density on Appearance, saved on this Mac.

**Intake.** Not drawn by the book. *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); decided in [#397](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/397) and [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394).)*

- Reads ~~`plugin.read` and `connection.read`, which return~~ the plugin's value from `plugin.query`, which the shell already holds, and a Connection's from `connection.read`; both return the value in effect for every kind with the level it comes from. It reads `ignoreRule.query` ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#ignorerule)). Writes `plugin.updateIntake` ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#runner-plugin-provider-controller-grant-family-infra)), the `intake` field of `connection.update` ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#connection)) and `ignoreRule.delete`. Live topics: `plugin` and `connection`. What the switches mean, and how the three levels combine, is [./05-plugins.md](./05-plugins.md#82-intake-settings)'s; the server works out the levels, so the section merges nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#528](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/528).)* `plugin.query` already returns each plugin's Intake settings, so the plugin's level needs no `plugin.read`, and the section reads `connection.read` only for a Connection whose segment is shown. A plugin-level write changes what every Connection of the plugin resolves to, so a `plugin` push also reads every Connection's page again.
- A lead line at the top: "Workflows and subscriptions receive every event, whatever you choose here."
- One `set-sec` per plugin that declares a signal kind or an event kind, with its mark and name. Under the heading, a segmented control: "All GitHub connections | work | personal", one segment per Connection of the plugin. "All" edits the plugin's level, and a Connection's segment edits that Connection's.
- Three lists in each `set-sec`:
  - **Signals:** one row per signal kind, with its label, its description and a toggle.
  - **Events available to triage:** one row per event kind, with its ~~label~~ description *(amended 2026-10-11, [#528](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/528): a plugin declares no label for an event kind, so the description is the row's label and the kind's id is its hint)* and a toggle.
  - **Ignore Rules:** one row per rule of the plugin's kinds, with the rule's text, "caught 142 in the last 90 days", and Delete. A rule belongs to no Connection, so the list is the same under every segment. With no rules, the list is not drawn. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* The hint reads "Caught 142 in the last 90 days". A rule's text wraps rather than being cut, and Delete… lines up with the switches above it. *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532).)* A plugin's rules stay listed after it stops declaring the kind or is removed, because the rule is kept ([Ignore Rules](./10-triage-intake-and-notifications.md#99-ignore-rules)): its section stays, with its heading and its Ignore Rules only. A removed plugin's section comes after the installed plugins' and is named by the plugin's id.
- **A row that differs from the level below shows where its value comes from,** under its label: "Off · all GitHub connections" when a Connection follows the plugin's choice, or "On · this connection" when the Connection has its own. Such a row has Reset, which ~~writes the value the level below gives, so the server removes the entry~~ writes the level's choices without that kind *(amended 2026-10-11, [#528](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/528): the app does not know the value the level below gives, and a write that leaves the kind out removes the entry just the same)*. Only a row whose value comes from the level the segment edits has Reset.
- Saves on change, as every section does. A toggle that fails goes back to its saved value and shows the error under its row.
- The search parameters `kind` and `connection` ([The frame](#the-frame)) select the segment, then scroll to the kind's row and highlight it once, with the selected tint, which fades over `--dur-3`.
- Differs from the web screen: the web app has no Intake in v1, so it has no such section ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md#the-intake-view)).

**Assistants.** The book's `settings-assistants.html`.

- Reads `assistant.query`, `provider.query` and `profile.query`, and *(amended 2026-10-07, [#414](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/414)/[#415](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/415))* `settings.read`: the user's time zone places the heartbeat timeline's "now" line when the heartbeat sets no time zone of its own. The profiles and the settings have no live topic, so the loader reads them each time the section opens. Writes `assistant.create`, `assistant.update` and `assistant.delete`. Live topics: `assistant` and `provider`.
- The book's tabs, one per assistant, with its face and name, and New assistant in the header.
- The book's profile block: the large face and the name.
- How it works:
  - *(Amended 2026-10-07, [#414](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/414)/[#415](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/415).)* a lead under the heading that says when a saved change reaches the assistant's session, as spec 12 §7 asks of the Assistants settings screen. The book draws no lead there. The sentence is the web form's, from one `client-core` function both apps call;
  - the web form's fields: name, persona (`systemPrompt`), provider instance and model, permission profile, access mode, and reply mode (Turn end or Segments);
  - disallowed tools, as the book's chips with Add, and an × on each chip;
  - ~~the delete move, at the foot of the section, with a confirmation.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-07, [#414](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/414).)* The delete move is its own section, "Delete `<name>`", at the foot of the page after Rotation, with a confirmation. At the foot of How it works it would sit between the fields and the Heartbeat, where a destructive move is easy to hit while editing.
- **Heartbeat,** as the book draws it: the toggle, the lead, "Every `<n>` h from `<hh:mm>` to `<hh:mm>` in Web chat", and the day's timeline with a tick at each beat and a "now" line. Under it, the prompt the heartbeat sends.
  - The contract stores the schedule as a five-field cron expression. Two `client-core` functions, each with its own tests, convert between the two:
    - one that reads a cron expression as an interval and a window of hours, or answers that it is not one;
    - one that builds the cron expression from an interval and a window.
  - A schedule that is not an interval and a window, set from the CLI for one, shows as its cron expression, with the line "Set outside the app. Choosing an interval here replaces it."
  - The target is always Web chat, because `target` has no other value yet. The select is drawn with one choice.
- **Rotation,** as the book draws it: "At `<n>`% of the context or `<n>`k tokens, and daily at `<hh:mm>`", from `contextFraction`, `maxContextTokens` and `dailyAt`.
- *(Amended 2026-10-07, [#414](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/414)/[#415](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/415).)* Until the controller runs heartbeats and rotations ([#94](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/94)), each of the two sections says so in one muted line under its lead, so the user is never told a schedule runs when it does not. The lines go when #94 lands.
- Differs from the web screen: disallowed tools, heartbeat and rotation are new, because the web screen has no fields for them.
- **Left out of the book's page,** because no operation reads or writes them, or the desktop has no screen for them:
  - the role beside each name ("personal", "ops"), because an assistant has no role;
  - the line under the name, "personal assistant · working in Web chat", ~~and Open Conversation, because the desktop has no conversation screen~~ because an assistant has no role and the desktop shows no channels. *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448).)* Open Conversation is drawn, and opens [the assistant's Conversation](#design-system);
  - the context bar and Start fresh under Rotation;
  - Where the assistant listens, its channel bindings;
  - ~~the Memory and Reminders column. Without it, the body is the one centred column.~~ the Reminders section of the right-hand column *(amended 2026-10-11, [#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93))*. The Memory column is drawn, below.
- *(Added 2026-10-11, [#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93).)* **Memory,** the book's right-hand column, beside the settings. The body is the book's two columns, 940px wide; below 740px of body the column moves under the settings.
  - Reads `memory.list` and `memory.read` for `core` of the picked assistant. Memory has no live topic, so the loader reads both each time the section opens or another tab is picked, and a change an assistant makes shows the next time. Writes `memory.write` and `memory.delete`.
  - As the book draws it: "Memory" with Edit, the core note's size against its cap, "1,284 of 4,000", with a meter in the assistant's hue, the start of the core note, cut after four lines, one row per topic with its name, size and gist, and "`<n>` of 24 topics · up to 12,000 characters each". A topic's size is its exact count below 1,000 characters, "604", and thousands with one decimal above, "1.2k". The topics are in name order, as `memory.list` returns them; the book's are in no order.
  - **New topic,** under the list, which the book does not draw, asks for the topic's name in a dialog, and creates it with an empty text. A name the list already holds, or `core`, is refused in the dialog before anything is sent, because a write to an existing name replaces that document. The write is sent with `expectedVersion: 0`, so a topic the assistant created since the list was read is refused too; the column then reads the index again and lists it. The controller's own refusals, such as a 25th topic, show in the dialog as it words them.
  - Edit, and each topic row, open the document's page, `/settings/assistants/$id/memory/$name`, which the book does not draw. Its crumb leads back to the assistant's tab. It holds one section, the document's name with its size against its cap, the meter, and the fields:
    - a topic's gist, a one-line field of at most 200 characters;
    - the text, a text area. The core note says it cannot be deleted, only emptied.
    - Each field saves on blur or ⌘↵, and Esc puts back the saved value. A write replaces the whole text, so a save must never replace text the user did not see. When the user begins typing in a field, the page notes the text the field showed and the document's version. The save then sends as `expectedVersion` the version the page holds now if that field still holds the noted text (the user's own earlier saves move it on this way, and a change an agent made to the other field is kept, because a save sends the current value of every field it does not edit), and otherwise the version noted, so the controller refuses it with `conflict`. A read that brings an agent's text while the user types never moves the version on. While a field's save runs, the field shows the text being saved and is read-only, so no edit begins on text not stored yet; the other field can be edited, and its save waits for the running one. The saves of one document run in order, also across leaving and opening the page again.
    - A failed save puts the field back and shows the controller's error as it words it, "Could not save: …", under the row. When the controller refuses a text that cuts most of a large document (`shrink_rejected`), the row also offers "Save the shorter text…", which asks first, says how many characters the document goes from and to, and sends the text again with `confirmShrink`. When it refuses a save because an agent wrote the document since (`conflict`), the page reads the document again and shows what the agent wrote, and the row offers "Save my text anyway" (or "Save my gist anyway"), which sends the user's text again over that version; an agent's write to the field after that version is refused again, also when the cut it makes is then confirmed. An offer clicked after the field was saved again saves nothing. Each field's error and offer stay under its own row until that field is saved again, also while the other field saves and across leaving and opening the page again.
    - A topic's page ends with "Delete `<name>`", with a confirmation, "Delete `<name>`?". The core note has no Delete.
  - Not drawn: memory in the Conversation's drawer, which still shows "Nothing remembered yet" ([#450](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/450)).

**Skills.** Not drawn by the book. *(Added 2026-10-18, [#312](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/312).)* The Skill Library ([./02-domain-model.md](./02-domain-model.md) §Skill) as the desktop shows it. The prototype on branch `prototype/desktop-skills` ([#731](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/731)) draws it, and Rogier approved it on 2026-10-18 ([./16-open-items.md](./16-open-items.md), Pending prototypes). [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732) builds the list, a skill's page, import, Upload new version and Delete; [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735) and [#736](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/736) add their states, below.

- **The pixel reference.** The book has no Skills page. The section is drawn from the pieces of the book's Settings pages ([Settings](#settings)): the list follows the table of `desktop/settings-profiles.html`, whose rows carry a line under the name ("Shipped with Hercule") and lead to a page of their own, and the row's parts follow `desktop/settings-connections.html`'s table. A skill's page is `set-sec` sections, as a profile's page is.
- Reads `skill.query` and `skill.read` ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md) §2, `skill`). Writes `skill.import` and `skill.delete`. ~~Live topic: none. The Skill Library has no live topic, so the loader~~ Live topic: `skill`, held only while the section is open, the list or a skill's page *(amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735))*. ~~The loader reads the list each time the section opens, and a skill each time its page opens, with `readOnOpen`, as Permission profiles reads its profiles.~~ The loaders read the list, a skill and a waiting revision's diff only when the cache does not hold them, as Wiki does, because the topic's first push on each subscription reads the list and the open skill again. A waiting revision's diff is read with its skill, and again whenever its skill is: the controller compares the revision with whatever is current when it answers, so a diff kept apart could show the changes against a revision that is no longer current. After each of its own writes, the section reads the list, and the open skill, again. ~~A change made elsewhere, from the CLI or by a session, shows the next time the section or the page opens.~~ A push on `skill` reads the list again, and the open skill when the push names it, so a change made elsewhere, from the CLI, by a session or by an update check, shows at once ([./16-open-items.md](./16-open-items.md) A).
- **Words.** The screen calls a Skill Source "source", never "origin": `origin` is a Skill Revision's field, and the History labels below are the only place it shows. The destructive action is "Delete", as on Assistants and in the API.
- **The lead.** Above the list, one paragraph: "A skill is a folder of instructions, led by its `SKILL.md`, that an agent loads when a task calls for it. The library keeps one copy of each, for every runner." ~~Under it, the banner.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#734](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/734).)* No banner follows it.
- **The banner.** One muted line: "Threads don't use the library yet." Until [#733](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/733) and [#734](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/734) build delivery, the [#75](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/75) skill links remain, and the line keeps the user from believing an imported skill already reaches a Thread, as Assistants' muted lines do for heartbeats. #733 changes it to "Codex and pi Threads don't use the library yet." #734 removes it. *(Amended 2026-10-18, #733: the line now reads "Codex and pi Threads don't use the library yet.")* *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#734](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/734).)* The banner is gone: every Thread, on every harness, uses the library.
- **The list.** A table with the columns Skill and Size, one row per skill, sorted by name. Every row starts with the skill icon, the one the Settings list draws beside Skills, in the row's icon tile:
  - **The built-in `hercule` skill comes first.** Its row shows the icon and the name, with "Built in" under it, and nothing else. It has no actions, no badge and no page, and a click on it does nothing. The built-in skill is not in the Skill Library and not in the API: the runner always adds it ([./06-providers.md](./06-providers.md) §9.3). The row is drawn from the reserved name `BUILT_IN_SKILL_NAME` in `@hercule/contract`, and reads nothing.
  - **Every other row** shows the name, with its source under it: "GitHub: mattpocock/skills" for a GitHub source, "Uploaded" for an upload. Then its badges, then its size, "2 files · 6.5 KiB" from `currentFileCount` and `currentSizeBytes` (bytes through `formatBytes` in `@hercule/client-core`), then a chevron. A click opens the skill's page.
  - **Badges**, each shown only when it applies, from the Skill record's fields:
    - "Update available" while `availableRevisionHash` is set ([#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735)). When the update's `availableApprovalEffects` differ from the `currentApprovalEffects`, or the update has hooks at all, the badge says "Update may change approvals" instead, so the user sees before applying that the update can change what runs without asking. An update with unchanged hooks counts too, because a hook can run a script in the skill that the update changes. Client-core decides, with its own test. *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735).)* An update whose `allowedTools` is set counts too, even unchanged, for the same reason: a rule such as `Bash(./scripts/run.sh:*)` runs without asking a script the update can change.
    - "Proposed", with the count when `proposedCount` is more than one ([#736](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/736)). *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735).)* #736 draws it, with the proposed revisions on the skill's page; a badge drawn before then would lead to a page that cannot show what it names.
    - "Check failed", in `--fail`, while `checkError` is set, with the error as its tooltip.
  - A skill that exists only as a proposed Skill Revision, with no current revision, shows "Proposed by Ada, session s_12" under its name in place of a source, its "Proposed" badge, and no size. Its page shows the proposed revision only, under the banner "A new skill: no Thread gets it until you apply it.", with Reject and Apply.
  - With no skill in the library, the list holds only the built-in row, and under it the empty block Settings pages use: "No skills yet", the line "Import a folder of skills, such as `~/.agents/skills`.", and the button Import from a folder.
- **A skill's page**, `/settings/skills/$name`:
  - **The lead,** first: the current revision's `description` as the page's lead paragraph, and under it two chips. The source chip reads "mattpocock/skills · skills/grilling" after a globe icon for a GitHub source, with the branch when it is not the default one, or "Uploaded". The size chip reads "2 files · 2.1 KiB".
  - **SKILL.md,** with the file's size on the right of its heading, and the current revision's `SKILL.md` in a card under it, rendered with the app's `Markdown` component, as a wiki page is. The frontmatter is not drawn: client-core drops the lines from the opening `---` to the closing one, without parsing them, since the lead already shows the `description`. A relative link, such as one to `reference.md`, draws as plain text, because the page cannot open the skill's other files ([#741](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/741)). #732 adds that option to the component.
  - **A warning line** under the chips when the current revision's `currentApprovalEffects` name `allowed-tools` or `hooks`, because on Claude Code such a skill can let tools run without Hercule's approval card ([./06-providers.md](./06-providers.md) §9.1, [./13-security.md](./13-security.md) §8). For `allowed-tools`, the line names the rules: "Lets tools run without asking: `Bash(git:*)`". `hooks` gets a line of its own: "Has hooks, which run without asking". The renderer parses no YAML. An import is the user's own files, so it applies at once, and this line is where the user sees what it does afterwards.
  - **Files,** with "2 files · 2.1 KiB" on the right of its heading, and one row per file of the current revision: its path, its size, and a mark for an executable file. A row is not a link: reading a file other than `SKILL.md` waits for the skill editor ([#741](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/741)).
  - **History,** one row per revision that has been current, each once, newest `appliedAt` first: how the revision arrived, from its `origin` ("Imported", "Updated from GitHub" or "Proposed by a session"), and who applied it and when, "applied by you, 14:02" or "applied by Ada, session s_12, 14:02", under it, after the applier's face. On the right, the hash's first seven characters, and on the current revision the chip "Current" before them. A session the shell does not hold is read once with `session.read` for its name, and kept.
  - **Delete `<name>`,** the last section, as Assistants' and Permission profiles' delete sections are. Delete… opens a confirmation, "Delete `<name>`?", with "This deletes `<name>` and all its revisions. This cannot be undone.", then calls `skill.delete` and goes to the list. A refusal stays in the confirmation.
- **Import from a folder,** the header's button:
  1. It calls `skillFolders.pick` ([The IPC contract](#the-ipc-contract)). `Cancelled` does nothing. `Unreadable` shows its reason under the header.
  2. `Picked` opens a glass dialog, "Import from `~/.agents/skills`", with the path `Picked` gives, with one row per skill folder found: a checkbox, the name, and its source under it as the list words it, "GitHub: mattpocock/skills" (from `.skill-lock.json`) or "Uploaded". Every row is checked. A lock file that could not be read shows its line at the top of the dialog; the import still runs, with every skill as "Uploaded". A folder with no skill folder in it shows "No skills in `<path>`. A skill is a folder that holds a `SKILL.md`." and only Done.
  3. Import runs the checked skills one at a time: `skillArchive.build`, then `skill.import` with the archive and, for a skill the lock file gives a GitHub source, `sourceRepo` and `sourcePath`. The dialog shows "Importing 3 of 24". `TooLarge` marks the row in `--fail`, "`teach` is over 10 MiB, the most a skill can hold.", and `Unreadable` marks it with its reason; neither is sent. A skill whose content is already the current revision answers `unchanged`: no revision is stored, but the skill's source is still set from the lock file, and the list shows it after the import. A refusal from the controller, such as a broken content rule, shows under its row in `--fail`, worded as the controller words it. Either way the import goes on with the next skill. Two answers stop the import at that skill instead, because every later skill would get them too: `forbidden` or `unauthenticated`, which are about the credential, and any failure that is not the controller answering, such as a controller that cannot be reached. The rows keep what the skills before it got, and the error shows once, in the summary's place: "The import stopped at `teach`: …". The CLI stops at the same answers ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md) §6.3, `hercule skill import`). *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732).)*
  4. A skill whose name exists with different content answers `conflict`. Once every checked skill has run, the dialog lists those skills in one confirmation: "`grilling` exists with different content." per skill, each with a checkbox, and Skip and "Import as new revisions". Skip leaves those skills as they are. Confirming imports each again with `replace`, which makes the imported content the skill's current revision; it never makes a second skill.
  5. The dialog ends with a summary, "Imported 21, 2 unchanged, 1 refused", and Done.
- **Upload new version,** on a skill's page, calls `skillFolders.pick` too. The picked folder must be this one skill: a folder that holds another skill, or several, shows "The folder holds `teaching`, not `teach`." under the header, and uploads nothing. Otherwise the app builds its archive with `skillArchive.build` and sends it with `skill.import` and `replace`, and with no source fields, so the skill's source becomes "Uploaded". For a skill with a GitHub source, a confirmation says first that the skill's source becomes "Uploaded", so update checks stop ([./16-open-items.md](./16-open-items.md) B). Content that is already the current revision stores no revision and shows "No new revision: these files are the current revision." The source still becomes "Uploaded", as the confirmation said.
- **Updates,** from [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735). Writes `skill.check`, `skill.apply` and `skill.applyUpdates`, and reads `skill.diff`.
  - The list's header gains "Check now", which calls `skill.check` for every GitHub-sourced skill, and "Update all" while any skill has an update. A skill's page gains "Check now" for that skill, in its header, after "Checked 2 hours ago" from `lastCheckedAt`, once a check has run. A check never blocks. ~~With no live topic, its result shows the next time the list or the page is read ([./16-open-items.md](./16-open-items.md) A).~~ Its result arrives on the live topic `skill` when the check ends, and the list or the page reads it then ([./16-open-items.md](./16-open-items.md) A). *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735).)* "Update all" carries the number of updates, "Update all 2", as the prototype draws it. "Check now" on the list shows only while some skill has a GitHub source. While a write runs, its button is disabled, and Update reads "Updating…". A refused write shows under the header in `--fail`: "Could not check for updates: …" or "Could not update: …". The list's header does not draw "Checked 2 hours ago": its skills were checked at different times, so one age would be wrong for most of them. Only a skill's page draws it.
  - Update all opens a confirmation, "Update 3 skills?". It names each skill whose update may change approvals, by the badge's rule, with that update's warning line, so the user never applies such an update without seeing it. *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735).)* An update that drops every approval effect the current revision has reads "No longer lets tools run without asking". Confirming calls `skill.applyUpdates` with each skill and the revision the confirmation showed. A `conflict`, because a check found a newer update meanwhile, stays in the confirmation, which reads the list again and shows the new updates. *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735).)* The confirmation shows the controller's message above the new updates, and confirming again sends the revisions it now shows.
  - A skill with an update shows, on its page under the lead, a banner, "Update available from mattpocock/skills. Nothing changes until you update.", with Update, which calls `skill.apply`. Under the banner, the section "What it changes": the warning line when the new revision carries `allowed-tools` or `hooks`, "After the update, lets tools run without asking: …" (and "After the update, has hooks, which run without asking" for `hooks`), and the diff of each changed file against the current revision, read with `skill.diff`. A file whose `patchOmitted` is `tooLarge` shows "Too large to show", and one that is `binary` shows "Binary file". *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735).)* A file whose mode alone changed (`modeChanged`) shows "Now executable" when its `mode` is `755`, or "No longer executable" when it is `644`, in place of a patch. An added or modified file whose mode is `755` carries the chip "Executable". The card's head counts the changed files and their added and removed lines, and each file's row its own; a file with no patch shows why in place of its counts. A check that finds an update while the page is open shows "Reading the changes…" until the diff answers. A long patch is drawn in steps, so the page never mounts a line per row of a 1 MiB patch at once: when the card opens, each file draws at most 500 patch lines and the card at most 2,000 across its files, in file order. A file not fully drawn ends with a quiet button, "Show 500 more lines" (fewer when fewer are left), or "Show 120 lines" when none of its lines are drawn yet; each press draws up to 500 more lines of that file. Nothing is applied until the user presses Update or confirms Update all.
  - A failed check shows on the skill's page with its reason, "Last check failed: `<reason>`". *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735).)* The line sits under the lead, in the `--fail` colours, while `checkError` is set, and goes when a check succeeds.
- **Proposed revisions,** from [#736](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/736). Writes `skill.apply` and `skill.reject`, and reads `skill.diff`.
  - A skill's page shows each proposed Skill Revision under the lead, as a banner and a "What it changes" section, as an update is shown. The banner has the proposer's face, "Proposed by Ada, session s_12, 14:02" and under it "Nothing changes until you apply it.", and Reject and Apply on the right. When `includesThirdPartyContent` is true, the chip "Includes third-party content", in `--fail`, follows the proposer, with a tooltip saying the session had read third-party messages, so the changes may carry their words. The section holds the warning line when the revision carries `allowed-tools` or `hooks`, and the diff against the current revision. For a new skill the banner's second line is "A new skill: no Thread gets it until you apply it.", the lead shows the proposed revision's `description`, and the section is "What it adds". Apply calls `skill.apply`; Reject calls `skill.reject`. Rejecting the last proposed revision of a skill with no current revision deletes the skill, and the page goes to the list.
  - A proposed Skill Revision raises no Signal and no notification: the badge and the page are the only places it shows.
- Differs from the web screen: the web app has no Skills screen ([#312](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/312)).

**Connections.** The book's `settings-connections.html`.

- Reads `connection.query` and `plugin.query` (for the Connection types). Writes `connection.create`, `connection.update`, `connection.delete`, `connection.setCredentials`, `connection.startOAuth`, `connection.startDeviceFlow` and `connection.pollDeviceFlow`. Live topic: `connection`.
- One section, "Connections", in the book's table: each row has the type's mark (a generic mark for a type with none), "`<type>` · `<label>`" with the account name under it, its health, and `⋯` with Reconnect, Configure and Delete.
  - Health is the status as one word, as the book draws "healthy": `connected` reads "healthy", `needs-reauth` "needs sign-in", `disabled` "disabled", and `error` shows its `statusDetail` in `--fail`.
- Add a Connection, in the header, lists the types the plugins declare and runs their setup:
  - pasted credentials;
  - the device flow, as the first run's GitHub step runs it;
  - OAuth: the app calls `connection.startOAuth` with the controller's origin, the origin the web app sends, so the redirect the provider has registered still matches. It opens the authorization URL in the default browser with `link.open`. The browser ends on the web app's Connections page, which shows the outcome. The app shows "Finish in your browser" with Cancel, and the new Connection arrives on the `connection` topic. A failed flow creates no Connection, so the browser is where a failure shows.
- Same as the web screen in what it does; the web draws it as rows, the desktop as the book's table.
- *(Added 2026-10-11, [#571](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/571).)* Configure draws a config field that loads its choices from the account as a picker, with the same rules as the web ([./05-plugins.md](./05-plugins.md) section 10.1), reading `connection.listChoices`.
- *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#416](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/416).)* Decisions the book leaves open:
  - Setup and Configure open in glass dialogs, because the book draws neither.
  - Configure saves with a Save button, because it edits several fields at once. This is the one exception to Settings' rule that a control saves when it changes.
  - Reconnect stays in the `⋯` menu for every status. It is disabled only when the Connection's type is no longer in the binary, because then there is no setup to run.
  - Only `error` is drawn as a failure: the row's tint, a red dot and `--fail` ink. `needs-reauth` and `disabled` are drawn as plain words.
  - The reference script reduces the book's 7-column grid to the mark, the name, the health and `⋯`, and the book's 900px column to the frame's 760px.
  - The mark is the mark of the plugin that declares the type, or the plugin's initial when the plugin has no mark of its own. A type with no plugin id gets the generic plug mark. Every type is qualified by its plugin's id, so that case only guards against a malformed type.
  - The account line under the name is left out when it is the same as the label, because it would repeat it.
  - While the body is narrower than 520px, each row stacks: the mark, then the name with the account line, then the health. The column headings are hidden, and nothing clips.
  - Titles name the Connection as its row does: Delete asks "Delete `<Type>` · `<label>`?", and Reconnect's dialog is "Reconnect `<Type>` · `<label>`". `<Type>` is the type's display name, or the raw type when the binary no longer holds it.
  - A dialog whose Connection is deleted elsewhere while it is open, such as from the web app or by an agent, closes, and the section shows one line saying why: "GitHub · work was deleted elsewhere, so its dialog closed."
  - OAuth: while the user signs in, the dialog shows "Finish in your browser" with the authorization URL, Open again and Cancel. Under it is a static line, "Waiting for `<Type>`. If the sign-in fails, the browser says why.", with no spinner: the user may give up in the browser, which then never answers, and a spinner promises a wait that ends. When the default browser cannot open the link, the error shows inline under that line.
  - An OAuth setup finishes when the `connection` topic brings its result: for Add, a new Connection of the type; for Reconnect, that Connection `connected` with a credential rotated since the setup started. A rename, a config edit or a status recovery alone does not count. One false positive remains: a background token refresh that rotates the credential while the user is still in the browser reads as the Reconnect finishing. An exact match needs the controller to report which setup wrote the credential, by a setup id.
- ~~**Left out of the book's page,** because no operation reads them: the stats strip (events in 24 hours, sources, channels, reconnecting), the Default Topic column ([#324](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/324) removed it from the web), the Last 24 hours and Events columns, and the Channels section with its bound assistants. With no channel plugin and no binding operation, every Connection is drawn in the one table, so the book's split into event sources and channels is left out too.~~
- *(Amended 2026-10-17, [#718](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/718).)* **The page is the book's,** with the event counts `event.countByConnection` reads ([./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md#event)). The section also reads `event.countByConnection`, each time it opens, with `readOnOpen`. The counts are not kept current while the section is open: no live topic carries them, and a push for every event would cost more than the counts are worth. When the counts cannot be read, the table stays, with Reconnect, Configure and Delete. Every count reads "–", never 0, and a screen reader reads it as "Event count unavailable". The bars are left out, also those an earlier opening read, which are out of date, and a line above the table says why: "Could not read the event counts: `<the error>`". The loader's read is the section's only read of the counts: the section does not read them again when it mounts, so the line stays, and a controller that refused once is not asked twice.
  - **The strip,** as the book draws it: the events all Connections received in the last 24 hours, the number of Connections ("event sources"), and the number failing, in `--fail-ink` while there is one. Then the note: "Triage reads these events / and prepares work for you." While the body is narrower than 640px, the note moves under the counts, left-aligned.
  - **"Event sources", "Where work comes from",** the book's table: the mark, "`<type>` · `<label>`" with the account under it, Last 24 hours, Events, Health and `⋯`. Every Connection type the plugins declare has an event source today, so every Connection is a row. Last 24 hours is 24 bars, one per hour, oldest on the left, each as tall as its share of the row's busiest hour, at least 2px; a bar's tooltip is "`<n>` events". Events is the sum of the 24 hours.
  - **The column is the book's 900px,** not the frame's 760px, because the book widens this page to fit its columns. While the body is narrower than 640px, a row stacks: the name with the account, the bars with the count beside them, then the health. The bars give up width first, so a row fits the narrowest window, 800pt wide.
  - **Left out of the book's page:**
    - the Default Topic column, because Connections no longer carry a topic;
