    - the Channels section and the strip's count of channels, because there is no channel plugin and no binding operation yet;
    - the Cron row, because cron ticks come from the core through no Connection;
    - "reconnecting", which no Connection status reads. The strip counts the failing Connections instead, those in `error`, the one status drawn as a failure;
    - the book's line under a name that describes what the Connection reads ("3 repositories"), because no operation describes it. The account stays there, as before.

**Machines.** Not drawn by the book. It is the web's Fleet list and runner page in one section.

- Reads `runner.query`, `runner.read`, `provider.query`, `session.query` (the runner's queue) and `runner.queryJoinTokens`. Writes `runner.update`, `runner.drain`, `runner.undrain`, `runner.refreshFacts`, `runner.retire`, `runner.installHarness`, `runner.probe`, `provider.login`, `provider.submitLoginCode`, `secret.set`, `runner.createJoinToken` and `runner.revokeJoinToken`. Live topics: `runner`, `provider` and `session`.
- One tab per runner, as Assistants has one per assistant, with its name and whether it is online.
- For the selected runner:
  - its facts and version;
  - its sessions against its capacity, as `describeCapacity` describes them;
  - its name and capacity, edited in place;
  - its provider rows: one `ProviderLogin` per provider instance, the row the first run and the draft's Log in already use, with Install, Log in, Enter key and Probe now;
  - Drain or Undrain, Refresh facts, and Retire with the question `buildRetireQuestion` builds.
- Add a machine, in the header, shows the join command and the open join tokens, each with Revoke.
- Differs from the web screen: the list and the runner page are one section with tabs, not two screens.

**Permission profiles.** The book's `settings-profiles.html`. *(Added 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)*

- Reads `profile.query`, `agent.query` and `assistant.query`. Writes `profile.create`, `profile.update` and `profile.delete`. Live topic: `assistant`. Profiles and agents have no live topic, so the loader reads them each time the section opens.
- Two pages, as the book draws them:
  - **The list,** `/settings/permission-profiles`. A lead line, "A profile bounds what a session may do through the API. Every agent and assistant runs on one.", then a table with one row per profile, sorted by name with the shipped profiles first. Each row has:
    - a shield mark;
    - the name, with "Shipped with Hercule" or "Made by you" under it;
    - the grant count as "`<n>` of 45", with a meter;
    - Used by: the faces of the agents and assistants on the profile, at most three, and up to two of their names, then "and `<n>` more", or "Nothing";
    - a chevron.
  - A click on a row opens the profile's page. **New profile,** in the header, calls `profile.create` for a profile named "New profile", or "New profile 2" and so on while the name is taken, with no grants, and opens its page.
  - The table narrows as the book draws it: below 600px of body, Used by is dropped, and below 400px the shield is dropped as well. This replaces, for this table, the narrow-window rule above that a table drops to one column per row.
  - **The profile's page,** `/settings/permission-profiles/$id`, in four sections:
    - **Profile.** The Name field, at most 128 characters. It saves on change with `profile.update`. A name that is empty or only spaces is not saved, and the field shows the saved name again. Assistants' Name field works the same way. A shipped profile can be renamed too; that a shipped profile is known by its name is tracked in [#496](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/496).
    - **Grants,** with "`<n>` of 45" at the heading's right. A lead, "What a session on this profile may do through the API. A change reaches its sessions on their next call." One row per grant family, in the contract's order, with the family's label and what it covers. The family's verbs are a group of toggle buttons in the segmented control's track: any number may be pressed, and a pressed verb shows a check. Each press saves on change, with `profile.update` carrying the whole grant list. A failed save puts the toggle back and shows the error under the row of the grant that failed, also when a later press was already saving behind it. *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#593](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/593).)* The `thread` family is drawn as "Threads" with one toggle, "Start". Its hint reads "Start opens or forks a Thread from inside a session. It also needs Sessions › Spawn." The book's Permission profiles page gained the same row.
    - **Used by.** The agents and assistants on this profile, read-only: the face, the name, and "Agent" or "Assistant". Its lead is "Change an assistant's profile on Assistants.", with Assistants a link to that section. With none, "No agent or assistant uses `<name>`."
    - **Delete `<name>`.**
      - A shipped profile has no Delete. It shows the line "`<name>` is shipped with Hercule, so it cannot be deleted. Edit its grants instead."
      - A profile that agents or assistants use shows Delete… disabled, with a hint that names its agents: "Milo and pr-review use it. Move them to another profile first."
      - Any other profile shows Delete…, with the hint "Removes it and its grants." It opens a confirmation, "Delete `<name>`?" with "This removes `<name>` and its grants. This cannot be undone.", and then calls `profile.delete`. If the controller refuses, for example because a live session still uses the profile, the confirmation stays open and shows the refusal.
- **`unrestricted` asks first.** Threads run on it unless the user picks another profile (the thread defaults in Settings › Threads), so a change to it reaches every such thread.
  - Its Grants section shows an amber note: ~~"Unrestricted is meant to hold every grant, so a session on it can do anything you can. Give it only to work you would do yourself."~~ *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732).)* "Unrestricted is meant to hold every grant but Write and Apply on Skills, so a session on it can do nearly anything you can. Give it only to work you would do yourself." The unrestricted profile is seeded without `skill.write` and `skill.apply` ([./13-security.md](./13-security.md) section 6.2).
  - Every change to its grants, a removal or putting a grant back, opens the confirmation "Change unrestricted?" first. It says which grant changes and that threads run on unrestricted unless the user picks another profile, with Cancel and Change. Cancel leaves the grant as it was.
- Differs from the web screen: the web app's screen is an empty state. Nothing is left out of the book's page.

**Plugins.** Not drawn by the book.

- Reads `plugin.query`. Writes `plugin.enable`, `plugin.disable`, `plugin.retry`, `plugin.resetState` and `plugin.configure`. Live topic: `plugin`.
- One `set-sec` per plugin: its name and status, what it contributes, its settings as fields built from its config schema by `client-core`'s config fields, and its moves.
- Same as the web screen.

**System.** Not drawn by the book.

- Reads `controller.read`: the controller's version and id, the default runner and the local runner. ~~Writes nothing.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#282](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/282).)* It also reads what text generation does now from `controller.read`: the model Automatic resolves to and the last failure. It reads `settings.read` and the providers the shell holds, and writes one setting, `textGeneration.model`, with `settings.update`, as soon as the user picks it.
- *(Added 2026-10-11, [#282](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/282).)* **Text generation** is the last row of the Controller section, a select in the shape of Profile's time zone row. Its hint reads "The model that does short jobs for Hercule, such as naming a thread." The book draws no editable row in System, so the row is the book's `set-row` with the book's select (`field field--select`), and saves as every other settings row does ([The frame](#the-frame), Saving).
  - Its options, in this order: **Automatic - currently <instance> · <model>** (or **Automatic - no model available** when `controller.read` reports no model), one option for each model of each provider instance that is logged in on some runner, and **Off**. A stored model that no logged-in instance lists any more stays in the list, so the select shows what is stored. Choosing an option saves `textGeneration.model` as `automatic`, `off`, or `{ instanceId, model }`.
  - Under the row, a quiet line (faint 12px text, which the book does not draw here) gives the last failure from `controller.read`: `Last failure 3 minutes ago - timed out: <the controller's message>`. It is worked out when the section opens and is not kept current. It is left out when text generation is off or has not failed since the controller started.
  - `client-core` builds the options and the line (`text-generation.ts`), so the screen holds no wording of its own.
- The web screen's text, the access-mode fallback chain, read-only.
- Differs from the web screen: it shows `controller.read`, which the web screen does not. `controller.update` is not used, as in the web app.

### What Settings costs

The rules apply as everywhere ([Rules](#rules)); the costs below are what each slice measures and records in [Measured](#measured).

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the foot button's handler, the `openSettings` command, the `/settings` route stub and `theme-init.js`'s reading of the Appearance | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| Settings' chunks | one per section, loaded the first time it opens. Appearance, the largest, draws five theme previews from components the first screen already holds | the bundle check's table, which lists every chunk |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with any section open: Settings holds one section's reads at a time | the perf script, with Appearance and then Machines open *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* and then Permission profiles *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#528](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/528).)* and then Intake *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#416](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/416).)* and then Connections |
| Idle | the idle row, with any section open: no timer, no polling, and no animation; the heartbeat timeline's "now" line moves only when the section is opened again | the perf script's idle sample, with Machines open *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* and then Permission profiles *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#528](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/528).)* and then Intake *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#416](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/416).)* and then Connections |
| Intake's reads *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* | ~~`plugin.read` once per plugin and `connection.read` once per Connection of it, each time the section opens or a segment changes~~ *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#528](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/528).)* no `plugin.read`, because the plugin's level comes from `plugin.query`, which the shell holds; `connection.read` once for a Connection, when its segment is shown; `ignoreRule.query` once *(comes with [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532), which builds the Ignore Rules list; #528 does not read it. Amended 2026-10-12, [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532): built)*; `plugin` and `connection` as its live topics, which the shell already holds. A save makes no read of its own: the controller's push for the write reads the plugin list, and the Connection pages, again. A save at the plugin's level also marks every Connection page out of date, so a Connection's segment shows its new value when shown again even if that push never arrives. No polling | the perf script, with Settings › Intake open |
| Connections' reads *(Added 2026-10-17, [#718](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/718).)* | `event.countByConnection` once each time the section opens, with no live topic and no polling. The controller answers it from the index `events (received_at, connection_id)` alone, never reading an event's row. The bars are plain elements, still, 24 a row | the bundle check's table, and the read timed on a database of 100,000 events in the last 24 hours |
| Memory's reads *(Added 2026-10-11, [#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93).)* | on Assistants, `memory.list` and `memory.read` of `core` for the picked assistant, each time the section opens or another tab is picked; on a document's page, `memory.read` of that document once, and once more before a gist is saved alone. No live topic and no polling. The page is its own chunk, loaded the first time a document opens | the perf script, with Settings › Assistants open, and the bundle check's table |
| Permission profiles' reads *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* | `profile.query` and `agent.query` once each time the section opens, no polling, and `assistant` as its only live topic. The page draws one toggle for each of the 45 grants and one row for each user of the profile. *(Amended 2026-10-13, [#593](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/593).)* The `thread` family adds one more static row: no process, no memory, no idle or per-token cost, and a few bytes of hint text | the perf script, with Permission profiles open |
| Launch | `appearance.read` adds one synchronous message, answered from memory, before the first paint | the perf script's launch steps, before and after |
| Text generation's row *(Added 2026-10-11, [#282](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/282).)* | no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic. System's row reads nothing the section did not already read: `controller.read` (which now also carries the text generation status) and `settings.read` once each time the section opens, and the providers the shell holds. The save is one `settings.update`. The failure line's age is worked out when the section renders, not kept current. All set reads `controller.read` once more when it opens, because the model that Automatic resolves to changes when the user logs in during the providers step. Per streamed token: nothing. Bundle bytes: a small `client-core` module (the choices and the failure line) in the chunks of System and the first run, and nothing on the first screen | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after, recorded in [Measured](#measured) |
| A change of theme or text size | one frame that restyles the page; the Office redraws once, from its existing watch on `data-theme` | the Performance panel, once, recorded in [Measured](#measured) |
| Dragging the Glass slider | a restyle per input event while the user drags, nothing after | the Performance panel, once |

Settings does not open a live topic the shell does not already need, except while a section that reads it is open.

### What Skills costs

*(Added 2026-10-18, [#312](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/312).)* The plan for [Settings > Skills](#settings), per [ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md). The Expected column is the section's budget. [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732) measures it and records the readings in [Measured](#measured); [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735) and [#736](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/736) measure what they add.

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added. Main reads a folder in its own process, only when the user picks one or an import reads a skill | the perf script's process count |
| Dependencies | none added. The page draws `SKILL.md` with the app's `Markdown` component, over client-core's `parseMarkdown`, which the thread, the Conversation, Intake, Discussions and Wiki already use. Neither main nor the renderer parses YAML: the controller returns the frontmatter fields the screen needs, and checks the content rules itself | `package.json` |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the Settings list's Skills row and the two routes' stubs: under 1 kB | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| Skills' chunks | one for the list and one for a skill's page, each loaded the first time it opens. The page reuses the Markdown chunk, which loads with the first open skill as it does with the first wiki page. No YAML parser joins it: the controller returns the frontmatter fields the page needs | the bundle check's table |
| Main's startup | the two channels' schemas stay in the startup file, because main checks every call against its schema from launch. The folder reader and the archive builder are imported the first time a channel is used, as the folder dialog's module is, so the startup file stays within its 160 kB | the startup file's size check, before and after |
| Reads | ~~`skill.query` each time the section opens, and `skill.read` each time a skill's page opens, with `readOnOpen`;~~ `skill.query` and `skill.read` when the cache does not hold them, and once more on the topic's first push *(amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735))*; ~~`skill.diff` once per waiting revision a page shows;~~ `skill.diff` with its skill: when the page opens, and again with every read of the open skill *(amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735))*; `session.read` once per session named in a history or proposed-revision line that the shell does not hold. After each write, the list and the open skill once more, except `skill.check`, which answers before any check has run: its result arrives on the topic. ~~No live topic and no polling~~ After each push on `skill`, the list once more, and the open skill when the push names it. No polling *(amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735))* | the stubbed controller's request log in the section's integration test |
| Live topics *(added 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735))* | `skill`, only while the section is open, the list or a skill's page. Leaving the section drops it | the section's integration test |
| Idle | no work: no timer, no polling, ~~no live topic~~ no push unless a Skill changes *(amended 2026-10-18, [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735))* and no animation, with the list or a skill's page open. "Checked 2 hours ago" reads the shared age clock, with no timer of its own | the perf script's idle sample, with Settings › Skills open |
| Work per streamed token | none: the section reads no transcript | by reading the section |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with 100 skills listed, and with a skill's page open. During an import, the renderer holds one skill's archive, at most 10 MiB, and drops it once its upload answers | the perf script, or a one-off run recorded in [Measured](#measured) |
| The import's IPC | at most 10 MiB per `skillArchive.build`, one call at a time, so no more than one archive is in flight across the bridge; nothing crosses while no import runs | the import's integration test, and one import of 24 skills timed once |
| Lists | the skill list, a skill's files and its history follow rule 1 ([Rules](#rules)) | the perf script, with 100 skills |

### What subagents cost

*(Added 2026-10-05, [#355](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/355); [ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md).)* The plan for the subagents build, measured by that build and recorded in [Measured](#measured):

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the spawn line and the tally; the side pane and the subagent page are their own chunk, loaded the first time one opens | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| Live topics | `subagent` only while a thread is open, on its own page or in the Office's drawer, and a nudge causes a refetch only when it names the open thread. ~~The thread's subagent list is read once more right after the topic is subscribed, because a subscription made after connect gets no re-read and a nudge sent before it is lost.~~ The thread's subagent list is read once more on the controller's first push on the subscription, which names no session, because a nudge sent before the subscription is lost *(amended 2026-10-07, [#454](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/454))*. The controller sends at most one nudge per session per second. A subagent's page holds one agent's `:stream` and `:tap` in place of the thread's, never both | the perf script, with a thread whose four subagents run |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged: a subagent's tokens reach the app only while its page is open, and then take the thread's path | the Performance panel, once, on a subagent's page |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with the side pane open on 20 subagents: the list is records, never transcripts | the perf script |
| Idle | the idle row with the side pane open and every subagent ended: no timer, and the durations of ended subagents do not tick | the perf script's idle sample |
| Running subagents | a running row's duration ticks at most once a second, and only while it is on screen; faces in the surface are still | the Performance panel, once |
| Resizing the pane | layout per pointer move while the user drags, nothing after | the Performance panel, once |

### What a step session's header costs

*(Added 2026-10-15, [#703](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/703).)* A step session is a session a run's agent step started. Its header shows its place in Workflows (see "The header is the book's" under [Design system](#design-system)), and the build measured it, as recorded here:

| Cost | Expected | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | none added |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the shared pills' slot for crumbs, the run's read in the thread's loader and the `run` topic's hook: under 1 kB | 329.8 kB to 330.2 kB gzipped (+0.4 kB), `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| The thread's chunk (`agent-page`) | grows by the branch to the lazy crumbs and the shared tab list | 46.3 kB to 47.1 kB |
| The crumbs' code | its own chunks, loaded the first time a step session opens: the crumbs (2.1 kB), the crumbs shared with the run's page (4.1 kB) and the run's start time (7.2 kB, which the workflow screens already load) | the bundle check's table |
| Reads | when a step session opens: the run, its workflow and the settings, once each, which the loader waits for at most `OPTIONAL_READ_WAIT_MS`, as it does for the senders. The sessions are the shell's own read of live sessions an Agent spawned. A thread opens with none of them | the stubbed controller's request log in the header's test |
| Live topics | `run` and `workflow` while a step session's header is mounted and the window is visible, held by `useScreenTopicsLive` with `WORKFLOW_TOPICS`, as the run's page holds them, so a workflow deleted while the header is open shows as deleted | by reading `useScreenTopicsLive` |
| Work per streamed token | none: the header reads no transcript | by reading the header |
| Idle | one clock tick for the start time while the header is on screen, as the run's page has | by reading `useStartTime` |

### What every session in the Office costs

*(Added 2026-10-15, [#704](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/704).)* The Office seats every session, not only threads, and draws the café across the street ([Every session in the Office](#every-session-in-the-office)). The build measured it, as recorded here:

| Cost | Expected | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | none added |
| Operations | none added. `session.query`'s `runId` filter takes several runs, so the Office reads the sessions of every live run in one read | by reading the contract |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by `isThreadSeated`, the two new hats in the sidebar's faces, and the Office's query options: about 1 kB | 333.8 kB to 334.5 kB gzipped (+0.7 kB), the bundle check on `main` and on the branch |
| The Office's chunk | grows by the café's furniture and the two hats, within its budget of 241.5 kB | 223.8 kB to 229.4 kB gzipped (+5.6 kB) |
| Reads | when the Office opens: the running runs and the pending ones (the workflow list's own two reads), and the sessions of those runs in one read. A `run` push reads the two run lists again; the sessions of the runs are read again only when the set of live runs changes | the Office's integration test counts them |
| Live topics | `run`, held by the shell while the window shows (`useScreenTopicsLive` with `RUN_TOPICS`), since [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801) on every screen, not only the Office. The controller pushes on it at every cron fire, which costs the two reads of the live runs | by reading the Office screen |
| CPU, memory and rebuilds | the same for the same threads as before; growing with the colleagues, not with their kind. The café, which always stands, costs a small building | `perf:office`, below |
| A failed read | a failed read of the runs' sessions shows the route's error, rather than taking a live run's finished sessions out | the Office's integration test |

`pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop perf:office` draws the Office from fixture worlds in the repository's Electron, in a hidden 1440 × 900 window at 2x, from the Vite dev server, and reads each process's CPU and working set from `app.getAppMetrics()`, the WebGL draw calls from the page, and the time to build the office and to build it again with one more run. It is not the packaged app the Office's budgets were set on, so its readings are compared with `main`'s on the same machine, not with the budgets. Measured on the reference machine, 2026-10-15, with other sessions running beside it:

| World | Build | Rebuild | Renderer CPU | GPU CPU | Draw calls a second | Renderer working set | GPU working set |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16 threads, `main` | 512 ms | n/a | 22.5% | 14.2% | 11,403 | 327 MB | 124 MB |
| 16 threads | 212 ms | 66 ms | 23.7% | 14.2% | 12,434 | 336 MB | 137 MB |
| 1 run of 3 | 191 ms | 53 ms | 20.1% | 15.9% | 8,337 | 361 MB | 134 MB |
| 5 runs of 3 | 193 ms | 57 ms | 23.4% | 14.9% | 12,463 | 374 MB | 145 MB |
| 20 runs of 3 | 242 ms | 97 ms | 25.2% | 11.7% | 29,455 | 416 MB | 169 MB |
| 50 threads, `main` | 221 ms | n/a | 24.7% | 11.7% | 24,737 | 404 MB | 147 MB |
| 50 threads | 238 ms | 88 ms | 24.9% | 11.9% | 24,037 | 394 MB | 163 MB |
| 100 threads, `main` | 252 ms | n/a | 27.0% | 12.9% | 42,939 | 455 MB | 168 MB |
| 100 threads | 309 ms | 106 ms | 25.7% | 12.8% | 42,209 | 444 MB | 175 MB |

- **The CPU readings swing** between runs of the script on a machine shared with other sessions: a second run read the 16 threads at 13.9% and 10.2%. Compare readings taken in one run, or `main` and the branch run back to back, and read again on a quiet machine before setting a limit from them.
- **CPU is unchanged** for the same threads, within that swing. It grows slowly with the colleagues: from 16 to 100 colleagues, about 3 points in the renderer, and none in the GPU process, which the 30 frames a second cap holds. So no cap on the colleagues of a run is needed now.
- **The café costs about 13 MB in the GPU process**, on 16 threads: one small building, its textures and its shadows, standing whether or not anyone sits in it. That is under 1% of the Office's memory budget of 1,602 MB.
- **A rebuild** takes 50 to 110 ms, growing with the building. A run that starts or ends rebuilds once, as a new thread's desk always did. `main` has no runs, so its rebuild has no reading. The first build on `main` read high once, a cold start of the page.
- **The draw calls** grow with the colleagues and their desks, as on `main`: each colleague's body, face and hat, and each desk, is its own draw.

### What arrivals and departures cost

*(Added 2026-10-15, [#706](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/706).)* Colleagues walk in and out through the front door ([Arrivals and departures](#the-office)). The build measured it, as recorded here:

| Cost | Expected | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes, operations, reads, live topics | none added | none added |
| Frames | walking draws frames only while someone walks, as before. A run that starts several sessions costs one walk of each, at most 30 frames a second. A world that changes nothing the Office shows, such as a thread's last activity, draws no frame of its own | `perf:office-arrivals`, below |
| Rebuilds | one per arrival, as a new desk always cost; one per room that colleagues walk out of, once the last of them is out | the sim's tests |
| Memory | the colleagues' bodies now outlast a rebuild, so a rebuild frees and builds only the building. Joins and leaves leak nothing | `perf:office-arrivals`, below |

`pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop perf:office-arrivals` draws the Office on 16 threads from the same fixture page as `perf:office`. It takes about a minute and a half on an otherwise quiet machine, and up to three minutes beside a full test run. It reads the frames a change draws until every colleague stands still, on the page's own clock. The leak check runs on a fast clock that the page installs in place of the browser's (`?clock=fast`): every frame is still drawn, and the Office runs 10 to 14 times faster than real time on a quiet machine, about 4 times beside a full test run. Measured on the reference machine, 2026-10-15, with other sessions running beside it:

| Change | Frames | Seconds until still | Frames a second |
|---|---|---|---|
| A run of 10 sessions starts | 897 | 31.4 | 28.6 |
| One thread changes pose | 589 | 20.7 | 28.5 |
| Nothing changes, 3 s | 84 | 3.0 | 28.0 |
| Only a thread's last activity changes, 3 s | 85 | 3.0 | 28.3 |

| 50 joins and leaves, 10 at a time | Buffers | Textures | Vertex arrays | Bodies |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Before | 700 | 34 | 311 | 16 |
| After the first 10 | 742 | 34 | 315 | 16 |
| After all 50 | 742 | 34 | 315 | 16 |

- **A run of 10 costs one walk-in**, at the 30 frames a second cap: its sessions leave the door 0.7 s apart, and the last one is seated after about 31 s.
- **The Office draws its calm ambient life at about 28 frames a second while it is lively**, whether or not the world changes. A change that shows nothing adds no frame to it. At liveliness Still, on battery or with Reduce motion, the Office draws nothing while nobody walks, as before.
- **Joins and leaves leak nothing.** The first round warms the shared caches of the bodies' parts; after it, the GPU objects and the bodies in the scene return to the same count.

### What walks under way at opening cost

*(Added 2026-10-18, [#729](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/729).)* A walk in or out that started while the Office was closed is still under way when it opens ([Arrivals and departures](#the-office)). The plan, and what the build measured:

| Cost | Expected | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes, live topics | none added | none added |
| Reads | each time the Office opens: two more, and a third only when a run finished in the last minute: `run.query({ activeSince })`, `session.query({ kind: ["agent", "run", "assistant"], status: "exited", activeSince })`, and the finished runs' sessions. The two reads of the live runs are made afresh rather than taken from the cache. None while the Office is open, and none when the drawer opens or closes. Each is served by an index, so it costs the sessions and runs live now or ended in the last minute, not every one the controller ever had | the Office screen's integration tests; the session repository's test of the query plan its `activeSince` list runs |
| Work on opening | the sim runs each walk under way on in steps of 1/30 s, at most a minute of walk per colleague, with no frame drawn | the opening of 16 threads on the real Bureau map, in vitest on Node on the reference machine, median of 9: **74 ms** with everyone at rest, **96 ms** with 8 walking in and 8 walking out. About 1.4 ms more per walk under way, most of it finding the walk's path, which a walk seen live costs too |
| Rebuilds | one more on opening for each room whose colleagues walked out before the Office opened, before its first frame: their desks are in the first building and leave with them. It is the rebuild a departure always costs | the sim's tests |
| Frames | a walk under way draws frames as any walk does, and only until it ends | unchanged: `perf:office-arrivals` plays the same walks |
| Memory | the screen keeps the world it opened on, one more `World` of the colleagues it holds, for as long as it is mounted | - |
| Idle, per streamed token | nothing | nothing: the times are read only as the Office opens |
| Bundle bytes | a few hundred bytes | `vite build`, against the commit before: the first screen's chunk, which holds the app's reads and client-core, grows by 1,123 bytes, and the Office's chunk by 1,034 bytes. The first screen was already over its guide budget before this change: it is 338.9 kB gzipped against 250 kB |

### What seating asleep and away threads costs

*(Added 2026-10-17, [#717](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/717).)* The Office seats every thread not marked Done, asleep and away ones too ([Who is in the Office](#the-office)). The change adds no process, no read, no live topic and no timer: the Office already read every thread, and only the rule that picks them changed. Its cost is one colleague and one desk per asleep or away thread, the same as for a working one. `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop perf:office` gained two worlds, `?asleep=<n>`, of 16 working threads plus 50 or 100 asleep ones. Measured on the reference machine, 2026-10-17, in one run, with other sessions running beside it:

| World | Build | Rebuild | Renderer CPU | GPU CPU | Draw calls a second | Renderer working set | GPU working set |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16 threads | 258 ms | 58 ms | 14.1% | 9.0% | 12,586 | 338 MB | 138 MB |
| 100 threads | 288 ms | 107 ms | 23.9% | 12.3% | 42,137 | 451 MB | 182 MB |
| 16 threads + 50 asleep | 260 ms | 96 ms | 23.6% | 11.4% | 29,537 | 405 MB | 171 MB |
| 16 threads + 100 asleep | 284 ms | 111 ms | 26.0% | 13.0% | 45,208 | 473 MB | 189 MB |

- **An asleep colleague costs what a working one does.** 16 working and 100 asleep threads read like 100 working threads, within the swing between runs. A dozing colleague still breathes, so it is drawn in every frame.
- **On `main`, those worlds were the 16 threads row:** the asleep threads had no colleague. A user with many unfinished threads now pays for each of them, the cost the earlier rule avoided. The sidebar lists every one of them already, and the Office's point is to show them all. If a fleet grows past what the Office's budgets allow, the answer is to draw distant colleagues more cheaply, not to hide unfinished work.
- **The first screen's JavaScript and the Office's chunk** do not change within rounding. `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, on `main` at 066803154 and on this change, read the first screen at 338.3 kB and 338.4 kB gzipped, and the Office's chunk at 231.3 kB and 231.2 kB, within its budget of 241.5 kB. The change moves rules rather than adding code: `isThreadSeated` loses a condition, `isRestingPose` joins client-core, and the sim gains the Lounge's doze and keeps a held armchair.
- **`pnpm test:desktop`**, on the reference machine on 2026-10-17, passes except `workspace-lifecycle.test.ts`. That test fails at a step that checks only the controller's and the runners' workspaces, which this change does not touch; it was not run on `main` to compare ([#491](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/491)). `intake.test.ts` failed once and passed on the next run.

### What the assistant costs

*(Added 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448); [ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md).)* The plan for [the assistant](#design-system), measured by slices 20 to 22 and recorded in [Measured](#measured):

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the sidebar's Assistants section, the Waiting on you row for an assistant, and the `/assistants/$id` route stub. The Conversation and its drawer are one chunk, loaded the first time a Conversation opens | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| Live topics | the shell adds `assistant`, because the sidebar lists assistants. A `session` nudge already reaches the shell; ~~with it, the shell reads the assistants' newest sessions again, as it reads the thread list again. A nudge cannot tell the shell which assistant a new session belongs to, so it cannot read less.~~ *(amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453): it reads an assistant's newest session again only when the nudge names a session of that assistant's conversation, or names none. A nudge about a thread or a workflow run's session reads no assistant's session, see [The assistant](#design-system))* `conversation` is subscribed only while a Conversation is open, and a nudge causes a read only when it names that Conversation. The open Conversation holds one session's `:stream` and `:tap`, as a thread does | the perf script, with a Conversation open while its assistant replies |
| Launch | the sidebar's reads grow by `assistant.query` and one `session.query` per assistant, ~~sent together with the thread list's~~ in two waves: `assistant.query` with the thread list's reads, then the sessions as soon as it answers, because each session is read by its assistant's main Conversation *(amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453))* | the perf script's launch steps, before and after, with 5 assistants |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged: the reply takes the thread's path | the Performance panel, once, while a reply streams |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with a Conversation of 2,000 messages open, scrolled to its top: only the pages read hold messages, and leaving the Conversation drops them | the perf script |
| Idle | the idle row, with a Conversation open and the drawer open: no timer, no polling and no animation. The drawer's sections and the header's pill compute nothing after they first draw; the heartbeat strip draws no "now" line | the perf script's idle sample |
| Opening the drawer | one transform transition of `--dur-3`, nothing after | the Performance panel, once |

### Images in prompts

*(Added 2026-10-08, [#465](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/465).)* The desktop composers attach images as the web app's do: behaviour, limits and wording are owned by [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Images in prompts. This section owns only how the desktop draws them and what that costs.

- **Where.** The thread's composer (`screens/thread/composer.tsx`), the new thread's composer (`screens/new-thread/draft-composer.tsx`) and their shared frame (`screens/session/composer-frame.tsx`) take paste, drop and the `+` attach's file picker. The user's bubble and the queued input rows show the images.
- **Drawn in Bureau's tokens.** The shelf, its 64×64 tiles, the 20px remove button with `aria-label="Remove <name>"`, the bottom strip, the dashed drop outline with "Drop images to attach", and the bubble's grid follow the shared measurements of spec 14, with Bureau's hairline, radius and focus-ring tokens in place of the web app's.
- **The lightbox** is a dialog in Bureau's `glass-dialog` style: the image at most 92vw × 86vh, the caption "<name> (i/n)", ← and → between images, Esc or a click on the scrim to close.
- **Only small thumbnails are kept.** A tile, a bubble image and a queued input's image each draw a thumbnail of their own size in device pixels. To build one, the image is decoded at full size with `createImageBitmap`, cropped to fill the box as `object-fit: cover` does, scaled down, and stored as a small WebP, which the `<img>` shows through an object URL; the full decode is closed straight away. Thumbnails are built one after another, so at most one image is decoded at full size at a time, and ten 4K screenshots on the shelf hold ten small WebPs, not ten full images. The query cache keeps a sent image's thumbnail, not its full bytes; only the lightbox reads and draws an image at full size, and an upload leaves its file in the cache, so the bubble of the message that sends it does not read the image back. *(Amended 2026-10-08, [#465](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/465): this said each image is decoded straight at its display size, which cannot crop it.)*
- **The strip holds one short word** on one line, "Uploading…", "Failed", "Expired" or "Unsupported", and the tile's tooltip the whole reason, as [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Images in prompts has it.
- **Object URLs are revoked** when a tile is removed, when its message is sent and when the component unmounts.

### What images in prompts cost

*(Added 2026-10-08, [#465](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/465); [ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md).)* The plan for the images build, measured by that build and recorded in [Measured](#measured):

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | the perf script's process count |
| Dependencies | none added | `package.json` |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows only by the shelf, the lightbox and client-core's attachment module | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| Memory | within the app's memory row with 10 pasted 4K screenshots on the shelf: thumbnails are decoded at display size, and object URLs are revoked when they leave the screen | the perf script, or a one-off run recorded in [Measured](#measured) |
| Idle | the idle row with images on the shelf: no timer and no polling | the perf script's idle sample |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged: a bubble's images load once, when the bubble first draws | the Performance panel, once |
| Uploads | at most three at a time, each on its own request; nothing runs after they answer | the Network panel, once |

### What thread copies cost

*(Added 2026-10-19, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796); [ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md).)* The plan for [thread copies](#thread-copies), stated before the build and measured by it, and recorded in [Measured](#measured):

| Cost | Expected | Measured by |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added: IndexedDB runs inside Chromium's existing processes | the perf script's process count |
| Memory | the same as the same thread read from the network. A copy is read only when a thread opens and is handed to the query cache, which already holds the thread's transcript while it is open. ~~Nothing else stays in memory.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796).)* Opening from a copy also puts every subagent transcript the copy holds into the query cache, which a network open would not read. The cache keeps them for up to 5 minutes after the page closes. | the perf script's renderer footprint with a thread of about 1 MB opened from its copy and from the network |
| Work while idle | none: no timer, no polling and no subscription of its own | the perf script's idle sample |
| Work per streamed token, and per stored row | none: a copy is saved only when the page closes and when a turn ends | the Performance panel, once, while a reply streams |
| Time to read a copy back | about 11 ms for a 3.1 MB transcript: 9 ms to read it as objects and 2 ms for the schema check. Benchmarked in Chromium with real transcripts, 5 rounds, medians | the same benchmark, run again on the built store, on the reference machine |
| The cost of a save | about 18 ms in all and a longest main-thread block of 13 ms, for the same 3.1 MB transcript, run when the renderer is idle. No long task (over 50 ms). *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796).)* An idle save can still run while another thread streams, because `requestIdleCallback` runs it after a 1 s timeout. Measured longest block, on Linux in Chrome 153: 16 ms at 3 MB and 41 ms at a 9.5 MB copy, both below the 50 ms long-task line | the same benchmark; a Chromium trace of a page closing and of a turn ending |
| Disk | about 0.38 times the transcript's JSON size, because Chromium compresses IndexedDB values: 2.77 MiB for four transcripts of 7.3 MB of JSON together. For 4 projects of 25 threads of about 1 MB each, about 36 MB. *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796).)* The bound is per project (25 copies), not in total: the copies of a project the user stops opening are never evicted. When the disk quota is exceeded, a save fails and is logged, and the thread opens from the network | the size of the IndexedDB folder in the user data directory after the reference fleet's threads are opened |
| Bundle bytes | a few kB gzipped: the store, the format and schema check, and the merge of the newer rows. Effect Schema was already in the first screen. Measured: +2.7 kB ([Measured](#measured)) | `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, before and after |
| Opening a thread | from a copy, close to a cached thread plus the copy's read. A cached 2.4 MB thread opens in about 42 ms. Read from the network over the Mac's model, it took 605 to 619 ms before [#791](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/791)'s compression and 266 to 269 ms with it | the same harness as #791, from the click to the first frame of the transcript, from a copy and from the network |

The values are recorded in [Measured](#measured). Every expectation held.

## Performance

*(Amended 2026-10-07, [#459](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/459).)* **Workspace lifecycle performance gates this change.** Before UI implementation, the expected cost is zero additional persistent processes, zero Git/inspection work while idle and zero Git/inspection work per streamed token. Inspection is bounded and coalesced per workspace on decision-menu open, explicit refresh, completed turns/exit and start/resume. Measure comparable before/after fixtures with the existing desktop scripts: total/per-process memory, idle CPU/wakeups, streaming work and bundle-byte delta. Record actual readings in this section when implemented. A new budget miss requires correction before #459 acceptance; previously accepted unrelated misses stay identified separately. The historical first-milestone guide below does not waive this issue's gate.

**Workspace lifecycle, issue #459**, measured 2026-10-07 against `cbb9719a` with the unchanged `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` fixture: two 40-Thread launches, Profile, 500 Threads, a 501-row transcript, streaming and subagents. Both builds use the compiled controller and disposable Homes. The final run used `caffeinate -di` only for the measurement's lifetime after macOS idle sleep invalidated an earlier attempt.

Footprint readings in MB and launch in ms. Each cell is Before / After, using the same fixture and sampling method:

| Fixture | Browser | GPU | Network | Renderer | Summed | Window shown |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 40 threads, run 1 | 51.3 / 53.4 | 180.5 / 590.1 | 7.9 / 7.7 | 48.5 / 48.3 | 288.2 / 699.5 | 352 / 396 |
| 40 threads, run 2 | 51.6 / 53.3 | 584.1 / 591.5 | 7.8 / 7.9 | 49.6 / 48.7 | 693.0 / 701.5 | 354 / 376 |
| 40 threads, Settings › Profile open | 51.8 / 53.7 | 106.8 / 112.0 | 7.7 / 7.8 | 54.9 / 55.0 | 221.2 / 228.4 | 362 / 377 |
| 500 threads, run 1 | 51.3 / 52.9 | 585.6 / 590.2 | 7.8 / 7.8 | 55.7 / 56.0 | 700.5 / 706.8 | 366 / 389 |
| 500 threads, a thread of 501 transcript rows open | 52.4 / 53.9 | 254.2 / 275.7 | 8.0 / 8.1 | 67.4 / 67.9 | 382.1 / 405.6 | 408 / 417 |

- The process count remains four. No persistent process, idle inspection or per-token Git work is added. Source-menu opening, explicit Refresh and session/turn completion initiate bounded, coalesced observations; the workspace live topic replaces polling. The source/renderer regression tests exercise these boundaries.
- Hidden renderer CPU is 0.0% with one wakeup/s in all five ordinary launches. Visible renderer CPU ranges from 0.7% to 1.3%; wakeups are one or five/s in both builds. GPU wakeups are zero or approximately 64/s in both builds. The age clock retains zero hidden fires and the expected visible count.
- At 500 Threads, renderer main-thread work per nudge is 13.7 / 14.6 ms for the list and 11.6 / 10.5 ms with the transcript open, within the 16 ms budget. Both builds perform 20 Thread-list reads for 20 nudges.
- The longest streaming task is 15.1 / 13.5 ms, with zero tasks over 50 ms. Paragraph writes are 1,173 in 2,435 frames / 1,191 in 2,437 frames, below one write per frame.
- With 20 subagents, renderer footprint is 69.6 / 68.1 MB with the pane closed and 86.0 / 84.5 MB open. Both builds use four processes and stop running-row work while hidden.
- Main's startup file is 157.5 / 158.5 kB, below 160 kB. The first screen is 313.6 / 315.5 kB gzip, an increase of 1.9 kB. The pre-existing 250 kB renderer guide overage remains separate from this change.
- Summed footprint already exceeds 220 MB in every baseline fixture, with large GPU variation; the baseline range is 221.2-700.5 MB and the final range is 228.4-706.8 MB. Visible GPU/renderer wakeup guide misses also occur in the baseline. These measurements identify the existing guide misses; they do not attribute variable GPU footprint to this feature. Renderer footprint stays below 100 MB throughout, and the measured launch, transcript, nudge and streaming budgets pass.

**The budgets guide the first milestone; they do not gate it.** *(Amended 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* Slices 1 to 4 were each measured against the budgets before they merged, and a slice that missed one did not merge. From slice 5 on, the milestone's functionality comes first, and performance passes follow it:

- **A slice is not measured against the budgets,** and a reading over one does not stop it. The perf script and the size checks in `pnpm build:desktop` report such a reading and pass.
- **The [rules](#rules) still apply to every slice.** They say how the app is built, and they cost little when followed from the start and much when added later.
- **A performance pass** measures the app, records the measurement in [Measured](#measured), and brings the app within the budgets.
- **Budgets:**
  - Raising a budget is a deliberate change, recorded here with its reason.
  - A budget is lowered once measurements show room to spare.

*(Amended 2026-10-03, [Office v1 in the desktop app (#332)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* **The Office's budgets gate the Office.** A change to the Office that misses one of [its budgets](#the-offices-budgets) does not merge. A 3D view that draws frames all the time is the one part of the app that can spin a fan and drain a battery, so its cost is held from its first merge, not by a later pass. The rest of the app keeps the rule above.

### Baseline

Measured 2026-09-29 with Electron 44.4.5 (Chromium 152) on macOS 15.8, an M4 Max with 64 GB. The page was an empty, hidden window served from a secure custom scheme:

| Measure | Value |
|---|---|
| Window ready to show, from process spawn | about 300 ms warm, 663 ms cold |
| Processes | 4: browser, GPU, network utility, renderer |
| Working set, summed across processes | about 308 MB: browser 136, GPU 56, utility 40, renderer 76. Shared pages are counted in each process. |
| CPU while idle | about 0% |
| Wakeups per second while idle | browser 1-3, GPU 6-12, renderer 0-2 |

Animation and glass were measured on a visible window on the same machine, counting wakeups per second:

| Page | GPU | Renderer |
|---|---|---|
| A still page | 6 | 0 |
| 40 idle faces, blinking as the Bureau pages draw them | 101 | 29-32 |
| 40 faces, 5 of them in the working pose | 241 | 64 |
| Text streaming at 30 updates a second, composer glass at level 0.4 | 261 | 59 |
| The same, with glass at 0 | 258 | 56 |

The physical footprint of an empty app, measured later with a visible 1440 by 900 window on the same machine's built-in display, is 119 MB summed: browser 40, GPU 54, network utility 7, renderer 18. That is the least any window of this app can cost. The same app with one focused text field read 124 and 139 MB: the extra is in the GPU process, up to 20 MB, about one window-sized frame buffer (2880 by 1800 pixels at 4 bytes each). The field's blinking caret keeps frames coming, and Chromium holds a third frame buffer while frames come.

What the numbers show:

- **Idle faces are not free.** Bureau starts each face's blink at a different time, so with 40 faces on screen one of them is almost always blinking. That keeps the GPU process awake about 16 times as often as a still page, and wakes the renderer about 30 times a second while nothing happens.
- **Glass costs almost nothing on this machine.** Composer glass while streaming measured within noise of no glass. *(Amended 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* That holds over a page that changes little. Over the Office's canvas, which changes with every frame, glass costs much more (see [Rules](#rules), rule 5).

### Budgets

The reference setup:

- the machine above
- the packaged app
- a controller on loopback
- a thread with 500 transcript rows open

These are starting budgets. The first performance pass measures the real thread screen, and each budget then comes down to the measurement plus 10%.

| Budget | Limit |
|---|---|
| Launch | The window shows within 500 ms of spawn (warm), and ~~the last open thread's transcript paints~~ the last screen's content paints within 800 ms *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394): the app reopens the last screen, which may be Intake, so the budget is measured with Intake reopening too.)* |
| Processes | The four of the baseline. No hidden windows, and no workers unless a slice justifies one. *(Amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* [The first run](#the-first-run) starts short-lived commands: the `hercule` binary, the login shell once and `git` once per folder. Each exits or is stopped before the step that started it ends, and none stays running. The controller and runner it starts are Hercule's own processes, under the Service Unit, not the app's |
| Memory | Summed physical footprint at most 220 MB, and the renderer at most 100 MB *(amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): with a text field focused and with none, because a focused field costs the GPU process 400 MB more while the glass blur is on screen; see [Measured](#measured))* |
| Idle, window visible, no thread working *(amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): and no text field focused)* | Renderer: no wakeups from the app except the live connection's 30-second keepalive *(amended 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): and the change of a time label on screen, which rule 4 allows)*. GPU: at most 12 wakeups a second, the still-page level |
| Idle, window visible, a text field focused | *(Added 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* No wakeups from the app beyond what a focused field costs an empty Electron window: at most 63 a second for the GPU and 4 for the renderer on the reference machine. The field's blinking caret keeps Chromium drawing frames, about 60 a second, however still the rest of the page is |
| Idle, window hidden or minimized | Renderer: no wakeups from the app *(amended 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): except the live connection's 30-second keepalive, because rule 3 keeps the `session` topic subscribed while hidden)* |
| Streaming | No task on the renderer's main thread longer than 50 ms while a turn streams at full speed. The paragraph being written is painted at most once per frame |
| The thread list's live updates | *(Added 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* At most 16 ms of the renderer's main thread for each `session` nudge, with 500 threads in the list: one frame at 60 Hz. Past it, the list stops reading every thread again on a nudge and updates only the threads the nudge names, and [./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Live model is amended in the same change |
| Renderer JavaScript | The JavaScript the first thread screen needs is at most 250 kB gzipped (the web app's budget), checked in CI like `scripts/check-bundle-budget.ts`. *(Amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* The first run and its room are one chunk, loaded only on a first run, and are not on the check's list of first-screen routes. The first screen grows only by the bridge calls the first run adds, and by New thread's Log in button, the one part of the first run the app keeps on the draft itself *(amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313))*. The project picker, the provider login and New project dialogs, and the draft's starter threads and Intake note are each loaded the first time they show, so they are not part of it |
| Main's startup | Main loads only what the first window needs, and imports everything else when it is first used. Main's startup file is at most 160 kB minified, checked in CI. *(Amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* Main imports the modules that run the Hercule binary, git and the folder dialog, which the first run and the New project dialog use, the first time they are used, so those modules add nothing to the startup file. The first run's IPC channels, their handlers and its progress in the settings file stay in the startup file, because main checks every IPC call against its channel's schema from launch. The 160 kB counts the startup file and every chunk it imports statically, because the bundler can move code the startup file shares with a lazy module into a chunk of its own. Main's build puts everything main imports statically into `index.js`, so today that is one file |

#### The Office's budgets

*(Added 2026-10-03, [Office v1 in the desktop app (#332)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* The reference Office is the reference machine above, with the Office open in a 1440 × 900 window at 2x, the Bureau map, 16 colleagues on 3 runners, and its light on Theme.

| Budget | Limit |
|---|---|
| Frames | The colleagues' ambient life draws at most 30 frames a second, never 60 or 120. While the camera moves, and while the user drags or zooms, the Office draws at most 60, so a glide stays smooth |
| Window hidden, minimized or covered | No frames, and no wakeups from the Office |
| Standing still: on battery, or with Reduce motion | No frames while nothing happens, so the idle row above applies. A thread whose pose changes walks where its new pose takes it, and frames stop once it arrives. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505).)* So does an assistant. The Secretariat's clock draws one frame a minute, so its hands keep the time |
| CPU, window visible, on mains power | Averaged over 30 seconds, with the camera at rest: the renderer at most 17.9% of one core, the GPU process at most 12.3% of one core: the first measurement's highest readings plus 10% ([Measured](#measured)) |
| Leaving the Office | Within 5 seconds, the app is back within the idle row: the Office releases its WebGL context and everything it built |
| The first screen's JavaScript | The Office and three.js are a chunk of their own, loaded the first time the Office opens. The first screen grows only by the sidebar's Office button, the Go menu item and the route |
| Memory with the Office open | Summed physical footprint at most 1,602 MB, and the renderer at most 181 MB: the first measurement's highest readings plus 10% ([Measured](#measured)) |
| The Office's chunk | At most 241.5 kB gzipped: the first measurement plus 10% |

- **The idle row and the Office.** The idle row allows the renderer no wakeups from the app and the GPU at most 12 a second. An Office drawing 30 frames a second wakes the renderer about 65 times a second, and the GPU process about 300 with the glass and about 430 without it, though its CPU then falls from 31% to 12% of one core. A living Office can never meet the idle row. The idle row is the limit for the app while nothing happens. On mains power, a visible Office is something happening: its colleagues live, and the CPU row above holds its cost instead. When the Office stands still, the idle row applies to it again.
- **An unfocused window keeps its 30 frames a second** while it is visible. A living Office on a second screen is what the Office is for. Whether the Office should stand still in more cases is decided after the first measurement.
- **Why the CPU limits are what they are.** The research for #332 ([#333](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/333)) measured the prototype with the reference fleet, in % of one core for the renderer and the GPU process:
  - as it was, with no cap: 88 frames a second, 45 and 85;
  - capped at 30 frames a second: 16 and 31;
  - capped, and without the glass: 14 and 12, so the glass costs the GPU process about 19;
  - capped, without the glass, and at the prototype's Medium quality (no ambient occlusion, a pixel ratio of 1.5, shadows drawn every frame): 9 to 10 and 7 to 8;
  - standing still: 0 and 0, with no wakeups;
  - hidden, and covered by another of the app's windows: 0 frames. A minimized window was not measured, because the research's harness could not minimize its window; Chromium's source treats it as hidden.

  The settings v1 ships (a pixel ratio of 2, shadows drawn once, no ambient occlusion), capped and without the glass, measured 9.1 and 7.7, the mean of two runs (from 7.6 to 10.6, and from 6.5 to 8.9).

  The limits started above the capped reading, at 20% and 35%, so v1 could ship with the cap alone. The Office's first measurement in the app brought them down to its highest readings plus 10%, as every budget comes down ([Measured](#measured)).
- **Memory and the chunk took their limits from the Office's first measurement** ([Measured](#measured)), because before it no measurement of the Office inside the packaged app existed, and a limit would have been a guess. The memory limits come from the highest of eight launches, so the swing between launches, about 7%, does not fail the next change. With the Office open, the app holds several times the app's own Memory row: almost all of it is the GPU process's, and bringing it down is [#370](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/370). The first run's room already reads above the app's memory budget ([#330](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/330)).

### Rules

These rules keep the budgets:

1. **The web app's six guardrails apply unchanged** ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md) §Performance guardrails):
   - the React Compiler
   - selector-based external stores, and never Context for changing data
   - every unbounded list virtualized
     - *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453).)* The sidebar's Assistants section is an exception: a plain list, at most 40% of the sidebar's height, that scrolls on its own. A user has a handful of assistants, so every row is drawn. Revisit this once people run many.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Settings › Permission profiles is another: the profile list, and the Used by list on a profile's page, are plain lists. A user keeps a handful of profiles, and each profile is carried by a handful of agents, so every row is drawn. Revisit this once people run many.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* [Workflows](#workflows) draws two things whole that are not lists: a graph, every one of its nodes, because its author bounds it; and the Runs tab's timeline, every run of its one day. The workflow list and the Runs tab's list are virtualized.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-15, [#669](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/669).)* [Discussions and Wiki](#discussions-and-wiki) draw three bounded lists whole: an open Discussion's Posts and its shared files, and a wiki page's History. The contract bounds each ([What Discussions and Wiki cost](#what-discussions-and-wiki-cost)). Their lists are virtualized.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732).)* [Settings › Skills](#settings) draws three lists whole, as plain lists: its skill list, at most 256 skills (`MAX_SKILLS`); a skill's files, at most 1,000 by the content rules; and a skill's History. The contract bounds the first two. History has one row per revision that has been current, and a user makes those one import at a time, so it stays short. Revisit this if a skill gathers many revisions.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#769](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/769).)* The composer's `$` and `/` skill lists are windowed lists with fixed 32 px rows. A thread offers up to 256 library skills plus its repository's, and an open list's cost grew with every row drawn (18 to 21 ms busy at 120 rows).
   - token deltas flushed at most once per animation frame
   - route-level code splitting with a CI bundle budget
   - TanStack Query for every HTTP read
2. **Nothing animates unless something is happening:**
   - *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394).)* Motion may tell the user that something happened. Such motion plays once, on `transform` and `opacity` of HTML elements, at most `--dur-3`, and only on elements on screen. It never loops or repeats. [Intake](#motion)'s arriving and leaving signals are the first.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* [Workflows' motion](#workflows-motion) is the second. A row the virtualizer mounts outside the scroll port plays no motion, because the virtualizer knows which rows are in view: a change to such a row shows still when it scrolls in. So this rule stays as written.
   - Faces are drawn still in their pose everywhere. Bureau's idle blink is left out of the first milestone, because of the measurement above. It comes back once research finds a way to draw it within the idle budget (see [Post-v1](#post-v1)).
   - Only one continuous animation is allowed: the working pose of the face of the message the open thread's agent is writing, while its turn runs. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#504](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/504): a face is drawn only beside text, so no face animates while the thread shows only a status line or a work divider.)* *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448).)* On an assistant's Conversation, it is the face of the newest reply, while its turn runs. The sidebar and every other list show still poses and still marks.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* The one other is a spinner, and only while Hercule starts or a login waits in [the first run](#the-first-run) or the draft's Log in. Each is a wait the user started, and each ends: the start after at most 90 seconds for the command and 30 for the answer, a login when it ends or its code expires.
     - The first run's room moves only when a step finishes: the camera moves one layer's `transform`, and new pieces settle with `transform` and `opacity`, each over `--dur-3`, by the rules below.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* The Office's colleagues live: they walk, type, sip tea and sleep, at most 30 frames a second, while the Office is on screen and does not stand still ([The Office's budgets](#the-offices-budgets)). The Office draws them into one WebGL canvas, and the rules below for CSS animations do not apply inside it. The Office's panels and its drawer follow those rules.
   - Animations change only `transform` and `opacity`, and only of an HTML element. Chromium runs such an animation on the compositor thread alone. When the animated element is an SVG element, even an outer `<svg>`, the renderer's main thread also runs style, layout and paint on every frame: 120 times a second on a 120 Hz display. So the working pose's paws are each drawn in an `<svg>` of their own, inside a `<span>` that moves.
   - Transitions answer a user action, last at most `--dur-3`, and change ~~only paint properties~~ paint properties: color, background, border-color, box-shadow, opacity~~ and~~, transform *(amended 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626))* and `content-visibility`. A `content-visibility` transition does not interpolate: it keeps the element shown until it ends, which lets an element turn hidden only when a slide ends. A transition of a layout property, such as `width`, `padding` or `grid-template-rows`, runs layout on every frame. Bureau's composer transitions some of these; slice 6 ports the composer without them, and uses a transform if its growth animates.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* **A layout property may transition when a measurement shows it is cheap.** The perf script records the transition's frames over content at the size the [cost table](#what-intake-costs) names, and the change records the numbers in [Measured](#measured). The transition passes when no frame is dropped and no frame spends more than 2 ms of the renderer's main thread on style and layout. A paint property needs no measurement. The rule used to forbid layout properties by name because layout on every frame can be expensive, but the cost is the work in each frame, not the property: Intake's split, which changes the list's width, lays out 0.1 to 1.1 ms a frame. What keeps such a transition cheap is content that does not reflow, such as rows of a fixed height whose lines are cut with an ellipsis; text that wraps measures again on every frame.
     - *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394).)* [The split](../design-language.md#the-split) is ~~the one exception~~ the first layout transition the rule above allows *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)*. Opening or closing its side pane may animate its sized column's width, for at most `--dur-3`, and only while that column's rows are ~~one line~~ lines of a fixed count, each cut with an ellipsis *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)*. Such rows do not wrap, so a frame of the transition lays out the column without measuring text again. Intake is the first to use it ([The split and its minimums](#the-split-and-its-minimums)), and [What Intake costs](#what-intake-costs) measures it.
     - *(Added 2026-10-13, [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626).)* The sidebar's slide is the second layout transition the rule allows. Hiding or showing the sidebar animates the width of the box that clips it, for at most `--dur-3`. The sidebar keeps its own width inside the box, so a frame lays out the box and the main pane, never the sidebar's rows. [The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout) names what the perf script measures.
     - *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394).)* A one-shot arrival the user's own action caused may chain steps, each within `--dur-3`, at most 800ms in all. Intake's inbox zero is the first ([Motion](#motion)). *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* A wait before the arrival, while the layout around it settles, does not count toward the 800ms, as long as nothing of the arrival draws during it. Inbox zero waits `--dur-3` for a closing pane.
   - A change of appearance snaps: the page switches in one frame, with no transition, as the window's native frame does.
   - Reduce motion turns every animation off. *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* macOS's Reduce motion or the Appearance page's. *(Amended 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* With Reduce motion, the Office stands still as it does on battery, and its camera moves in one step where it would glide.
3. **Work stops when nobody is looking.** While the window is hidden or minimized:
   - The renderer drops the open thread's `session:<id>:tap` subscription, and the open subagent's `session:<id>:subagent:<subagentId>:tap` *(amended 2026-10-05, [#355](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/355))*, and the open Conversation's newest session's `:tap` *(amended 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448))*. Chromium stops animation frames in a hidden window, so buffered token deltas would otherwise pile up without being painted. ~~The `session:<id>:stream` rows keep the transcript current, and the tap resumes when the window is shown.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-19, [#783](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/783).)* The `session:<id>:stream` stays subscribed, so no row is missed, but the rows it delivers while the window is hidden are kept aside instead of being written to the transcript, because each write cost the hidden page its block building and a render ([Measured](#measured)). When the window is shown, they go into the transcript in one write, so the page renders once, and the tap resumes. The stream is not dropped like the tap: a new subscription replays the first 500 rows it missed in one message and each later row in a message of its own, so after a long hide the page would take hundreds of rows one at a time, and could reach the controller's cap of 1,000 queued messages per subscription. *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796).)* While a read of the rows runs, the page does not follow the stream, hidden or not (see [Thread copies](#thread-copies)), so nothing is kept aside then. When the read ends, the stream is subscribed from the newest row held, counting the rows kept aside while they are still valid, so none of them is replayed. A read that wrote different rows has already dropped them.
   - The `session` topic stays subscribed, because the dock badge and notifications depend on it. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* So does the `signal` topic, because the Hercule segment's count and Now's notifications depend on it ([Intake](#what-the-screen-reads)).
   - `backgroundThrottling` stays on.
   - *(Amended 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* So does the `task` topic, because the Hercule segment's count takes in Tracking's waiting rows ([Tracking](#tracking)). The `run` topic does not: nobody sees the count while the window is hidden, so the shell drops it with the Workflows pages' `workflow` topic (next) and reads the runs again when the window shows.
   - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* The Workflows pages drop the `workflow` topic, and the shell drops the `run` topic, and both are read again when the window is shown ([What the pages read](#what-the-pages-read)).
   - *(Amended 2026-10-15, [#669](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/669).)* The Discussions and Wiki screens drop the `discussion` and `wikiPage` topics, and read them again when the window is shown ([./19-agent-coordination.md](./19-agent-coordination.md) section 8.5).
   - *(Added 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* The Office draws its frames from `requestAnimationFrame`, which Chromium stops in a hidden, minimized or covered window. The colleagues' timers count only the time the window is shown, so they pause with the frames, and the first frame after the window is shown again advances the colleagues by one frame, not by the time it was hidden. While the Office stands still, it runs no timer that repeats~~.~~ *(amended 2026-10-09, [#505](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/505))*, except the Secretariat's clock, which moves its hands once a minute. The clock's timer counts only the time the window is shown, as the colleagues' timers do, so a hidden window gets no wakeups from it, and the hands jump to the time when the window shows again. An Office with no assistants has no clock, so it runs no clock timer either.
4. **No polling, and no timers while idle:**
   - Every change reaches the app through a live topic. *(Amended 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): projects, workspaces and resources have no live topic yet. The app reads them again when the thread list names one it does not know, when a thread in a workspace being set up changes, and after a reconnect, so a rename made elsewhere shows at the next of these. [#279](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/279) adds the topics.)* *(Amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* The shell also subscribes to the `connection` topic, so a GitHub Connection made in the web app while the desktop app runs reaches the New project form and the starters' line without a reload. *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448).)* It also subscribes to the `assistant` topic, so the sidebar's Assistants section follows an assistant made, renamed or deleted elsewhere.
   - A label that counts time (such as `Worked for 31s`, or a Request's `10m`) runs one timer, only while the label is on screen and the window is visible.
     - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* That one timer is the age clock (`app/age-clock.ts`), shared by every such label. [Workflows](#workflows) adds no timer of its own: its ages, durations, the timeline's now line and a live run's bar all read the age clock.
   - *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* Intake runs one timer, set for the soonest snooze's `until`, because a snooze ends with no push: nothing changes on the controller when its time comes.
