- *(Amended 2026-10-07, [#440](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/440).)* **First paint no longer grows.** `main` now names the asking subagent on the Request, in the controller ([#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453)), so the shell no longer reads the subagent, and the 2.1 kB below left first paint with it. The thread's route grows by 8.4 kB, as the row above shows. The bullet below is the measurement before that merge.
- ~~**The first screen grows by more than the spawn line and the tally, a miss.**~~ The spawn line and the tally are not at first paint at all: they are in the thread's route chunk. First paint still grows by 1.4 kB. It grew by 3.4 kB until `nameSubagent` moved into its own module (`subagents/name.ts`) and the pills the new-thread screen's header shares moved out of `thread-header.tsx` into `header-pills.tsx`. The 1.4 kB is three things; the sizes are each source gzipped on its own, so they are approximate and do not add up exactly:
  - naming the asking subagent in a notification, about 2.1 kB: `useQueries` and its observer from TanStack Query (about 1.5 kB), the shell's read of each asking subagent, and `waiting-threads` with `nameSubagent`;
  - the routes' declarations and loaders, about 0.6 kB: the subagent route, the `$sessionId` route split into a layout and its index, and the route tree's entries, less the single `$sessionId` route they replace;
  - the query and live wiring, about 0.3 kB: the subagent queries in `queries.ts`, the `subagent` live topic in `live.ts`, `live/keys.ts` and the contract.

  The header pays some of it back: first paint now holds only the shared pills (1.3 kB) in place of the whole thread header (2.0 kB), which moved to the thread's route with the side pane's toggle. The thread's route grows by 8.0 kB: the spawn line, the tally, the Request pager and the thread header (they ship in the thread page's chunk, `agent-page`), the side pane's toggle and its state, and four small chunks the open thread loads with it: the subagents' descriptions and the Stop hook, the drafts, the route's component and its not-found screen.
- **A subagent's page costs no more per streamed token than a thread's page.** A first trace read 25% more on the subagent's page, but it compared unlike pages: the subagent's page with the side pane open and four subagents running, against another thread's page with the pane closed and nothing running. The row above is measured again with the same token stream on both pages:
  - One thread per run, with 20 subagents. The thread's own agent streams twice and one subagent streams twice, each for 10 s at a word every 2 ms, while the other agent waits on a command. Each stream is traced on its own page, once with the side pane closed and once open.
  - Each trace starts 1 s after the first token is on screen and lasts 7 s, so it ends before the stream does and holds no page load.
  - Four runs, each on a new thread and a new launch of the test package. Half the runs trace the thread's page first, half the subagent's page.
  - In each run, the subagent's page took 2% more to 7% less than the thread's page with the pane closed (median 4% less), and 2% to 7% less with the pane open (median 3% less). The two transcripts differ: the thread's holds the spawn lines and the composer, about 1,370 layout objects, and the subagent's the brief card and the status card, about 860.
- **Opening the side pane costs about 12% more renderer work per streamed token, on either page** (10% to 16% across the runs). The largest part, about a third, is pre-paint, which grows by about 80% (90 to 161 ms on the thread's page), and each layout then covers about 2,950 objects in place of 1,370; the pane itself is repainted once a frame, from cache, in about 0.02 ms. Its cause within the pane is not looked for here. It stays well inside the streaming budget.
- **The summed footprint is over the memory row, and the cause is not found.** The GPU process holds 692 to 722 MB. That is not the composer's blinking caret: the trace with four subagents running shows one painted frame a second, from the ticking durations. The renderer stays inside its 100 MB, and the side pane open on 20 subagents adds 20.2 MB to it. The new-thread screen in the same run reads 689 to 696 MB summed in both builds, so the GPU process is as large before this change as after it: the summed miss predates #440, and this change does not cause it.
- With four subagents running and the window visible, the renderer wakes 5 times a second and the GPU process 39 times; hidden, 0 each.
- The 4 layouts after the drag match the idle rate with durations ticking, 1.2 a second, so the release adds none of its own.
- The Office's drawer was not measured: the perf script covers the thread's page, a subagent's page and Settings.
- The one-minute load average was 5.5 to 18.2 at the Before launches and 5.7 to 16.4 at the After launches, and 4.1 to 5.3 during the streaming traces, which waited for it to fall to about 4 before each run. Other worktrees' test runs were on the machine.

**The assistant, slice 21: The Conversation** *(added 2026-10-07, [#454](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/454))* shipped without being measured. Its rows in [What the assistant costs](#what-the-assistant-costs) stay open: live topics with a Conversation open while its assistant replies, work per streamed token while a reply streams, memory with a Conversation of 2,000 messages open, and idle with a Conversation open. They are measured in a later performance pass, together with slice 22's rows. The budgets guide the first milestone and do not gate it ([Performance](#performance)), so the slice does not wait for them.

**Settings › Assistants,** measured 2026-10-07 on the reference machine with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks and one run of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts`, on the working tree of branch `t3/desktop-settings-414-415`, for [#414](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/414) and [#415](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/415). The perf script now opens Settings › Assistants as well as Settings › Profile, so the row with Profile open is from the same run. The section adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic of its own: its loader reads the profiles and the settings each time it opens, and the assistants and the providers only when the cache does not hold them yet, and the heartbeat timeline's "now" line moves only when the section is opened again.

| Memory, 40 threads | summed 220 MB, renderer 100 MB | 226.3 MB summed: browser 54.0, GPU 110.3, network utility 7.8, renderer 54.2 | **267.3 MB** summed: browser 54.1, GPU 147.2, network utility 7.7, renderer 58.2 |
| Idle, window visible | the idle row | renderer 0.9% of a core and 1 wakeup a second, GPU process 0% and 0; hidden, renderer 0 | renderer 0.4% of a core and 1 wakeup a second, GPU process 0% and 0; hidden, renderer 1 a second |
| Launch, spawn to window shown | 500 ms | 379 ms | 363 ms; Settings is not on the launch path |

- **Summed memory is over its budget with either section open,** by 6 MB with Profile and by 47 MB with Assistants. The renderer holds 4 MB more with Assistants open, for the section's reads and its longer page. The GPU process holds 37 MB more, and the cause is not found: the GPU process wakes 0 times a second with the section open, so no caret or animation is drawing. The budget is a guide during the first milestone ([Performance](#performance)), so the slice does not stop here.
- **Accepted** (decided 2026-10-07, [#414](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/414)/[#415](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/415)): the section ships drawn as the book draws it, and the summed footprint stays over its budget while it is open. The budget is not raised. A later performance pass finds what the GPU process holds, together with the other GPU overages recorded here ([#301](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/301), [#330](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/330)).
- **One run, not the median of three.** The numbers move by a few MB between runs; the slice 20 rows above read 220.4 to 221.9 MB summed for Profile across three runs.
- **The one-minute load average was 2.1** at the Profile launch and **4.2** at the Assistants launch, from other programs on the machine.

**Images in prompts,** measured 2026-10-08 on the reference machine with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks and one run of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf-images.ts`, on the working tree of branch `feat/465-images-in-prompts`, for [#465](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/465). The script opens a thread on the 40-thread fixture, attaches ten generated 3840 × 2160 PNG screenshots (4.0 MB of PNG in all) through the composer's file picker, and reads the footprint after each step, each time after a garbage collection and 10 s of settling. The plain `<img>` comparison runs last, because Chromium keeps its full decodes after the URLs are revoked, which would inflate every reading after it.

| Step | Summed | Browser | GPU | Renderer | Processes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thread open, 10 files held, nothing drawn | 300.2 | 54.6 | 194.0 | 43.6 | 4 |
| 10 on the shelf, WebP thumbnails | 307.4 | 53.9 | 192.5 | 52.7 | 4 |
| All 10 removed | 300.7 | 48.3 | 191.4 | 52.8 | 4 |
| Sent: 10 in the bubble, 210 × 158 | **825.2** | 53.5 | **702.8** | 60.6 | 4 |
| 10 plain `<img>` on object URLs, 64 × 64 | **1,400.4** | 51.8 | 992.3 | **348.6** | 4 |
| Plain images removed, URLs revoked | 1,113.6 | 51.5 | 995.1 | 59.4 | 4 |

| Measure | Budget | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4 |
| Dependencies | none added | none |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | grows only by the shelf, the lightbox and client-core's attachment module | 322.2 kB, up 6.3 kB from 315.9 kB; this includes the contract's and client-core's attachment code. The lightbox is a chunk of its own, 0.8 kB, loaded the first time an image is previewed. Main's startup file is 159.0 kB, inside its 160 kB |
| Idle, 10 on the shelf, window visible | the idle row | renderer 0.6% of a core and 1 wakeup a second, GPU process 0% and 0 |

- **The thumbnails stay.** Ten images on the shelf cost the renderer 9 MB as WebP thumbnails, and about 290 MB as plain 64 × 64 `<img>` tiles on the files' object URLs, which decode each image at full size. The plain tiles also grow the GPU process by about 290 MB, and it does not give that back when the tiles are removed and their URLs revoked. The difference is not small, so the simpler plain approach is not used.
- **Sending ten images grows the GPU process by about 510 MB, and the cause is not found.** The renderer stays at 61 MB. The bubble already draws 210 × 158 thumbnails at the device pixel ratio, not the full images, in this run. Building the thumbnails is not the cause: a one-off variant of `perf-images.ts`, not kept, built ten thumbnails without drawing or sending them, in three ways, and the GPU process stayed at 184 MB after each: from a full decode at 128 × 128, from a full decode at 420 × 316, and straight from the file at 420 × 316. Whether drawing the bubble's thumbnails, or something else in the send, holds the memory is still open, and a text-only send, as a control, was not measured. The budget is a guide during the first milestone ([Performance](#performance)), so the build does not stop here; the later performance pass that looks at the GPU process ([#301](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/301), [#330](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/330)) takes this one too.
- **Summed memory is over the 220 MB row before any image is attached,** at 300 MB, as the other entries on the thread page record.
- **Work per streamed token and the uploads' network use were not measured.** A bubble's images are read once, when the bubble first draws, and at most three uploads run at a time.
- **One run, not the median of three.** The one-minute load average was 5.6 at spawn and 5.5 at the end, from other worktrees' runs on the machine.

**Settings › Appearance, slice 12,** measured 2026-10-08 on the reference machine with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, five runs of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` before the change and one after (its five launches on the new-thread screen), and one Chromium trace each of a theme change and a Glass drag in the packaged app, on the working tree of branch `feat/411-appearance-themes-glass`, for [#411](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/411). The page adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic.

| Measure | Budget | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4 |
| Launch, spawn to first paint and to window shown | `appearance.read` adds one synchronous message, answered from memory | before: first paint 236 to 288 ms, shown 346 to 420 ms; after: first paint 216 to 270 ms, shown 343 to 368 ms. The message is lost in the spread between launches |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | grows only by the foot button's handler, the route stub and `theme-init.js`'s reading of the Appearance | 324.2 kB, up 1.2 kB from 323.0 kB; `theme-init.js` is 1.5 kB, up from 0.8 kB. The first screen was over its 250 kB guide before this change |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 148.0 kB, down from 159.1 kB: `@hercule/protocol` now declares `"sideEffects": false` and keeps the identity port in a module of its own, so main no longer takes in the whole protocol through the contract (spec 14) |

- **The theme change restyles twice in its frame,** because `theme-init.js` reads a computed style while transitions are off, so no control fades. The second pass costs 1.6 ms, and a snap without it would start every transition on the page.
- **A few small style passes follow a theme change,** 0.2 ms in all, over the next few frames. Their cause was not traced; they end on their own.
- **Memory was not measured with the Appearance page open.** The perf script opens Profile and Assistants, and the Appearance page reads nothing from the controller.
- **The one-minute load average was 3.1 to 9.5** at the After launches, from other worktrees' runs on the machine.

**The flat sidebar,** measured 2026-10-08 on the reference machine with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on `main` at d1c8e7a4 (Before) and on the working tree of branch `t3/desktop-sidebar-flat-threads` (After), for [#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473). The hover card is one popover element for the whole list, and its 400ms delay is the only timer, running only while the pointer rests on a thread row. Typing in a composer does not draw the sidebar again: the set of threads with unsent work keeps its identity until a thread gains or loses unsent work. A thread row grows from 35px to 59px for its third line and the space around its text, so the virtualized list mounts fewer rows for the same height. The end-to-end suite's check that a project of 500 threads mounts about as many rows as one of 40 still passes.

**Settings › Connections,** measured 2026-10-12 for [#416](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/416). **Work stretch rows,** for [#479](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/479). The rows add no process, no read, no live topic and no timer. Everything comes from the transcript the thread already holds.

| Processes | none added | none added |
| Reads and live topics | none added | none added: a step's result text is already in the cache |
| Memory | unchanged | a step's result content is held by reference to the transcript row it came from, never copied; its text is in the page only while that step is open; which rows are open is a set of keys in the transcript's state, never saved |
| Work while idle | none | none: the time on hover is CSS, with no timer |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged | unchanged: a token streams in the tail and rebuilds nothing. The transcript's blocks are rebuilt when its rows change, as before, and the `item.completed` handler in that pass only stores a reference to the result content. A stretch's rows, and its steps' result text, are read only while the stretch is open |

Bundle: the first screen is 327.5 kB gzipped before (14 chunks) and 331.6 kB after (20 chunks), 4.1 kB more: the row code, its styles, and the step icons the app did not draw before, which split into chunks of their own. Measured with `pnpm build:desktop`'s budget check on the change's base and on the change.

The tiles, the thumbnails and the lazy lightbox were already in the bundle for images in prompts |

**The sender of a message,** measured 2026-10-10 with `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop build`, the renderer's `check-bundle-budget` and `gzip -9` on the built files, on the working tree of branch `feat/511-agent-message-sender-clients`, for [#511](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/511). Before and After were built from the same tree, which already held client-core's side of #511; only the renderer's change lies between them. The change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic: each sender is one cached read, made by the thread's loader or when the sender first appears.

| Measure | Budget | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | grows only by the sender's read and the loader's prefetch | 329.3 kB, up 1.7 kB from 327.6 kB. The first-screen chunk is 255.9 kB, up 1.0 kB: the sender's read, `describeSender`, and `collectSenderSessionIds`, which finds the senders in one pass over the rows without grouping them into turns. The first screen was over its 250 kB guide before this change |
| The thread's chunks, gzipped | the chip and its case in the transcript and the queue | the transcript's chunk 53.0 kB, up 0.4 kB; the agent page's chunk 14.1 kB, up 0.3 kB |
| CSS, gzipped | a few rules | the transcript's CSS up 53 bytes, to 1,311 bytes; the messages' CSS up 59 bytes, to 904 bytes; the agent page's CSS up 117 bytes, to 1,254 bytes. The first screen's CSS is unchanged |
| Work while idle | none | none: the chip has no state, no timer and no animation |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged | unchanged: tokens are still painted into the paragraph being written, without a render. A message from an agent is a memoized component whose props stay the same from one stored row to the next, except a message sent with images, whose list of images is built again with each row |

- **A sender is read once and not again while the app holds it,** which is while a screen shows it and for five minutes after. Its key is outside the `session` prefix, so the pushes of a sender that is itself busy read nothing. The name comes from fields of the session that never change, so nothing is lost.
- **The owner's bubble and queued row are pixel-identical before and after,** in both themes at 1x and 2x: the specimen's screenshots compare byte for byte. The one change to the owner's rows is in a stack of them, by design: a row under another draws no line along its top, so two rows meet in one 1px line rather than two.
- **The review's fixes,** measured the same way against the renderer built from `76f4a1cd`: the chip's width left out of the bubble's, the linked chip's hover, the dark agent bubble mixed into the neutral bubble, the chip's edge on the bubble's, one outline for a stack of queued rows, and each row's and each message's markup written once. The first screen is 8 bytes larger, at 328.9 kB across its chunks. The transcript's chunk is 69 bytes larger and the agent page's chunk 444 bytes smaller, because the queued row's markup is written once. The transcript's CSS is 35 bytes larger and the messages' CSS 30 bytes larger; the agent page's CSS is 14 bytes smaller. The hover adds no work while idle: it is a CSS rule, with no handler.
- **The loader waits for senders at most one second,** measured the same way against the renderer built from `a8f51a4c`: the thread's loader waits for its senders' reads up to `OPTIONAL_READ_WAIT_MS` (named `OPTIONAL_READ_WAIT_MS` when this was measured), one second, and never longer, and a read that failed is not made again when a message mounts or the thread opens again. The first-screen chunk is 103 bytes larger, at 258.8 kB gzipped; the transcript's chunk and the agent page's chunk change by 2 to 4 bytes. The wait is one timer per thread open, cleared as soon as the reads settle, so it adds no work while idle; it removes work too: a sender whose read fails was read again each time one of its messages mounted, and is now read once.
- **The queued row keeps the book's items as its direct children,** measured the same way against the renderer built from `550bff43`: two wrapper spans fewer per queued row, and a grid track per item in their place. The agent page's CSS is 81 bytes larger, at 1,321 bytes, and its chunk 13 bytes smaller, at 13.8 kB; the first screen is unchanged. Every box of the owner's and the agent's rows, with and without images, buttons and notes, measures the same as before, except the text beside a linked chip, which stood 6px further right until the fix below, and the change adds no work while idle or per streamed token.
- **The second review's fixes,** measured against the renderer built from `1b6bf8ab`: a linked chip in a queued row keeps the 6px its hover light takes back, so the row's text starts where it did before the grid, and a sender's read that failed is not made again when the window is focused. The agent page's CSS grows by one rule and stays at 1.31 kB gzipped as Vite reports it; the first-screen chunk gains one query option and stays at 258.8 kB. Neither adds work while idle or per streamed token, and the focus change removes a read per focus for each failed sender.
- **The numbers above were measured before #479 and #478 merged into this branch,** on a base without the work stretch rows or the tool result images. After the merge, `pnpm build:desktop`'s budget check reads the first screen at 334.1 kB gzipped across 20 chunks, 1.8 kB over main's 332.3 kB, in line with the 1.7 kB measured above.

**The Hercule face,** measured 2026-10-10 on the reference machine for [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512), slice 24. Memory and idle come from one run of the perf script, in which the launch "40 threads, the Hercule face showing" opens on the new-thread screen like the plain 40-thread launches, then shows the Hercule face through View › Hercule before it reads. The load average was 2.2 to 12.2 while measuring, and another worktree's perf run was going at the same time. The bundle comes from `pnpm build:desktop`'s budget check on `a9fc51cf`, the change's base (Before), and on the change (After). For memory and idle, Before is the Threads face in the same run, from the plain 40-thread launches: a base run would differ from it by load, not by the face.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4 | 4: browser, GPU, network utility, renderer |
| Memory, 40 threads, new-thread screen | the face's rows only | renderer 49.3 and 51.5 MB, the Threads face in the same run | renderer **50.3 MB**, Hercule face; summed 671.5 MB against 693.7 and 694.7 MB |
| Idle, window visible | no timer, no polling, no animation | the Threads face in the same run: renderer 1.2 to 1.3% of a core and 5 wakeups a second; hidden, 1 | renderer **1.1%** and **5** wakeups a second; hidden, 1 |
| Reads | none | - | none added: the System section reads the Connections the shell's loader keeps cached for the life of the window. A switch to the Hercule face reads them again in the background only when a push marked them out of date while the Threads face showed, and never waits for that read |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | grows by the switch, the face's rows and the two View commands | 334.1 kB across 20 chunks; first-screen chunk 256.4 kB | **335.8 kB**, up 1.7 kB; first-screen chunk 258.3 kB, up 1.9 kB |
| The first screen's CSS, gzipped | a few rules | 9.99 kB | 10.06 kB |
| Main's startup file | the View menu | 148.1 kB | 148.5 kB |

- **The summed footprint is over its row on both faces, as before this change.** The new-thread screen focuses its composer, and the GPU process holds 563 to 587 MB and wakes 63 to 67 times a second for its caret, whichever face shows. That miss predates #512 and is already recorded above, where Settings' Profile reads 105.5 MB in the GPU process against 586 MB for this screen. The renderer's 5 wakeups a second while visible are over the 2 a second row for the same reason, on both faces: the Settings launches of the same run, which focus no field, read 1 a second.
- **A face switch keeps one list mounted.** Both faces draw into the same virtualized list, with other items: the Hercule face passes Waiting on you, with the System section's plain rows after it. So a switch mounts no second list, and a focused Waiting on you row, which both faces draw, keeps its focus.
- **The launches after "500 threads, run 1" were not measured.** Before the long-transcript launch, the perf script opens "Thread 500" from the sidebar, and that step timed out in two runs, one of them at load 2.0. The cause is not this change, which touches neither the sidebar's order nor its folding: with 500 threads the sidebar shows five rows a project and folds the rest under "120 more threads", and "Thread 500" is among the folded rows, so the script finds no row to click. [#538](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/538) fixes the step. The launches before it completed, and the script now reports each launch as soon as it is measured, so the failure no longer takes their readings with it.
- **The switch has no state outside the sidebar.** The face is not saved, and a launch on a screen that keeps the face shows the Threads face, as [The Hercule face](#the-hercule-face) requires.

**Launch on the last screen, slice 36,** measured 2026-10-10 on the reference machine with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks and one run of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` on each side, on `main` at a9fc51cf (Before) and on the working tree of branch `t3/launch-on-last-screen` (After), for [#535](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/535). The slice adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic. Each navigation writes one `localStorage` entry. Intake is not built yet, so reopening Intake is not measured.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Launch, spawn to window shown, new-thread screen | 500 ms | 346 to 379 ms over five launches | 371 to 399 ms over five launches |
| Launch steps, page start to first screen, new-thread screen | none new | 128 to 140 ms | 132 to 140 ms |
| Launch, spawn to the last thread's transcript painted, 501 rows | 800 ms | 417 ms | 414 ms |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | none new | 334.1 kB | 334.2 kB; the first-screen chunk is 256.5 kB, up 0.1 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 148.1 kB | 148.2 kB |

- **Launch time is unchanged.** The renderer's part of a launch, from the page's start to its first screen, is the same within 4 ms on both sides. The windows were shown 12 ms later in the median After, but the difference lies before the page starts, in main's Node start and the GPU and renderer processes, which this change does not touch. It is the spread between runs on a machine shared with other worktrees.
- **A launch with a saved controller reads the Appearance once more,** a synchronous message to main answered from memory, because Open on decides the first screen. `theme-init.js` reads it once already before the first paint. The second read is lost in the spread above, as the first one was ([Settings › Appearance, slice 12](#measured)).
- **The perf script was repaired to take these numbers.** It could no longer run on `main`: the scripted runner imported a value from the protocol package, which plain Node cannot load, and the script opened threads by their sidebar rows, which the flat sidebar ([#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473)) hides behind a section's "more" row. The script now stores the long thread as the last screen instead, and the subagent scenario opens "more" rows and scrolls the sidebar until its thread's row shows. The Before ran the same repaired script, storing the thread under the old `last-thread` key that build reads. Storing the thread is also what fixes the "Thread 500" timeout that [The Hercule face](#measured) entry above reports. The scripted runner's import was fixed on `main` in the meantime as well, the same way.

**Intake: the first signal on screen,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base 5d260fee (Before) and on this change (After), for [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524). Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB. The rows `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` measures come from one run of it on this change, in the second table below.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | none new | 336.1 kB | 340.0 kB across 21 chunks; the first-screen chunk is 262.5 kB |
| Intake's chunk, gzipped | none | - | 12.1 kB of JavaScript, 2.0 kB of CSS |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | not measured | 150.1 kB |

*(Re-measured 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* The After column first read 339.9 kB, 262.3 kB, 11.6 kB and 148.7 kB. Matching the Intake drawing added the plugin marks and the workflow's face on Hand to an agent, which grew the first two rows by 0.1 to 0.2 kB and Intake's chunk by 0.5 kB. In main, the same change adds only `https:` to the Content-Security-Policy: main's startup file is 153,692 bytes on the commit before it and 153,699 bytes after, 150.1 kB both times. The first 148.7 kB was not reproduced, and its cause is not known. The Before cell no longer reads "unchanged by this change", because Intake adds the urgent-signal notifications and Go › Intake to main; main was not measured before Intake.

- **The first screen grows by the sidebar's Work section:** the Intake row, its count of urgent signals, the `signal` subscription that keeps the count current, and client-core's reading of that count. The screen, its list and pane, and the three icons only it draws (alarm, arrow, diff) are in Intake's chunk, loaded the first time Intake opens.

The perf script's rows were measured 2026-10-11 on the reference machine, in one run of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` on this change. The launch "500 threads, Intake open on 200 signals" stores Intake as the last screen, so the app reopens on it, as [Open on](#appearance) does for a user. A second, plain launch on Intake then raises signals and opens and closes the split. The one-minute load average was 2.5 to 26 over the run, and 3.2 to 4.6 at the two Intake launches, because other worktrees' runs shared the machine. Before is "500 threads, run 1" in the same run, which opens on the new-thread screen: a base run would differ from it by load, not by Intake.

| Row of [What Intake costs](#what-intake-costs) | Expected | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4 | 4: browser, GPU, network utility, renderer |
| Memory, Intake open, 200 signals on To do | within the app's memory row: renderer at most 100 MB, summed at most 220 MB | renderer 57.5 MB, summed 701.7 MB | renderer **59.7 MB**, summed **220.0 MB** |
| Idle, window visible | no work, except the snooze timer | renderer 1.3% of a core and 5 wakeups a second; GPU 63 | renderer **0.4%** and **1** wakeup a second; GPU **0** |
| Idle, window hidden | the same | renderer 1 wakeup a second | renderer **1** wakeup a second |
| Idle, ages on screen | none | - | 15 ages on screen, all over an hour: the age clock fired 0 times in 60 s, visible and hidden |
| Lists | To do read whole | - | To do read once a push, 20 reads for 20 pushes; a burst of 30 read it once |
| Per push, one signal raised a second, 20 times | the read and the list's render | - | renderer main thread **4.8 ms** a push; renderer CPU 9.5 ms; controller CPU 10.0 ms |
| Per push, a burst of 30 | one block | - | longest renderer task **1.7 ms**, none over 50 ms; one read of To do |
| Opening the split, 250 rows | no dropped frame | - | 3 runs, 39 to 40 frames over the 320 ms transition: longest gap 9.5, 21.4 and 17.1 ms against 8.3 ms usual; longest renderer task 7.7 to 8.0 ms: **a dropped frame in 2 runs of 3**. *(Re-measured 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* Once the split moves a frame after the click: longest gap 9.6 to 10.4 ms, **no dropped frame in 4 runs of 4** (see below) |
| Closing the split, 250 rows | no dropped frame | - | 3 runs, 39 to 40 frames over the 320 ms transition: longest gap 10.4, 16.8 and 16.8 ms against 8.3 ms usual; longest renderer task 3.3 to 5.2 ms: **a dropped frame in 2 runs of 3**. *(Re-measured 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524).)* Once the split moves a frame after the click: longest gap 10.0 to 11.8 ms, **no dropped frame in 4 runs of 4** (see below) |
| Launch, spawn to the last screen's content painted | 800 ms, [Launch](#performance) | 426 ms, the last thread's transcript, the launch before | **397 ms**, Intake's signals; window shown at 408 ms |

- **The summed footprint is within its row on Intake, and over it on the new-thread screen.** The difference is the GPU process: 101 MB on Intake against 586 MB on the new-thread screen, whose focused composer draws a caret 63 times a second. That miss predates Intake and is recorded in the entries above. Intake focuses no text field, so it draws nothing while idle.
- *(Re-measured 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524), once the split animates.)* **Opening and closing the split both miss their rows: each dropped one frame at 120 Hz in 2 runs of 3.** The first measurement, before the split had a transition, recorded the second after the click: opening dropped no frame in three runs, and closing dropped one in two runs of three. The split now animates the list's width over `--dur-3`, so the perf script records the frames from the click's frame until one frame after the 320 ms transition. The three runs ran the Intake steps alone, at 40 threads, with a one-minute load average of 2.3, 3.6 and 3.7. A run at 120 Hz has 8.3 ms a frame, and a 17 ms gap is one frame dropped.
  - **The frame dropped is the transition's first frame, not one in its middle.** A traced session of eight more opens and closes put the long gap after the click's frame in six of eight, and one gap in the middle once.
  - **The frames in the middle are cheap:** each lays out 8 of about 600 layout objects in 0.1 to 1.1 ms, because the list's rows are one line and do not wrap. So "layout on each frame of the transition" holds and is small.
  - **The likely cause is the GPU process, with the renderer's work in front of it.** The renderer's main thread runs the click and renders the pane in the first frame: up to 8 ms on the first open, 2 to 5 ms after, and never more than 9 ms for any task. In seven of the eight traces, the GPU process's main thread waited 7.5 to 11.8 ms in one swap (`SkiaOutputDevice->ScheduleOverlays()`, waiting in `IOSurfaceImageBacking::WaitForCommandsToBeScheduled`), and its raster tasks ran up to 3 to 4.4 ms. The traces were not lined up frame by frame, so that the long swap falls in the first frame is inferred, not shown: that frame is the one that rasters new content, the pane's on opening and the list's newly shown part on closing. A fix to try is to have that content rastered before the click, by keeping the closed pane's layer painted, or to slide with a compositor-only transform, which spec 17's motion rule would have to allow. Neither is tried here.
- *(Re-measured 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524), once the split moves a frame after the click.)* **Opening and closing the split now drop no frame at 120 Hz in 4 runs of 4.** The fix is not the one the entry above guessed: nothing is rastered ahead and the motion is the same width transition.
  - **What changed:** the list's `has-pane` class, which starts the transition, now changes one frame after the frame that draws the click or the key. The pane's state, its `inert`, the toggle's pressed state and the keys change at once, as before. Under Reduce motion the class changes at once, because nothing moves.
  - **Why the first frame dropped:** traces of six closes lined up frame by frame showed it. After the screen has been still, the frame that handles a click starts late, 4 to 5 ms into its 8.3 ms. That frame's work, the click's render and the style that starts the transition, then ended after the frame's deadline in the closes that dropped, and the transition's first frame was shown one frame late. The GPU's raster and swap were no longer than in the closes that did not drop. Opening is the same, with the pane's render, up to 12 ms, in that frame too.
  - **What the perf script records now:** the frames from the click's frame until one frame after the 320 ms transition, as before, but the transition's frames are counted from the frame whose style changes the class. The frames before it hold the click's render and are reported apart, as "transition starts N ms after the click". A frame that ends late there delays the start, not the motion.
  - **The numbers.** Four full runs of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts`, 250 rows in the list. The one-minute load average at the Intake launch was 15.8, 4.5, 57.0 and 2.7, because other worktrees' runs shared the machine. A gap of 12.5 ms, one and a half frames, counts as a dropped frame.

    | Run | Opening: longest gap, frames | Opening: transition starts after the click | Closing: longest gap, frames | Closing: transition starts after the click |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | 1 | 10.3 ms, 41 | 29.2 ms | 10.3 ms, 40 | 33.5 ms |
    | 2 | 9.6 ms, 40 | 33.6 ms | 11.8 ms, 39 | 34.4 ms |
    | 3 | 10.3 ms, 40 | 29.4 ms | 10.0 ms, 40 | 25.1 ms |
    | 4 | 10.4 ms, 41 | 33.1 ms | 10.4 ms, 40 | 35.1 ms |

    The longest renderer task was 11.0 to 11.8 ms on opening, the pane's render before the list moves, and 2.0 to 5.3 ms on closing. Eighteen more opens and closes in one session, at a load average of 4, had no gap over 9.4 ms in any transition.
  - **The cost is latency:** the list starts to move 25 to 35 ms after the click. Before, it started in the frame that drew the click. The frame that draws the click still sometimes takes two frames, 17 ms, before any motion starts.
- **The split was measured with 250 rows in the list,** the 200 raised for the launch and the 50 the per-push steps raised, against the cost table's 50. The list is virtualized, so its row count does not change how many rows are laid out.
- **Idle was measured with no snooze due,** against the cost table's "a snooze due in an hour". Snooze is not built in this slice, so the snooze timer does not exist yet; the slice that builds it measures it.
- **The per-push rows measure the read and the render, not an animation.** To do is sorted oldest first, so a raised signal lands below the rows on screen, and the motion of arriving signals is [slice 35](#slices). ~~The Performance panel rows, the per-push animation and inbox zero, and the Network panel's Images row, were not run: this slice draws no motion and loads no image.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534).)* The per-push animation and inbox zero are now measured by the perf script, in the entry below. The Network panel's Images row was not run.

**The list's motion and inbox zero,** measured 2026-10-12 on the reference machine, a 120 Hz display, for [#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534), on a build with the hold over every view and inbox zero's wait for a closing pane. Three runs of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --intake-only`, which runs only the plain launch on Intake above, on 40 threads. After the split steps, the script moves the pointer over the sidebar, off the list, and marks To do done down to 3 signals, so that a raised signal lands on screen. Then it measures, each in its own recording:

1. one signal raised, which arrives on screen;
2. a burst of 30 raised at once, of which 11 land on screen;
3. inbox zero: the script marks To do done down to one signal, clicks it, which opens the pane, and presses E, so the user's own Done empties To do with the pane open.

For each, the page records its animation frames from the change until the first frame with no animation running on Intake, and a Chromium trace records the renderer's main thread from the change until 3.5 s after it. The recording asks for every frame while it runs, so its frame count is what the page could draw, not only what the motion asked for. It stops when the motion ends, so the frames after it are only those something else asked for. The one-minute load average was 3.2, 4.1 and 4.3.

| Row of [What Intake costs](#what-intake-costs) | Expected | Measured, 3 runs |
|---|---|---|
| Per push, one arrival on screen | one compositor-only animation, only for rows on screen | 1 animation. 25 to 26 frames over 200 to 208 ms (`--dur-2`), longest gap 9.6 to 10.1 ms against 8.3 ms usual: **no dropped frame**. Renderer main thread 13.1 to 25.7 ms from the raise to the motion's end, the read of To do included; longest task 4.0 to 6.6 ms |
| Per push, a burst of 30 on screen | one motion | 11 animations, one for each row on screen, all with **one start time**. 25 to 26 frames over 200 to 209 ms, longest gap 9.3 to 10.2 ms: **no dropped frame**. Renderer main thread 19.5 to 24.9 ms; longest task 5.9 to 8.8 ms |
| After an arrival or a burst | nothing after | the main thread draws one more frame, which drops the finished animation, and then none in the rest of the second: **0.3 to 1.1 ms** of main thread in it. No animation and no leaving row's copy is left |
| Inbox zero, with the pane open | 800ms of compositor-only animation per arrival after a `--dur-3` wait, nothing after | 39 animations: the leaving row's fade, the arrival's rays, sparks, face and text, the toast's rise and the list's widening. 126 to 127 frames over 1,041 to 1,050 ms, longest gap 10.3 to 10.4 ms: **no dropped frame**. Renderer main thread 110.8 to 183.2 ms from the key press to the arrival's end, the Done, the reads of To do and Done and inbox zero's first render included; longest task 11.1 to 14.5 ms |
| After inbox zero | nothing after | **one render at 1,120 ms**, when the arrival's class comes off, so a part that mounts later does not play its rise alone. Its frames end **1,129 to 1,140 ms** after the first frame; 3 frames and 5.0 to 14.5 ms of main thread in the second after. No animation is left |

- **The arrivals miss nothing.** Each lands in one motion, drops no frame, and leaves nothing running.
- **Inbox zero's animations have three start times, and none starts after 800 ms.** The first holds the leaving row's fade and every part of the arrival, the cleared count included, because each waits through a CSS delay rather than starting late. The second is the toast's rise, 142 ms later, and the third the list's widening as the pane closes, 157 ms after the first.
- **The picture no longer slides.** Sampled every frame with the pane open: the list widens from 432 to 1,168 px between 150 and 450 ms after inbox zero mounts, and the art moves from 380 to 748 px with it, while nothing of the arrival is drawn yet. The face starts to show 565 ms after the mount. The first ray starts at 440 ms, when the list has under 1 px left to move.

**The Permission Request dock,** measured 2026-10-10 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, building the renderer on both sides against the same contract and client-core, for [#86](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/86).

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | none new | 336.2 kB | 337.7 kB; almost all of it in the `transcript` chunk, 53.0 to 54.4 kB |
| All renderer JavaScript, gzipped | none new | - | +2.2 kB |
| CSS | none new | - | +57 B |

- **No process, timer, idle work or work per streamed token is added.** The dock draws the waiting pose, which is static.
- **Profiles are read only while a Permission Request is open.** Opening a thread or Conversation that already has one costs one `profile.query`; one that arrives live costs one the first time, if the profiles are not cached. The list then stays cached; a dock that finds the cached list lacks the session's profile reads it once more, never twice. No live topic is added.

**Loaders split off with their route,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s bundle check, on `main` at 0397b60d (Before) and on branch `t3/confirm-dialog-and-split-loaders` merged with it (After), for [#542](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/542). The router plugin now puts a route's loader in the chunk of its component, so a loader, and every module it imports, loads the first time its route opens instead of with the first screen. The routes the app starts on keep `codeSplitGroupings: []` and stay whole. The change adds no process, no read and no timer, and touches no file main links.

| Measure | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | 338.5 kB | 338.4 kB |

- **Today it saves little,** because today's loaders import only what the shell already loads: query options and client-core code. A loader that imports code of its own saves more. On the Workflows prototype, the loaders of the three workflow routes bring the workflow source parser, and keeping them with the first screen cost 4.8 kB gzipped.
- **A loader now waits for its chunk before it starts.** The chunk is a local file, served on the `app://` origin, so the wait is a few milliseconds.
- **Hot reload still works.** In the dev server, a split loader that was edited twice was replaced in place each time, and its route loaded again without an error. A specimen page that removes a route's loader fails with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'ensureQueryData')" after such an edit, with or without this change: the hot reload puts the route's real loader back, and the specimen's router has no query client. Reloading the page fixes it.

**Settings › Assistants, memory,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, for [#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93). After is this branch rebased on `main` at df714ba2. Before is `main` at df714ba2 for the first screen, as measured for #542 above, and `main` at 0397b60d for the chunks, which #542 grew a little by moving each route's loader into its chunk; sizes are gzipped, 1,024 bytes to a kB. The rows `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` measures were not run for this change and stay TBD. The change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic: memory is read when the section or a document opens.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | - | TBD |
| The first screen's JavaScript | the memory queries and the new route's entry only | 338.4 kB | 339.4 kB across 20 chunks; first-screen chunk 259.6 kB |
| The Assistants chunk | its own chunk | 10.8 kB | 12.5 kB, and 0.7 kB of CSS |
| A memory document's page | its own chunk, loaded the first time a document opens | - | 5.1 kB, and 0.8 kB of confirmation dialog it now shares with Assistants |
| Memory, 40 threads | summed 220 MB, renderer 100 MB | - | TBD |
| Idle, window visible | the idle row | - | TBD |

- **Opening Assistants costs two reads more,** `memory.list` and `memory.read` of `core`, and so does picking another assistant's tab. A failure of either fails the section's loader, as a failure of `assistant.query` does.
- **The meter is one rule now.** Permission profiles drew its grant meter with rules of its own, the same as the book's `.meter`; both screens now use the shared `.meter` in `controls.css`.

**Intake's moves: Done, Snooze, Unsnooze and Not urgent,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base 3eebc8c8 (Before) and on this change (After), and with one run of `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` on this change, for [#525](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/525). *(Added 2026-10-11, [#525](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/525).)* Sizes are gzipped bytes. The change adds no process, no polling and no live topic. It adds one timer, the snooze timer, set for the soonest snooze's `until`, and one read the shell keeps, Later with a limit of one, for that `until`. The perf script now raises one more signal and snoozes it for an hour before the launch that opens on Intake, so the timer is set while the idle samples run; To do still holds 200 signals.

| Row | Expected | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Intake's chunk, JavaScript | recorded; not budgeted | 11,501 bytes | 20,211 bytes |
| Intake's chunk, CSS | recorded; not budgeted | - | 3,346 bytes |
| Idle, window visible, Intake open, a snooze due in an hour | no work, except the snooze timer | renderer 0.4% of a core and 1 wakeup a second, without a snooze, for #524 above | renderer **0.6%** and **1** wakeup a second; GPU **0** |
| Idle, window hidden, the same | the same | renderer 1 wakeup a second | renderer **1** wakeup a second |
| Idle, ages on screen | none | 0 fires | 14 ages on screen, the youngest 2 h: the age clock fired 0 times in 60 s, visible and hidden |
| Memory, Intake open | renderer at most 100 MB, summed at most 220 MB | renderer 59.7 MB, summed 220.0 MB | renderer **66.6 MB**, summed **226.2 MB**: over by 6 MB |
| Launch, spawn to Intake's signals | 800 ms | 397 ms | **337 ms**; window shown at 342 ms |

- **The snooze timer adds no wakeup while idle.** It is one `setTimeout` an hour away, so in the 10-second samples the renderer wakes once a second, as it did for #524 with no snooze. The perf script does not count timers; that one timer is pending comes from the code, which keeps at most one.
- **The summed footprint is 6 MB over its row, a budget miss.** The renderer grew by 7 MB, from 59.7 to 66.6 MB; the GPU process held 100.7 MB, against 101 MB then. Each side is one run on a shared machine, with a load average of 5.1 at this launch, so the growth is not yet separated from the noise between two runs. The miss is accepted for this change; a later measurement on a quiet machine settles it.
- **Splitting the moves into their own chunk was not tried.** Intake's chunk grew by 8.7 kB, and the moves show on every row, so they load with Intake whatever the split.

**GitHub review requests and mentions,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, for [#526](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/526). Before is this branch at 6063467a, before the client changes; After is this change. Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB; the byte counts are gzip -9. The perf script was not run: the change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic, so it adds no idle work. A plugin's mark arrives with its record in the `plugin.query` read Intake already makes.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | none new | 343.4 kB across 21 chunks; first-screen chunk 263.6 kB | 343.4 kB across 21 chunks; first-screen chunk 263.6 kB (+73 bytes: client-core's Screening line and the plugin named for an end on the source) |
| Intake's chunk, gzipped | none | 11.4 kB (11,475 bytes) | 11.3 kB (11,416 bytes): the app's fallback to its own GitHub mark is gone |
| A plugin's mark in its `plugin.query` record | none | - | GitHub: 835 bytes of JSON, one path of 812 characters; a plugin with no mark: 0 |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | not measured; this change touches no code in main | 150.1 kB |

- **The app's copy of GitHub's mark stays.** Intake now draws only the mark the plugin returns, but first run, the Office, New project and the new-thread start cards still draw `GitHubMark` from `logos/brand-mark.tsx`, which lives in the first-screen chunk. Removing it is left to the change that moves those screens to the plugin's mark.

**Intake: Everything,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base cafe506b (Before, the base of the Everything work) and on this change (After), for [#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529). Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB; the byte counts are gzip -9.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | none new | 347.8 kB across 22 chunks | 349.6 kB across 22 chunks |
| Intake's chunk, gzipped | none | 19.6 kB of JavaScript (20,103 bytes; 56,828 unzipped), 3.3 kB of CSS | 21.9 kB of JavaScript (22,422 bytes; 61,318 unzipped), 3.6 kB of CSS: the screen is 18.7 kB, and the bar, view switcher and split that Everything shares with it 3.2 kB |
| Everything's chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | - | 7.7 kB of JavaScript (7,850 bytes; 19,148 unzipped), no CSS of its own |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | not measured; this change touches no code in main | 150.2 kB |

- **Intake's chunk grew by 2.3 kB gzipped,** and the first screen by 1.8 kB. Both include everything the branch added since its base, not only Everything: the source tabs worked out from the plugins' Intake settings, the route's search params and its loader. Everything's list, pane and search are in the chunk of their own, which loads the first time the view opens.

The perf script's rows were measured 2026-10-11 with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --everything-only`, twice, on a shared machine. Its launch has 1,000 events of Intake, in 16 resolved signals that name them, on top of the 200 signals on To do. The fixture titles the events, sets their authors, and spreads their arrival over the last 80 days. The one-minute load average was 26.7 at the first run and 7.6 at the second. The app is launched on Intake's To do list. A click on "Everything" opens the view, and the script scrolls the list to its end line in steps of 1,500 px, 50 ms apart. Memory is read with no connection to the page, on To do after 35 s and on Everything 4 s and 34 s after the scroll.

| Row of [What Intake costs](#what-intake-costs) | Expected | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Opening Everything, controller local | recorded | - | first row 324 to 326 ms after the click, which includes loading the view's chunk; longest renderer task 4.4 to 4.8 ms |
| Scrolling 1,000 events | no task over 50 ms | - | longest renderer task 5.8 to 5.9 ms, none over 50 ms; the end line shows after 2.9 to 3.1 s |
| Lists | page by page | - | 20 reads for 1,000 events, one for each page of 50 |
| Virtualization | a few dozen rows drawn | - | 29 rows in the page at the list's end |
| Memory, renderer | at most 100 MB | 47 MB on To do | **79 MB** 34 s after the scroll (79 MB at 4 s): 32 to 33 MB more |
| Memory, summed | at most 220 MB | 266 MB on To do | **342 to 344 MB**: 78 MB more; over, as is To do on this machine |

- **The renderer stays within its row; the summed footprint does not, and was already over before the click.** On To do the app held 266 MB, 165 MB of it the GPU process, against 101 MB in the Intake entry above. The scroll adds 40 MB to the GPU process (165 to 205 MB) and 32 MB to the renderer. The renderer reads 79 MB at 4 s and at 34 s after the scroll, so the 32 MB is not shrinking while the app idles. The reading was not split further: how much of it the 1,000 events in the query cache hold, and how much the rows and the scroll left behind, is not known.

**Workflows: the workflow list,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base b700bd2f (Before) and on this change (After), for [#550](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/550). Sizes are gzipped and counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | grows only by the Go item, the face's row, "From runs" and the routes' stubs | 343.4 kB | 344.9 kB across 21 chunks |
| The workflow list's chunk, gzipped | none | - | 6.8 kB of JavaScript, 1.1 kB of CSS |
| The open workflow's stub, gzipped | none | - | 0.05 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 150.1 kB | 150.2 kB |

- **The first screen grows by 1.5 kB, against 3.7 kB in the prototype.** What remains is what [What Workflows costs](#what-workflows-costs) allows: the Go item, the face's Workflows row, "From runs" in Waiting on you and the session read behind it, and the two routes' stubs. Built the way the prototype was, the list added 3.8 kB, because the client-core index linked the row builder, schedule text and cron words into the first screen. They are now reached only through the subpath exports `@hercule/client-core/workflow-rows` and `@hercule/client-core/list-time`, which only the list's chunk imports.
- ~~**`decodeWorkflowDefinition` stays where it is.** About 1.5 kB of the workflow schema is on the first screen before this change, because `HttpApi.make` builds every group of the contract. The list does not parse a workflow, so moving the function saves nothing.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#551](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/551): once the open workflow parsed its YAML, the decode in the schema's module put 1.8 kB gzipped on the first screen, so `decodeWorkflowDefinition` moved to its own module. See "Workflows: the open workflow" below.)*

The perf script's rows were measured 2026-10-11 on the reference machine with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --workflows-only`. Its launch lists 50 workflows: 10 have 5 live runs each, 50 in all, and 40 have one completed run. Each push cancels one live run. Before is the same script on the branch before the strips became one read, in three runs at a one-minute load average of 12 to 17. After is one run on this change at a load average of 41.7, because other worktrees' runs shared the machine; its per-push times are therefore an upper bound.

| Row of [What Workflows costs](#what-workflows-costs) | Expected | Before: a read per strip | After: one read for the strips |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4 | 4: browser, GPU, network utility, renderer |
| Memory, 50 workflows listed | within the app's memory row: renderer at most 100 MB, summed at most 220 MB | renderer 69 to 70 MB, summed 329 to 334 MB | renderer **60 MB**, summed **307 MB** |
| Idle, window visible | no work | renderer 0.7 to 0.8% of a core, 1 wakeup a second; age clock fired 0 times | renderer **0.4%**, **1** wakeup a second; GPU 0; age clock fired 0 times |
| Idle, window hidden | no work | renderer 0 wakeups; age clock fired 0 times | renderer **0** wakeups; age clock fired 0 times |
| Opening the list, controller local | at most 500 ms until the last strip draws | 100 strip reads: the first 50 answered at 87 to 91 ms, the last at 147 to 152 ms | **2** strip reads: the first answered at 74 ms, the last at 129 ms |
| "From runs" at launch | no more than one read added to each round of the launch's reads | 2, as many as the thread list | **2**, as many as the thread list |
| Per push, reads | each run read on screen read once | 50 strip reads and 2 live run reads | **1** strip read and **2** live run reads |
| Per push, renderer main thread | within 16 ms | 18 to 23 ms: **over** | **8.1 ms** |
| Per push, renderer and controller CPU | recorded | renderer 48 to 51 ms, controller 39 to 45 ms | renderer 16 ms, controller 19 ms |

- **One read per strip missed the per-push row, so the strips are one read, as [What Workflows costs](#what-workflows-costs) planned.** A profile of the Before build put the time in handling 52 responses one by one, not in React: starting the requests, decoding and storing each answer, and invalidating each query. `run.query` gained the `newestPerWorkflow` filter for it ([spec 11](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md)), and `invalidateWithoutCancelling` now invalidates each query without hashing every key in the cache, which saved about 2 ms a push on its own.
- **Opening the list reads the strips twice.** The loader reads them, and the `run` subscription's first push says that every record may have changed, so the list reads them again. The shell's reads and the subagents' do the same at launch. Before this change that doubled 50 reads; now it doubles one.
- **The seed's 90 runs fit one page.** With 20 runs for each of 50 workflows, the strips are 1,000 runs, two pages of 500 read one after the other. That case was not measured.
- **The summed footprint is over its row, as in most entries above.** The GPU process holds 187 MB of the 307 MB, a miss that predates this change. The renderer is within its row.
- **Opening the list against a remote controller was not measured.**

**Workflows: the open workflow,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base 581d0361 (Before) and on this change (After), for [#551](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/551). Sizes are gzipped and counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB. The After column was measured again after the review fixes: the page compiles under the React Compiler, the timeline's minute clock runs only while the timeline shows, motion left the slice for [#553](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/553), and action steps draw their plugin's mark.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | grows only by the open workflow route's stub | 347.4 kB | 348.3 kB across 21 chunks |
| The open workflow's chunk, gzipped | the YAML parser, the layout and the graph: 62 kB in the prototype | - | 68.3 kB of JavaScript, 2.5 kB of CSS |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 150.2 kB | 150.2 kB |

- **The first screen grows by 0.9 kB.** It is the open workflow route's stub (its search check and its loader's dependencies) and its reads in `app/queries.ts`, which [What Workflows costs](#what-workflows-costs) allows. The search check caps `daysBack` with `MAX_TIMELINE_DAYS_BACK`, which lives in `@hercule/client-core/timeline-limit`, a module of its own: imported from `@hercule/client-core/run-timeline`, it put the whole timeline module on the first screen, 351.7 kB.
- **`decodeWorkflowDefinition` moved to its own module, `packages/contract/src/groups/workflow-definition-decode.ts`, with its error messages.** The desktop build puts every module the first screen imports into one chunk. With the decode in the workflow schema's module, the YAML decode added 1.8 kB gzipped to the first screen. This supersedes the #550 bullet above that kept it in place.
- **The YAML parser and the layout (dagre) load only with the open workflow's chunk.** In the built renderer, the parser's and the layout's code and the decode's error messages are in `_workflowId-*.js` alone: not in the first screen, and not in the list's chunk. Neither loads at launch or with the list.
- **The open workflow's chunk is 6.3 kB over the prototype's 62 kB.** It grew 2.2 kB after the review fixes, with the plugin marks on action steps, the on-screen watch for the timeline's bars and the save reconciliation. The prototype's figure is a reference, not a budget; the chunk is loaded only when a workflow opens.

The perf script's rows were measured 2026-10-11 on the reference machine with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --workflows-only`, in one run. Its seed is the #550 list's 50 workflows, with the last one replaced: it has an event trigger and three steps (`fetch`, then `check` and `notify` side by side), and 300 completed runs spread evenly over yesterday in the user's timezone. The launch opens that workflow beside the list, on its Runs tab. Each push cancels one live run of another workflow, so the open workflow's own runs do not change; what each push costs is the reads on screen. The visible idle samples with the workflow open last 70 s, longer than a minute, so a clock that ticks once a minute would show in them. The machine was busy with other work: the one-minute load average was 7.09 when the run started, 20.21 before the list's launch and 32.16 before the open workflow's launch.

| Row of [What Workflows costs](#what-workflows-costs) | Expected | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4: browser, GPU, network utility, renderer |
| Memory, 50 workflows listed and one open | within the app's memory row: renderer at most 100 MB, summed at most 220 MB | renderer **65 MB**, summed **307 MB**: **over**, as in the list's entry |
| Idle, window visible, workflow open on the Runs list, 70 s | no work: no timer of its own | renderer **0.1%** of a core, **1** wakeup a second; GPU 0.0%, 1 wakeup a second; age clock fired 0 times |
| Idle, window visible, Runs tab on today's timeline, 70 s | no work but the time now, which moves once a minute | renderer **0.0%** of a core, **1** wakeup a second; GPU 0.0%, 2 wakeups a second; age clock fired **1** time |
| Idle, window hidden, workflow open | no work | renderer **0** wakeups; age clock fired 0 times |
| Lists: the Runs tab's list | virtualized | **20** rows mounted of the 50 read |
| Lists: one day of the timeline | drawn whole | **300** bars of 300, on 12 tracks |
| Per push, on the Runs list: reads | each run read on screen read once | **1** strip read, **2** live run reads, **1** read of the list's one loaded page |
| Per push, on the Runs list: renderer main thread | within 16 ms | **5.3 ms** |
| Per push, on the timeline of the 300-run day: reads | each run read on screen read once | **1** strip read, **2** live run reads, **1** read of the day |
| Per push, on the timeline of the 300-run day: renderer main thread | within 16 ms | **6.6 ms** |
| Per push, renderer and controller CPU | recorded | Runs list: renderer 11 ms, controller 14 ms. Timeline: renderer 15 ms, controller 16 ms |
| Drawing the 300-run day | recorded | 72 ms of renderer main thread, from picking the timeline until 1 s after all 300 bars showed |

- **The page adds no timer of its own.** No `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `requestAnimationFrame` or `requestIdleCallback` is in the open workflow's route or in `screens/workflows/`. Its times read the age clock. On the Runs list the clock did not fire in 70 s. On today's timeline it fired once in 70 s, to move the line that marks the time now; that is the one tick a minute the timeline needs. The clock runs for the timeline only while the timeline shows.
- **The summed footprint is over its row, as in most entries above.** The GPU process holds 182 MB of the 307 MB, a miss that predates this change. The renderer is within its row. The same run's list launch, with no workflow open, was 61 MB and 302 MB.
- **Each push reads every run read on screen once.** The page of the Runs list and the day are each read once a push, with the strips and the live runs; nothing else is read.
- **The load average was high during the run.** Every idle and per-push row is within its budget with room to spare, so a quieter machine would not change which rows pass; the per-push times may be a little lower on one.
- **The perf rows were measured before a timeline bar counted its time only while on screen.** That change only removes age clock subscriptions, for bars scrolled out of view, so the timeline's rows are an upper bound.
- **Not measured:** opening a workflow against a remote controller; memory with a run's page open, which is a later slice; the timeline's arrival, which the Performance panel measures once by hand. Motion is not measured, because it left this slice for [#553](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/553).

**Workflows: a run's page,** measured 2026-10-12 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base 7598415d (Before) and on this change (After), for [#552](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/552). JavaScript sizes are gzipped and counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB. CSS sizes are Vite's gzipped figures, 1,000 bytes to a kB, which is how the #551 entry's 2.5 kB was counted.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | does not grow (the ticket) | 320.0 kB across 22 chunks, of 69 built | 320.4 kB across 22 chunks, of 76 built: over, by 0.4 kB |
| The run page's chunk, gzipped | none; no YAML parser | - | 12.3 kB of JavaScript, 1.3 kB of CSS |
| The graph's chunk, shared by the run page and the open workflow, gzipped | none | - | 29.4 kB of JavaScript, 3.1 kB of CSS |
| The list's times, shared by the list, the open workflow and the run page, gzipped | none | in the open workflow's chunk | 3.0 kB of JavaScript |
| The open workflow's own chunk, gzipped | none | 68.3 kB of JavaScript, 2.5 kB of CSS | 45.3 kB of JavaScript, no CSS of its own |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 150.2 kB | 150.2 kB |

- **The first screen grows by 0.4 kB, so it misses the ticket's budget.** The first-screen chunk grows from 268.8 to 269.3 kB, and the thread route's chunk shrinks by 0.1 kB. The growth has two parts, measured by building with and without the second:
  - 0.2 kB for the run page's route. That is its three reads in `app/queries.ts` (`runQuery`, `runSessionsQuery` and `rerunsQuery`), which the first-screen chunk holds because the shell imports that module; the route's stub, with its search check, its title and the lazy import of its chunk, which the router needs before the chunk loads; and the `runId` check in `app/last-screen.ts`. The route's loader and component are already in the run page's chunk. The reads could move into that chunk only by leaving `app/`, where the renderer keeps every read, so they stay there;
  - 0.2 kB for the sidebar on a run's page: it shows the Hercule face with Workflows selected, as the book draws it, in place of the threads. The sidebar is on the first screen, so this part cannot move.

  The Before column is already 28.3 kB below the #551 entry's 348.3 kB, because of changes merged in between, such as [#566](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/pull/566).
- **The run page loads 44.7 kB of JavaScript: its own 12.3 kB, the graph's 29.4 kB and the list's times, 3.0 kB.** A few chunks under 1.3 kB each come with them, such as the confirm dialog and the workspace's details. The graph's chunk holds the graph, its node card and the layout (dagre). The bundler split it out because the open workflow and the run page both draw the graph. The list's times (`screens/workflows/list-time.ts`, with `formatStartTime`) are a chunk of their own for the same reason.
- **The YAML parser is in the open workflow's chunk alone.** The run page does not parse the workflow's source; in the built renderer, the parser's error messages are only in `_workflowId-*.js`.
- **Opening a workflow now loads 77.7 kB, 9.4 kB more than before.** That is its own chunk, the graph's chunk and the list's times. Moving `formatStartTime` out of `run-page-rows` did not shrink the open workflow's chunk: the open workflow still loads the list's times, now as a chunk it shares. Most of the growth is the graph and its node card, which gained the run states the run page draws: a step's visits, its duration and its status, which the open workflow loads too. The 9.4 kB was not broken down further.

The perf script's rows were measured 2026-10-12 on the reference machine with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --workflows-only`, in one run, in a throwaway Hercule Home. The script seeds the #551 entry's workflows, then opens a run's page for a live run whose one step has waited for a day, so its "Running for" changes every second. Each push cancels one live run of another workflow. The one-minute load average was 10.10 before the run page's launch.

| Row of [What Workflows costs](#what-workflows-costs) | Expected | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4: browser, GPU, network utility, renderer |
| Memory, a run's page open | within the app's memory row: renderer at most 100 MB, summed at most 220 MB | renderer **53 MB**, summed **680 MB**: **over**. The GPU process holds 567 MB |
| Idle, window visible, a live run's page, 10 s | no work but the age clock | renderer **1.0%** of a core, **4** wakeups a second; GPU 0.8%, **34** wakeups a second; age clock fired **10** times: **over** the idle rows of the other entries (renderer at most 2 wakeups a second, GPU at most 12) |
| Idle, window hidden | no work | renderer **0** wakeups; age clock fired 0 times |
| One tick of the age clock | within 16 ms | **2.8 ms** of renderer main thread, Chromium's own work included |
| The run's graph | its nodes drawn | **2**: the trigger and the one step |
| Per push: reads | each run read on screen read once | **1** read of the run; **0** strip reads, **0** live run reads, **0** reads of a Runs list's pages or a day |
| Per push: renderer main thread | within 16 ms | **5.4 ms** |
| Per push, renderer and controller CPU | recorded | renderer 9 ms, controller 20 ms |

- **A live run's page wakes once a second, so it misses the idle rows.** Its "Running for" counts seconds, as the book draws it, and so does the duration on the running step's card. Both read the age clock, which fires once a second for them and draws one frame; the page has no timer of its own. The renderer's 4 wakeups and the GPU's 34 come with that one frame a second. How the GPU's 34 split was not looked for. A run that has ended counts nothing, and the hidden window does nothing.
- **The summed footprint is over its row, as in most entries above.** The GPU process holds 567 of the 680 MB, close to the 692 to 722 MB the #440 entry recorded with ticking durations. The renderer is within its row.
- **Each push reads the open run once, and nothing else.** The run page draws no strips, no Running section, no Runs list and no day.
- **Not measured:** opening a run against a remote controller.

**Workflows: motion,** measured 2026-10-12 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base 7b7271e0 (Before) and on this change (After), for [#553](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/553). The first screen's size is the budget script's, 1,024 bytes to a kB; the other sizes are Vite's gzipped figures, 1,000 bytes to a kB.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 322.4 kB across 22 chunks: over the guide | 322.5 kB across 22 chunks, of 81 built: over the guide, by 0.1 kB more |
| The list's route chunk, gzipped | none | 7.26 kB of JavaScript, 1.38 kB of CSS | 8.22 kB of JavaScript, 1.54 kB of CSS |
| The open workflow's chunk, gzipped | none | 46.34 kB | 46.92 kB |
| The graph's CSS, which holds the timeline's, gzipped | none | 3.08 kB | 3.23 kB |
| `change-motion`, a chunk the list and the timeline share, gzipped | none | - | 0.14 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 150.2 kB | 150.2 kB |

The perf script's rows were measured 2026-10-12 on the reference machine with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --workflows-only`, on this change and then on the branch base, one run each, back to back, with the one-minute load average at 3.7 (this change) and 4.1 (the branch base) before each launch. The pushes and the seed are the #552 entry's. The motion rows come from a Chromium trace of the `blink.animations` category: an animation's length there includes its delay.

| Row of [What Workflows costs](#what-workflows-costs) | Expected | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Per push: renderer main thread, the list | within 16 ms | 13.6 ms | **23.8 ms: over** |
| Per push: renderer main thread, the Runs list | within 16 ms | 10.6 ms | 10.9 ms |
| Per push: renderer main thread, the timeline | within 16 ms | 14.6 ms | 15.0 ms |
| Per push: renderer main thread, a run's page | within 16 ms | 6.2 ms | 5.8 ms |
| Motion: a run joins a strip, its two pushes | at most each changed visible row's marks, 21 when a run joins a strip, on the compositor alone | - | 6 animations: `strip-step` 5, `mark-in` 1; the longest 333 ms; all on the compositor alone |
| The timeline's arrival, the 300-run day | within 640 ms, on the compositor alone | - | 17: `bar-line-sweep` 8, `mark-in` 8, `lanes-in` 1; the arrival ends at 617 ms; all on the compositor alone |
| Idle, hidden, the list | no work | 0 renderer wakeups | 0 renderer wakeups |

- **The list's pushes miss their budget by 7.8 ms, because Chromium draws a main-thread frame on every vsync while an animation plays.** Each of the script's pushes changes one run's mark in a strip, so the mark pops in for 330 ms. The mark's layer paints once and the compositor animates it; even so, the renderer's main thread runs a frame on each vsync while the animation plays, about 33 frames on the reference machine's 120 Hz display, at about 0.6 ms each. No single frame takes more than about 1 ms. A trace of the ten pushes, Before and After, shows where the time goes:
  - 113 main-thread frames After, against 20 Before;
  - 40 forced style and layout updates (22 ms in all), from the checks that tell whether a row's motion is still playing, against none Before;
  - 20 `animationstart` and `animationend` dispatches (18 ms in all).

  With Reduce motion on, the list's trace has Before's 20 frames and no forced updates, so the rest of the change costs the list almost nothing. The push budget is the thread list's nudge limit, one push's handling in a frame. Here the metric sums every frame of a 330 ms animation as well. Whether this row should count the motion's frames, or the list's motion should change, is a decision this entry leaves open.
- **The timeline stays within its budget, by 1.0 ms.** Its pushes pop only the marks of the bars that changed.
- **The script's timings are noisy at this machine's load.** Earlier runs of this change measured the list at 19.5 and 20.8 ms a push, and the branch base between 7.0 and 9.0 ms. The pair above was measured back to back, at the lowest load of the session.
- **Not measured:** the graph's motion on a run's page, which was on `main` before this change.

**Text generation, desktop slice,** measured 2026-10-11 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the parent commit (Before) and the working tree of branch `agent/282-desktop` (After), for [#282](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/282). This change adds no desktop process, timer, polling or live topic, and it changes nothing per streamed token. Runtime memory, CPU and wakeups were not remeasured, because nothing in them changes.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Renderer JavaScript for the first screen, gzipped | 250 kB, guide | 343.6 kB across 21 chunks | 343.8 kB across 22 chunks |
| System's chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | 1.40 kB | 2.06 kB |
| `text-generation` chunk (the choices and the failure line), gzipped | shared by System and the first run | - | 0.52 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB, guide | 150.1 kB | 150.1 kB |

- **The first screen grows by 0.2 kB** and by one chunk, as the bundler splits the shared code. The renderer was already over its guide before this change.
- **The first run's All set reads `controller.read` once more** when it opens, so the Text generation row names the model that exists after the user's login. System reads nothing it did not already read, plus `settings.read`.
