**Ignore Rules, desktop slice,** measured 2026-10-12 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on `main` (Before) and the working tree of branch `t3/implement-ignore-rules` (After), for [#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532). This change adds no desktop process, timer, polling or live topic, and it changes nothing per streamed token. Runtime memory, CPU and wakeups were not remeasured, because nothing in them changes.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Renderer JavaScript for the first screen, gzipped | 250 kB, guide | 322.9 kB | 323.5 kB across 22 chunks |
| Intake's chunk (Signals, Everything, the Ignore dialog), gzipped | its own chunk | 22.6 kB | 24.9 kB |
| Settings › Intake's chunk (the rules list), gzipped | its own chunk | 4.1 kB | 5.3 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB, guide | 150.2 kB | 150.2 kB |

- **The first screen grows by 0.6 kB.** The contract's rule schema and helpers load with it, because client-core reaches them; the dialog and the rules list load with the screens that draw them. The renderer was already over its guide before this change.
- **Settings › Intake reads `ignoreRule.query` once** when it opens.
- **The offer's pane and the Ignore dialog read Later of the rule's kind once** when they open, every page of it, to count the open signals a rule would take. To do is already held by the shell. The read is kept under its own key, outside the signal lists, so a push for the `signal` topic does not read it again, and it is dropped when the last pane or dialog that reads it closes, so the next opening reads it fresh.
- Nothing else reads more than before.

**A message on its way into the session,** measured 2026-10-12 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the branch base 4e8be802 (Before) and on this change (After), for [#620](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/620). This change adds no desktop process, timer, polling or live topic, and it changes nothing per streamed token: the thread's page reads the queued inputs the composer already reads and keeps live, and adds no block while nothing is sending, so the blocks keep their identity. Runtime memory, CPU and wakeups were not remeasured, because nothing in them changes.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Renderer JavaScript for the first screen, gzipped | 250 kB, guide | 322.6 kB across 22 chunks | 323.0 kB across 22 chunks |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB, guide | 150.2 kB | 150.2 kB |

- **The first screen grows by 0.4 kB**: the rule that splits the queued inputs and the block for a message being sent. The renderer was already over its guide before this change.

**The sidebar's layout,** measured 2026-10-13 with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts` and `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on the working tree of branch `t3/626-sidebar-done-resize-collapse`, for [#626](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/626). The sidebar rows come from the sidebar scenario ([The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout)): a 1440px window, 500 threads, a thread of 501 transcript rows open. Four runs with `--sidebar-only`, at a one-minute load average of 2.7 to 7.9. Style and layout is the time of every style and layout event on the renderer's main thread between two frames, added up; the usual frame is 8.3 ms apart.

| Measure | Budget | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Hiding: dropped frames | none | none; longest gap 8.9 to 10.2 ms |
| Hiding: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 0.53 to 0.70 ms |
| Showing: dropped frames | none | none; longest gap 9.8 to 10.3 ms |
| Showing: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 0.76 to 1.12 ms |
| Dragging the edge, 106 frames: dropped frames | none | none in three runs; one 12.8 ms gap in the fourth |
| Dragging: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 1.02 to 1.29 ms |
| Pressing the edge and letting go: dropped frames | none | one in three runs, 15.5 to 17.2 ms, see below |
| Pressing: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 0.45 to 0.63 ms |
| → on the edge: dropped frames | none | one in two runs, 13.6 and 17.0 ms |
| → on the edge: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 1.51 to 1.59 ms |
| Processes | 4 | 4, as before |
| Footprint, renderer, 500 threads and the long thread | at most 100 MB | 72 MB |
| Idle visible and hidden, renderer | at most 2 wakeups/s | 1.0/s |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 328.6 kB across 22 chunks: over the guide, as before |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 150.4 kB |

- **Every frame stays under 2 ms of style and layout.** The worst, 1.59 ms, is the frame of an arrow key, which resizes the sidebar and stores the layout, so React draws the shell once.
- **The dropped frames do not show the sidebar's work in the trace.** A frame dropped on a press or a key spent at most 3.6 ms of main-thread work. Pressing the empty main pane the same way, with nothing to restyle, also dropped a frame, 17 ms, in one of two runs, so the perf script's simulated press likely drops one by itself. The cause is not proven. The drag's 12.8 ms gap had 1.02 ms of style and layout.
- **The first version stalled 232 ms when the sidebar hid.** Setting `inert` on the sidebar restyled its 500 threads in the slide's first frame.
- **The second version spent up to 2.2 ms in a frame when the sidebar showed.** A hidden sidebar was `visibility: hidden`, which every element inside it inherits, so turning it visible restyled all 500 threads, and turning it hidden at the slide's end cost 1.2 ms. It is now `content-visibility: hidden` on the sidebar alone, and Chromium keeps its style and layout while it is hidden.
- **The first version also spent 2.1 to 2.2 ms a frame in a drag.** `--sidebar-w` was an inherited property on the shell, so each new value restyled every element of the open transcript. It is now registered as not inherited and set on the sidebar's box and the handle alone, and the Show button's offset in a screen's top band moved from an inherited property to a rule per screen.
- **The slide starts one frame after the click.** In the first trace the click's own render and the slide's first frame shared a frame, and that frame was dropped. The longest task is now the click's render, 2.6 to 3.5 ms, in a frame of its own; it lays out nothing that slides.
- **The transform fallback was not needed.** Each frame of the slide lays out the sidebar's box and the main pane and never the sidebar's rows.
- **The bundle grows by about 2 kB gzipped,** estimated by minifying the new modules on their own: the handle 0.9 kB, the layout's store 0.4 kB and `fitSidebarWidth` 0.4 kB, plus the shell's own lines. The first screen's 328.6 kB also holds the other changes on the branch, so this entry has no clean Before figure.
- **A streamed token costs the layout nothing,** and nothing in it runs while idle: no timer, no polling, no live topic and no read.

**Spawn bounds and invalid workflows,** measured 2026-10-13 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on `main` at 738cdb2b (Before) and on this change (After), for [#87](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/87). The desktop app gains no screen in this change: it only stops showing a next fire time, or the runs a trigger will start, for a workflow marked invalid. It adds no desktop process, timer, polling or live topic, and it changes nothing per streamed token. Runtime memory, CPU and wakeups were not remeasured, because nothing in them changes.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Renderer JavaScript for the first screen, gzipped | 250 kB, guide | 323.5 kB across 22 chunks | 323.8 kB across 22 chunks |
| An open workflow's chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | 46.97 kB | 47.01 kB |
| A run's page chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | 12.56 kB | 12.56 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB, guide | 150.2 kB | 150.2 kB |

- **The first screen grows by 0.3 kB:** the contract's spawn bound, `pausedBy`, held count and `invalidSince` load with it. The renderer was already over its guide before this change.
- Nothing reads more than before.

**Text generation steps,** for [#286](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/286). Planned cost, stated before the build: the desktop gains an icon, a kind label and one waiting line for the third step kind, and nothing else. It adds no desktop process, timer, polling or live topic, does no work while idle, and changes nothing per streamed token. Runtime memory, CPU and wakeups are not remeasured, because nothing in them changes. Sizes come from `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, Before on the branch base and After on this change.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Renderer JavaScript for the first screen, gzipped | 250 kB, guide | 329.1 kB | 329.2 kB |
| An open workflow's chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | 46.96 kB | 47.22 kB |
| The workflow graph's chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | 30.09 kB | 30.29 kB |
| A run's page chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | 12.54 kB | 12.89 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB, guide | 150.4 kB | 150.4 kB |

The first screen was already over its guide budget before this change; the step adds 0.1 kB to it, and the rest lands in the chunks of the workflow and run pages, which load only when one opens. (Measured 2026-10-14.)

- **A run's page reads `controller.read` once more when it opens,** for the waiting line's reason. The shell does not cache that read: it uses the read-on-open options, as Settings > System does, and `ensureShellData` does not load it. A push on the `runner` or `provider` topic fetches it again, because which runners are online and logged in decides the reason. That is one small read per push, only while a page that shows it is open.

**Discussions and Wiki,** measured 2026-10-15 with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --discussions-only` and `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on `main` at 427c3cc9 (Before) and on this change (After), for [#669](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/669). The scenario seeds 60 Discussions written by busy agent sessions, one of 190 Posts and the rest of 1 to 3, so the list has more than one page, and a wiki page of 60 revisions. It opens the long Discussion, then has an agent post 10 more Posts to it, 1 s apart, which brings it to the 200-Post cap. One run, at a one-minute load average of 2.6 to 4.3.

| Measure | Budget | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | 4 | 4, as before |
| Footprint, renderer, the 190-Post Discussion open | at most 100 MB | 89.5 MB |
| Footprint, renderer, the 200-Post Discussion open after the pushes | at most 100 MB | 73 MB |
| Footprint, all processes | 220 MB | 341.8 MB, of which the GPU process 188.9 MB: over, as every fixture already is, because of the GPU process |
| Idle visible, renderer | at most 2 wakeups/s | 1.0/s, 1.3% CPU; the age clock did not fire |
| Idle hidden, every process | at most 2 wakeups/s | 0.0/s, except the Browser process at 0.1% CPU |
| Topics held: Discussion open; window hidden; shown again; wiki page open; Settings open | the screen's topic only while it is open and visible | `discussion`; none; `discussion`; `wikiPage`; none |
| A push, with the 200-Post Discussion open: reads | the loaded pages and the open item, once each | 1 read of the list's one loaded page and 1 of the Discussion, nothing else |
| A push: renderer main thread | at most 16 ms | 13.4 ms |
| A push: renderer and controller CPU | none set | 26 ms and 20 ms |
| A push: until the last read answered | none set | median 68 ms, longest 71 ms |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 329.8 kB across 22 chunks of 85 before, 332.9 kB across 24 of 98 after |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 150.4 kB, as before |

- **The push rows are each window's total divided by 10,** so the idle work between pushes is counted in them and they err high.
- **The second memory reading is not comparable with the first:** it was taken after the perf script's DevTools connection had attached and detached, which frees memory. It shows the 200-Post cap fits the renderer's budget.
- **The first screen grows by 3.1 kB.** The two sidebar rows and their icons are part of it, and so is the change that names an author from the shell's threads list. The rest is small modules the new routes share with the first screen, `use-sender-reading` and `sender-chip` among them, which the build now splits into chunks of their own.
- **Each screen is chunks of its own,** gzipped: the Discussions list 2.4 kB, the open Discussion 3.3 kB, the Wiki list 2.3 kB, the open page 2.7 kB, and their shared parts: the grouped list 2.9 kB of JavaScript and 0.4 kB of CSS, the actor's mark 1.0 kB and 0.3 kB, the overlap mark with the Discussions styles 0.8 kB and 0.7 kB, and the Wiki styles 0.7 kB.
- **A streamed token costs these screens nothing,** and nothing in them runs while idle: no timer, no polling, and no topic while they are closed or the window is hidden.
- **The numbers above were measured before #691 merged into this branch.** After the merge, `pnpm build:desktop`'s budget check reads the first screen at 333.0 kB gzipped across 24 chunks: the 0.1 kB the text generation step adds to it.

**Embedded HTML in markdown,** measured 2026-10-16 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s renderer build and bundle check, on `main` at 3cdecae7 (Before), at 77984c82 (the parser, before the renderers) and on this change (After), for [#714](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/714). Each was built in its own worktree. Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB; the byte counts are gzip -9. The change adds no desktop process, timer, polling or live topic. The perf script was not run, because its fixture holds no HTML message and nothing else it measures changes. The parse times have no Before: `main` has no `embeddedHtml` option, so the figure with the flag off was measured on this change, and shows what the parser costs a message with no HTML. Memory was read from the Intake specimen, dev-served, in a hidden window, 3 s after it drew. Its working set (323 to 406 MB across four runs, in either scene) is too noisy to tell the two apart, so the JS heap is the reading. `<details>` is the browser's own element, so a disclosure runs no script of ours.

| Measure | Budget | Before | Parser only | After |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| The markdown chunk, gzipped | its own chunk | 16.4 kB | 18.6 kB | 19.9 kB |
| Renderer JavaScript for the first screen, gzipped | 250 kB, guide | 334.4 kB across 24 chunks | 336.6 kB | 338.0 kB across 24 chunks |
| Intake's CSS (`use-intake-tabs`), gzipped, 1,000 bytes to a kB | its own chunk | 3.75 kB | - | 3.91 kB |
| The first screen's CSS, gzipped, 1,000 bytes to a kB | - | 12.04 kB | - | 12.06 kB |
| The messages' CSS, gzipped, 1,000 bytes to a kB | - | 0.97 kB, one chunk | - | 1.35 kB: `messages` 0.45 kB and `markdown` 0.90 kB |
| Parsing a 2.5 kB message of 20 blocks, in Bun | - | - | - | 0.23 ms, and 0.26 ms with `embeddedHtml: false` |
| Parsing a 9.7 kB bot comment of 10 tables and 10 `details`, in Bun | - | - | - | 0.51 ms, and 0.08 ms with `embeddedHtml: false` |
| Processes | 4 | 4 | - | 4 |
| Memory, renderer's JS heap, the Intake specimen | at most 100 MB | - | - | 62 MB with a Cypress comment and Renovate's release notes open, and 61 to 62 MB with a plain review request open |

- **The first screen grows by 3.6 kB, and all of it is the markdown chunk,** which the thread's route loads and is counted with the first screen. The parser's scanner and allowlist are 2.2 kB of it, and the rest is column spans and drawing `details`, sized images and a table with no header, 1.3 kB. The renderer was already over its guide before this change. The web app's markdown chunk went from 16.7 kB to 20.3 kB, and its first screen stayed at 229.8 kB, because the web app loads markdown on demand.
- **A streamed token costs nothing more.** The paragraph being written is still plain text with no render, and markdown is parsed once when a paragraph or a message settles, as before. A message with no HTML tag parses as fast as before, within the noise of the measurement, and a bot's comment full of tags takes 0.4 ms longer, the scan of the tags it holds. The times are Bun's, not the renderer's, so they show how the cost grows, not what a Mac pays.
- **The markdown rules have a stylesheet of their own,** `markdown.css`, which `markdown.tsx` imports, so a screen that draws markdown gets them without `messages.css`. Intake's routes never loaded `messages.css`. The stylesheet is 0.38 kB more than the rules were inside `messages.css`, because it is a chunk of its own, and it holds the new `details` rules.
- **The user's own bubble parses with `embeddedHtml: false`,** so it does the work it did before.
- **Idle work is unchanged.** The disclosure marker is drawn from two borders in CSS, with no transition and no image.

**Settings > Skills,** measured 2026-10-18 with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --skills-only` and `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on `main` at edb155e4 (Before), built in its own worktree, and on this change (After), for [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732). The scenario imports 100 skills with `hercule skill import`, each a `SKILL.md` of a few paragraphs and one reference file, so a skill's page draws its `SKILL.md`, two files and a History of one revision. The perf script then measures three launches over its 40-thread fixture: Settings › Profile open, as the Before of the memory rows; Settings › Skills open on the 100 skills; and the page of `perf-skill-001` open. One run, at a one-minute load average of 2.1 to 3.5. Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB, gzipped.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4, Profile open | 4, with the list open and with a skill's page open |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | grows by under 1 kB; a guide of 250 kB | 339.0 kB across 24 chunks of 102 | 340.7 kB across 25 of 108: 1.7 kB more, **over its budget by 0.7 kB** |
| Skills' chunks, gzipped | one for the list and one for a skill's page, each loaded when it first opens | - | the list and the import dialog 4.3 kB, a skill's page 4.7 kB, their shared module 1.2 kB, their CSS 1.2 kB, the skill icon 0.4 kB. The page reuses the Markdown chunk |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 150.4 kB | 153.2 kB. The folder reader is its own chunk, 6.7 kB (3.0 kB gzipped), loaded on the first call |
| Footprint, renderer | at most 100 MB | 66.2 MB, Profile open | 76.3 MB with the 100-skill list open; 80.0 MB with a skill's page open |
| Footprint, all processes | 220 MB | 234.7 MB, of which the GPU process 110.1 MB | 268.7 MB with the list open, GPU 133.7 MB; 248.4 MB with a skill's page open, GPU 109.1 MB: over, as every fixture already is, because of the GPU process |
| Idle visible, renderer | no work; at most 2 wakeups/s | 1.0/s, 1.0% CPU | 1.0/s, 1.1% CPU with the list open; 1.0/s, 0.6% CPU with a skill's page open; the age clock did not fire |
| Idle visible, GPU | at most 12 wakeups/s | 3.0/s | 3.0/s with the list open; 2.0/s with a skill's page open |
| Idle hidden, renderer | at most 2 wakeups/s | 1.0/s | 1.0/s in both |
| The window shown, from spawn | 500 ms | 334 ms | 388 ms and 390 ms |
| One import of 24 new skills | none set | - | 162 ms, with `hercule skill import` |

- **The first screen grows by 1.7 kB, 1,738 bytes, and misses its budget of under 1 kB.** Measured chunk by chunk against the Before build, with each source file's share of the first-screen chunk read from a source map:
  - **The skill schemas, 0.76 kB.** `api.ts` adds the `skill` group to the one `HttpApi`, and client-core derives every call from that `api`, so every group's schemas load with the first screen: the `skill` group 0.65 kB, and the protocol's `SkillRevisionHash` and `SkillFileMode` 0.11 kB. About 0.06 kB of the protocol's share is dead: the archive code's two error classes and its `TextEncoder`, which the bundler keeps because they run when the module loads. Client-core's `skills.ts` and `skill-folder.ts` are not on the first screen; only the Skills chunks load them.
  - **The two routes, 0.25 kB:** their stubs in the route tree, their four rows in the operation table, `skillsQuery` and `skillQuery` in `app/queries.ts` with their query key, and the `skill` grant family with the grants only the user gives.
  - **The globe icon's chunk, 0.25 kB.** The globe icon shared a chunk with the terminal icon, both imported by the thread's work rows and Assistants' disallowed-tools row. The skill page imports the globe alone, so the build gives it a chunk of its own, and the first screen loads two small files where it loaded one.
  - **The relative-link rule in the markdown code, 0.09 kB,** in the Markdown chunk, which the thread's route loads.
  - **0.34 kB of bundler noise:** the names the new Skills chunks import from the first-screen chunk, and the minifier's renaming.
  - **The Settings list's Skills row costs nothing here:** the Settings layout is a chunk of its own, which the first screen does not load.
- **No clean change brings the growth under 1 kB.** The schemas are 0.76 kB alone, and moving them off the first screen means loading a group of the contract on demand, which client-core does for no group. The globe icon's split has no fix short of a chunking rule for icons, which would move every lazy screen's chunks; the eight one-icon chunks the first screen loads, 2.5 kB together, are a candidate for that rule in a ticket of its own. Marking the dead protocol code and the grants' refusal text pure removes 0.05 kB, too little to change the outcome, so neither was done. The budget was written assuming the contract was free and the Skills row counted; in fact the row is free and the contract is not. ~~Until the budget is restated, for example as "the two routes' stubs and reads, the `skill` group's schemas, and the globe icon's chunk: under 2 kB", the change does not merge ([ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md)).~~ *(Amended 2026-10-18, [#732](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/732).)*
- **The miss is accepted for #732** (decided by Rogier, 2026-10-18), so the change merges ([ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md)). The budget is not restated: the 1.7 kB is the cost of the contract's new group, which the first screen loads with every other group, and of the globe icon's chunk.
- **The first screen was already over its guide of 250 kB,** gzipped, as it has been since the thread's route was counted with it (282.1 kB), and at 320 kB to 350 kB in every reading since. The 84.6 kB of slice 1 was the empty shell with no controller.
- **Main's startup file grows by 2.8 kB,** within its 160 kB: the `skillFolders.pick` and `skillArchive.build` channels' schemas, which main checks every call against from launch, and the Effect Schema internals they bring.
- **The import was timed with the CLI, not the app.** `hercule skill import` sends one request per skill, one at a time, against the throwaway controller, so the 162 ms shows the controller's cost of 24 imports. It does not include the renderer's `skillArchive.build` calls across the bridge, which the section's integration test bounds to one archive at a time.
- **The reads were not re-measured here.** The section's integration test counts them: one read of the list each time the section opens, and one of the open skill after each write.
- **The three lists draw whole,** as rule 1 now allows: the 100-skill list, a skill's files and its History. The renderer's footprint with the 100-skill list open stays at 76.3 MB.
- **A streamed token costs these screens nothing,** and nothing in them runs while idle: no timer, no polling and no live topic, with the list or a skill's page open.

**Settings > Skills, updates,** measured 2026-10-18 with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --skills-only` and `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, on `main` at 962feaba (Before) and on this change (After), for [#735](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/735). The scenario is #732's: 100 skills imported with `hercule skill import`, then three launches over the 40-thread fixture, with Settings › Profile, Settings › Skills, and the page of `perf-skill-001` open. One run, at a one-minute load average of 3.4 to 14.1, because other work shared the machine. The Before of the memory and idle rows is #732's After, measured the same way. Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB, gzipped.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4 | 4, with the list open and with a skill's page open |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | no growth beyond the contract's four new operations and the `skill` topic | 341.2 kB across 25 chunks of 108 | 341.3 kB across 25 of 110: 0.1 kB more, the operations' rows and schemas |
| Skills' chunks, gzipped | each loaded the first time its screen opens | the list 4.3 kB, a skill's page 4.5 kB, their shared module 1.4 kB, their CSS 1.06 kB | the list 5.4 kB, a skill's page 6.5 kB, their shared module 2.4 kB, their CSS 1.6 kB, and the layout route that holds the topic 0.2 kB |
| Topics held: the list open; a skill's page open; another Settings section open | `skill` only while the section is open | none | `skill`; `skill`; none. One subscription across the list and a page, dropped while the window is hidden |
| Reads: a push naming the open skill; a page with an update waiting | the list, the open skill and its diff once each | - | the list, the skill and its diff once each, counted by the section's integration test |
| Footprint, renderer | at most 100 MB | 76.3 MB with the list open; 80.0 MB with a skill's page open | 73 MB with the list open; 81 MB with a skill's page open |
| Idle visible, renderer | no work; at most 2 wakeups/s | 1.0/s in both | 1.0/s in both; the age clock did not fire |
| Idle hidden, renderer | at most 2 wakeups/s | 1.0/s in both | 1.0/s in both |

- **The section costs what #732 measured, plus its own chunks.** The renderer's footprint and idle wakeups are within the noise of #732's readings, and the first screen grows by 0.1 kB. The chunks grow by 4.9 kB together: the update band, the Update all confirmation, the file-changes card and their CSS, all loaded only when Skills opens.
- **The perf fixture has no update waiting.** Its skills are uploaded, not imported from GitHub, so it measures the section as most users open it. The cost of a waiting update is bounded by design rather than measured here: one `skill.diff` read with the skill, and a patch drawn in steps of 500 lines, at most 2,000 when the card opens, so a 1 MiB patch never mounts a row per line. The section's integration test checks that a card of five 600-line files opens with 2,000 lines drawn. The adversarial review drew a 400,000-line patch's card: 508 elements, where every line drawn at once had made 400,009.
- **A patch stays one string.** The card counts a patch's lines in one pass and splits out only the lines it draws, so a waiting patch costs the renderer about its own size, which the query cache holds anyway. Measured once with Node and `--expose-gc` on a 1 MiB patch of 524,001 lines: counting it retains no heap, where turning every line into an object first retained 24 MiB, and the card draws in 4.8 ms instead of 17.6 ms. The packaged app was not measured with a waiting update, because one needs a check to reach GitHub.
- **A check pass sends one push per repository,** not one per skill, so an open list reads itself again about once per GitHub repository the library uses.

**Runtime errors in the transcript,** measured 2026-10-18 with the renderer's `vite build` and its bundle check, on the branch base b1223ecd (Before) and on this change (After), for [#733](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/733). An error is one more transcript row, read and streamed like every other, so it adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic.

| Measure | Budget | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript and CSS, gzipped | grows only by the note's component, its case in the transcript and its CSS | 340.8 kB, up 0.1 kB from 340.7 kB. The first screen was over its 250 kB guide before this change |
| Work while idle | none | none: the note has no state, no timer and no animation |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged | unchanged: an error note is a memoized component whose props never change |

**The composer on Tiptap,** measured 2026-10-19 on the reference machine with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks and `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts`, on `main` at e0c4b5abd (Before, the textarea) and on this change (After, the Tiptap message field), for [#767](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/767). The change swaps the message field of the three composers and adds no process, timer, polling or live topic. The plan's expected costs are in the Budget column. Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB, gzipped. The launch rows give the one-minute load average of each launch in brackets, from 2.8 to 15.1, because other work shared the machine. Where the After has two readings, it ran twice.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB; expected about +89 kB | 341.7 kB across 25 chunks of 110 | 432.8 kB across 25 of 128: 91.1 kB more, **over the guide by 182.8 kB**, 2.1 kB more than expected |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 155.0 kB | 155.8 kB: 0.8 kB more, the Edit menu's own Undo and Redo items |
| Processes | 4, none added | 4 | 4 |
| Launch, window shown / content painted, new-thread screen, 40 threads, launch 1 | 500 / 800 ms | 382 / 372 ms (load 2.8) | 391 / 380 ms (load 7.6) and 407 / 401 ms (load 12.2) |
| Launch, the same, launch 2 | 500 / 800 ms | 372 / 364 ms (load 5.1) | 402 / 392 ms (load 3.4) and 394 / 387 ms (load 4.1) |
| Launch, new-thread screen, 500 threads | 500 / 800 ms | 400 / 393 ms (load 15.1) | 399 / 391 ms (load 10.5) and 393 / 387 ms (load 4.2) |
| Launch, a thread of 501 rows open | 500 / 800 ms | 404 / 395 ms (load 4.0) | 471 / 464 ms (load 11.2) and 394 / 388 ms (load 10.9) |
| Footprint, summed over the 4 processes, a thread open, nothing focused | 220 MB | 396 MB, renderer 78.5 MB | 384 MB and 384 MB, renderer 75.8 and 75.6 MB |
| Footprint, summed, new-thread screen, composer focused | 220 MB | 698 to 704 MB, renderer 53.5 to 62.4 MB | 703 to 711 MB, renderer 57 to 65 MB |
| Footprint, summed, new-thread screen, nothing focused | 220 MB | 297 MB, renderer 54.3 MB | not measured |
| Wakeups a second, GPU / renderer, thread open, nothing focused | GPU at most 12; renderer at most 2 | 0 / 1 | 0 / 1 |
| Wakeups a second, GPU / renderer, new-thread screen, composer focused | at most 63 / 4 | 43 to 69 / 4 to 5 | 63 to 64 / 5 to 6 |
| Wakeups a second, renderer, window hidden | at most 2 | 0 to 1 | 1 |
| Work per keystroke, busy main thread in 199 keys, one per 50 ms: mean / p95 / longest task | within a 16 ms frame; expected small | 1.26 / 1.55 / 3.71 ms | 1.45 / 1.79 / 4.34 ms |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged | - | unchanged |

- **The first screen was already over its guide, and it now misses it by far more.** The 91.1 kB is Tiptap and ProseMirror in the first-screen chunk, because the composer is on the first screen. Tables, the drop cursor and the gap cursor are tree-shaken out. Tiptap's stylesheet string is bundled and never injected ([Content-Security-Policy](#content-security-policy)).
- **The editor loads with the first screen on purpose** (decided by Rogier, 2026-10-19, [#767](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/767)). A lazy editor would leave the draft field not interactive on launch. So the launch rows are the hard gate, and every one is within 500 and 800 ms. The After reads up to 30 ms above the Before on the 40-thread screen and level with it on the 500-thread screen.
- **The 471 ms launch is load noise.** It ran at a load average of 11.2, and the second run at a similar load (10.9) read 394 ms. It is also within the 500 ms budget.
- **The summed footprint was over its 220 MB budget before this change,** and stays over it. Almost all of the focused figure is the GPU process, about 586 MB, whichever field has the focus ([Measured](#measured), the first milestone's finish). With a thread open and nothing focused, the sum fell by 12 MB and the renderer by about 3 MB. The renderer is within its 100 MB everywhere.
- **The nothing-focused new-thread screen has no After.** macOS focused the window in all 6 After launches, and the script cannot control that ([Measuring](#measuring)).
- **The focused wakeups are the caret's and the focus's, not the editor's.** The Hercule face, which has no composer, reads 69 / 5 before and 64 / 5 after. The renderer's 5 to 6 is 1 to 2 over the focused-field row of 4, where the Before's 4 to 5 was already over. The editor runs no timer and no polling. It holds one `MutationObserver` on the field, and one `selectionchange` listener that returns at once when the field is not focused.
- **Work per keystroke** comes from `--keystrokes-only`, a mode of the perf script added by this change. It types 199 keys, one per 50 ms, into the composer of a thread whose transcript holds 500 rows, and reads the busy time of the renderer's main thread per key from a Chromium trace. The Before ran the same script, copied into a worktree of `main`. Each side ran 7 times, interleaved, and the table shows the medians.
  - **A key costs about 0.2 ms more** (1.26 to 1.45 ms), and the longest task about 0.6 ms more (3.71 to 4.34 ms). Both are far inside a 16 ms frame.
  - **One run on each side was 3 to 5 times slower, with no load to explain it:** the Before's third run read 3.14 / 6.10 / 4.33 ms, and the After's second 4.99 / 7.30 / 5.91 ms. They are kept in the 7 runs, not left out. The median does not move for them.
- **A streamed token costs the field nothing.** The editor runs no transaction per token: its effects run only when the text, the read-only state or the placeholder changes.
- **Pixels are unchanged.** The 32 captures of the three composers (empty, focused, one line, three lines, maximum height and shrunk, each in light and dark) are byte-identical to the textarea's, and `pnpm compare:bureau` reports 0 differing pixels.

**Thread blocks, one stored row,** measured 2026-10-19 with a render count in jsdom (the renderer's React Compiler config), a sampling CPU profile of the packaged app, and `pnpm build:desktop`'s renderer bundle check, on `main` at 4c352601c (Before) and on this change (After), for [#778](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/778). The thread page now builds its blocks in the transcript query's `select`, so Query keeps each unchanged block as the same object, and a stored row no longer draws every work divider, spawn line, turn ending and expanded stretch again. The change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic. A streamed token costs nothing more, because tokens reach the open paragraph outside React. The bundle was built in a throwaway worktree for each side, with sizes counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB, gzipped.

The render count uses a transcript of 499 rows (17 turns, about 109 blocks, 5 ending blocks, 3 subagent spawns) with one older stretch expanded, and delivers 20 stored rows one at a time. Each cell is renders, with how many drew unchanged content in brackets.

| Component | 900 px page, Before | 900 px page, After | 1600 px page, Before | 1600 px page, After |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `WorkDivider` | 123 (108) | 15 (0) | 183 (168) | 15 (0) |
| `SpawnLines` | 123 (109) | 14 (0) | 183 (169) | 14 (0) |
| `TurnEnding` | 20 (20) | 0 | 40 (40) | 0 |
| `WorkRowList` | 20 (20) | 0 | 20 (20) | 0 |

The profile uses a thread of 741 rows (30 tool-heavy turns with subagents, interrupted and failed turns), a 1600 px page with one stretch expanded, and then a running turn that stores 44 rows over 3.3 s. The profile samples every 50 µs, and function names are resolved through source maps. The values are medians of 6 interleaved Before and After pairs, at a one-minute load average of 4 to 15.

| Over the 44 rows | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Block components (`WorkDivider`, `SpawnLines`, `TurnEnding`, `WorkRowList`) | 6.5 ms | 2.2 ms |
| React render and commit | 65.2 ms | 36.2 ms |
| `replaceEqualDeep` | 1.5 ms | 11.6 ms |
| `buildThreadBlocks` | about 12.5 ms | about 12.5 ms |
| JavaScript, without native work and GC | 93.6 ms | 86.7 ms |
| Renderer main thread busy | 141.5 ms | 129.8 ms |

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 434.0 kB across 25 chunks of 110 | 434.3 kB across 25 of 110: 0.3 kB more |
| The thread's page chunk (`agent-page`), gzipped | its own chunk | 16.7 kB | 17.0 kB |

- **The page's `select` runs once per stored row** (20 for 20 in the render count) and does not run on a render with no new row. Before, `buildThreadBlocks` ran in every render of the page.
- **Rendering is cheaper, building is not.** React's render and commit work falls by 29 ms over the 44 rows, but `replaceEqualDeep` now compares the whole block list and adds 10 ms. The net gain is 7 ms of JavaScript, 0.16 ms a stored row, and the profile's runs overlap.
- **A Chromium trace of the same scenario could not tell the two builds apart:** 6 interleaved pairs at 1600 px and 3 at 4000 px. The same build varied 20 to 50% between runs. The longest task stayed near 4 to 5 ms in both, far below the 50 ms Streaming budget. Only the render count and the profile show the change.
- **A stored row still costs about 2.95 ms.** Native rendering (paint, layout, layerize) is about 0.9 ms of it. `buildThreadBlocks` and Query's comparison are about 0.55 ms together, and both walk the whole transcript, so they grow with its length. Building the blocks incrementally is the next step and is not part of this change.

**Composer skills,** measured 2026-10-19 on the reference machine with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks and `apps/desktop/scripts/perf-composer-skills.ts` (`pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop perf:composer-skills`), on the final build of [#769](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/769), after the list was windowed. The change adds the `$` and `/` lists to the message field and turns each Skill Reference into a chip. The plan's expected costs come first, then what was measured.

The plan's expected costs:

- **Processes and live topics:** none added. The lists use the existing request path, and no `skill` topic is held for them.
- **Work while idle:** none. The composer reads its thread's skills (`session.querySkills`) when it mounts, and a list reads them again when it opens, unless they were read in the last 5 seconds (`staleTime` 5 s). A read is not repeated on window focus or reconnect, so a window left alone makes no request. A failed read is not retried.
- **Work per streamed token:** unchanged. The assistant's messages never chip, and a sent message draws its chips from a set of names that keeps its identity until the names change.
- **Work per keystroke in the field:**
  - The filter runs over the thread's skills only while a list is open. The list draws only the rows in view and four more on each side, plus the active row, in fixed 32 px rows, so a key costs the same for 12 skills or 120.
  - On every transaction, a caret move included, the field reads the paragraph before the caret to see whether it ends in a `$` or `/` token that opens a list. That is one paragraph. Once a token is found, the field reads the whole document's text twice and scans it once, to check that the token would be a Skill Reference.
  - On every change of the document, the chip plugin reads the whole document's text. When the text holds a `$`, it scans the text once for Skill References, to find the chips that no longer read as one and the references to turn into chips, and applies both in one transaction. The scan is linear in the length of the text. A document with no `$` costs the read of its text and one `includes` check.
- **Bundle bytes:** the first screen's JavaScript grows by about 8 kB gzipped.

Sizes are counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB, gzipped. The Before is the merge base of this branch with `main` (4c352601c, the composer on Tiptap), built the same way. The Before column of the list rows is the list as first built, drawing every row, measured by two complete runs of the script on one build. The first ran at a one-minute load average of 11.2 and the second at 3.6; the table shows the second, and the notes below give the first where it differs. The After column is one run of the windowed list at a load of 1.8 on a fresh `pnpm build:binary` and `pnpm build:desktop`. The thread is a finished thread with a short transcript, in a project with a checkout of 20 repository skills; the Skill Library holds 100, so a list opens with 120 rows. Latency is the time from the key's `keydown` to the frame that drew the last change the key caused, read in the page, p50 / p95. Busy is the renderer main thread's time in a Chromium trace, per key, from its `keydown` until the next key 100 ms later: mean / p95 / longest task. The two are measured in separate passes over the same steps.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 434.0 kB across 25 chunks of 110 | 441.8 kB across 25 of 109: 7.8 kB more, **over the guide by 191.8 kB** (it was over by 184.0 kB) |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 155.8 kB | 155.8 kB |
| Processes | 4, none added | 4 | 4 |
| Open `$`, first open after the thread mounts, 20 reloads: first frame / frame with the last change | within a 16 ms frame | 9.5 / 11.0 ms and 19.2 / 24.8 ms | 7.2 / 9.5 ms and 7.2 / 9.5 ms |
| Open `$`, skills read less than 5 s ago, no request, 29 opens: latency; busy | within a 16 ms frame | 11.7 / 15.9 ms; 12.25 / **18.65** / 10.45 ms | 8.7 / 11.6 ms; 7.64 / 9.58 / 7.09 ms |
| Open `$` in the back-to-back runs when the skills were older than 5 s, so a request ran behind the open, 12 opens: latency | within a 16 ms frame | 14.6 / 17.0 ms | 8.6 / 12.7 ms |
| Open `$`, skills older than 5 s, 20 opens: latency; busy | within a 16 ms frame | 12.6 / 15.8 ms; 14.17 / **21.12** / 13.34 ms | 8.6 / 12.1 ms; 9.58 / 11.04 / 7.29 ms |
| Filter `review` while the list is open (120 rows narrow to 10, and 10 to 12 rows are drawn), 120 keys: latency; busy | within a 16 ms frame | 6.3 / 14.7 ms; 8.44 / **16.60** / 14.00 ms | 6.5 / 8.2 ms; 7.73 / 10.54 / 8.84 ms |
| Filter `team-w` while the list is open (all 120 rows stay, each with a highlight), 120 keys: latency; busy | within a 16 ms frame | 10.2 / 14.2 ms; 11.51 / **17.82** / 15.49 ms | 6.4 / 8.1 ms; 7.94 / 10.38 / 8.84 ms |
| Typing after a message of about 4,000 characters with 10 chips, list closed, 264 keys: latency; busy | within a 16 ms frame | 3.8 / 6.3 ms; 2.97 / 7.34 / 6.07 ms | 3.6 / 5.9 ms; 6.14 / 8.24 / 5.44 ms |
| The same message with the names written without `$`, 264 keys: latency; busy | within a 16 ms frame | 3.6 / 6.1 ms; 3.36 / 7.67 / 5.47 ms | 4.1 / 6.1 ms; 6.13 / 8.08 / 5.75 ms |
| Work per streamed token | unchanged | - | unchanged by design; not measured |

- **Before tuning, opening and filtering a list was over the 16 ms guide at the 95th percentile of busy time,** in all four rows that have the list open: 18.65 (cached open), 21.12 (older than 5 s), 16.60 (`review`) and 17.82 ms (`team-w`). Every mean (8 to 14 ms) was inside a frame.
- **What was found:** a Chromium trace and a V8 CPU profile of an unminified build, per key, for an open. About 7 of the 12 ms scaled with the number of rows mounted:
  - JavaScript, about 3.0 ms, mostly React rendering and committing the 120 rows.
  - Style, 1.2 ms, and layout, 1.3 ms.
  - Paint, pre-paint, layer and commit, about 1.0 ms.
  - The filter, the highlight, the `$` probe and the editor's own transaction each cost under 0.1 ms. Caching row elements would not have helped the open or the `team-w` filter, because every row changes in them.
- **What was changed:** the list draws only the rows in view, with `@tanstack/react-virtual` (already in the first-screen chunk, so the bundle does not grow). The rows are a fixed 32 px, drawn in flow inside one box of the full height, so their pixels, the scroll position and the keyboard behaviour are unchanged. The active row is always drawn, so `aria-activedescendant` always names a row in the page, and each option carries `aria-setsize` and `aria-posinset`. The list scrolls the active row into view itself, because the virtualizer's own scroll measures 1 px off the list's padded scroll area. After the change a key costs the same for 12 or 120 skills, and every list row is inside 16 ms at the 95th percentile of both busy time (at most 11.04 ms) and latency (at most 12.7 ms).
- **Pixels are unchanged by the windowing.** The still captures of the lists and the composer (`capture-composer-skills.ts stills`; the `$` list, chips, selected and hovered chip, refusal and queued skill, in both appearances) differ in 0 pixels in the composer and list area from the captures before the change. The only differences elsewhere in those images are the agent avatars, which are drawn differently on each capture, and one `now` label.
- **The cold row's latency once read 19.2 / 24.8 ms, because the probe counted the page's start-up sentinel.** After a reload, the page removes a 1 px element it uses to time its first screen, 15 to 23 ms after the first key. That draws nothing, but the probe counted it as a change of the key. The probe now ignores a change that only adds or removes that sentinel. The Before figure of this row was measured without that rule, so it is not comparable; the first frame (9.5 / 11.0 before, 7.2 / 9.5 ms after) is.
- **The run before tuning, at load 11.2, read the same, a little higher on busy and with outliers.** Busy p95 was 18.6 (cached open), 19.3 (older than 5 s), 19.9 (`review`) and 20.6 ms (`team-w`), and latency p95 15.0, 16.8, 14.0 and 14.4 ms. Two keys took 150.6 and 159.1 ms, one in the `team-w` filter and one in the baseline typing with no `$`: load, not the list, since the baseline has no list.
- **Typing with the list closed reads higher in this run than before (busy mean 6.1 against 3.0 to 3.4 ms), and the same with and without chips.** The list is closed there and the code it runs is unchanged, so this is the machine, not the change. One baseline key took 95 ms.
- **The first open after the thread mounts is not a cold read.** The script reloads the page and types `$` as soon as the message field exists. In all 20 reloads the composer's own read of the skills had already answered, so the list never showed "Reading skills…" and the first frame already held rows. The frame that drew the last change came about 10 ms after the first; what it drew was not examined. A read that is still under way shows "Reading skills…" and takes the controller's and the runner's time on top; that case was not caught.
- **A chip costs the keys after it nothing measurable.** Typing after the 4,000-character message with 10 chips reads the same as typing after the same text with no `$`: busy 6.14 against 6.13 ms mean and 8.24 against 8.08 ms p95, latency p95 5.9 against 6.1 ms (before tuning: 2.97 against 3.36 ms and 7.34 against 7.67 ms). The scan runs after each key only where the text holds a `$`, and is linear in the text.
- **An open that reads again is 1 to 3 ms slower.** 12 of the 40 back-to-back opens found skills older than 5 s and made a request behind the open. Before tuning their latency p50 was 14.6 against 11.7 ms for the 28 that made none (first run: 10.1 against 9.4 ms). After tuning it is 8.6 against 8.7 ms: the difference is gone. The rows come from the cache, so the open does not wait for the request.
- **The first screen was already over its guide.** The 7.8 kB is what this change adds to the first-screen chunks (the lists, the chip plugin and its hover card, the Skill Reference scanner), because the composer is on the first screen. It was not broken down further. Main's startup file is unchanged. Built again after `main` gained the thread blocks change above (0.3 kB on its own), the first screen still reads 441.8 kB across 25 of 109 chunks, not the 442.1 kB the two increases add up to. Why they do not add up was not examined.
- **Work per streamed token is unchanged by design and was not measured.** Only a user message with skills reads the session's skills, once, and nothing in the stream touches the composer.

**Compressed API answers,** measured 2026-10-19 on Rogier's MacBook (Chromium, controller and bundle checks) and on the Beelink (compression cost), with Playwright 1.63's Chromium, a controller started from source in a throwaway home, and `pnpm build:desktop`'s renderer bundle check, for [#791](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/791). The controller now compresses a JSON answer of 1 KiB or more when the request allows it, with brotli if the client accepts it and gzip otherwise ([11 section 1.4](./11-public-api-and-agent-surface.md)), because a thread that is not cached reads its whole transcript, 0.5 to 3 MB of uncompressed text, over Wi-Fi before the screen draws. The renderer changes in one line: `setupQuery` is kept for the life of the window (`gcTime: Infinity`), so the entry guard does not read `setup` again on the first navigation 5 minutes after the last one.

The plan's expected costs:

- **Processes and memory:** none added. The renderer decodes the answer in Chromium's network service, off the main thread, and holds the same decoded JSON as before. The controller holds the compressed copy of one answer while it sends it.
- **Work while idle:** none. Nothing is compressed unless a request asks for it.
- **Work per streamed token:** none. The live socket and its frames are untouched.
- **Bundle bytes:** none measurable. One property on one query option.
- **Controller CPU:** extra work for each large answer, on the controller's work pool rather than its event loop. Its size is measured below, on the MacBook and on the Beelink.

The page measured in Chromium is a stand-in the size of a large transcript page: 500 tasks of 3.5 KB of source text each, 1.9 MB of JSON. The client is Chromium; its network is modelled with the DevTools protocol at the Mac's measured 13 ms round trip and 45 Mbit/s. The time runs from the `fetch` call to the parsed JSON, 8 runs each, with the HTTP cache off, in two rounds. The Before is the same request with `Accept-Encoding: identity`, which is what the controller answered every request with before this change. "gzip only" is a request that asks for gzip alone, as the Android app's OkHttp does; Chromium's own request accepts brotli too.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After, gzip only | After, brotli (Chromium's own request) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Bytes on the wire for a 1.9 MB page | - | 1,903 kB | 169 kB | **105 kB** |
| Fetch and parse over the modelled Wi-Fi, median (range) | - | 425 (419 to 435) and 428 (419 to 437) ms | 66 (59 to 68) and 66 (64 to 68) ms | **59 (51 to 61) and 63 (55 to 64) ms** |
| The same page over loopback, median | - | 9 ms | 15 ms | 14 ms |
| Controller CPU for one such page on the MacBook, 60 reads each, two rounds | - | 3.8 and 3.5 ms | 8.8 and 8.7 ms | 7.3 and 7.0 ms |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 441.8 kB across 25 of 109 chunks | 441.8 kB | 441.8 kB |
| Processes | 4, none added | 4 | 4 | 4 |

- **On the Beelink, 15 real transcript pages (254 to 2,417 kB, as the controller sends them, compact JSON) compress 4.2 to 8.0 times with gzip level 6 and 5.7 to 10.3 times with brotli quality 4,** and brotli costs less CPU on 9 of the 15 pages and a little more on the other 6. Examples, with the page's size, then gzip 6 and brotli 4 as size and CPU: 849 kB: 120 kB in 4.8 ms and 82 kB in 3.2 ms. 1,307 kB: 294 kB in 10.7 ms and 227 kB in 8.3 ms. 2,417 kB: 571 kB in 21.7 ms and 301 kB in 11.2 ms. The pages that gzip compresses worst (4 to 5 times) probably hold the same large text repeated far apart, which brotli's far larger window finds and gzip's 32 kB window does not; this was not checked. The same 1,423 kB page took 5.3 ms of gzip CPU on the Beelink and 4.5 ms on the MacBook (1,457 kB), so an idle Beelink is not slower per byte here. The issue's own proxy, which ran in Node, measured about 30 ms of CPU per 1.8 MB page at level 6.
- **That is why brotli is offered first, and why nothing else is.** Brotli at quality 4 is 11 to 47% smaller than gzip at level 6 on these pages, which on the 2.4 MB page is about 270 kB, 48 ms at 45 Mbit/s, and costs about half the CPU there. Quality 5 and 6 cost about 1.7 times the CPU for 6 to 8% fewer bytes. For gzip, on the 2.4 MB page: level 1 gives 869 kB in 8 ms, level 4 gives 586 kB in 19 ms, level 6 gives 571 kB in 25 ms and level 9 gives 563 kB in 53 ms. The Android app's OkHttp asks for gzip alone, so it gets gzip; Chromium and Bun's `fetch` ask for both. `zstd` and `deflate` are not offered, so no client is sent a coding it cannot read.
- **Compressing does not hold the controller's event loop.** `node:zlib` runs a compression on Bun's work pool. On the Beelink, 16 compressions of the 2.4 MB page at once took 492 ms of CPU in all, 39 ms by the clock, and delayed a 1 ms timer by at most 1.4 ms. Against the real controller on the MacBook, with 8 clients each reading the 1.9 MB page 15 times at once, a small `setup.read` took 12.5 to 13.1 ms at the 95th percentile with gzip, 19.8 to 20.7 ms with brotli and 24.4 to 25.3 ms with no compression, and at most 30 ms in any case. The small reads and the big ones ran in one process on one machine, so those figures show only that compression adds no stall, not what a request costs.
- **Under contention, gzip took about 1.5 times its idle CPU and brotli 1.8 to 2.8 times, so the two cost about the same.** The Beelink was idle (a load average of 0.3), not at the 10 to 40 the issue describes, so 24 busy loops were run on its 16 cores for the measurement, and stopped after it. The 2.4 MB page then took 32.8 ms of CPU and 55.4 ms by the clock with gzip, and 31.0 and 51.7 ms with brotli; the 1.3 MB page 15.5 and 22.0 ms with gzip and 19.4 and 35.2 ms with brotli; the 0.85 MB page 7.0 and 11.8 ms with gzip and 5.7 and 7.6 ms with brotli. The timer's delay was at most 12 ms. This is the compression's cost beside CPU-bound neighbours, not the Beelink's usual load, which is a mix of work that was not reproduced.
- **Not measured: a thread open from click to screen after this change.** The issue's own run, with a proxy compressing at level 6, read 605 to 619 ms down to 266 to 269 ms for the 2.4 MB thread and 287 to 306 ms down to 142 to 158 ms for the 1.15 MB one. That harness was not repeated. The Chromium rows above are the same effect on one page.
- **A thread that is already cached is unchanged,** and so is a short answer. An answer under 1 KiB is not compressed, and an attachment's image never is.
- **The first screen's JavaScript did not change at the precision of the check.** The renderer was built with and without the `gcTime` line, on this branch's base, and read 441.8 kB across 25 of 109 chunks both times. It is still over its 250 kB guide, as it was before this change.

**Thread blocks, a usage-only nudge,** measured 2026-10-19 with a render count in jsdom (the renderer's React Compiler config), a microbenchmark of the real block code, and the renderer's `vite build` counted by the bundle budget script, on e1a43298 (Before, the change's parent) and on 6ce3839b (After), for [#785](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/785). A usage-only `session` nudge changes only Token Usage or the tool call count. The controller sends one about once a second while a turn runs, whether the thread is visible or hidden. Such a nudge no longer rebuilds the open thread's blocks and no longer draws its spawn lines again. `AgentPage` builds its agent state in a `select` on the session read, so Query keeps the same agent state object while it is equal, and the transcript's blocks `select` stays the same function. `SpawnLines` selects only the open Requests. The change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic. A streamed token and a stored row cost the same as before, except that the agent `select` now runs on each render of the page: a filter over the open Requests and a compare of four fields, which takes microseconds. The bundle was built for each side, with sizes counted the way the budget script counts them, 1,024 bytes to a kB, gzipped.

The render count uses a thread of 531 stored rows (17 turns, 8 commands a turn, 3 subagent spawns in turns 14 to 16), with the last turn open and the session `busy`. The page is 1200 px wide. It delivers 10 usage-only nudges, and each re-read returns the session with `toolCalls` one higher and a changed `usage`.

| Over the 10 nudges | 900 px page, Before | 900 px page, After | 1600 px page, Before | 1600 px page, After |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `buildThreadBlocks` calls | 10 | 0 | 10 | 0 |
| `SpawnLines` renders | 20 | 0 | 20 | 0 |
| `Transcript` renders | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

The microbenchmark runs the real `buildThreadBlocks`, `buildSessionAgentState` and Query's `replaceEqualDeep` (5.102.8) on Node 24.14 (V8, the renderer's engine). The transcripts are synthetic, about 26 rows a turn (commands, tool calls, file changes, reasoning, agent messages), with the last turn running. Before is the cost of one nudge: build the agent state, build the blocks and compare the blocks. After is build the agent state and compare it. Each value is the median of 40 interleaved samples after 20 warm-up rounds, at a one-minute load average of 2 to 3.5.

| Stored rows (blocks) | Before, median / p95 | After, median |
|---|---|---|
| 262 (32) | 0.071 / 0.182 ms | 0.0003 ms |
| 526 (64) | 0.312 / 0.406 ms | 0.0006 ms |
| 1,021 (124) | 0.460 / 0.772 ms | 0.0003 ms |
| 2,011 (244) | 0.526 / 0.699 ms | 0.0003 ms |

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 441.8 kB across 25 chunks | 441.6 kB across 25 chunks |
| The thread's page chunk (`agent-page`, holds `SpawnLines`), gzipped | its own chunk | 17.1 kB | 16.9 kB |

- **Two spawn lines are mounted at both page heights.** `Transcript` drew on neither side, because the compiler keeps its element while its props are equal.
- **Before grows with the transcript and After is flat.** A warm microbenchmark has no GC and a hot JIT, so it reads lower than the packaged app: the profile behind [#783](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/783) measured about 2 ms for the same `select` and compare at about 950 rows. At one nudge a second, that is 0.05 to 0.2% of a core for the open thread, for as long as a turn runs. The change removes it.
- **No packaged-app trace was taken.** A Chromium trace on this machine varies 20 to 50% between runs and cannot resolve a saving of this size (see the notes of the Thread blocks entry above).
- **What still runs per usage nudge:**
  - On a session's own page, `AgentPage` renders once (cheap lookups and the agent `select`) and `ThreadHeader` draws.
  - On a subagent's page, `splitSubagentBrief` still copies the block list on each render, so `Transcript` draws again there while its memoized blocks skip. This is a follow-up and is not part of this change.

**A thread that is still opening,** measured 2026-10-19 for [#792](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/792) with the renderer's `vite build` and the first-screen check of `pnpm build:desktop`, on `main` at 0d1c1bac (Before) and on this change rebased on it (After).

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 441.6 kB across 25 chunks of 109 | 441.6 kB across 24 of 108: no growth, still over the guide by 191.6 kB |
| Processes | 4, none added | 4 | 4 |
| Reads | none added | - | none: the opening state draws from the shell's session lists, already cached |
| Work while idle, and per streamed token | none added | - | none: the state exists only while the thread's loader runs, and holds no timer, subscription or stream |

- **The previous thread stays mounted, hidden, until the new one renders.** React hides it behind the opening state rather than unmounting it, so its live subscriptions run for the length of the load, as they did before, when it stayed on screen.
- **Pixels.** In the thread specimen, at 1440 x 900 in both appearances, the opening state's header is the loaded thread's header pixel for pixel; only the transcript area and the composer differ.

**Thread copies, issue [#796](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/796),** measured 2026-10-19 on the reference Mac (MacBook Pro Mac16,5, Apple M4 Max, 64 GB, macOS 15.8) against the plan in [What thread copies cost](#what-thread-copies-cost). The Mac was shared with other agents, with a load average of 1.9 to 3.3 during the runs, so the noise is large. The browser is Chrome for Testing 153.0.8010.12, headless (Electron 44 is Chromium 152). The build is the branch at 7824c06b; the review fixes after it do not change the timing of the open or read path. The Mac reached Rogier's controller on the Beelink over Wi-Fi and Tailscale: a tiny GET took 12.9 to 17.0 ms (median about 15.4 ms), with no stall in 10 samples.

The method for opening a thread: the built renderer is served by a local Node server that proxies `GET /api` to the real controller. There is no network model, and writes are refused. The bridge is a fake and the live connection is stubbed. Playwright clicks the thread's sidebar row. The time runs from the click to the second animation frame after the header shows the thread and a transcript row exists. Each thread was opened 7 times. The four threads are real transcripts of 0.54 MB (236 rows), 1.15 MB (209), 2.40 MB (708) and 3.08 MB (1204).

The deployed controller predates `after` and ignores it. So the read that brings a copy up to date was measured two ways:

- **Emulated:** the proxy answered `after` as this branch's controller does, after one real read to the controller for the network cost.
- **Against the deployed controller.** The app now recognises a controller that ignores `after` and uses its answer as the full read, so this mode is only the version-skew case.

Opening a thread, from the click to the first frame, in ms, median (minimum to maximum):

| Thread | No copy (network) | From copy | Cached in the query cache |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.54 MB | 211 (166 to 305) | 95 (40 to 126) | 58 (29 to 116) |
| 1.15 MB | 264 (162 to 526) | 145 (71 to 153) | 94 (48 to 110) |
| 2.40 MB | 325 (314 to 398) | 92 (58 to 168) | 50 (30 to 81) |
| 3.08 MB | 399 (356 to 552) | 104 (54 to 172) | 70 (26 to 108) |

The from-copy and cached columns use the emulated `after`. Against the deployed controller, the open from a copy took 114, 113, 71 and 97 ms.

- **The open from a copy does not grow with the transcript.** It stays near 0.1 s from 0.54 to 3.08 MB, while the open from the network grows from 0.2 to 0.4 s over this link. A cached thread opens in about 0.05 s.
- **The open from a copy waits on nothing from the network.** With every read of the thread held for 2 s at the proxy, it still opened in 60 to 152 ms. "Syncing…" showed, at 0.9 s and at 3.4 s.
- **The read that brings a copy up to date (`after`) costs 14, 9, 13 and 18 ms** (medians, in the order of the table), in one request. The threads had finished, so it returned no rows and "Syncing…", which shows after 400 ms, never showed.
- **Network stalls ([#793](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/793)) hit a few no-copy runs:** 0.98 s in one, and 1.4 to 2.5 s in a first pass. They only touch the open from the network.

The cost of reading and saving a copy, in ms, medians of 9 (the structured clone is the part of the save that runs inside the `put`):

| Transcript | Read, with the schema check | Longest block during the read | Save, `put` to commit | Structured clone in the `put` | Longest block during the save |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.54 MB | 5.3 | 1.8 | 4.2 | 1.2 | 2.1 |
| 1.15 MB | 7.0 | 3.2 | 5.2 | 2.1 | 4.1 |
| 2.40 MB | 11.1 | 6.1 | 8.9 | 5.2 | 5.3 |
| 3.08 MB | 13.6 | 7.0 | 9.3 | 5.6 | 5.8 |

- **The first read after a page load, which opens the database, took 1.8 to 16.8 ms.**
- **No long task (over 50 ms) comes from a read or a save.** The longest block is 7.0 ms at 3.08 MB.
- **In the app, the longest block around a page close with its save was about 25 ms at every size.** The screen change causes it, not the save.

| Measure | Plan | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | none added |
| Work while idle | none | none: the save runs only when a page closes and when a turn ends |
| Work per streamed token | none | none, by the same design |
| Memory | the same as a network open | JS heap after a forced GC, 2.4 MB thread, 7 runs: 20.3 MB with no thread open, 25.7 MB opened from the network and 25.7 MB opened from its copy, identical in every run |
| Disk | about 0.38 times the JSON size | 2.48 MB for the four copies (2,456 KB of blobs and 24 KB of LevelDB) against 7.17 MB of transcript JSON, 0.35 times |
| Bundle bytes | a few kB gzipped | the first screen, gzipped: 441.6 kB at 046f7120, 444.3 kB on the branch, +2.7 kB |

- **Every expectation held.** One number is above its plan: the read of the 3.08 MB copy took 13.6 ms against about 11 ms for 3.1 MB. That is inside a frame and inside the noise of a shared Mac. The save (9.3 ms, longest block 5.8 ms) is under the plan's 18 and 13 ms, and the open from a copy (about 0.1 s) is under the 0.2 to 0.4 s of a network open over this link.
- **The first screen is over its 250 kB guide by 192 to 194 kB, before and after.** That predates this change; the guide passes.

**A hidden window and the thread's rows,** measured 2026-10-19 with a Chromium trace and `app.getAppMetrics()` on the packaged app against a scratch controller, on `main` at f09116eea (Before) and on this change (After), for [#783](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/783). A hidden window keeps the `:stream` subscribed, but the open thread, subagent page and Conversation now keep the rows it delivers aside, and write them to the transcript in one write when the window is shown. The kept rows are dropped if something else writes the transcript first, such as the read after a reset. The change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no topic.

The scenario is a thread of 709 rows open, then a tool-heavy turn that stores 256 rows in 22.8 s. Before and After runs were interleaved, at a one-minute load average of 2 to 8. Before is 3 runs, and its cells are ranges. After is 1 complete run for the hidden and visible rows. Three more After runs failed in the harness's own sign-in step, where a typed key reached the window, and were discarded. After is 2 runs for each show scenario.

| Hidden, turn running | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Renderer main thread busy (trace) | 1638 to 1857 ms | 1127 ms |
| JavaScript (trace) | 1184 to 1340 ms | 635 ms |
| React commits (batches of DOM mutation records) | 61 to 63 | 0 |
| Timer fires | 527 to 531 | 279 |
| Renderer CPU, share of one core | 4.6 to 6.2% | 4.2% |

A hidden window with no turn running costs the same in both builds: the renderer is busy 166 to 173 ms and uses 0.4 to 0.7% of a core. A visible window with the turn running is unchanged within the noise: busy 591 to 981 ms Before and 975 ms After, 170 to 188 commits Before and 172 After.

A CPU profile of the Before build, hidden with the turn running, shows where a stored row went. The values are medians of 3 runs, per stored row.

| Per stored row, Before | JavaScript |
|---|---|
| The page's `select` inside `setQueryData` (`buildThreadBlocks` and its comparison) | about 2.0 ms |
| React render and commit | about 2.3 ms |
| Receiving and decoding | about 0.8 ms |
| The hook's merge | about 0.5 ms |
| Invalidations from `session` nudges, not from the stream ([#785](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/785)) | about 0.5 ms |

After was not profiled. The page's `select` runs only when the transcript is written, which a hidden window no longer does, and the After trace shows no commits.

| Showing the window again | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| 256 rows missed: `show()` to the last row on screen | 15 to 20 ms | 16 to 25 ms |
| 256 rows missed: longest task | 5 ms | 5 ms |
| 1624 rows missed: `show()` to the last row on screen | 13 to 19 ms | 11 to 14 ms |
| 1624 rows missed: longest task | 5 ms | 4 ms |

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | 434.3 kB | 434.4 kB: 0.1 kB more |

- **A hidden row costs about half the JavaScript, and the hidden page no longer commits.** The trace's JavaScript falls from 1184 to 1340 ms to 635 ms, and React commits from 61 to 63 to 0.
- **What remains is receiving and decoding the rows, and the `session` nudges.** The nudges do not come from the stream; see [#785](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/785).
- **Showing the window costs the same as before, whatever the number of rows kept aside.** 256 and 1624 rows both take 11 to 25 ms to the last row on screen, with a longest task of 5 ms or less, and the transcript ends at the bottom in both builds. Nothing is subscribed again and nothing is replayed.
- **The CPU share is one After run, so the trace counters carry the comparison.** The share's Before range (4.6 to 6.2%) is wide, and the After value sits just below it.

**Scroll bars in the theme's ink** (thin, with a hover state, 2026-10-20), measured in scratch Electron 44.4.5 renderers with the app's own stylesheets, `main` against the change. These are scratch pages, not the packaged app: the perf script needs `./hercule` and the packaged test app, which were not built. A review run by another agent measured them independently of the change's author.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| A theme change, 4,005 elements | a frame that restyles the page | 2.9 to 3.1 ms | 3.15 to 3.16 ms |
| A theme change, 48,005 elements | - | 30.9 ms | 33.1 to 33.7 ms |
| Blink memory, 48,005 elements | - | 48,437 KiB | 48,440 KiB |
| Style work over 100 pointer moves, 4,005 elements | - | 0 ms | 5.6 to 7.0 ms |
| Renderer wakeups while idle | none | 1 to 2 a second | 1 a second |
| Renderer CSS, raw and gzipped | a guide | 178,393 and 48,921 bytes | 178,599 and 48,963 bytes: +206 and +42 |

- **A pointer move costs about 0.06 ms of style work,** rising to 0.15 ms with 48,005 elements. `*:hover` matches every ancestor of the pointer, so plain transcript text now restyles where only rows and buttons did before. No layout and no content paint followed in any hover sequence.
- **`scrollbar-color` is declared on every element on purpose.** Set once on the root and overridden on hover, one hover restyles every descendant: 1.6 ms for 3,900 elements, against 0.1 ms declared on each.
- **Scrolling stays on the compositor.** The scroll bar is a composited layer in the classic and the overlay style, before and after, and wheel scrolling caused no layout or style recalculation in either.
- **Not measured: a streamed token in the real transcript.** A streaming-shaped text write on a 4,208-element fixture cost 0.10 ms before and 0.10 to 0.11 ms after, which resolves no difference. The packaged app's launch, memory and streaming rows stay unproven until `pnpm test:desktop` and the perf script run on a built app.

**Collapsed bubbles,** measured 2026-10-20 with `vite build` of the renderer on `main` at 5394ba8fc (Before) and on this change (After), for [#810](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/810). The change adds no process, no timer, no read and no subscription.

| Measure | Budget | After |
|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | none added |
| Memory | one `ResizeObserver` per mounted bubble with text | the bubbles on screen and the list's overscan of 6 blocks each way |
| Work while idle, and per streamed token | none added | none: the observer reports only when a bubble changes size, and a bubble is `memo`, so a streamed token does not draw it again |
| The first screen's JavaScript | grows by nothing | the same bytes |
| The transcript's chunk | - | 25,879 to 26,881 bytes: 1,002 bytes more, 322 gzipped |
| The messages' and markdown's CSS | - | 300 and 10 bytes more, 138 and 4 gzipped |

**Tracking, slice 1,** measured 2026-10-20 with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --tracking-only`, `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks and `pnpm test:speed`, on the branch base e65cb2a82 (Before) and on this change (After; the controller read, the bundle sizes and one more perf run again at f8533d92c), for [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801). *(Added 2026-10-20, [#801](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/801).)* Sizes are gzipped bytes, counted the way the budget script counts them. The change adds no process, no timer and no polling. It adds one read the shell keeps, `task.queryTracking`, and two live topics, `task` (also while hidden) and `run` (visible only). The perf script takes the pair of idle launches from one controller, one preparation and one warm-up: the 10 Tasks are handed off first, `prepareLaunch` moves their runs three hours back, the launch with 10 tracked comes first, and `releaseTracking` marks the Tasks done before the launch with nothing tracked. Each launch is read with nothing attached, at 13 s and 30 s as the canonical launches are; the age clock is not counted in them. Footprint is the median of 6 launches at Before and 12 at After, on a shared machine at a load average of 3 to 29. The interaction rows come from one app with a CDP collector attached from before the page loads, so a push and a read are counted on the wire, a request is timed by the protocol, and the motion rows err long by the cost of the attachment.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes | none added | 4 | 4 |
| Footprint, summed, Intake open on 200 signals | at most 220 MB | 222.1 MB (219.9 to 239.0) | nothing tracked **218.9 MB** (215.6 to 242.3); 10 tracked **218.8 MB** (217.3 to 245.4) |
| Footprint, renderer | at most 100 MB | 62.3 MB (61.5 to 64.3) | nothing tracked 62.9 MB; 10 tracked **64.2 MB** (63.9 to 64.6) |
| Idle, visible and hidden | no work added | renderer **1** wakeup a second visible and **0** hidden, GPU **0** | the same, with 10 tracked and with nothing tracked |
| Launch, spawn to Intake's signals | 800 ms | 355 to 444 ms to the window shown | 382 to 491 ms to the window shown, both launches |
| Reads while the shell loads | one `task.queryTracking`, and one more for each first message of `task` and `run` | - | 3 `task.queryTracking` (1 and the first messages of the 2 topics), 0 `run.read` |
| A push with nothing tracked | one read for each `task` push; at most one for each run's creation | - | 10 reads for 10 `task` pushes; 10 reads for 10 runs, which made 22 `run` pushes |
| A push with 10 tracked | the same | - | the same limits met: 10 reads for 10 `task` pushes, 10 for 10 runs and 21 pushes; renderer main thread 2.7 to 4.6 ms a push |
| 10 handoffs, one at a time | at least one read for each handoff, at most one for each push, no `run.read` | - | 21 reads for 31 pushes, **0** `run.read`; the body is 6.5 kB at 10 rows |
| The window hidden | `task` held, `run` dropped; a Task reads once; a run reads nothing; showing the window reads once | - | met: 9 topics held hidden without `run`; 1 read for the Task; 0 for the run; 1 read when shown, with `run` held again |
| The read on the controller | on an index | - | 10 tracked Tasks **0.21 ms**; 1,000 **6.86 ms** (tracked Tasks 1.97 ms, newest runs 3.97 ms; 534 kB); with 100,000 handoffs on a done Task 6.79 ms; with 10,000 unrelated in-progress Tasks **9.44 ms** (tracked Tasks 4.61 ms); with 10,000 handoffs on one Task 7.29 ms; a handoff's Task lookup 0.04 ms. The benchmark's ceiling is 100 ms at 1,000 |
| Opening Tracking | 14 animations (1 + 13), 450 ms, compositor only | - | 14 animations, **452 ms** to the last frame, no dropped frame (longest gap 9.7 ms against 8.3 ms), 0 left after |
| Closing Tracking | one animation, 180 ms | - | **1** animation, 188 ms to the last frame |
| A state change, a handoff, a take-off | compositor only; nothing left after | - | 3, 5 and 4 animations; last frames at 453, 446 and 448 ms; 0 animations and 0 copies left after each |
| The first screen's JavaScript | none new beyond the count's reads | 445.0 kB across 24 chunks (first-screen chunk 390,089 bytes) | **446.8 kB** across 24 chunks (392,023 bytes) |
| Intake's chunk | recorded; not budgeted | 24,623 bytes of JavaScript (69,958 raw), 3,971 of CSS | **31,874** bytes (91,881 raw), 4,006 of CSS |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 155.8 kB | 155.8 kB |
| The perf script's runtime, `--tracking-only` | none | about 9 to 10 minutes, by the sum of its fixed waits; not run again | **185 s**; `--tracking-fast` **130 s** |

- **The summed footprint is over 220 MB, and the base is already over it.** Before measured 222.1 MB at the median, with one launch at 239.0 MB. The renderer grows by 1.3 MB from nothing tracked to 10 tracked and by 1.9 MB from Before to 10 tracked, which is 10 rows and their run facts in the query cache; it stays 36 MB under its 100 MB. The rest of the swing is the GPU process, which sits at 96 to 101 MB in most launches and at 112 to 118 MB in some: 1 of 6 launches at Before, 2 of 12 with nothing tracked and 4 of 12 with 10 tracked. The 230 MB seen earlier on this branch was such a launch. The counts do not show the GPU's high mode following Tracking, but 4 of 12 against 2 of 12 is too few launches to rule it out. A run with more launches, or a trace of the GPU process, settles it.
- **The first screen's JavaScript is 1.8 kB bigger, on a budget it is already 195 kB over;** the budget is a guide in this milestone. The reads, the two subscriptions and the state function join the first screen because the To do count shows on every screen. Intake's chunk is 7.3 kB bigger, for the sheet, its motion, the flight and the rows.
- **Opening Tracking ends 2 ms after the 450 ms the spec names.** The measure allows three frames after a motion's length, as Intake's rows do, and the last frame comes 452 ms after the first. An earlier run on this branch, before the sheet's close became a real exit, read 458 ms and 22 animations, 8 of them hover and focus transitions. The script now leaves transitions out of the count, and the sheet's own animations are 14.
- **A handoff's flight starts about 0.9 s after the click:** the time of the `signal.handOff` request, the `task` and `run` pushes and the read. The motion is counted from the flight's first frame. The perf script's Review PR waits 4 s, so the run is still Working when the recording ends. With a wait of 1 s, the row's change to Finished fell inside the recording and was counted in the handoff's motion.
- **No `run.read` is made for a Failed row's reason.** An earlier run on this branch, before `task.queryTracking` landed, made 21 `task.query` and 21 `run.query` reads and 2 `run.read` for the same 10 handoffs; the reason is now in the read.
