- **One launch read 565 ms to the window shown,** in a re-run at a load average of 54 over the minute before it; two more runs at a load of 4 to 8 read 399 to 434 ms. The first run's range of 382 to 491 ms stands as the quiet-machine reading.
- **The perf script's time fell from about 9 to 10 minutes to 185 s.** The old script slept a fixed time after each step, counted the age clock for 60 s in each launch and seeded one Task at a time. The new one waits for the state, the reads and the animations, seeds in parallel, and uses one controller, one preparation and one warm-up for the pair. The warm-up stays: without it the renderer reads 3 MB higher. The age clock is not counted in Tracking's launches, because the rows show hours and read the shared clock, which Intake's own launch counts.

**Tracking, slice 2,** measured 2026-10-21 with `apps/desktop/scripts/perf.ts --tracking-only`, `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks, `pnpm test:speed` and a throwaway controller, on main at 491a5075a (Before) and on this change at 76b3aebea (After), for [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802). *(Added 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* The change adds no process, no timer and no polling. In the design measured here it adds one read the shell keeps, Your work (`task.query { refs }`), one subscription to the query cache that keeps it current, `liveRunIds` on Tracking's read, and the Needs you rows built from reads the shell already held.

*(Added 2026-10-21, [#802](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/802).)* **These numbers are for an earlier design.** They were taken at 76b3aebea, where the client read Your work with batched `task.query { refs }` requests. The final design, `signal.queryYourWork`, replaced the batched read and was not measured, because on 2026-10-21 Rogier paused desktop perf runs until the perf script is made much faster. What is known of the final design's cost is the controller's read time (12 to 17 ms, see Tracking: Your work above) and the request counts the integration tests assert. The rows below that mention `task.query { refs }`, batches, slim Tasks or refs describe the earlier design only.

How it was measured:

- **The data.** The perf script makes 60 of the 200 To do signals signals about a thread, and 10 open Tasks that carry the refs of 3 of those threads each, plus two refs no signal is about. `signal.raise` makes core signals only, so the fixture rewrites the origin of the 60 oldest open signals in the stopped controller's database, as it already does for their age. A Task has a 600-character description and five provenance entries, which is 1,244 bytes on the wire. The Tasks are not tracked, so no row turns Needs you during the run and the read is measured on its own. `--tracking-thread-signals=200` makes all 200 signals about a thread (2 runs of each build, in fast mode, for the batches).
- **Before and After** are two clean worktrees (`git worktree add`) with the same perf scripts, each with its own build of the binary and the app. 16 runs of each, taken one after the other in turn (the order swapped every pair), on one machine shared with other sessions. Every run has two idle launches, one with 10 Tasks tracked and one with nothing tracked, so the footprint and launch rows are 32 launches of each build. The interaction launch of each run gives the other rows: 16 runs of Before and 15 of After, because one run of After stopped in the state-change step (see the last findings). The load average before the launches was 3.2 at the median (2.0 to 13.9) for Before and 2.9 (2.1 to 6.5) for After. Medians are given with the range in brackets. Sizes are gzipped bytes, counted the way the budget script counts them.
- The requests are counted from the wire by the CDP collector, which tells `task.query { refs }` (Your work) and the To do read apart from other reads.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |

| Footprint, renderer | at most 100 MB | 64.2 MB (62.9 to 67.2) | 65.5 MB (64.1 to 68.1) |
| Footprint, GPU process | recorded | 96.8 MB (95.4 to 112.9); above 105 MB in 1 launch | 96.8 MB (95.5 to 97.3); in none |
| Footprint, summed without the GPU process | recorded | 121.8 MB (119.8 to 125.1) | 123.6 MB (121.1 to 126.3) |
| Idle, visible and hidden | no work added | renderer **1** wakeup a second visible in all 32 launches, **0** hidden in 30 and 1 in 2; GPU 0 | renderer **1** visible and **0** hidden in all 32 launches; GPU 0 |
| Launch, spawn to Intake's signals | 800 ms | 378 ms (353 to 474) | 381 ms (363 to 395) |
| Launch, spawn to the window shown | 500 ms | 434 ms (372 to 508) | 417 ms (371 to 472) |

| A `task` push, nothing tracked / 10 tracked | one pass of the batches for each push | 10 `task.queryTracking` for 10 pushes, 0 for Your work | 10 `task.queryTracking` and **10** `task.query { refs }` for 10 pushes, in both cases. 200 refs: 40 |
| A `run` push | none for Your work | 22 to 23 pushes read 10 times for Tracking (the 10 runs' creations) | the same 10 reads, **0** for Your work, for 21 to 23 pushes, both nothing tracked and 10 tracked |

| Tracking's reply with 10 tracked / after 10 handoffs | recorded | 5,910 / 6,564 bytes | 6,184 / 6,952 bytes (27 bytes a row more: `liveRunIds`) |

| Renderer main thread for a `run` push, nothing tracked / 10 tracked | recorded | 3.2 ms / 5.8 ms | 3.1 ms / 5.0 ms |

Each read took 2.0 to 2.6 ms from the client to the end of the body. Not measured again: `task.query` did not change |

- **The summed footprint is at its budget, and over it in nearly half the launches (earlier design).** Before was over 220 MB in 7 of 32 launches (22%; the median is 218.4 MB). After is over in 15 of 32 (47%; the median is 219.9 MB, the worst 223.1 MB). The GPU process is 96.8 MB of it in both builds and is not the app's code. Without it the sum is 1.8 MB higher in After. The renderer, at 65.5 MB, is 34.5 MB under its own 100 MB. The GPU's high mode (104 to 118 MB) showed in 1 of 32 Before launches and 0 of 32 After launches, and in 1 of 10 Before and 2 of 10 After launches in a second series, so it does not follow the build.
- **The 1.3 MB of renderer growth is still there after slim Tasks, and it is not Your work's data or read.** (Slim Tasks were an intermediate step of the earlier design: the client kept only a Task's id, title, status, update time and refs. The final design has no such step, because the server sends only those fields.) An intermediate build with the slim Tasks and the shared build (a3e4e9c6d) read 66.7 MB for the renderer in 6 launches, so neither change lowered it. What was found:
  - *Not the read.* A build in which `yourWorkQuery` answers from the cached To do list with no request and no Tasks, measured in 10 launches next to 10 of each other build in one series: renderer 65.4 MB, against 65.1 MB with the read and 64.1 MB Before; summed 219.4, 219.1 and 218.4 MB.
  - *Not live JavaScript objects.* After a forced garbage collection, the renderer's JavaScript heap holds 12.02 MB in After and 11.84 MB in Before, which is 0.19 MB more. Code is 66 KB of that, closures 20 KB, and the page has 6 DOM nodes more.
  - *One memory region.* `vmmap` of the renderer at the 13 s reading, in 10 launches of each build: the median footprint rises 0.95 MB, and all of it is the region "Memory Tag 255" (31.95 to 32.95 MB dirty). The malloc zones, the stacks and "Memory Tag 253" (-0.1 MB) do not move. The page's live heap does not account for it, so it is space the engine keeps after the extra work at the start (the two reads of Your work, the matching of 200 signals).
  - *Not data kept.* With a forced collection before the reading, both renderers fall by about 14 MB. Then After is 1.4 MB over Before at the median (51.6 against 50.1 MB), with ranges that overlap (49.7 to 53.9 and 49.4 to 53.4 MB) in 8 launches each, which is too few to call it a difference.
  - It is not attributed to one piece of code. The footprint was not measured at 200 refs.
- **A `task` push costs 2.6 to 3.7 ms more renderer main thread, and about 6.5 ms more at 200 refs.** The read, its JSON and the matching are in it. The measure counts the script's polling too, so read it as the cost of one request. It is recorded, not budgeted. A `run` push, 10 handoffs and a signal about no thread cost the same as Before.
- **The read is on the shell's critical path, and costs nothing visible (earlier design).** It reads To do first (one read the shell makes anyway, joined, not repeated), then the batches in parallel. The window shows no later than Before (417 ms against 434 ms).
- **The first message of the `task` topic reads Your work again at every start,** as it reads Tracking again (3 `task.queryTracking` at load). That is 2 passes at load where 1 would do. The same two reads are in slice 1's numbers for Tracking, so this slice does not make it worse. The final design keeps this: one `signal.queryYourWork` at load, plus at most one for the first message of each of the `signal` and `task` topics.
- **`task.queryTracking` is 27 bytes a row bigger** for `liveRunIds`, and the controller's read 3% slower at 1,000 tracked Tasks (6.86 to 7.07 ms). The ids come from the statement that already finds the live run.
- **The perf script's state-change step failed in 2 of 17 runs of an earlier build, and 1 of 16 runs of After here (Before 0 of 16). The cause is the script, not the app, and it is fixed.** The step cancels a Working row and waited for the number of Stopped rows on screen to grow. Folded Tracking shows only the two rows that changed state last. Cancelling the Stopped row of the handoffs a moment before the last Review PR run finishes leaves the two newest rows as Finished and that Stopped row. Cancelling the next run then pushes the old Stopped row out of the fold as the new one comes in, so the count stays at 1 and the step never sees its end state. The old cancel comes 0.01 to 0.07 s after the second newest finish in 11 of the 12 runs where it was logged and 0.01 s before it in the failing run, so the order depends on timing. In 12 more runs of After with that gap logged, the one that failed was the one run where the old Stopped row was among the two newest ("finished", "stopped" on screen), and the error said the end state was not drawn and nothing was running. A forced version of the same order (a Review PR run finishing after a cancel, before the state change) failed the old script 2 of 2 in the state-change step on Before's build, and the fixed script, which looks for the row of the cancelled run by its title, passed 2 of 2. The failure message now also says whether the end state was drawn and what was still running.
- **The motion rows passed in every run** (16 of Before, 15 of After). Opening Tracking was 14 animations, with the last frame at 460 ms at the median (452 to 466) Before and 461 ms (453 to 469) After, inside the three frames the measure allows after 450 ms.

**What Rogier must decide.** The summed footprint budget of 220 MB is missed in 47% of After's launches (15 of 32, at most 3.1 MB over), where Before missed it in 22% (7 of 32). The median moved from 218.4 to 219.9 MB. The desktop rule in AGENTS.md ([ADR 0037](../adr/0037-the-desktop-app-is-its-own-electron-client-of-the-public-api.md)) says a change that misses a budget does not merge. The growth is 1.0 MB in one memory region of the renderer that is not live data, and no piece of code was found to cause it. Options: accept it as measured; raise the summed budget (225 MB covers every After launch and all but one Before launch); or look for the 1 MB in the engine's heap sizing before this merges. The first screen's JavaScript is 198.2 kB over its guide, 1.4 kB more than Before, and the guide does not gate. These figures are from the earlier design, and the final design was not measured, so the decision is open: it needs a measurement of the final design once the perf script is fast enough to run again.

**Concerns found while measuring the earlier design** (for the pane ticket [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803) or a follow-up). Both are gone in the final design, where `signal.queryYourWork` returns only the fields Your work uses and is one request for each push, whatever the number of signals:

- **`task.query` returns whole Tasks:** the description and every provenance entry, 90 bytes each, which only ever grow. Your work uses a Task's id, title, status, update time and refs. At 200 entries a Task is 19 kB, and a pass reads each Task that carries one of the refs. A read that returns just those fields, or a `fields` option on `task.query`, would cut the bytes by about 90% for old Tasks.
- **A push re-reads every batch.** Requests for each push are the number of batches, 4 at 200 signals, and a change to the thread refs re-reads all of them, not only the new ref's batch. Busy agents write Tasks all day. Tracking's read is 1 request for each push, and Your work is up to 4 on top of it. Reading again only when the push names a Task of Your work cannot be done: a new ref added to any Task makes it Your work.

**Tracking, slice 3,** measured 2026-10-21 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks (the renderer and main builds and both budget checks, without the packaging), the controller's speed test (`RUN_SPEED_TESTS=1`, `tracking.speed.test.ts`) and the integration tests, on main at 9f1a7b2ef (Before) and on this change at b647335c4 (After, after the review fixes; the sizes were first read at 10e84dce8, before them), for [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803). *(Added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* The change adds no process, no timer and no polling, and no live topic: the pane's two reads are made only while a pane is open, and are kept current by the `task` and `run` topics Tracking already holds. **The perf script was not run**, because on 2026-10-21 Rogier paused its Tracking runs until it is made faster. So every row that needs the running app is not measured (see the table, and the second finding). Sizes are gzipped bytes, counted the way the budget script counts them, from two builds of the renderer at Before (the numbers were the same) and one at each of the two After commits. The requests are counted in `intake.tracking.integration.test.tsx`, which renders the app in jsdom against a stub of the controller's API and counts the stub's calls by path. That is a count of requests the client sends, not of the wire, and it says nothing of time. The controller's read is the median of 25 reads in the speed test, run 3 times, on a shared machine at a load average of 6: one Task with 1,000 runs of a 50-step plan each, and a 1 MB text output on the newest run.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| Processes, footprint, idle, launch, motion, main-thread time | as in the rows of slices 1 and 2 | - | **not measured** (perf script paused) |
| Opening a row's pane | one `task.readTracking` and one `signal.read` | - | 1 `GET /tasks/:id/tracking` and 1 `GET /signals/:id`, no other read of a Task, a run or a signal |
| `J` to the next row | the same for its own Task, and nothing more for the row left | - | 1 and 1 for the new row; 0 more for the row left |
| `J` three times with the pane hidden (Esc) *(added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803))* | nothing: no pane is open | the route's loader read the detail of each selected Task: 1 read for each `J` (found in review) | **0** detail reads, tested in `intake.tracking.integration.test.tsx`; the loader's prefetch is gone |
| A `run` push that names the open row's newest run | one read of the open pane's detail | - | 1 detail read, 0 `signal.read`, and Tracking's own read as before |
| A `run` push for a run no row holds | none | - | 0 requests of any kind |
| A `run` push that names another row's run, while a pane is open *(added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803))* | the open pane's detail is not read | every push that read Tracking again also read every cached detail again, so the open pane's (found in review) | **0** detail reads of the open pane and of the other row; Tracking read again once. `findTrackingTasksAffectedByRunPush` names the Tasks a push reaches, and a push with no ids or the creation of a run still reaches every detail. Tested in `live.test.ts` and the integration test |
| A `task` push that names the open row's Task, a `signal` push on its handoff signal | one detail read for the `task` push | - | **not counted by a test**; only the keys that go stale are tested (`live.test.ts`) |
| The pane's read on the controller | bounded reply; on an index | - | `task.readTracking` **0.58 to 0.60 ms** (3 runs), reply **7,385 bytes**: 20 runs, the output cut to the limit. The Task has 1,000 runs, and its 19 runs before the newest hold 20 more iterations of the agent step each, with a session and a 50,000 character output (3.3 ms before the `run_steps_session` index of migration 0089, which the read of the runs' sessions walked past those outputs without). A newest output of 12 keys of 100,000 characters: 0.61 to 0.62 ms, 7,627 bytes, the labels cut to 80 characters. The test's ceilings are 50 ms and 20,000 bytes |
| The shell's read of Tracking, 1,000 tracked Tasks | on an index | `task.queryTracking` 7.07 ms, 559 kB (slice 2) | 6.8 to 6.9 ms (3 runs), 559 kB; 10 tracked Tasks 0.2 ms; the ceiling is 100 ms |
| The first screen's JavaScript | none new beyond the count's reads | 447.8 kB across 24 chunks (first-screen chunk 398,307 bytes) | **448.1 kB** across 24 chunks (398,557 bytes, 250 more; 447.9 kB and 398,481 bytes before the review fixes) |
| Intake's chunk | recorded; not budgeted | 34,377 bytes of JavaScript (97,803 raw), 1,891 of its own CSS, 4,074 of CSS shared with Tracking | **43,670** bytes (125,763 raw; 40,543 before the review fixes), **2,964** of its own CSS, the shared 4,074 unchanged |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 155.8 kB | 155.8 kB |

- **The pane costs two requests when it opens and none after, except the pushes that can change it.** `J` to another row costs the same two, and leaves the row it left alone: it is not read again until it is opened, and its read is kept in the cache. A push for a run no row holds sends nothing. What the test does not cover is a `task` push or a `signal` push while a pane is open. By the keys (`trackingDetail` is marked out of date by a `task` push that names the Task, or none) the detail is read once for a `task` push that names the Task. Whether the handoff signal is read again on a `signal` push was not checked. Neither was counted.
- **`IntakeScreen` is now compiled by the React Compiler.** *(Added 2026-10-21, [#803](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/803).)* Since before this ticket the compiler skipped the whole component ("Existing memoization could not be preserved"): it cannot keep a `useCallback` that reads a ref, and `runRowAction` was one. The function is now kept by `useState`, and `react-compiler.test.ts` covers the screen and the Tracking hooks. The compiled screen was not measured, because the perf script is paused: the render work of Intake should fall, not rise, but that is not shown.
- **The pane's chunk is 9.3 kB of JavaScript (9,293 bytes) and 1.1 kB of CSS bigger, all in Intake's chunk,** which is read when Intake opens, not on the first screen. The first screen is 0.3 kB bigger (250 bytes), which is probably the key and the push rule for the detail in the shell's live code. That was not checked. The first screen is still about 198 kB over its guide, and the guide does not gate.
- **The controller's read is far from its ceiling.** 0.6 ms for a Task with 1,000 runs, loops of large outputs and a 1 MB output, against 50 ms; the reply is 7.4 kB against 20 kB, because the runs are cut to 20, and the text, the values and the labels to their limits, whatever the history. `task.queryTracking` did not change by more than the spread of the runs. The controller's rows were measured again after the review fixes (the first handoff's workflow name, the cut labels and the `run_steps_session` index), with the speed test as it is now.
- **The footprint decision of slice 2 is still open.** The summed footprint missed its 220 MB in 15 of 32 launches of the earlier Your work design, and neither that design's final form nor the pane has been measured. The pane keeps one more read in the query cache while it is open, and the pane's DOM; what that costs in memory is not known. A run of the perf script is what settles it, once it is fast enough to run.

**Tracking, slice 4,** measured 2026-10-21 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks and the integration tests, on main at ef0c72bbc (Before) and on this change at 9385ceb2f (After), for [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804). *(Added 2026-10-21, [#804](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/804).)* The change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic. Each press of Try again sends one `run.rerun` and, when it succeeds, one `task.queryTracking` as a backstop, besides the reads the new run's `run` push already starts. **The perf script was not run**, as for slice 3.

| Measure | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| The first screen's JavaScript | none new | 448.1 kB gzipped across 24 chunks (first-screen chunk 1,256,649 bytes raw, 398,596 gzipped) | **448.1 kB** across 24 chunks (1,256,649 bytes raw, 398,593 gzipped) |
| Intake's chunk | recorded; not budgeted | 43,869 bytes gzipped (126,309 raw), 2,964 of its own CSS | **44,632** bytes gzipped (128,504 raw; 44,215 and 127,268 before the review fixes), 763 and 2,195 more; own CSS unchanged at 2,964 |
| Requests for a press of Try again | one `run.rerun`, then the backstop read | - | 1 `run.rerun` for a press; 0 for a second press and 0 for `E` while the re-run is starting. Tested in `intake.tracking.integration.test.tsx` ("the pane's Try again") |

- **All of the change is in Intake's chunk, none in the first screen's.** The first-screen chunk is the same size raw in both builds. Its gzipped size differs by 3 bytes, which comes from the content hash in its file name, not from code. `listTrackingAnswers` is in Intake's chunk too.
- **One new read for each press, no live topic or timer.** The backstop `task.queryTracking` makes sure the lock ends if the `run` push is lost. The new run's `run` push makes Tracking and the open pane read again, as before, and Tracking's read joins the backstop when one is under way.

**Done's trip, slice 5,** measured 2026-10-22 with `pnpm build:desktop`'s size checks (the renderer and main builds and both budget checks, without the packaging), the controller's speed test (`RUN_SPEED_TESTS=1`, `tracking.speed.test.ts`) and the integration and unit tests, on main at 34beb532 (Before) and on this change at 05f2cdb9 (After), for [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805). *(Added 2026-10-22, [#805](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/805).)* The change adds no process, no timer, no polling and no live topic. It adds one read, `task.queryLegs`, made after each Done page and for a pane whose leg is on no loaded page, and it makes the `task` and `run` pushes the shell already receives also mark the Done lists out of date. It adds one index (migration `0090-handoff-run`). **The perf script was not run**, because on 2026-10-21 Rogier paused desktop perf runs, so footprint, launch, idle wakeups and the main-thread time of a push are not measured. Both builds were made in clean throwaway worktrees. The later commits on the branch (7ce792ea and the ones after it) change component props, a client-core rule and an error line only; the bundle was not measured again after 05f2cdb9, so its bytes may differ by a few dozen. Sizes are gzipped bytes, counted the way the budget script counts them. The requests are counted with a fake client driven through `invalidateForPush`, the code path the shell's subscriptions take, and by `queries.test.ts`, `live.test.ts` and `intake.tracking.integration.test.tsx`. That is a count of requests the client sends, not of the wire, and it says nothing of time. The controller's read is the median of 25 reads in the speed test, run 3 to 4 times.

| `task.queryLegs` on the controller: 50 handoffs, each on a Task of 100 handoffs and a leg of 200 re-runs (half chained) | on an index; ceiling in the test | does not exist | **1.55 ms** median (1.55, 1.55, 1.61 in 4 runs), reply **24,703 bytes** (about 494 bytes a leg) |
| The same, all 50 handoffs on one Task of 10,000 handoffs | does not follow the Task's history | does not exist | **1.04 to 1.10 ms**, reply **24,536 bytes** |
| `task.queryTracking`, 1,000 tracked Tasks (unchanged; a regression check) | ceiling 100 ms | 14.2, 14.4, 14.3 ms; 559,025 bytes | 14.1 to 18.5 ms over the cases (14.51 ms in the first run); 559,025 bytes: unchanged within the spread |
| `task.queryTracking`, 10 tracked Tasks | - | 0.40 to 0.41 ms; 5,613 bytes | 0.39 ms; 5,613 bytes |
| Migration | adds an index only; no fixture needed | - | `0090-handoff-run` adds the partial index `task_provenance_handoff_run (run_id, task_id) WHERE signal_id IS NOT NULL`; no column and no row changes |

Requests, for the events that read a Done page (a Done page is one `signal.query`, then one `task.queryLegs`):

| Event | Budget | Before | After |
|---|---|---|---|
| One Done page read | 1 `signal.query` and at most 1 `task.queryLegs` | 1 `signal.query` | 1 `signal.query` and 1 `task.queryLegs` with the page's distinct run ids; **0** `task.queryLegs` when no signal on the page has a run id (`queries.test.ts`) |
| The foot's cleared-today count | no legs | 1 `signal.query` a page | the same, 0 legs (`queries.test.ts`) |
| A pane for a signal whose leg is on no loaded page | 1 read | - | 1 `task.queryLegs { runIds: [runId] }`, kept current like a Done list |
| A `task` push naming a held leg's Task | one read of the held pages | - | **+1 `signal.query` and +1 `task.queryLegs`** for each held page (3 pages held: +3 and +3; the pages are read one after the other) |
| A `task` push naming an unrelated Task | none | - | **0** |
| A `run` creation push while Done is shown | one read of the held pages | - | **+1 and +1** for each held page (a re-run is created before any leg holds it) |
| A `run` update push naming a held leg's newest or live run | one read of the held pages | - | **+1 and +1** for each held page |
| A `run` update push naming an unrelated run (a cron fire, for one) | none | - | **0** |
| A push while a Done page is being read | one more read after it settles | - | one more read; 3 relevant pushes in the same tick make 2 reads (1 running, 1 queued), not 3 (`live.test.ts`) |
| A push while Done is not shown | none | - | 0 requests: the lists are marked out of date and read when Done shows again |
| A `signal` push | unchanged | the Done lists read again, 1 request a page | the same and the page's `task.queryLegs`: 2 requests a page |
| Work while idle, work per streamed token, new processes, new timers | none | none | none (`ageClock` is unchanged and the legs do not use it) |

- **A page read grows from 1 request to 2, only for a page that holds a handoff.** A burst of relevant pushes joins into at most 2 reads. The reply grows by about 494 bytes a leg, 24.7 kB for a page of 50 handoffs, the largest case.
- **The controller's read does not follow the Task's history.** 50 handoffs on a Task of 10,000 handoffs read as fast as on a Task of 100 (about 1 ms), because the handoff of a leg is found by its run through the new index and the next one by time.
- **The spec's slice 2 row for Intake's chunk (34.34 kB) is older than the Before column here.** Other work between then and 34beb532 grew it to 43.7 kB, so compare the After column with the Before column of this table.
- **What is not measured.** The perf script is paused, so footprint, launch, idle wakeups and the main-thread time of a push are not measured. Nothing is added to boot, and the first screen grows by 886 bytes, so no change in idle or launch is expected, but that is a reading of the diff, not a measurement. The footprint decision of slice 2 stays open.

**The sidebar's rows as sessions of one workspace,** measured 2026-10-23 on atlas (an Apple Silicon Mac, 16 cores) with `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop perf -- --sidebar-groups-only`, on branch commit 70d2d36a, packaged app, for [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828). *(Amended 2026-10-23, [#828](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/828).)* This states what "What rows of sessions cost" in The sidebar promised. The new scenario has 500 Threads, shown as groups: one group of 10 (a busy lead and a waiting review), 12 groups of 3 of which 5 also hold an Agent session, 39 groups of 2 and 376 Threads alone. 100 of the lone Threads are Done, 40 of them 15 days or more ago. Two calm runs, at a one-minute load average of 6.5 and 5.5. The usual frame is 8.4 ms apart; style and layout is the time of every style and layout event on the renderer's main thread in one frame, added up.

| Measure | Budget | Measured |
|---|---|---|
| The sidebar's one read at launch | one read, every page | 465 sessions (460 Threads and 5 Agent sessions; the 40 Threads Done more than 14 days ago are left out), 2 requests (pages), 829,664 bytes, 79 to 81 ms |
| Mounted at rest | a few rows, not 500 | 20 items, 10 rows (each project shows at most 5 groups) |
| Unfolding the group of ten by its dots button: longest gap | no dropped frame | 17.0 to 17.8 ms (one dropped frame) |
| The same: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 1.25 to 1.56 ms |
| The same: longest task | - | 7.0 to 8.3 ms |
| Unfolding by `→`: longest gap | no dropped frame | 17.0 to 18.1 ms (one dropped frame) |
| The same: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 0.26 to 0.32 ms |
| The same: longest task | - | 3.0 to 3.3 ms |
| Mounted with the group of ten unfolded | a few rows | 20 items |
| A live push that changes one session's status: mounted items whose DOM changed | 1 | 1 of 20 |
| The same: longest gap | no dropped frame | 17.7 to 17.8 ms (one dropped frame) |
| The same: style and layout in one frame | at most 2 ms | 0.57 to 0.63 ms |
| The same: longest task | - | 6.3 to 7.5 ms |
| The first screen's JavaScript, gzipped | a guide of 250 kB | desktop **456.4 kB** (main 450.0 kB, +6.4 kB); web 233.5 kB of 250 kB |
| Main's startup file, minified | 160 kB | 155.8 kB, unchanged |
| Work per streamed token, idle work, new processes, timers | none | not measured: nothing in the sidebar runs per streamed token, and the change adds no timer, no polling and no process |

- **Style and layout passes in every frame of every run, and the dropped frames do not.** Each run misses the "no dropped frame" rule once per scenario, by one frame of about 17 to 18 ms. The worst style and layout is 1.56 ms, under the 2 ms limit.
- **Main shows the same single dropped frame.** `--sidebar-only` on main on this machine: Hiding 16.9 to 17.0 ms, Dragging 17.0 to 17.4, Pressing the edge 17.1 to 20.5, `→` 17.1 to 17.4, with 2.05 to 2.09 ms of style and layout. On the branch (load 4.5): Hiding 24.8 ms gap and 0.81 ms of style and layout, Showing passes (9.3 ms), Dragging 17.6 and 0.83, Pressing 16.7 and 0.82, `→` 16.8 and 1.61. So the branch makes nothing worse, and its style and layout is lower than main's. One dropped frame of this size also showed in [The sidebar's layout](#the-sidebars-layout), where it was not traced to the sidebar's work. The Hiding gap of 24.8 ms is the largest and was seen in one run; it was not looked into.
- **A live push redraws one row.** Of the 20 mounted items, only the one that holds the changed session changed its DOM.
- **The read is bigger than the old one for the same Threads, by design.** It holds Agent sessions as well. The 829,664 bytes are for 465 sessions with long fixture titles, and the old read for 292 real Threads was about 500 KB, so the two are not like for like. The read does not bound Agent sessions by time, because they are never Done.
- **The bundle grows by 6.4 kB gzipped on the desktop's first screen.** Main's startup file is unchanged.
- **Not measured:** per-token cost (nothing in the sidebar runs per streamed token).

## Slices

Each slice is a reviewable change. The performance budgets guide it and do not gate it ([Performance](#performance)), except the Office's budgets, which gate slices 9 and 10 *(amended 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332))*.

1. **Shell and safety.**
   - the `apps/desktop` package
   - `tokens.css` and the font files, because the window's background needs `--bg` in both appearances
   - the `app` scheme and the CSP
   - the security baseline
   - the window: title bar, no flash, theme, remembered state, single instance
   - the IPC contract
   - the dep-lint rules
   - the perf script, and the baseline measured again in the real shell
   - the first end-to-end test
2. **Connect and sign in.**
   - CORS on the controller
   - the connect screen
   - the stored token
   - sign in and sign out. Sign Out lives in the app menu, so this slice brings forward the Sign Out item and the `menu.command` channel. The rest of the menu stays in slice 8.
   - the refused arguments
3. **Design foundation.**
   - font preloading
   - faces, poses, icons and marks as typed modules
   - the live theme
   - the screenshot comparison tool, run on the pieces: slice 3 draws no screen that a Bureau page shows
4. **Sidebar.** Waiting on you, New thread, and threads grouped by project and workspace, kept live.
5. **Thread view.**
   - the transcript and streaming. ~~`transcript.read` returns the whole transcript, with no paging.~~ *(Amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275): `transcript.read` has pages of up to 500 rows since slice 2. The app reads every page in order before it draws the thread, as the web app does.)* A performance pass measures a long thread.
   - ~~turn dividers~~ work stretch dividers *(amended 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275), see **The thread** in [Design system](#design-system))* and markdown
   - the Requests dock
   - queued inputs, with Steer and Cancel
   - the composer drawn inert, at its full size, so the dock and the queued inputs sit where they will stay *(added 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275))*
6. **The composer on an active thread.**
   - send, steer and queue, stop
   - the model and its options, which stay live
7. **New threads.**
   - the project picker and the Draft Thread
   - the pickers, with the local-runner probe through main
   - starting the thread
8. **Menu and notifications.** The menu, shortcuts, dock badge and notifications.
9. **The Office.** *(Added 2026-10-03, [Office v1 in the desktop app (#332)](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* [The Office](#the-office), on the user's real threads:
   - the prototype's code moved to `apps/desktop/src/renderer/office/`, without the Tower, the Campus, the controls and Simulate
   - the route, the sidebar's top row and Go › Office
   - `decideOfficeSeating` in `@hercule/client-core`, with its tests, and the Bureau's `OfficeMap` value
   - the card, answering from it, and the drawer
   - the performance work: the 30-frame cap, standing still on battery and with Reduce motion, no ambient occlusion, shadows drawn once, and the static furniture merged by material
   - the bug fixes: colleagues walking through walls, legs inside chairs and sofas, poor paths, and hats painted on
   - room names and plaques in the UI face
   - the first measurement, recorded in [Measured](#measured)
10. **The Office's life.** *(Added 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* Printer rage, the tache on four of eight looks, and the Office's own details on the colleagues.

*(Added 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* Slices 11 to 19 build [Settings](#settings). Slice 11 comes first, and slice 13 needs slice 12 and slice 16 needs slice 15; the others can be built in any order after slice 11. Each slice records its costs in [Measured](#measured), as [What Settings costs](#what-settings-costs) lists them.

11. **Settings' frame, with Profile and System.**
    - the `/settings` layout route and one child route per section, each a chunk of its own

*(Added 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448).)* Slices 20 to 22 build [the assistant](#design-system), in order. Slice 22's heartbeat needs slice 16's cron functions, and its Open Conversation needs slice 15. Each slice records its costs in [Measured](#measured), as [What the assistant costs](#what-the-assistant-costs) lists them. *(Amended 2026-10-07, [#454](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/454).)* Slice 21 shipped without recording them; see [Measured](#measured).

20. **Assistants in the sidebar.**
    - the `assistant` topic in the shell, and each assistant's newest session, read by `Assistant.mainConversationId`

    - the `/assistants/$id` route, drawing ~~the floating header~~ the floating header's left pill (face, name and word) over an empty Conversation; the drawer's pill and "`<name>`'s record" come with slice 22 *(amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453))*

22. **The drawer.**

*(Added 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Slice 23 builds Settings' Permission profiles section, and records its costs in [Measured](#measured), as [What Settings costs](#what-settings-costs) lists them. It needs slice 11, the frame.

23. **Permission profiles.**
    - the list, its table and New profile
    - a profile's page: Name, Grants with their toggles, Used by and Delete
    - the confirmation for a change to `unrestricted`, and the refusal shown in the delete confirmation
    - the Bureau comparison of the four states of the book's page

*(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* Slice 24 builds [the Hercule face](#the-hercule-face) ([#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512)). Slices 25 to 35 build [Intake](#intake), in order, after slice 24. Each of them is vertical: it ships its backend, as the specs that own it describe, and its desktop part together. So Intake shows from slice 25, and fills in as each feature lands. Slice 36 stands alone and needs none of them. Each slice records its costs in [Measured](#measured), as [What the Hercule face costs](#what-the-hercule-face-costs) and [What Intake costs](#what-intake-costs) list them; a slice that changes the bundle or the work done while idle names the rows it records.

24. **The Hercule face.** The sidebar gains the Threads | Hercule switch, with the System section's Fleet and Connections rows, the face rule, and View › Threads `⌥⌘1` and View › Hercule `⌥⌘2`. Records: the first screen's JavaScript, memory and idle, with the Hercule face showing.
25. **The first signal on screen.** ([#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524)) A signal raised with `hercule signal raise` shows on Intake. The slice adds the Signal record and `signal.raise`, `signal.query`'s To do view, `signal.read`, `signal.act` and `signal.withdraw`, so a proposal raised by hand can be accepted and creates its Task; the Intake row, Go › Intake `⌘⇧I` and the Hercule segment's count; the list in its Now and Signals sections; the pane with all four block types and the fallback line; the answers with their describe lines and the Reply box; the keys; the split and its minimums; and the notification for a new Now signal, with `urgentSignals.set`. Blocked by slice 24 ([#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512)). Records: the first screen's JavaScript, Intake's chunk, idle with Intake open, and opening or closing the split.
26. **Done, Snooze and Not urgent.** ([#525](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/525)) A signal can leave To do by the user's hand. The slice adds `signal.markDone`, `signal.snooze`, `signal.unsnooze` and `signal.lowerPriority`; the row controls and checks; the bulk bar and its highlight; the snooze menu and "Back" rows; the Later and Done views; the foot; and the toast. Records: idle with a snooze due in an hour, because the snooze timer is the screen's one idle timer.
27. **GitHub review requests and mentions.** ([#526](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/526)) The first plugin signals arrive: a review request and a mention raise signals on their own. The slice adds the plugin path that raises them, the manifest's marks on tabs, rows and the provenance line, the GitHub tab, GitHub's answers, and "Resolved elsewhere" when the source answers.
28. **GitHub's other kinds.** ([#527](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/527)) Assignment, requested changes and failed checks raise signals too. The slice adds those kinds, and the Checks block's log lines. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#527](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/527).)* Built with no change to the renderer: slice 25 already draws the Checks block with its log lines, so this slice records no rows.
29. **Settings › Intake.** ([#528](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/528)) The user can switch each signal kind on or off, and choose which event kinds are available to triage. The slice adds the [Intake section](#the-sections) with its Signals and Events available to triage lists, `plugin.updateIntake`, `connection.update`'s `intake`, and the provenance line's link with its `kind` and `connection` parameters. Records: the section's chunk.
30. **Everything.** ([#529](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/529)) The Everything view lists Intake's events and what came of each. The slice adds `event.query`'s `intake` flag, the handlings, the search and the filter, and the event pane. Records: the view's memory with a long list.
31. **Screening and the Screener.** ([#530](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/530)) A signal can wait for the shipped Screener before it shows. The slice adds the Screener and the Screening path, the "Waiting for the Screener" and "Screened out" handlings, and the "Not screened" lines in the pane.
32. **Gmail signals.** ([#531](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/531)) Mail that needs the user shows on Intake. The slice adds Gmail's signal kinds, its tab and mark, and the Messages block's recipients and attachments.
33. **Ignore Rules.** ([#532](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/532)) The user can stop a kind of signal by its match fields. The slice adds Ignore Rules, Hercule's offer in Signals, "Ignore signals like this…" and its dialog, the Ignore Rules list in Settings › Intake, and the "ignored" handling in Everything.
34. **The Triage tab.** ([#533](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/533)) Triage's signals get their own place. The slice adds the shipped Triage workflow raising core signals, the From triage section, and the Triage tab with its header, summary and workflow picker. A proposal's Accept already works from slice 25, where a proposal can be raised by hand.
35. **Inbox zero and changes from elsewhere.** ([#534](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/534)) Intake moves when something happens. The slice adds inbox zero's arrival and its line, the motion of signals that arrive and leave without the user, bursts as one block, and nothing moving under the pointer. Records: per push with a burst of 30, and inbox zero.
36. **Launch on the last screen.** ([#535](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/535)) The app reopens where the user left it. The slice adds the stored last screen for each controller URL, Open on's Where I left off, and reading a stored `threads` value and an old stored thread id. It needs no other slice. Records: the launch budget, reopening a thread and reopening Intake.

37. **The workflow list.** ([#550](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/550)) *(Added 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* The user sees every workflow and how it is doing. The slice adds Go › Workflows `⌘⇧W` and the Hercule face's Workflows row; `/workflows` with the list as a table: its columns, the groups, the filters with their counts, and search; the reads behind them, with `run` and `workflow` held while the page is mounted; "From runs" in Waiting on you, a shell read; the one virtualized list the sidebar and the table share; and the list's interpretation in client-core. Blocked by the contract additions ([#549](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/549)). Records: the first screen's JavaScript, the list's chunk, and the reads one `run` push causes with 50 live runs across 10 workflows.
38. **The open workflow.** ([#551](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/551)) The user opens a workflow and sees what it does and how its runs went. The slice adds `/workflows/$workflowId` with the column and full widths; the lead with the workflow's switch and the chips; the graph with no run, and a step's card; the four tabs with their keys; each start trigger's pause switch; the Runs tab's List and Timeline with the day stepper; and one graph layout both apps import. Blocked by slice 37. Records: the open workflow's chunk, with the YAML parser and the graph layout in it, and that the first screen did not grow.
39. **A run's page.** ([#552](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/552)) The user follows one run, answers it and starts it again. The slice adds `/runs/$runId`: the crumb, the lead and the earlier-version note; Graph and Timeline; the run on the graph and a node's card with Open thread; the Cancel and Re-run dialogs; and what the web app's run page shows besides. Blocked by slice 38. Records: the run page's chunk, and that the first screen did not grow.
40. **Motion on Workflows.** ([#553](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/553)) The pages move when something changes, and only then. The slice adds `mark-in`, the open pane's settle, the strip's step, the run moving on the graph with the ring's ping, and the timeline's arrival ([Workflows' motion](#workflows-motion)). Blocked by slice 39. Records: per pushed change, which elements animate and that they run on the compositor alone; the timeline's arrival.

## Testing

- **Unit tests** sit next to the code they test (AGENTS.md §Source layout). They cover:
  - main's logic
  - decoding against the IPC contract
  - that a face's seed always gives the same face
- **Renderer component tests** work as they do in `apps/web`.
- **End-to-end tests** live in `e2e/desktop/`. They use Playwright's Electron driver to launch the test package (see [Security baseline](#security-baseline)) against the compiled `hercule` binary, in a scratch `HERCULE_HOME`. Each slice adds at least one. They run the production origin, so they cover the CORS path. ~~CI runs them on macOS, the target platform.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-01, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* They run on a developer's Mac, before a pull request that changes the desktop app, and not in CI. They check windows, pixels and timing, and CI's virtual Macs differ from a user's Mac in all three: a 1024 × 768 screen at 1x with the Dock showing, Reduce motion and Reduce transparency on, and a slow machine. There, 5 of the 98 tests that pass on a developer's Mac failed. In one, macOS shrank a saved 1000 × 700 window to fit a work area 679 points high, as it should, and the test expected the window to keep its size.
  - *(Amended 2026-10-01, [#308](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/308).)* One file does run in CI: the `edge-build` job runs `e2e/desktop/package.test.ts` on the certificate-signed release package, which only that job builds, under the hardened runtime. The test checks the fuses, `app.asar`, the signature, and that the app starts and quits; none of that depends on the screen or the machine's speed.
  - Each launch passes `--user-data-dir=<scratch dir>`, so the settings file, the token and the single-instance lock never touch the real app's.
  - Each launch passes `--use-mock-keychain`, so no test touches the real Keychain.
  - Each launch removes `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` and every `HERCULE_*` variable from Electron's environment.
  - *(Added 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* Each launch that opens main's inspector passes `--hercule-binary=<path in the user data dir>`, so main runs that path in place of `~/.local/bin/hercule` and no test runs this Mac's own `hercule service`: even `status` reads this Mac's launchd, and `install` writes `~/Library/LaunchAgents/` whatever the Home is. With no file there, main finds no binary. A first-run test writes a stand-in there with `e2e/desktop/stand-in-binary.ts`: a script with a scratch Home that answers `service status --json`, `service install --json` and `setup-url`. Its `serve` kind starts the compiled `hercule serve` in that Home on install, in a session of its own as launchd would, with a `PATH` of only `/usr/bin` and `/bin`, so its runner finds no coding agent and the providers step is never done whatever the Mac has installed *(amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313))*; the others fail the install with one line (`start-error`), report a runner (`runner`), or report no Service Unit with nothing answering (`fresh`). Main accepts the switch only where it accepts every argument: in a development run, or with the inspector open. A release package refuses it.
- **Screenshots.** Every slice takes light and dark screenshots of its screens, and they are compared with the Bureau pages before review.
  - *(Added 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop capture:sidebar-states` captures the sidebar states the book never draws, in both themes, for a check by eye: ~~workspace labels~~ workspace lines and unsent rows *(amended 2026-10-08, [#473](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/473))*, offline, queued, asleep and away threads, long names, the caps and their "more" rows, and "No project". It writes them to `apps/desktop/out/sidebar-states/`.
- **The Bureau comparison.** `pnpm compare:bureau` compares the app's pieces with the book's, pixel for pixel, in Whitehaven and Orient Express. CI runs it.
  - Two sheets draw the same cells in a 1440 × 900 window: the reference sheet with the book's own `crew.js` from ~~`docs/design/crew-bureau/`~~ `docs/design/crew-bureau-2/` *(amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313))*, the specimen sheet with the app's components. A cell is a face in a pose, size, shape or wardrobe, the user avatar, a mark or an icon.
  - *(Added 2026-09-29, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* It also compares the app's sidebar with the sidebar of the book's `session-active.html`, item by item, with the app fed the book's threads at the book's time.
  - *(Added 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* It also compares the app's thread screen with the main pane of the book's `session-active.html`, item by item, twice: once at rest, and once with the transcript scrolled away from its bottom and the composer shrunk. The book's page is edited where the app draws other words or marks (see **The thread** in [Design system](#design-system)).
  - *(Added 2026-09-30, [#275](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/275).)* It also compares the app's draft screen with the main pane of the book's `session-empty.html`, item by item, with the book's page edited where the app draws other words or marks (see **A new thread** in [Design system](#design-system)).
  - *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#551](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/551).)* It also compares the app's Workflows page with the main pane of the book's `workflows.html`, item by item, with the app fed the book's workflows and runs at the book's time: the list as a table, and Ship release open, with its runs as a list, beside the list drawn as a column (`?view=split`) and across the whole pane (`?view=full`), and with today's runs on the timeline (`?view=full&runs=timeline`). Only the items inside the window count, because the app draws only the rows near it. The book's page is edited in two places: the timeline's hour axis moves into the tab band, where [The open workflow](#workflows) puts it; and Ship release's `open_pr` step draws the GitHub mark, because a plugin's action step draws its plugin's mark ([Workflows](#workflows)), where the book draws a puzzle piece for every action. The app is fed the GitHub plugin with the mark it declares. Neither page draws the graph's fade at a cut edge: Chromium draws the masked pane a little differently from one capture to the next, on either page. The items still check that both pages cut the graph on the same side.
  - Both sheets render in one Electron, hidden, at DPR 2, in sRGB, with GPU rasterization off. With GPU rasterization, a change in one cell also moved pixels in its neighbours, and the result could vary with the machine's GPU.
  - It fails first if the book's `tokens.css` or font files differ from the app's, or if any cell sits in a different place, so each pixel it reports is a drawing difference.
  - The comparison is ~~exact: no tolerance,~~ near exact: a pixel differs when one of its channels differs by more than 8 levels of 255, on every channel of every device pixel *(Amended 2026-10-12, [#553](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/553): once the open workflow pane has faded in, Chromium draws its graph up to 3 levels off for the life of the page. No eye sees that, and a wrong token or a moved piece differs by far more.)*. It writes the reference, the app's capture and a diff image to `apps/desktop/out/bureau-compare/`.
  - The specimen sheet is served only by the dev server. The release build never bundles it, and an eslint rule forbids importing it.
- **The first-frame check.** `apps/desktop/scripts/first-frame.ts` records the window with ScreenCaptureKit as macOS first puts it on screen, and checks that it already holds the whole first screen, focus ring included, for the sign-in screen and the shell in both themes. Re-run it after every Electron upgrade. It rests on Chromium behaviour no other test covers, and an upgrade can change any of it:
  - a hidden window still draws and presents its page's frames;
  - an Element Timing entry's `renderTime` is when its frame was presented, or failed to present;
  - `show()` shows the frame the window holds, not an empty one.
- *(Added 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332).)* **The Office's tests:**
  - `decideOfficeSeating` has unit tests for the rooms and their order, the desks inside a room, the queue and the Lounge, and the threads left out;
  - an end-to-end test opens the Office from the sidebar's button and from `⌘⇧O`, and checks that a hidden window draws no frames.
- *(Added 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* **Settings' tests:**
  - `pnpm compare:bureau` compares the main pane of each of the book's three settings pages with the app's section, item by item, then pixel for pixel, in Whitehaven and Orient Express: Appearance, Assistants and Connections. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Permission profiles joins them, in the four states of `settings-profiles.html`: the list, Reviewer's page, `unrestricted`'s page, and `unrestricted`'s page with the confirmation open. The app is fed the book's profiles and their agents. The book's sidebar is not compared there, because it is the Hercule face; the sidebar is compared against `session-active.html` as before. As for the thread, the book's page is edited where the app leaves something out or draws other words ([Settings](#settings)): the Assistants page loses the role, the line under the name, ~~Open Conversation,~~ *(amended 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448): Open Conversation stays)* the context bar, Start fresh, the bindings and the Memory and Reminders column *(amended 2026-10-11, [#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93)): the Reminders section; Memory stays, with its topics in name order, a size under 1,000 characters as its exact count, the fixture's core note and New topic)*; the Connections page loses ~~the stats strip, three columns~~ the Default Topic column, the Channels section with the strip's count of channels, and the Cron row *(amended 2026-10-17, [#718](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/718))* ~~and the Channels section~~; the Appearance page takes the desktop's Density line.
  - The book's `settings-assistants.html` draws its sidebar's and its Settings list's group headings larger than its two other settings pages do. Slice 16 finds the cause. If the page is at fault, the slice fixes the book's page, as design tickets edit the book, and says so in its pull request.
  - `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop capture:settings` captures every section in all five themes, and Appearance at each text size and density, for a check by eye. It writes them to `apps/desktop/out/settings/`. The sections the book does not draw are checked this way.
  - Unit tests cover the heartbeat's two `client-core` functions, main's Appearance in the settings file, `theme-init.js` with a stored Appearance (one theme, and the day and night themes as macOS changes), and the window background of all five themes.
  - An end-to-end test opens Settings from the foot button and from `⌘,`, checks that the inert rows do nothing, changes the theme and the glass level, relaunches, and checks that the window's first frame already has the chosen theme. A second checks that Open on The office launches into the Office. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* The first no longer checks Permission profiles' row, which now leads to its section. A test of its own, `e2e/desktop/settings-permission-profiles.test.ts`, creates a profile in Permission profiles, changes one of its grants, renames it and deletes it through its confirmation, and checks that a shipped profile shows no Delete.
- *(Added 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448).)* **The assistant's tests:**
  - `pnpm compare:bureau` compares the Conversation's column with the book's `assistant.html`, item by item, then pixel for pixel, in Whitehaven and Orient Express: a reply, an owner's message, a day stamp, a notice and the composer. The book's page is edited where the app leaves something out or draws other words: the quiet check-ins, the refs chips, the reminder card, the action buttons, "heartbeat · 09:00", the mention chip, the channel pick and the model pill go, and the notice loses its bold. *(Amended 2026-10-07, [#454](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/454): the composer's lip goes too, because memory and heartbeats are not built yet.)* The header and the drawer are not compared, because the book draws a bar and a rail; they are checked by eye, against the prototype.
  - `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop capture:assistant` captures ~~the Conversation and the open drawer in all five themes, and the sidebar with an assistant in each pose,~~ *(amended 2026-10-06, [#453](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/453): the sidebar with an assistant in each pose, plus the assistant screen (header pill, empty Conversation, not found), in all five themes; ~~the Conversation and the open drawer join it with slice 22~~)* *(amended 2026-10-07, [#454](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/454): the Conversation's scenes join it with slice 21, in all five themes; the open drawer joins it with slice 22)* for a check by eye.
  - An end-to-end test opens an assistant from the sidebar, sends a message, answers a Request on the dock and stops a turn, against a real controller whose assistant runs on a scripted runner, as the composer's test does. A second checks that the window, hidden while a reply streams, drops the tap and shows the whole reply when shown again.
- *(Added 2026-10-10, [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512).)* **The Hercule face's tests:**
  - `pnpm compare:bureau` compares the face switch as part of the sidebar, against `session-active.html`, item by item, then pixel for pixel, in Whitehaven and Orient Express. The Hercule face itself is checked by eye: `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop capture:sidebar-states` captures it.
  - A unit test covers the face rule, `decideScreenFace`. Integration tests cover the switch, its arrow keys and the list its tabs control, View's two commands, the inert Fleet and Connections rows with the Connections dot, the face following the screen, a new Draft Thread included, a Waiting on you row that keeps its focus when it opens a thread, and the Go menu, which lists the Threads face's rows on either face.
  - End-to-end tests change the face from the switch, from View › Threads and View › Hercule, and with ← and →, without leaving the open thread, and check that the switch, inside the window's drag strip, does not drag the window. A second checks that the Office and Settings keep the Hercule face and that opening a thread shows the Threads face.
- *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395).)* **Intake's tests:**
  - Unit tests cover each `client-core` function Intake calls ([What the screen reads](#what-the-screen-reads)): the sections and their order, the row controls, the tabs and More, the bulk plan with its wording, the foot's counts, the triage header, the snooze choices at each hour that changes them, the handlings' cut-off, and the inbox zero line at each moment, the weekend included.
  - `pnpm compare:bureau` compares the app's Intake with the Intake drawing, [`docs/design/intake-directions/asks/desktop/intake.html`](../design/intake-directions/asks/desktop/intake.html), item by item, then pixel for pixel, in Whitehaven and Orient Express. The app is fed the drawing's signals at the drawing's time. As for the thread, the drawing's page is edited where the app draws other words: ~~the section names, the `↩` hint and the toast.~~ *(amended 2026-10-11, [#524](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/524): the list is the one `apps/desktop/src/renderer/specimens/intake-reference.ts` keeps, and the drawing's page is edited where the app leaves something out or draws other words.)* The edits remove what later tickets build: the views over the list, the hourly triage (its tab, its section and its header line), the "waiting" labels, the cleared count, the Snooze… and Stop asking answers, the fine print under an answer, and the pane's foot. They change the alert row's time to its age, the All tab's count to the rows that are left, Marta's message time to the app's clock, the middle section's name to "Signals", the alert row's line to who asks, the kind and where, the provenance line to the source and the kind's label, the asked line, the keys (E, H and M removed, Esc added), and the GitHub kinds' labels for a review and for checks. They add the avatar before a message's author, which the drawing does not draw, with the app's centred message head. The sidebar is compared against `session-active.html` as before, and the Hercule face is checked by eye. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#525](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/525).)* Done, Snooze and Not urgent bring back what the edits removed for them: the views over the list (To do with its count of the rows that are left, Later and Done), the cleared count, the Snooze… answer, and the E and H keys. The edits now remove only the hourly triage, the "waiting" labels, the Stop asking answer with its M key, the fine print under an answer, and the pane's foot. The cleared count reads "3 left on their own" where the drawing reads "3 of them left on their own", and ends in a full stop after "See Done", as [The foot, the toast and failures](#intake) writes it.
  - `pnpm --filter @hercule/desktop capture:intake` captures the states the drawing does not show, in all five themes, for a check by eye: each block type and the fallback line, the build-failed line, a failed answer, Resolved elsewhere, the bulk highlight, Later, Done, Everything and its event pane, the Triage tab with a failed run, the Ignore dialog, inbox zero, tabs moved into More, and the pane hidden in a narrow window. It writes them to `apps/desktop/out/intake/`.
  - End-to-end tests, each against the compiled binary in a scratch `HERCULE_HOME`. The tests of the screen raise their signals with `hercule signal raise`:
    - Intake opens from the Hercule face's row and from `⌘⇧I`, and the face follows the screen; `⌥⌘1`, `⌥⌘2` and the switch's arrow keys change the face without leaving the screen.
    - The Hercule segment's count follows a raised signal, a Done and a snooze.
    - A new Now signal notifies only while the window is not focused, and a click on the notification opens Intake with the signal selected.
    - The keys answer a signal: `J`, `↩`, a second `↩`, and `E` on a checked row.
    - Settings › Intake switches a kind off for one Connection, and Reset brings it back.
    - The app relaunches on Intake, and on a thread, when that was the last screen.
    - With the window hidden, the snooze timer still brings a signal back and its notification shows.
- **The check commands.** The four check commands (AGENTS.md §Check commands) cover `apps/desktop` like every other package.

## Post-v1

The desktop app is itself post-v1 in [./01-overview-and-scope.md](./01-overview-and-scope.md). These are what later milestones add after the first:

- **The Hercule face's other screens:** ~~Intake,~~ Check-in, Tasks, Runs, ~~Workflows, Fleet, Connections~~ and Notifications. Bureau adds the office to them. *(Amended 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#512](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/512).)* The face and Intake are slices 24 to 35. Fleet and Connections are rows that open Settings › Machines and Settings › Connections; screens of their own come later, if ever. *(Amended 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548).)* Workflows is slices 37 to 40.
- *(Added 2026-10-11, [#548](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/548); [#542](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/542).)* **On Workflows:**
  - creating and editing a workflow ([#543](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/543));
  - Run now, with the run form;
  - the Runs screen: every run of every workflow, as a day board and a list;
  - a row that changes group moving there with motion, as Intake's rows do; until then it moves in one frame;
  - "From runs" on the dock badge, in notifications and in the Go menu;
  - Office desks for the agents of one run ([#471](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/471)).
- *(Added 2026-10-10, [#395](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/395); [#394](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/394).)* **On Intake:**
  - Undo, for Done, snooze and answers;
  - raising a signal to Now, the reverse of Not urgent;
  - "Accept and start *agent*" on a proposal, which creates the Task and starts work on it;
  - ordering the list by hand ([#513](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/513));
  - Run now on the Triage tab;
  - the web app's Intake ([./14-web-app.md](./14-web-app.md#the-intake-view));
  - images in blocks loaded through the controller, not from their source ([#503](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/503)).
- ~~**Assistants,** with~~ *(Amended 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448): assistants are slices 20 to 22.)* **The stored look:** the book's stored look (Spec change 3) and a run that wears its workflow's face (Spec change 4).
- *(Added 2026-10-06, [#448](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/448).)* **On an assistant's Conversation** ([#450](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/450)): the drawer's Heartbeat, Reminders and Memory live, once heartbeats fire ([#94](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/94)) and operations read reminders and memory ([#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93)); the header pill's numbers and the composer's lip with them; the refs chips; and channels, with Slack and Discord conversations.
- **All sessions ~~and Settings,~~ ~~including Appearance: Bureau's five themes, System, and the glass level~~.** *(Amended 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* Settings is slices 11 to 19, except its Providers section, which comes with the provider remodel ([#406](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/406)).
- *(Added 2026-10-04, [#402](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/402).)* **Settings' Identities, Secrets and Bounds,** drawn inert until then. *(Amended 2026-10-09, [#494](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/494).)* Permission profiles is slice 23. **On Assistants:** ~~memory,~~ reminders, channel bindings and Start fresh *(amended 2026-10-11, [#93](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/93))*,, once operations read and write them. **On Connections:** the event counts and the Channels section, once operations read them.
- **The desktop app as installer:** it ~~runs and~~ installs and upgrades ~~a local controller~~ Hercule's binary ([./15-packaging-and-operations.md](./15-packaging-and-operations.md) §Post-v1). Starting a local controller with the binary already there is in v1, as part of [the first run](#the-first-run) *(amended 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313))*.
- ~~*(Added 2026-10-02, [#313](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/313).)* **The live Office.** The first run's still room grows into the Office: live updates, the other wings, filing cabinets and capsules in Triage's tube.~~ *(Amended 2026-10-03, [#332](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/332): the live Office is slice 9.)* **The Office Map system:** growth by wings built as the fleet grows, more maps, importing a map, maps of a code base, colleagues that go where their work takes them, and more activities ([#336](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/336)). Event flow and the tubes ([#331](https://github.com/theagenticage/hercule/issues/331)). The first run's room drawn as a still view of the 3D Office.
- **The idle blink, back.** Bureau's idle blink returns once research shows how to draw it within the idle budget. Bureau draws it as an animation that repeats every 7.2 seconds on the SVG group of each face's eyes. The eyes move for only about 0.2 seconds of that, but Chromium draws frames for the whole 7.2 seconds. And because an SVG group is animated on the renderer's main thread, the renderer wakes for every frame. Techniques to measure:
  - one shared timer that starts a single 0.2-second blink on one face at a time, so frames are drawn only while an eye is actually closing
  - eyes drawn in their own compositor layer, so a blink never wakes the renderer's main thread
  - blinking only faces in view, and only while the window has focus
- **Auto-update.**
- **Several controllers.**
- **Windows and Linux.** Main keeps the platform calls in one place so they can be swapped.
- **Destroying the renderer when the window closes,** while main keeps a light watch for the dock badge and notifications. This is a candidate if measurements show that the hidden renderer's memory matters. In the first milestone, closing the window hides it ([Native behaviour](#native-behaviour)).
